# Audit: the Raspberry Pi side of Projection5000

Date: 20-21 September 2026. Branch `audit-pi` from `origin/main` at `4a5f6e6`. Scope: first boot, provisioning, the player daemon, updates, and everything else that runs on the Pi (installer, systemd units, mpv, camera, projector power, tunnel). The flasher GUI and the console are out of scope except where the Pi depends on them. Nothing was fixed; this is the report.

## Summary for Matt

1. Nothing found loses videos or wrecks a Pi outright: no Critical finding. Eight High findings should be fixed before more players go out; most are a few lines each.
2. On Raspberry Pi OS from April 2026 onwards, the login user the flasher creates cannot run admin commands: its password is random and never shown, and the OS no longer allows admin work without one. Every "log in and run this" instruction in the docs is a dead end. One line in the first-boot script fixes it (H1).
3. A projector driven by a one-button remote can end up the wrong way round (on at night, off in the day), because the player re-sends the power code every time it restarts, including after the nightly update. The docs promise it never does this (H6).
4. If the projector or its IR blaster is not answering at the moment the schedule says "on", the player never tries again that day (H7).
5. If power is cut during a software update, or during the first-time install, the Pi can be left with a black screen and no way to recover without a visit (H2, H5).
6. Sound goes to the headphone socket, not to the projector over HDMI, on every board except the Pi 5 (H3).
7. When the projector cannot show anything (unplugged at boot, a bad video file) the projector shows a fault screen, but the console says the device is fine (H4).
8. The hardware checklist says to tape a spare flashed card inside the projector mount. That card holds the console's enrollment key in clear text, which can unlock every device (H8).
9. Most error messages the console and the projector show are developer text (program names, exit codes, web addresses, Linux paths). A dozen findings; one pass rewords them all.
10. The docs and the code disagree in thirteen places (which Pis get remote access or a camera, what first boot looks like, what "Update all players" does), and there are eleven gaps in the tests, each listed with the test to add.

## How this audit was done

- Read every line of `tools/flasher/firstboot.py`, the rendered `firstrun.sh` and `projection5000-provision.sh` (four variants: Wi-Fi, Ethernet with static IP, enrollment key, device token), `player/deploy/*` (installer, both update scripts, four units, `mpv.conf`), all of `player/player/*.py`, the tests, and the docs, plus the console's `cloud/src/api.js`, `manifest.js`, `cloudflare.js` and `alerts.js` where the Pi depends on them.
- Upstream sources were checked rather than assumed: `RPi-Distro/userconf-pi` (`userconf`, `cancel-rename`), `raspi-config`, `raspberrypi-sys-mods` (`imager_custom`, `98-rpi.conf`), `pi-gen`, the Raspberry Pi OS Lite release notes (2026-09-15 image), `python3-pip-whl` (piwheels `pip.conf`), piwheels' Trixie wheel index, and mpv's `ipc-unix.c`.
- `bash -n` and shellcheck 0.11 (`-S style`) on every script and rendered variant: clean. `player` tests: 277 pass. `tools/flasher` tests: 97 pass. Where a claim could be settled locally it was, with the existing bash fake harnesses (`test_installer_pi.py`, `test_updater.py`, `test_firstboot.py`) or a scratch script.
- Ten independent readers, one per subsystem, produced 126 candidate findings. After de-duplication each candidate was checked twice: once by a reviewer told to refute it against the real code, once by a reviewer calibrating severity, the fix and the test coverage. Fifteen candidates were rejected (listed at the end, with the reason). A final pass looked for checklist items nobody had covered and added one.
- No Pi was available. Every finding that needs hardware carries a "Verify on hardware" block with the exact commands; the ids are collected in one list near the end.

Severity: **Critical** = data loss, bricked Pi, remote root, or the owner stuck without a re-flash on a common path. **High** = a common path fails or a real security hole. **Medium** = wrong under plausible conditions, misleading text to the owner, unpinned supply chain. **Low** = hygiene, cosmetic, edge case. Findings are numbered H1..H8, M1..M45, L1..L34 in severity order; the table below is the index.

## Findings by severity

87 findings after de-duplication: 0 Critical, 8 High, 45 Medium, 34 Low.

| # | Severity | Area | Where | Finding |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| H1 | High | First boot | `tools/flasher/firstboot.py:274` | Pi user has no usable sudo: random never-shown password and no NOPASSWD rule on current Trixie images |
| H2 | High | Provisioning | `player/deploy/install-player.sh:180` | Power cut during apt leaves dpkg interrupted; every retry and every later boot fails the same way |
| H3 | High | systemd units and mpv.conf | `player/deploy/mpv.conf:39` | mpv audio goes to the analogue jack (ALSA card 0 = Headphones), not HDMI, on every board but the Pi 5 |
| H4 | High | Player daemon | `player/player/daemon.py:276` | PLAYER FAULT (mpv cannot start any item) is shown on the projector but never reported to the console |
| H5 | High | Updates and commands | `player/deploy/update-player.sh:153` | Power loss between the mv to .prev and the end of install-player.sh leaves the Pi in a 203/EXEC restart loop forever; nothing restores .prev at boot |
| H6 | High | Projector, camera, tunnel | `player/player/projector.py:155` | Every daemon restart re-sends the projector code blindly; a toggle remote inverts the projector (03:00 nightly update turns it on) |
| H7 | High | Projector, camera, tunnel | `player/player/projector.py:159` | A transiently unreachable RM4/CEC at the moment want changes leaves the projector in the wrong state all day |
| H8 | High | Docs versus code | `docs/hardware.md:218` | Checklist tells the owner to tape a pre-flashed spare card inside the projector mount; the flasher README says an un-booted card is a password |

| M4 | Medium | Provisioning | `player/deploy/install-player.sh:429` | 'Ready. Waiting for the first video.' is shown without checking that the player actually started |
| M5 | Medium | Provisioning | `tools/flasher/firstboot.py:449` | REASON says 'The console did not accept this projector' for any curl failure; console detail discarded; permanent errors retried for 20 minutes |
| M6 | Medium | Provisioning | `tools/flasher/firstboot.py:470` | Network wait blames the Wi-Fi name and password for every cause, including wired Pis and a down console, and never gives up |

| M10 | Medium | Installer | `player/deploy/install-player.sh:227` | Python dependencies unpinned and unhashed, and the whole venv is rebuilt from PyPI on every nightly update |
| M11 | Medium | Installer | `player/deploy/install-player.sh:238` | Tarball installs write RELEASE=unknown: the console shows '<version>+unknown' and the first nightly update always reinstalls |
| M12 | Medium | Installer | `player/deploy/install-player.sh:305` | ARMv6 cloudflared: raw 'latest' binary with no checksum, no run check, never refreshed (contrary to its own comment), and it contradicts the docs' 'no remote access on Zero / Pi 1' |
| M13 | Medium | systemd units and mpv.conf | `player/deploy/projector-mpv.service:16` | mpv holds no display while idle, so the Linux text console shows on the projector after every boot and whenever the daemon is down |
| M14 | Medium | systemd units and mpv.conf | `player/deploy/projector-wyze-bridge.service:24` | Wyze bridge runs an unpinned :latest image and re-pulls it in ExecStartPre under the 90 s start timeout |

| M16 | Medium | Player daemon | `player/player/daemon.py:305` | The 24 h full re-hash runs at daemon-start time plus 24 h, competes with playback for SD bandwidth and blocks commands/screenshots for its duration |
| M17 | Medium | Player daemon | `player/player/sync.py:199` | No free-space check before a download: an oversized item fills the card, is retried every poll, and the STORAGE FULL screen itself cannot be written |
| M18 | Medium | Player daemon | `player/player/sync.py:215` | Media files and JSON caches are renamed into place without fsync: after a power cut a zero-filled file can be trusted as verified for 24 h |

## High

### H1. Pi user has no usable sudo: random never-shown password and no NOPASSWD rule on current Trixie images

- Severity: **High**. Area: First boot. Where: `tools/flasher/firstboot.py:274`.
- Existing test covers it: **no**. No test touches sudoers: test_firstrun_key_only_ssh (test_firstboot.py:127) only checks authorized_keys and sshd_config.d; the bash harness stub userconf (line 518) is `exit 0` and the harness never looks at /etc/sudoers.d. Covering tests: (1) in test_firstrun_key_only_ssh assert `printf "%s ALL=(ALL) NOPASSWD: ALL\n" projector-admin >/etc/sudoers.d/010_projection5000-nopasswd` and `run chmod 0440 /etc/sudoers.d/010_projection5000-nopasswd` are in s, and s.index("# user") < s.index("# sudo") < s.index("# key-only login"); (2) in test_firstrun_key_and_static_ip_under_bash, after adding `.replace("/etc/sudoers.d/", tmp + "/etc-sudoers/")` to _run_firstrun, assert (tmp_path / "etc-sudoers" / "010_projection5000-nopasswd").read_text() == "projector-admin ALL=(ALL) NOPASSWD: ALL\n" and "chmod 0440 -> rc=0" in log. test_firstrun_wipes_itself_and_logs_rc keeps passing only with that harness replace (it asserts exactly one failed step).

**What is wrong.** firstrun.sh creates projector-admin through /usr/lib/userconf-pi/userconf with a hash of a random 24-char password the flasher never shows or stores (flasher.py:61 says 'sudo needs none on Pi OS'). That assumption no longer holds: raspberrypi-sys-mods 1:20260121 'Drop ownership of /etc/sudoers.d/010_pi-nopasswd', pi-gen c020178c (2026-03-02) 'raspberrypi-sys-mods no longer ships 010_pi-nopasswd', PASSWORDLESS_SUDO defaults to 0, and Raspberry Pi Imager 2.x now has a separate opt-in that drops its own NOPASSWD sudoers file. userconf only rewrites 010_pi-nopasswd if it exists; firstrun.sh writes no sudoers file of its own. Result on a freshly downloaded Trixie Lite image: the SSH key gets a shell as projector-admin, but every 'sudo ...' the docs and the provisioner's give-up message tell people to run (sudo systemctl start projection5000-provision.service, sudo journalctl -u ..., sudo cat /etc/projector-player/config.toml) asks for a password nobody has. The only root path is pulling the card. Setup itself is unaffected (provision runs as root under systemd).


**How to reproduce.** Code trace: firstboot.py:271 hashes c['password'] (flasher.py:502, random, never shown); :273-280 create the user via userconf (or useradd -G sudo) and write nothing to /etc/sudoers.d; grep -n sudoers tools/flasher/firstboot.py returns nothing. imagefetch.py:10 resolves raspios_lite_arm64_latest / raspios_lite_armhf_latest, i.e. the current 2026-09-15 Trixie Lite. Upstream: curl -sL https://downloads.raspberrypi.com/raspios_lite_arm64/release_notes.txt | grep -n -B1 "Passwordless sudo" -> "2026-04-13: * Passwordless sudo now disabled by default"; raspberrypi-sys-mods pios/trixie changelog 1:20260121 "Drop ownership of /etc/sudoers.d/010_pi-nopasswd"; pi-gen commits c020178 and 567a833 (PASSWORDLESS_SUDO default 0); RPi-Distro/userconf-pi pios/trixie rename_user only seds 010_pi-nopasswd if the file exists. Harness: the rendered docs/audits/2026-09-20-pi-os/rendered/firstrun.sh contains no sudoers write (grep -n sudoers firstrun.sh is empty).


**Fix.**

```text
In render_firstrun, after the user block's closing "fi" (firstboot.py:280) and before the blank line, add (unconditionally, both userconf and useradd branches):
        "# sudo without a password: the SSH key is the credential (current Pi OS images no longer ship 010_pi-nopasswd)",
        "install -d -m 0755 /etc/sudoers.d",
        f'printf "%s ALL=(ALL) NOPASSWD: ALL\\n" {user} >/etc/sudoers.d/010_projection5000-nopasswd',
        "run chmod 0440 /etc/sudoers.d/010_projection5000-nopasswd",
(file name has no '.' or trailing '~', so sudo reads it; {user} is shlex-quoted and bare for projector-admin). In tools/flasher/tests/test_firstboot.py _run_firstrun add `.replace("/etc/sudoers.d/", tmp + "/etc-sudoers/")` next to the /etc/ssh/ replace (line 528) so the existing harness tests keep their "1 step(s) failed" count, then add the assertions named in test_note. Fix the comment at flasher.py:61 to "the sudoers drop-in firstrun.sh writes makes sudo passwordless" and README.md:88 likewise. Do not store or display the password: the key-only design stands.
```


**Evidence.** tools/flasher/firstboot.py:271-274 hashes the flasher's random password (flasher.py:502 secrets.token_urlsafe(24), regenerated at :1182, never displayed) and hands it to /usr/lib/userconf-pi/userconf; nothing in render_firstrun (:233-340) writes anything under /etc/sudoers.d, and the useradd fallback at :276 only adds the sudo group (password-gated). Upstream, verified today: userconf on pios/trixie only rewrites 010_pi-nopasswd `if [ -f ... ]`; raspberrypi-sys-mods changelog 1:20260121 "Drop ownership of /etc/sudoers.d/010_pi-nopasswd"; pi-gen c020178 ("raspberrypi-sys-mods no longer ships 010_pi-nopasswd", PASSWORDLESS_SUDO default 0) and 567a833; and the official raspios_lite_arm64 release_notes.txt entry 2026-04-13 says "Passwordless sudo now disabled by default". imagefetch.py:10 always fetches raspios_lite_{arm64,armhf}_latest (currently 2026-09-15), so every card the flasher writes gets a sudo that prompts for a password nobody has. The only NOPASSWD drop-in the project installs (install-player.sh:277-297) is for the `projector` service user, not projector-admin. Setup is unaffected (provision runs as root under systemd), but the give-up text at firstboot.py:492 and docs/adding-a-pi.md (sudo journalctl, sudo cat config.toml, sudo systemctl ...) all send the owner to a dead end; README.md:88 and flasher.py:61 state the opposite of current behaviour.


**Verify on hardware.** ssh -i %APPDATA%\Projection5000\ssh\id_ed25519 projector-admin@<device-id>.local 'sudo -n true; echo rc=$?; ls -l /etc/sudoers.d/; cat /etc/rpi-issue; dpkg -l raspberrypi-sys-mods | tail -1'   (expected on a fresh card: rc=1 with "a password is required", no 010_pi-nopasswd, rpi-issue dated 2026-04-13 or later)


### H2. Power cut during apt leaves dpkg interrupted; every retry and every later boot fails the same way

- Severity: **High**. Area: Provisioning. Where: `player/deploy/install-player.sh:180`.
- Existing test covers it: **no**. Nothing covers it. tools/flasher/tests/test_firstboot.py::test_provision_retries_enrollment_and_install replaces the installer with a stub (install_fails=True) and never models apt/dpkg. player/tests/test_installer_pi.py only runs the extracted pi_caps/wyze blocks (caps_and_wyze_blocks), never the apt lines; its line 107 is a static ordering assert on SRC.index("apt-get update") and is unaffected by inserting `dpkg --configure -a` between screen_init and apt-get update. player/tests/test_updater.py stubs install-player.sh entirely and asserts the exact last three shim-log lines for update-os.sh at line 329 (["apt-get update", "apt-get -y -o Dpkg::Options::=--force-confold upgrade", "apt-get -y autoremove"]): adding a `dpkg --configure -a || true` line to update-os.sh is safe (no dpkg shim exists, the command is simply not found and `|| true` swallows it, nothing lands in the shim log), but adding an `apt-get -f install` line there would break that assert. Minimal test that would cover it: in test_installer_pi.py add `assert SRC.index("dpkg --configure -a") < SRC.index("apt-get update")` next to line 107, and the mirror assert on update-os.sh source in test_updater.py; a stronger one runs the "Installing system dependencies" block with a dpkg() stub that logs and an apt-get() stub that exits 100 unless "configure -a" is already in the log.
- Also reported as: An interrupted apt run makes every later install/upgrade attempt fail (no dpkg --configure -a).

**What is wrong.** install-player.sh runs `apt-get update` (line 176) and `apt-get install -y ...` (line 180) under `set -e` without first repairing an interrupted dpkg run. `apt-get update` only takes the lists lock and succeeds, but `apt-get install` locks the dpkg system, finds a dirty /var/lib/dpkg/updates journal and aborts immediately with "dpkg was interrupted, you must manually run 'dpkg --configure -a'". A power cut or brown-out during the multi-minute mpv/ffmpeg unpack (the screen says "about 10 minutes") leaves that journal dirty. provision.sh re-extracts the archive and re-runs the same installer 20 times a minute apart, then shows "Setup did not finish. The player did not install." and exits; the still-enabled projection5000-provision.service repeats the identical 20 failures on every later boot. Nothing in the repo or in stock Pi OS Lite runs `dpkg --configure -a`, so the owner (key-only SSH, non-technical) can only re-flash.


**How to reproduce.** Code trace: rendered provision.sh line 84-87 (firstboot.py 482-484) -> install-player.sh line 176 (`apt-get update`, lists lock only, passes) -> line 180 (`apt-get install`, debSystem::Lock -> CheckUpdates sees /var/lib/dpkg/updates/NNN -> "dpkg was interrupted" error, exit 100) -> `set -e` (line 27) aborts the installer -> provision.sh loop line 94-105 retries 20x with identical input -> line 99 "Setup did not finish." -> exit 1; unit stays enabled (only disabled on success, line 89) so every boot repeats. `grep -rn "dpkg --configure\|configure -a\|apt-get -f\|--fix-broken" --include=*.sh --include=*.py .` in the worktree returns nothing. No harness models apt; see hardware_verify.


**Fix.**

player/deploy/install-player.sh, insert before line 176 (`apt-get update`), after the screen call at 174-175:
```
# A power cut mid-apt leaves dpkg interrupted and every later apt-get refuses to run: repair first.
dpkg --configure -a || true
apt-get -y -f install || true
```
player/deploy/update-os.sh, insert before line 64 (`apt-get update`, inside main after `export DEBIAN_FRONTEND=noninteractive`):
```
dpkg --configure -a || true
```
(`|| true` keeps set -e / the ERR trap quiet; if the repair fails the following apt-get fails with the real error as today.) This also covers update-player.sh --upgrade because it runs install-player.sh. Drop the provision.sh belt-and-braces copy: the installer is re-extracted from the card archive on every attempt, so there is no hand-patched-installer scenario. DPkg::Lock::Timeout is optional: the 20 x 60 s retry loop already outlasts apt-daily's lock; if wanted, `-o DPkg::Lock::Timeout=300` on the apt-get calls in install-player.sh only, since test_updater.py:329 asserts the exact argv of the update-os.sh calls. Tests: none break; add `assert SRC.index("dpkg --configure -a") < SRC.index("apt-get update")` in player/tests/test_installer_pi.py beside line 107 and the same on the update-os.sh source in player/tests/test_updater.py.


**Evidence.** player/deploy/install-player.sh runs under `set -euo pipefail` (line 27) and calls `apt-get update` (line 176) then `apt-get install -y ...` (line 180) with no `dpkg --configure -a` or `apt-get -f install` anywhere before them; a repo-wide grep for `dpkg --configure`, `configure -a`, `apt-get -f`, `--fix-broken`, `DPkg::Lock` returns nothing in any .sh or .py file. apt's install path (pkgCacheFile::Open with lock -> debSystem::Lock -> CheckUpdates on /var/lib/dpkg/updates) refuses with "dpkg was interrupted, you must manually run 'dpkg --configure -a'" whenever the dpkg journal is dirty, while `apt-get update` only takes the lists lock and passes, so the abort is at line 180, not 176. provision.sh (firstboot.py lines 478-505, rendered lines 84-105) re-extracts the archive and re-runs the identical installer 20 times with `sleep 60` between, then shows "Setup did not finish. The player did not install." and exits 1; the unit (firstboot.py lines 316-329, Type=simple, WantedBy=multi-user.target, still enabled) runs the same loop on every later boot. Raspberry Pi OS Lite ships no unattended-upgrades, so nothing on the OS side clears the journal either. The installer's own screen says "about 10 minutes" (line 174), and the apt unpack/configure phase for mpv+ffmpeg on a Pi is a multi-minute window in which a pulled plug or a brown-out from a weak PSU leaves the journal dirty; the only owner recovery is a re-flash (or SSH from the flasher PC and `sudo dpkg --configure -a`, which a non-technical owner will not know to do from a log line that is not plain English).


**Verify on hardware.** On a freshly flashed Pi: boot, wait for "Step 3 of 4: installing the player", pull the power about 60-90 s in (mpv/ffmpeg unpack), power back up. Then from the flasher PC: `ssh projector-admin@<pi>`; `sudo dpkg --audit`; `ls /var/lib/dpkg/updates/`; `sudo tail -n 60 /var/log/projection5000-provision.log`; `journalctl -u projection5000-provision -n 200 --no-pager`. Expected: /var/lib/dpkg/updates/ is non-empty and every "install attempt N" in the log ends with "E: dpkg was interrupted, you must manually run 'dpkg --configure -a' to correct the problem." followed by "attempt N failed, retrying in 60 s", then "GAVE UP after 20 attempts"; `sudo reboot` shows the same sequence again. `sudo dpkg --configure -a && sudo systemctl start projection5000-provision.service` then completes the install, proving the missing step is the whole defect.


### H3. mpv audio goes to the analogue jack (ALSA card 0 = Headphones), not HDMI, on every board but the Pi 5

- Severity: **High**. Area: systemd units and mpv.conf. Where: `player/deploy/mpv.conf:39`.
- Existing test covers it: **partial**. player/tests/test_installer_pi.py::test_mpv_conf_gets_hwdec_on_videocore_iv_boards (line 133) exercises exactly this block with a fake /proc and asserts conf == dist for pi4/pi5 (line 152) and that hwdec= is the last line for VC4 boards (line 147); it pins the current silent-audio behaviour rather than testing for HDMI audio. To cover the fix: extend fake_proc() to write proc/asound/cards per board (pi4: Headphones, vc4hdmi0, vc4hdmi1; pi5: vc4hdmi0, vc4hdmi1; pi3/zero2/pi2/zero: Headphones, vc4hdmi), then assert "audio-device=alsa/default:CARD=vc4hdmi0" (pi4, pi5) or "CARD=vc4hdmi" (others) appears exactly once and that it is absent when no asound/cards file exists (off-a-Pi case). Change line 152 to compare against dist + the expected audio block, and line 147 to allow the audio lines after hwdec (or append audio before hwdec). test_pi_caps_off_a_pi (line 120) keeps passing as long as the new pi_caps lookup swallows a missing file.
- Also reported as: Audio goes to the wrong ALSA device by default (Pi 4 headphone jack), no HDMI audio.

**What is wrong.** mpv.conf leaves ao/audio-device unset (player/deploy/mpv.conf:39-41) and nothing else in the install path picks an output: install-player.sh:202-222 only copies the file and appends hwdec=, projector-mpv.service passes no audio flag, and the flasher/firstboot never edit config.txt (default dtparam=audio=on stays) or write asound.conf. mpv runs as the projector system user with no PipeWire/Pulse daemon, so it ends at ao=alsa with the ALSA "default" PCM = card 0. On Raspberry Pi OS Lite the bcm2835 Headphones card is card 0 on Pi 3, Zero 2 W and (per pi-gen#742, Lite Bookworm 64-bit) Pi 4, with vc4hdmi as card 1/2, so videos play silently on the projector while sound goes to the 3.5 mm jack. On Pi 4 the order is a module-load race (some boots/images enumerate vc4hdmi0 first), so the failure is common rather than universal there; only the Pi 5/500 (no analogue card) reliably gets HDMI audio. README:174 tells a non-technical owner to hand-edit mpv.conf over SSH.


**How to reproduce.** Code trace: player/deploy/mpv.conf:39-41 (no ao/audio-device); player/deploy/install-player.sh:202-222 (copy + hwdec append only); player/deploy/projector-mpv.service:16 ExecStart has no --audio-device; `grep -rn "dtparam=audio\|audio-device\|asound\|amixer" .` in the worktree returns only comments and the README line. Harness: cd player && python -m pytest -q tests/test_installer_pi.py -k mpv_conf shows the only mpv.conf assertions are hwdec-related. Field data for the enumeration premise: RPi-Distro/pi-gen issue #742 (Pi 4, RPi OS Lite 64-bit Bookworm): card 0 Headphones, card 1 vc4-hdmi-0, card 2 vc4-hdmi-1.


**Fix.**

```text
Detect the card inside pi_caps (install-player.sh:49-64) so the fake-/proc harness can drive it, and pin HDMI whenever a vc4hdmi card exists (no Headphones gate; that still relies on enumeration order and does nothing for the Pi 5):

pi_caps() {
    ...
    PI_SOC="$(tr '\0' ' ' < "${proc}/device-tree/compatible" 2>/dev/null || true)"
    # First HDMI sound card (vc4hdmi on Pi 0-3, vc4hdmi0 on Pi 4/5); empty off a Pi
    PI_HDMI_CARD="$(awk -F'[][]' '/vc4hdmi/ {gsub(/ /,"",$2); print $2; exit}' "${proc}/asound/cards" 2>/dev/null || true)"
    ...
}

and in the fresh-install branch (after line 212 cp, before the hwdec if at 213 so the VC4 hwdec line stays last):

    if [[ -n "${PI_HDMI_CARD}" ]]; then
        # ALSA "default" is whichever card probed first (the 3.5 mm jack on most
        # boards), so name the HDMI card. Pi 4/5: vc4hdmi0 is the HDMI socket next
        # to the power connector; use vc4hdmi1 for the other one.
        echo "    sound to HDMI (ALSA card ${PI_HDMI_CARD})"
        printf '\n# Sound goes to the projector over HDMI, not the headphone jack\nao=alsa\naudio-device=alsa/default:CARD=%s\n' \
            "${PI_HDMI_CARD}" >> "${MPV_CONF}"
    fi

Update mpv.conf:39-41 comment to say the installer appends the HDMI line and how to switch to the jack (audio-device=alsa/default:CARD=Headphones), and README.md:174-175 to match. Existing installs keep their mpv.conf (line 204-210), so already-deployed Pi 4s stay silent until the owner deletes mpv.conf and reinstalls; optionally treat an existing file that is byte-identical to the previous ${MPV_CONF}.dist as untouched and regenerate it. Tests: update test_mpv_conf_gets_hwdec_on_videocore_iv_boards as described in test_note (its line 152 conf == dist and line 147 last-line assertions change).
```


**Evidence.** Code side is exactly as described: player/deploy/mpv.conf:39-41 leaves ao/audio-device commented out; install-player.sh:202-222 only copies that file and appends hwdec= for VideoCore IV; projector-mpv.service ExecStart passes no audio option; the player Python has no audio-device handling; firstboot.py/flasher never touch config.txt or write asound.conf (grep for dtparam=audio|asound|amixer across the repo is empty). mpv runs as system user projector with no session daemon, so its ao probe (pipewire, pulse fail) ends at alsa "default" = ALSA card 0. Public field data confirms the enumeration premise on the exact target OS: RPi-Distro/pi-gen#742 (Pi 4, Raspberry Pi OS Lite 64-bit Bookworm) lists card 0 Headphones, card 1 vc4hdmi0, card 2 vc4hdmi1, and Zero 2 W Bookworm reports show card 0 Headphones, card 1 vc4hdmi. The one caveat: on Pi 4 the order is a module-load race (desktop images often show vc4hdmi0 as card 0 and Headphones as card 2), so "every board but the Pi 5" is slightly overstated, but on Lite images the jack-first order is what is reported, and even the lucky order sends sound to HDMI0 only. Severity High stands: a video with a soundtrack plays silently on the projector in the default install path and the only documented remedy (README:174) is hand-editing mpv.conf over SSH.


**Verify on hardware.**

```text
cat /proc/asound/cards
aplay -l
sudo -u projector aplay -L | head -20
grep -n 'dtparam=audio' /boot/firmware/config.txt
sudo -u projector XDG_RUNTIME_DIR=/run/projector-mpv mpv --no-video --ao=help
sudo -u projector speaker-test -D sysdefault:CARD=vc4hdmi0 -c2 -t sine -l1   # expect a tone from the projector
sudo -u projector speaker-test -D default -c2 -t sine -l1                     # expect the tone from the 3.5 mm jack instead
journalctl -u projector-mpv -n 200 | grep -i 'ao\|audio\|alsa'
```


### H4. PLAYER FAULT (mpv cannot start any item) is shown on the projector but never reported to the console

- Severity: **High**. Area: Player daemon. Where: `player/player/daemon.py:276`.
- Existing test covers it: **no**. test_mpv_idle_for_four_cycles_shows_player_fault (player/tests/test_daemon.py:448) asserts only the screen and log; no test inspects cms.sync_calls[-1]["sync_error"] on the fault path. Add to that test, after the 4-cycle loop and the extra run_cycle at line 462: `assert "could not start any of 2 items" in cms.sync_calls[-1]["sync_error"]`; and after the c.mp4 recovery push at line 466, one more run_cycle then `assert cms.sync_calls[-1]["sync_error"] == ""` (console error cleared). If line 232's wording is changed to plain English, update the assertion at test_daemon.py:460 ("mpv could not start any of 2 items") to the new text. No other existing test breaks: test_daemon.py:222/228 and test_sync.py:70-77 cover download failures only, and test_player_fault_retries_content_slowly never reads sync_error.

**What is wrong.** When mpv fails to start every entry (no display at boot, undecodable media, broken VO) the daemon detects it after 4 idle cycles and shows the PLAYER FAULT screen, but nothing about it goes up on the sync: _gather_mpv_status reports player_status=idle (the fault screen or the failed PNG is 'idle' by design at line 277-278) and sync_error is only ever built from download failures. The console therefore shows a healthy, online, idle device with no last_error while the projector shows a fault screen, and none of alerts.js KINDS fires (screenshot-stale only fires if a screenshot ever succeeded). For a remotely managed kiosk this is the one failure the console most needs to see. The fault screen's advice 'restart mpv from the console or reboot' is also wrong for the undecodable-media case.


**How to reproduce.** cd <checkout>/player && python -m pytest -q "docs/audits/2026-09-20-pi-os/repro/daemon_audit/test_audit_daemon.py" -k player_fault -p no:cacheprovider  (passes: after 6 idle cycles screens.current.reason == "player" while cms.sync_calls[-1] has player_status "idle" and sync_error ""). Code trace: daemon.py:230-234 shows the screen only; daemon.py:276 status from _gather_mpv_status (idle at :74-75); sync.py:127 sends sync_error from the download-failure text at sync.py:447 only; cloud/src/api.js:55 last_error from sync_error only; cloud/src/alerts.js:42-49 no kind for idle.


**Fix.**

```text
In run_cycle, replace the sync_once call at daemon.py:309-312 with:

    sync_error = state.last_sync_error
    if screens is not None and screens.current is not None and screens.current.reason == "player":
        sync_error = sync_error or screens.current.message   # the fault screen goes up on the console too
    _changed, manifest, state.last_sync_error = sync_once(
        cfg, status=status, sync_error=sync_error,
        verify_all=verify_all, should_stop=_stop_requested, on_progress=on_progress,
    )

Do not touch player_status: it is already "idle" on both fault sub-cases (daemon.py:74-75 and 277-278). Do not store the fault text in state.last_sync_error, or it would leak into _idle_screen at line 173 and into storage_full at line 317; sync_once returns the fresh download-failure string every cycle, so state stays clean and the console clears automatically once the screen is gone. Plain-English pass, since the same text now reaches the owner on the console under the label "Sync problem:": change daemon.py:232 to `message = f"the player could not start any of {len(items)} items (is the projector connected and switched on?)"` and screens.py:193 second line to "check the projector is connected and on, then restart the player from the console or reboot" (covers the undecodable-media case too, where restart alone will not help). Update test_daemon.py:460 accordingly. Console needs no change: api.js:55 already stores sync_error as last_error and alerts.js:45 raises sync-error on it.
```


**Evidence.** Confirmed by code trace and by running the finder's scratch test (1 passed). player/player/daemon.py:230-234 only calls screens.show(...) for the fault; nothing touches state.last_sync_error or the status dict. run_cycle builds status from _gather_mpv_status (daemon.py:276), which reports player_status="idle" when mpv's idle-active is true (daemon.py:74-75), and the only sync_error source is sync_once's download-failure text (player/player/sync.py:447, via fetch_manifest sync.py:127). On the console, api.js:55 stores last_error only from sync_error and alerts.js:42-49 fires nothing for player_status "idle" with no last_error and no prior screenshot (screenshot-stale needs a non-null last_screenshot_at). Partial mitigation only: if mpv did manage to render the fault PNG, the periodic screenshot upload (screenshots.py:25) would show the PLAYER FAULT image in the console thumbnail, but in the no-display-at-boot case mpv cannot render that either, so the console sees a healthy idle device. Existing test test_daemon.py:448 asserts the screen and log only, never cms.sync_calls.


**Verify on hardware.** Boot the Pi with the projector/HDMI unplugged, wait 5 poll intervals, then: journalctl -u projector-player -n 200 | grep -i 'player fault'  (expect "showing the player fault screen"); on the console Devices page confirm the device shows online/idle with no last error and no open alert. Plug the projector in and confirm the console never showed anything while the fault was up.


### H5. Power loss between the mv to .prev and the end of install-player.sh leaves the Pi in a 203/EXEC restart loop forever; nothing restores .prev at boot

- Severity: **High**. Area: Updates and commands. Where: `player/deploy/update-player.sh:153`.
- Existing test covers it: **no**. test_update_player_script_end_to_end_in_a_fake_root (player/tests/test_updater.py:267) only exercises the fail trap (installer 'exit 1') and the postcheck rollback; nothing kills the run or boots with .prev present and player broken. test_installer_pi.py does not assert on projector-player.service contents, so adding ExecStartPre breaks nothing. Test to add in test_updater.py: reuse fake_pi with INSTALLER_STUB % 'mkdir -p "${PIPLAYER_ROOT}/opt/piplayer/player"; kill -9 $PPID $$', assert player.prev/RELEASE exists and install/.venv does not; then run the recovery command from the unit (extract the ExecStartPre line from player/deploy/projector-player.service, substitute PIPLAYER_ROOT prefix, run with bash) and assert install/RELEASE == sha1 and player.prev is gone. If install-player.sh is changed to stage into player.new, INSTALLER_STUB's D= path must become ${PIPLAYER_ROOT}/opt/piplayer/player.new and the 'a failing installer restores .prev' block must assert player.new is removed.

**What is wrong.** update() moves the running install to /opt/piplayer/player.prev (line 153) and then runs install-player.sh --upgrade from the checkout (line 157), which spends minutes in apt-get update / apt-get install (install-player.sh lines 176-180), rsync (195), venv creation (225) and pip (226-227) before /opt/piplayer/player/.venv/bin/python exists again. Power cut anywhere in that window (nightly window on a projector whose outlet staff switch off, or 'Update all players' pressed during shutdown) and the next boot has no /opt/piplayer/player, or one without a venv. projector-player.service ExecStart=/opt/piplayer/player/.venv/bin/python (unit line 12) fails with status 203/EXEC and Restart=always (line 13) retries every 5 s for good. The transient projector-player-update unit and its fail trap are gone, the postcheck timer was never armed (and is not persistent anyway), and the only code that ever touches .prev is update-player.sh itself, which is inside the missing directory (the sudoers path /opt/piplayer/player/deploy/update-player.sh no longer exists either). The console shows the device offline; mpv keeps running with an empty playlist so the projector is black after the reboot. Recovery needs SSH plus 'sudo mv /opt/piplayer/player.prev /opt/piplayer/player', which the non-technical owner cannot do; the practical recovery is a re-flash.


**How to reproduce.** cd "<checkout>/player" && python -m pytest -q -s "docs/audits/2026-09-20-pi-os/repro/test_powerloss.py" -p no:cacheprovider  -- reuses fake_pi from tests/test_updater.py with INSTALLER_STUB % 'kill -9 "$PPID"; kill -9 $$' (SIGKILL = power loss after the mv at update-player.sh:153, before the venv exists). Output: install exists: False, prev exists: True (old RELEASE), no update-status.json. Code trace: update-player.sh:151-157 (mv then install, no boot-time restore), install-player.sh:176-227 (the multi-minute window), projector-player.service:12-14 (203/EXEC, Restart=always every 5 s, start limit never hit).


**Fix.**

```text
The unit-level guard alone is the minimal correct fix for the boot case; the staging-dir refactor is optional hardening. Use the `-+` prefix so a missing .prev does not itself fail the start, and run the restore from the .prev copy of update-player.sh so the owner gets a plain-English status line in the console instead of silence:

projector-player.service, before ExecStart (line 12):
ExecStartPre=-+/bin/sh -c 'test -x /opt/piplayer/player/.venv/bin/python || { test -d /opt/piplayer/player.prev && rm -rf /opt/piplayer/player && mv -T /opt/piplayer/player.prev /opt/piplayer/player; }'

(`+` runs it as root despite User=projector and skips ProtectSystem/ProtectHome; `-` ignores a non-zero exit; `mv -T` refuses to nest the directory if a race ever leaves /opt/piplayer/player present.) Optionally, in update-player.sh main() add a `--recover` branch (restore_prev; write_status false "restored the previous version: the last update was interrupted before it finished (power lost?)"; exit 0) and have the ExecStartPre exec /opt/piplayer/player.prev/deploy/update-player.sh --recover instead of the inline rm/mv, so update-status.json explains the rollback to the owner on the next sync.

Caveat on the guard alone: ExecStartPre runs on every (re)start, and update-player.sh leaves /opt/piplayer/player half-built for minutes while the old daemon keeps running; a daemon crash-restart inside that window would now roll the half-built tree back under a running install-player.sh. That is the reason to also do the staging-dir change (install-player.sh --upgrade honouring PIPLAYER_INSTALL_DIR=/opt/piplayer/player.new, update-player.sh doing `rm -rf .prev; mv -T player player.prev; mv -T player.new player` at the end, fail() rm -rf player.new instead of restore_prev). With staging, the venv is created at player.new and moved: .venv/bin/python is a symlink to /usr/bin/python3 and pyvenv.cfg's home= points at the system python, so 'python -m player.daemon' still works; only the pip/activate shebangs go stale, which nothing on the Pi runs after install. That refactor touches INSTALLER_STUB in test_updater.py (D= path) and the rsync/venv/deploy/RELEASE lines 190-240 of install-player.sh. The postcheck-after-reboot gap (timer not Persistent) is a separate, lesser issue: a marker file checked by the same ExecStartPre is fine but not required for this finding.
```


**Evidence.** player/deploy/update-player.sh:151-157 does `rm -rf .prev; mv INSTALL_DIR PREV_DIR; bash install-player.sh --upgrade`, and install-player.sh:176-180 (apt-get), 195 (rsync), 225-227 (venv + pip) run for minutes before /opt/piplayer/player/.venv/bin/python exists again. The only code that restores .prev is restore_prev() at update-player.sh:73-79, called from fail() (line 110, a trap inside the same bash process) and postcheck() (line 99, a timer only armed at line 162 after a successful install, OnActiveSec with no Persistent=); nothing at boot touches player.prev (grep across player/deploy and tools/flasher hits only the --uninstall rm at install-player.sh:135). projector-player.service:12-14 ExecStart=/opt/piplayer/player/.venv/bin/python with Restart=always RestartSec=5 and no StartLimitIntervalSec=0: at 2 starts per 10 s the default burst (5/10 s) is never reached, so it retries 203/EXEC forever. Reproduced in the fake root: an INSTALLER_STUB that does `kill -9 "$PPID"; kill -9 $$` (SIGKILL to update-player.sh and itself, i.e. power loss) leaves opt/piplayer/player absent, player.prev present with the old RELEASE, and no update-status.json (docs/audits/2026-09-20-pi-os/repro/test_powerloss.py, 1 passed). Severity stays High rather than Critical because the window only exists on the nights a new commit is actually installed.


**Verify on hardware.** On a spare Pi with a commit ahead of RELEASE: sudo /opt/piplayer/player/deploy/update-player.sh main; sudo tail -f /var/lib/projector-player/update.log and pull the power while 'Creating virtualenv' / 'Collecting' lines scroll. After boot: systemctl status projector-player (expect 'status=203/EXEC' and 'activating (auto-restart)'); systemctl show -p NRestarts,Result,ExecMainStatus projector-player; ls -d /opt/piplayer/player /opt/piplayer/player.prev (expect only .prev, or player without .venv); journalctl -u projector-player -n 50; systemctl list-timers projector-player-postcheck.timer (expect not listed); cat /var/lib/projector-player/update-status.json (expect stale or missing). Recovery to confirm the diagnosis: sudo rm -rf /opt/piplayer/player && sudo mv /opt/piplayer/player.prev /opt/piplayer/player && sudo systemctl restart projector-player.


### H6. Every daemon restart re-sends the projector code blindly; a toggle remote inverts the projector (03:00 nightly update turns it on)

- Severity: **High**. Area: Projector, camera, tunnel. Where: `player/player/projector.py:155`.
- Existing test covers it: **no**. No existing test builds a second Projector (or a second daemon process) with an unchanged want; test_auto_mode_applies_want_on_change_only and test_run_cycle_reports_projector_state_and_applies_auto_mode only check the within-process change-only rule. Scratch repro docs/audits/2026-09-20-pi-os/repro/peri/test_repro.py::test_daemon_restart_resends_want_and_toggles_projector confirms. Test to add in player/tests/test_projector.py: test_auto_mode_toggle_code_is_adopted_not_resent_after_restart: p = Projector(); p.set_block(block(mode="auto", want="off", codes={"power_on": B64, "power_off": B64})); assert p.maybe_apply() is False and p.applied_want == "off" and rm.sent == []; p.set_block(block(mode="auto", want="on", codes=same)); assert p.maybe_apply() is True and rm.sent == [PACKET]. Also a discrete-code control: fresh Projector with distinct codes still sends on first apply. Required test change: block() helper default codes must become distinct (e.g. B64_OFF = base64 of a different packet) or the three tests named in reason fail.
- Also reported as: Auto projector mode re-sends the power code on every daemon start (toggle remotes invert the projector).

**What is wrong.** Projector.applied_want (projector.py:105) lives only in memory, so after every daemon start (reboot, Restart=always after a crash, and the nightly update-player.sh whose last line is `systemctl restart projector-player.service`, triggered by updater.maybe_auto_update from daemon.py:386 in the default 03:00-05:00 window) the check at projector.py:155 sees want != None and line 161 sends the code even though nothing changed. docs/automation.md:597-599 tells the owner a single-button toggle remote may be learned as both power_on and power_off because the player "only sends a code when the wanted state changes, never blindly". With identical packets the projector is off at night, want is still "off" after the 03:00 restart, the toggle is sent and the projector turns ON overnight, then OFF again when want flips to on in the morning. Every crash restart, reboot and power cut (Pi reboots, want=off, projector already off, toggle turns it on) inverts it the same way. Discrete codes and CEC only get a harmless redundant send.


**How to reproduce.** cd "<checkout>/player" && python -m pytest -q "docs/audits/2026-09-20-pi-os/repro/peri/test_repro.py::test_daemon_restart_resends_want_and_toggles_projector" (passes, i.e. confirms: second Projector with the same want=off block sends a second packet). Trace: daemon.main -> PlayerState.projector = Projector() (daemon.py:126) -> run_cycle set_block (daemon.py:376) -> maybe_apply (daemon.py:387) -> projector.py:155 want != applied_want(None) -> projector.py:161 power("off") -> send_code(power_off packet == power_on packet).


**Fix.**

```text
In Projector.maybe_apply (player/player/projector.py:148-162), after the want check at line 155, add:
    codes = block.get("codes") or {}
    toggle = block.get("control") == "broadlink" and codes.get("power_on") == codes.get("power_off")
    if self.applied_want is None and toggle:
        self.applied_want = want   # fresh process, single-button remote: the projector is presumably already there
        log.info("projector auto: adopting want=%s after restart, nothing sent (toggle code)", want)
        return False
Keep the existing resend for discrete codes and CEC (harmless, recovers a projector after a power cut). Do NOT change the retry at line 131 (coin flip either way, already disclosed at docs/automation.md:600, and test_auto_mode_failure_is_reported_not_retried_until_want_changes pins two attempts). Test changes required in player/tests/test_projector.py: (1) block() at line 75-76 must default to distinct codes, e.g. B64_OFF = base64.b64encode(PACKET + b"\x01").decode() and codes {"power_on": B64, "power_off": B64_OFF}, otherwise test_auto_mode_applies_want_on_change_only, test_auto_mode_failure_is_reported_not_retried_until_want_changes and test_run_cycle_reports_projector_state_and_applies_auto_mode fail (verified by applying the fix through a pytest plugin: 3 failed, 16 passed); (2) add test_auto_mode_toggle_code_is_adopted_not_resent_after_restart as in test_note. Update docs/automation.md:597-599 to say the first sync after a restart adopts the current want without sending for toggle remotes. Persisting applied_want to /var/lib/projector-player is optional; skip it (ponytail: a crash between a failed send and the next poll adopts the wrong state once; add persistence only if that is seen in the field).
```


**Evidence.** player/player/projector.py:105 initialises `applied_want = None` in memory only; nothing persists or reloads it (grep for applied_want outside projector.py finds nothing). daemon.py:126 builds one Projector per process and run_cycle (daemon.py:376, 387) calls set_block then maybe_apply every poll, so the first manifest after any process start hits projector.py:155 with `want != None` and projector.py:161 calls power(want) regardless of what was sent before. update-player.sh:164 ends with `systemctl restart projector-player.service` and updater.maybe_auto_update (updater.py:178-190) runs it inside the default 03:00-05:00 window; projector-player.service:13 has Restart=always, so crash restarts and reboots do the same. docs/automation.md:597-599 tells the owner to learn a toggle button as both power_on and power_off because the player "only sends a code when the wanted state changes, never blindly", which is exactly what line 161 does on restart: with identical packets a resend of want=off toggles an off projector on. The scratch harness test_daemon_restart_resends_want_and_toggles_projector passes (two Projector instances, same block want=off, second maybe_apply returns True and a second packet is sent). Severity stays High: the doc-recommended configuration plus the default nightly update inverts the projector every night.


**Verify on hardware.** On a Pi with a toggle remote learned as both codes, mode auto, projector currently off and want=off (outside any schedule): sudo systemctl restart projector-player.service; sleep 40; journalctl -u projector-player.service -n 50 --no-pager | grep 'projector auto'   (expect "projector auto: unknown -> off" within one poll and the projector powering on). Cross-check with the console: the device's projector_state shows "off" while the lamp is on.


### H7. A transiently unreachable RM4/CEC at the moment want changes leaves the projector in the wrong state all day

- Severity: **High**. Area: Projector, camera, tunnel. Where: `player/player/projector.py:159`.
- Existing test covers it: **partial**. player/tests/test_projector.py::test_auto_mode_failure_is_reported_not_retried_until_want_changes (line 249) pins the current behaviour but only asserts the very next poll is not retried, which remains true with a cooldown, so it keeps passing unchanged. No test covers a later retry. Add (verified passing in a scratch copy): test_auto_mode_failure_is_retried_after_the_cooldown: monkeypatch.setattr(projector, "RETRY_COOLDOWN_SECONDS", 0); p.set_block(block(mode="auto", want="on")); rm.fail_sends = 5; assert p.maybe_apply() is True and rm.fail_sends == 3 and p.error; assert p.maybe_apply() is True and rm.fail_sends == 1 and p.error; assert p.maybe_apply() is True and p.state == "on" and p.error == "" and rm.sent == [PACKET]; assert p.maybe_apply() is False. test_run_cycle_reports_projector_state_and_applies_auto_mode (line 291) is unaffected (successful sends are still change-only).

**What is wrong.** maybe_apply sets applied_want before calling power(), so two failed sends (10 s total: two 5 s discover/hello timeouts) mark the transition as done and it is never attempted again until want flips. Common path: a site power cut; the Pi boots in ~30 s and syncs, the RM4 mini needs a minute to rejoin Wi-Fi, want is 'on', both attempts fail, and the projector stays dark until 17:10 and then off again at 08:57 the next day, i.e. the whole day. The console shows 'projector on: no Broadlink RM found on the LAN' but nobody non-technical knows to click On. automation.md documents this as intended, but the consequence (one Wi-Fi blip at 08:57 = no projector all day) is not acceptable for an unattended room.


**How to reproduce.** cd "<checkout>/player" && python -m pytest -q tests/test_projector.py -k not_retried_until_want_changes (passes: asserts maybe_apply() is False after two failed sends). Scratch: python -m pytest -q docs/audits/2026-09-20-pi-os/repro/peri/test_repro.py -k rm4_offline (passes: 100 polls after the RM4 returns send nothing). Code trace: projector.py:155 guard, :159 applied_want set, :161 power(), :127-135 two attempts then return without touching applied_want.


**Fix.**

```text
Minimal fix in player/player/projector.py (no error-class split needed): add module constant RETRY_COOLDOWN_SECONDS = 300 after ERROR_MAX_LEN (line 27); in Projector.__init__ add self.retry_at: float | None = None; replace maybe_apply lines 155-162 with:
        if want not in ("on", "off"):
            return False
        if want == self.applied_want and not (self.retry_at and time.monotonic() >= self.retry_at):
            return False
        self.applied_want, self.retry_at = want, None
        log.info("projector auto: %s -> %s", self.state, want)
        self.power(want)
        if self.error:   # blaster unreachable or send failed: try again after the cooldown, error stays visible meanwhile
            self.retry_at = time.monotonic() + RETRY_COOLDOWN_SECONDS
        return True
A want change still applies immediately (want != applied_want) and clears the pending retry; a success clears the error and returns to change-only. Cost while the blaster stays missing: one 10 s stall of the sync loop every 5 min. Also update docs/automation.md lines 647-648 ('is not tried again until `want` changes') to 'is tried again every 5 minutes until it succeeds or `want` changes', and the module docstring sentence 'retries it once' (line 11) and the ponytail comment at lines 157-158. If the owner's projector only has a single Power toggle IR code, the finder's split (retry only when discover/hello/auth or the CEC --playback claim failed) avoids toggling a projector that did receive the packet; but the existing immediate retry already re-sends in that case, so treat the split as optional.
```


**Evidence.** player/player/projector.py:159 sets self.applied_want = want before line 161 calls self.power(want); power() (lines 127-135) makes exactly two attempts and on failure only sets state/error and returns, never resetting applied_want. maybe_apply (line 155) then returns False on every later cycle while want is unchanged, so a Broadlink discover/hello failure (line 40-42, two 5 s timeouts) at the moment want flips is final until the console flips want again (docs/automation.md:647-648 documents this). daemon.py:387 is the only auto-mode caller and it passes no retry signal; only a daemon restart (applied_want back to None) or a manual On command (commands.py:177) recovers. The existing test test_projector.py:249-259 and the scratch harness test_repro.py::test_rm4_offline_at_0857_is_never_retried_all_day both pass and pin this behaviour. Mitigation the finder omitted: the console raises a 'projector error' alert (cloud/src/alerts.js:25,48) every 5 min cron, so the owner is told, but still has to click On; the room stays dark until then.


**Verify on hardware.** On the Pi in auto mode with control broadlink: unplug the RM4 mini, then on the console flip want (start a schedule or toggle the playlist) and watch: journalctl -u projector-player.service -f | grep -i projector. Expect 'projector on failed (...); retrying once' then 'projector on: no Broadlink RM found on the LAN' once, then no further projector lines. Plug the RM4 back in, wait 10 min: the projector stays off and the Devices page keeps the error until the next want change or 'sudo systemctl restart projector-player' (which resets applied_want and sends).


### H8. Checklist tells the owner to tape a pre-flashed spare card inside the projector mount; the flasher README says an un-booted card is a password

- Severity: **High**. Area: Docs versus code. Where: `docs/hardware.md:218`.
- Existing test covers it: **no**. No test reads docs/hardware.md. tools/flasher/tests/test_firstboot.py::test_provision_contents_with_token only checks the token variant omits ENROLL_KEY; nothing asserts the key-variant card is treated as secret in the docs. A cheap guard would be a doc-consistency test in tools/flasher/tests (assert "taped inside" not in hardware.md and that the checklist mentions the un-booted-card warning), but it is hygiene-only; the fix is a doc edit and breaks no existing test (player 277 / flasher 97 untouched).

**What is wrong.** Step 12 ("Pocket the spare card in a labelled bag taped inside the projector mount") and hardware.md:69 (a spare pre-flashed card per projector) contradict tools/flasher/README.md:337-348: until first boot the FAT partition holds the console's enrollment key, the Pi user's password and the pre-hashed Wi-Fi key in plain text, "treat an un-booted card like a password, do not leave it lying around". Because re-enrolling the same device_id returns the existing device token (cloud/src/api.js:239-241), whoever lifts the card from a lobby mount can enroll as that device and then pull its media, the site's Wyze email/password/API key from /api/camera-config and its tunnel token, or enroll rogue devices with the key. The checklist is the document the owner works from on site.


**How to reproduce.** cd "<checkout>" && grep -n "ENROLL_KEY=" "docs/audits/2026-09-20-pi-os/rendered/provision.sh" (line 8 shows the key in clear); sed -n 1203p tools/flasher/flasher.py (GUI never sets a device token, so every GUI-flashed card carries the enrollment key); sed -n 239,241p cloud/src/api.js and sed -n 283,287p (re-enroll of an existing device_id returns row.token); sed -n 218,219p docs/hardware.md versus sed -n 345,348p tools/flasher/README.md.


**Fix.** Three doc edits, no code. (1) docs/hardware.md:217-218, replace step 12 with: "12. **Take the spare card home.** A flashed card that has not booted carries the console's enrollment key in plain text (`tools/flasher/README.md`, Security note). Keep it with the operator, never at the projector; if a card dies, bring the spare or flash a new one (ten minutes, same device id)." (2) docs/hardware.md:69, Purpose cell: "Same endurance card as above, flashed with the same device id (a re-flash enrolls the same device). Kept by the operator, not at the projector: an un-booted card holds the enrollment key. Swap in on site if a card dies." (3) docs/hardware.md:178-179, after "Flash the spare card the same way with the same device id." add "It stays with you (step 12)." Optional hardening: if a spare is lost, rotate the enrollment key on the console (automation.md section B) and re-flash any un-booted spares.


**Evidence.** docs/hardware.md:218-219 (step 12) tells the owner to tape the spare card inside the projector mount, and hardware.md:69 lists a "Spare pre-flashed microSD ... flashed with the same device id" per projector. The flasher GUI always takes the enrollment-key path (tools/flasher/flasher.py:1203 sets cfg["token"] from a field that has "no widget", so it is empty), and tools/flasher/firstboot.py:414-415 then writes ENROLL_KEY=<key> in clear into projection5000-provision.sh; the rendered scratch card shows it at provision.sh:8. tools/flasher/README.md:339-348 states the un-booted card carries that key plus the Wi-Fi key and says to treat it like a password and never leave it lying around, which directly contradicts the checklist. cloud/src/api.js:239-241 and 283-287 confirm re-enrolling an existing device_id returns the stored device token, and that token unlocks /api/camera-config (api.js:311-327, Wyze email/password/api_key) and the tunnel token in sync (api.js:97-110); the key alone also enrolls arbitrary new devices. The defect is a documentation contradiction that leads the owner to leave a fleet credential in a public room, so it is real as described.


**Verify on hardware.** On a flashed but never-booted spare, in Windows open the boot (FAT) partition and confirm projection5000-provision.sh contains ENROLL_KEY=... in clear. On a booted Pi: ls /boot/firmware/projection5000-provision.sh /boot/firmware/firstrun.sh should report no such file, and sudo ls /usr/local/sbin/projection5000-provision.sh should also be gone once the player is installed, confirming the live card no longer holds the key while the spare still does.



## Medium

### M1. run userconf always reports rc=1 on Pi OS (cancel-rename hits the masked getty@tty1), so firstrun.ok is never written

- Severity: **Medium**. Area: First boot. Where: `tools/flasher/firstboot.py:274`.
- Existing test covers it: **no**. tools/flasher/tests/test_firstboot.py::test_firstrun_wipes_itself_and_logs_rc (line 518 stub userconf is "exit 0", line 551 asserts "userconf pi -> rc=0") encodes the wrong assumption and cannot see the defect; the only failure it exercises is the deliberate set_keymap rc=3. test_setup_screen_in_firstrun_and_provision (line 75) checks mask ordering only. A covering test: stub userconf as "exit 1" (cancel-rename's tail on a masked unit) plus an "id" stub "exit 0" via _run_firstrun's stubs param, then assert "id -u -> rc=0" in the log, "1 step(s) failed" (set_keymap only) and, with an imager stub whose set_keymap succeeds, that boot/firstrun.ok exists. After the fix, line 551's "userconf pi -> rc=0" must become "id -u -> rc=0" and the harness needs the id stub (Git Bash id -u pi fails for a non-existent user).

**What is wrong.** firstrun.sh masks getty@tty1.service (line 256) before it calls userconf (line 274). The Trixie userconf ends with '/usr/bin/cancel-rename "$NEWNAME"' and has no set -e, so its exit status is cancel-rename's. cancel-rename (no set -e) ends with 'systemctl --quiet enable getty@tty1' and, because PID 1 is systemd, 'systemctl --quiet --no-block start getty@tty1' as its last command; both fail with 'Unit getty@tty1.service is masked' (rc 1). run() therefore logs '/usr/lib/userconf-pi/userconf projector-admin -> rc=1', FAILS is at least 1 on every normal first boot, the log ends 'firstrun done ...: 1 step(s) failed' and finish() never touches firstrun.ok. tools/flasher/README.md:384-385 and the flasher's own log line (flasher.py:1334) tell the owner that firstrun.ok exists when everything passed, so a healthy card looks broken and a genuinely failed user step is indistinguishable from the baseline. The user is in fact renamed correctly (rename_user, chpasswd -e, disable userconfig, rm rename_user.conf all run before the failing lines).


**How to reproduce.** Code trace: userconf (userconf-pi pios/trixie): last statement '/usr/bin/cancel-rename "$NEWNAME"'. cancel-rename: 'systemctl --quiet disable userconfig; systemctl --quiet enable getty@tty1; if [ "$INIT" = systemd ]; then ...; systemctl --quiet --no-block start getty@tty1; fi' with INIT=$(ps --no-headers -o comm 1)=systemd. Both systemctl calls return 1 on a masked unit. In firstrun.sh line 10, run() turns that into FAILS=1; line 18 skips firstrun.ok.


**Fix.**

```text
Keep the mask at firstboot.py:256 (the setup screen depends on it). At firstboot.py:273-274 judge the userconf step by its effect, not by cancel-rename's exit status:

        "if [ -f /usr/lib/userconf-pi/userconf ]; then",
        "  # userconf ends with cancel-rename, whose 'systemctl enable/start getty@tty1' fails (rc=1) on the",
        "  # masked unit after the rename and password are already done: check the effect, not the rc.",
        f'  /usr/lib/userconf-pi/userconf {user} "$HASH"',
        f"  run id -u {user} >/dev/null",

Drop the finder's extra "systemctl disable userconfig" outside the else-branch: cancel-rename already does it and userconf always reaches it (no set -e). Do not move the mask. Tests to change: test_firstboot.py:551 ("userconf pi -> rc=0" -> "id -u -> rc=0") and _run_firstrun's harness (line 518) should stub userconf as "exit 1" and add ("id", "exit 0") to stubs so the run mirrors the Pi; add the regression assertion on firstrun.ok with a set_keymap-passing imager stub.
```


**Verify on hardware.** grep -E 'userconf|done' /boot/firmware/firstrun.log; ls -l /boot/firmware/firstrun.ok; systemctl is-enabled getty@tty1 userconfig; id projector-admin; journalctl -b -1 -o cat | grep -iE 'getty@tty1.*masked|cancel-rename'


### M2. Re-enroll hands the existing device token to anyone holding the site-wide enrollment key

- Severity: **Medium**. Area: Provisioning. Where: `cloud/src/api.js:286`.
- Existing test covers it: **no**. No test in player/tests or tools/flasher/tests touches enroll semantics (provision.sh's enroll() is only exercised for the JSON body shape in tools/flasher/tests/test_firstboot.py). The current same-token behaviour is pinned, and would need flipping, in: cloud/test/enroll.test.js:50-58 (toEqual token first.token, row.token toBe first.token), cms/tests/test_enroll.py:47-51 (test_reenroll_keeps_token_and_updates_name) and :63-66 (test_enroll_manually_registered_device_returns_its_token), and the e2e check cloud/e2e/run_e2e.py:1342 ('re-enroll keeps the existing token'). A covering test: enroll twice, assert the second token differs, the first token gets 401 on GET /api/sync/{id}, the second gets 200, and group_id/playlist_id are unchanged.
- Also reported as: Anyone with the enrollment key (on every unbooted card) can take over any existing device_id.

**What is wrong.** POST /api/enroll with a known device_id returns that device's current token (row.token) whenever the site enrollment key matches. The key is written in clear text to every card's FAT partition until firstrun runs, then sits in /usr/local/sbin/projection5000-provision.sh (root 0700, but on a removable card) until install succeeds, which for a Pi stuck in the give-up loop is forever. So a lost or stolen card is not one device's credential but a master key: device ids are derived from the display names (derive_device_id), so the holder can harvest every device's token via re-enroll and impersonate them (sync, screenshots, command results). docs/adding-a-pi.md tells the owner to rotate the key after losing a card, but rotation does not revoke tokens already harvested, and the provisioning script itself only needs re-enroll to work for a card that has never finished installing.


**How to reproduce.** Code trace: cloud/src/api.js:251 key check, :260 SELECT existing row, :278-283 name update only, :286 `return json({ device_id, token: row.token, ... })`. Runnable: cd cloud && npx vitest run test/enroll.test.js: the case at test/enroll.test.js:50 asserts `again.token === first.token`. Against a live console: POST /api/enroll twice with {key, device_id:"lobby-projector", name:"x"} from any host; the second response carries the first device's token, and `curl -H "Authorization: Bearer <token>" $CONSOLE/api/sync/lobby-projector` (and /api/camera-config) succeed.


**Fix.**

```text
cloud/src/api.js re-enroll branch (lines 278-286): replace the name-only UPDATE with one statement that also mints a token, then return the new one:
  const token = randomToken(32);
  await db.run(ctx.env, "UPDATE devices SET token = ?, name = ? WHERE id = ?", token, name, row.id);
  if (row.name !== name) await db.bumpCameraConfigVersion(ctx.env);
  ... return json({ device_id: deviceId, token, cms_url: ctx.url.origin });
Also: (1) do the same in cms/app/routes/api.py:298-305 (`token = db.new_token()`; `UPDATE devices SET token = ?, name = ? WHERE id = ?`; return that token) since the Python CMS mirrors this endpoint and has the same tests; (2) update the comment at api.js:240-241 and docs/adding-a-pi.md:59-61 to say a re-flash issues a new token and the old card stops working, and that after a lost card the owner should rotate the key AND click New token on any device that card had reached; (3) flip the expectations in cloud/test/enroll.test.js:55-58, cms/tests/test_enroll.py:50 and :66, and cloud/e2e/run_e2e.py:1342. Skip the optional 'refuse re-enroll while recently synced' gate: it adds a state machine to a path the non-technical owner cannot debug from the projector; a re-flash is the replace signal.
```


**Verify on hardware.** On a Pi with a card re-flashed under the same name: sudo journalctl -u projection5000-provision -n 100 (expect "enrolled as <id>"), then sudo grep device_token /etc/projection5000/config.toml and compare with the Devices page token; on the OLD card still running, journalctl -u projector-player -n 50 should show the 401 "check device_token" line after the fix.


### M3. --with-wyze makes an optional camera feature fatal for first boot and installs Docker via unpinned pipe-to-shell

- Severity: **Medium**. Area: Provisioning. Where: `player/deploy/install-player.sh:345`.
- Existing test covers it: **partial**. tools/flasher/tests/test_firstboot.py::test_provision_with_wyze_flag only checks the flag reaches the command line. player/tests/test_installer_pi.py::test_camera_bridge_only_on_arm64_with_enough_ram runs the Docker block but fakes curl/docker/systemctl as always-succeeding functions, so the fatal path is never exercised; it asserts "docker pull mrlt8/wyze-bridge:latest" and "systemctl enable --now docker.service" appear in the call log. Test to add (same file, reuse fake_proc/caps_and_wyze_blocks with board=pi4): fake `systemctl() { [[ "$*" == "enable --now docker.service" ]] && return 1; echo "systemctl $*" >> "$LOG"; }` and assert returncode 0, "WARNING: Docker could not be installed" in stderr, "WITH_WYZE=0 CAMERA_SUPPORTED=1" in stdout and no "docker pull" in the log. Tests needing edits for the fix: test_installer_pi.py:52 (block start marker string changes if the `if` header is rewritten) and :75 (drop or change the ":latest" assertion if the image is pinned); player/tests/test_camera_config.py::test_installer_wyze_env_block slices from `    WYZE_ENV=` and stays valid as long as the env block remains inside an if ... fi.
- Also reported as: The optional camera step (get.docker.com, docker.service) is unguarded: on the flasher's provision path its failure means 'The player did not install.'.

**What is wrong.** with_wyze is site-wide (the console's wyze_configured), so every projector at a site with a Wyze login gets --with-wyze (firstboot.py:410) even without a camera. On arm64 boards with 1 GB+ RAM (the camera gate at install-player.sh:165-168 already skips Docker on armhf and small boards) the installer runs `curl -fsSL https://get.docker.com | sh` (line 345) and `systemctl enable --now docker.service` (line 347) unguarded under `set -euo pipefail`. If get.docker.com or download.docker.com is blocked or flakes, or Docker's script rejects the OS, the installer exits before the systemd units are installed (lines 375-406), provision.sh retries 20 times and gives up with "The player did not install." The cloudflared install, the other optional dependency, is already non-fatal by design. get.docker.com is unpinned remote code run as root on every card, and mrlt8/wyze-bridge:latest is unpinned both at line 350 and in projector-wyze-bridge.service ExecStartPre.


**How to reproduce.** Extract the WITH_WYZE block (lines 342-373) into a bash script with `set -euo pipefail`, no `docker` on PATH, and `curl() { return 22; }`: the script exits 22 after "==> Installing Docker for the Wyze bridge" and never reaches the code after the block; with `curl() { return 0; }` it continues. Code trace: firstboot.py:410 -> rendered provision.sh install loop -> install-player.sh:345 fails -> set -e exits before line 375 -> provision attempt fails -> 20 retries -> "The player did not install."


**Fix.**

Keep the finder's intent (non-fatal like cloudflared, apt-signed docker.io from trixie main, pinned image) but restructure so a Docker failure also skips writing wyze.env and does not run apt-get when docker is already present (keeps the existing harness passing, where `docker` is a shell function and apt-get is not faked):

```bash
if [[ "${WITH_WYZE}" == 1 ]]; then
    echo "==> Installing Docker for the Wyze bridge"
    # optional feature (like cloudflared): a failure here must not fail the install
    if ! { { command -v docker >/dev/null 2>&1 || apt-get install -y docker.io; } && systemctl enable --now docker.service; }; then
        echo "WARNING: Docker could not be installed; the camera bridge is skipped (re-run the installer with --with-wyze later)" >&2
        WITH_WYZE=0
    fi
fi
if [[ "${WITH_WYZE}" == 1 ]]; then
    # Pre-pull the bridge image: ...
    docker pull "${WYZE_BRIDGE_IMAGE}" || true
    WYZE_ENV="${DATA_DIR}/wyze.env"
    ... (existing env block unchanged) ...
fi
```

Define `WYZE_BRIDGE_IMAGE=mrlt8/wyze-bridge:<current release tag>` near the top and use the same tag in projector-wyze-bridge.service lines 24 and 29 (the `ExecStartPre=-docker pull :latest` on every restart is the bigger supply-chain exposure: a bad upstream push breaks every site's camera at the next restart). Update docs/camera.md:103 (no longer get.docker.com) and the two test assertions named in test_note. apt-get update already ran in the "Installing system dependencies" step, so docker.io resolves. The daemon's camera_error path already tells the console the camera is down, so no new reporting is needed.


**Verify on hardware.** On a Pi 4/5 already running the player: sudo sh -c 'echo "0.0.0.0 get.docker.com" >> /etc/hosts'; cd /opt/projection5000-src/player 2>/dev/null || cd /opt/piplayer/player; sudo DEVICE_ID=test DEVICE_TOKEN=x CMS_URL=http://127.0.0.1:5000 bash deploy/install-player.sh --with-wyze; echo rc=$?  -> expect a non-zero rc right after "==> Installing Docker for the Wyze bridge" and no "==> Installing systemd units" line. For the first-boot path on a fresh card with the same hosts entry: cat /var/log/projection5000-provision.log and journalctl -u projection5000-provision.service -n 200 -> 20 "attempt N failed" lines ending in "GAVE UP", and the HDMI screen shows "Setup did not finish. The player did not install."; systemctl status projector-player.service reports the unit does not exist.


### M4. 'Ready. Waiting for the first video.' is shown without checking that the player actually started

- Severity: **Medium**. Area: Provisioning. Where: `player/deploy/install-player.sh:429`.
- Existing test covers it: **no**. Nothing exercises step 4 or the success branch outcome. player/tests/test_installer_pi.py:108 only asserts the Step 4 screen string precedes 'Starting services'; tools/flasher/tests/test_firstboot.py:89-100 asserts string ordering; test_provision_enrolls_then_installs / test_provision_retries_enrollment_and_install drive a stub installer that exits 0 or 9 and never model 'installed but not running'. Tests that would cover it: (1) in test_installer_pi.py, slice SRC from 'echo "==> Starting services"' to 'echo "==> Done."', run under bash with a stub systemctl whose `is-active` prints activating and `show -p NRestarts --value` prints 3 for projector-mpv.service, assert exit 3 and 'did not start' in stderr; then a stub answering active/0, assert exit 0. (2) in test_firstboot.py, add an install_rc=3 option to _provision_harness's stub installer and assert the give-up screen appears on attempt 1 with 'The player installed but did not start.' and that sbin/archive are kept. Existing tests need no changes: the stub installer's exit 9 still maps to 'The player did not install.' and the string-ordering marks are preserved.

**What is wrong.** Step 4 of 4 (install-player.sh:427-432) restarts projector-mpv and projector-player, both Type=simple with Restart=always, so `systemctl restart` returns 0 the moment the process forks and `systemctl status ... || true` hides a crash loop; the script exits 0 regardless. provision.sh (firstboot.py:485-489) then disables itself, deletes the archive and prints 'Ready. Waiting for the first video.' on tty1. The finder's example triggers do not produce this: a bad token is caught by the daemon (daemon.py:331-336) and shown as a TOKEN REJECTED screen via mpv within seconds (covering tty1), mpv --idle=yes does not touch DRM until it plays something, and the fonts ship in player/fonts. Only a daemon that dies at startup for an unforeseen reason (broken wheel, config _fail exit, permissions) leaves the misleading 'Ready' text on screen; rare, hence Low.


**How to reproduce.** Code trace: install-player.sh:427-432 (Type=simple units, `|| true`, no is-active check) -> exit 0 at 435-444 -> firstboot.py:484 `&& ... bash deploy/install-player.sh` true branch -> 485-489 disable + rm + screen "Ready". Bad-token path does NOT reproduce it: daemon.py:331-336 sets last_failure="token", daemon.py:148-149 shows the TOKEN REJECTED screen through mpv.


**Fix.**

```text
install-player.sh: replace lines 430-432 with a real check, reusing the is-active + NRestarts pattern from update-player.sh:86-88 (systemctl restart resets NRestarts, so any restart within the window means a crash):

    sleep 30   # long enough for a broken mpv/daemon to have died and been restarted at least once (RestartSec=5)
    for u in projector-mpv projector-player; do
        if [[ "$(systemctl is-active "$u.service" 2>/dev/null)" != active \
              || "$(systemctl show -p NRestarts --value "$u.service" 2>/dev/null)" != 0 ]]; then
            systemctl --no-pager --lines=20 status "$u.service" || true
            echo "!! $u.service did not stay running; see: journalctl -u $u.service -n 100" >&2
            exit 3
        fi
    done

Drop the `[ -S /tmp/projector-mpv.sock ]` poll from the proposed fix: the daemon already waits 60 s for the socket itself, and a socket check cannot distinguish a live mpv from one mid-crash-loop.

firstboot.py render_provision (lines 480-490): capture the installer's exit code and give up immediately on 3 instead of re-running the full installer 20 times for a deterministic failure:

    { [ -n "$DEVICE_TOKEN" ] || enroll; } && rm -rf /opt/projection5000-src && mkdir -p /opt/projection5000-src \
       && tar -xzf "$SRC" -C /opt/projection5000-src \
       && (cd /opt/projection5000-src/player && DEVICE_ID=... bash deploy/install-player.sh<flags>)
    rc=$?
    if [ "$rc" -eq 0 ]; then <existing success branch>; fi
    if [ "$rc" -eq 3 ]; then REASON="The player installed but did not start."; TRIES=20; fi
    <existing -ge 20 give-up block>

The archive and provision unit are then kept, the log lands on the FAT partition and tty1 shows 'Setup did not finish. The player installed but did not start. Log: /boot/firmware/setup-failed.log' within a minute. Keep the 'Ready. Waiting for the first video.' screen: once the units are verified it is true for the few seconds it is visible. The UPGRADE path (exit at line 416) and update-player.sh's own postcheck are unaffected.
```


**Verify on hardware.** On a provisioned Pi, force a crash loop that survives the installer's unit copy (drop-ins are kept): `sudo mkdir -p /etc/systemd/system/projector-player.service.d && printf '[Service]\nExecStart=\nExecStart=/bin/false\n' | sudo tee /etc/systemd/system/projector-player.service.d/break.conf && sudo systemctl daemon-reload`. Then re-run the installer from the kept source tree: `cd /opt/projection5000-src/player && sudo DEVICE_ID=$(sudo grep device_id /etc/projector-player/config.toml | cut -d'"' -f2) DEVICE_TOKEN=x CMS_URL=https://projectors.photogen5000.com bash deploy/install-player.sh; echo rc=$?`. Expected with the bug: `rc=0` printed while `systemctl is-active projector-player.service` prints activating or failed and `journalctl -u projector-player -n 20` shows the restart loop. Clean up: `sudo rm /etc/systemd/system/projector-player.service.d/break.conf && sudo systemctl daemon-reload && sudo systemctl restart projector-player`. To confirm the bad-token case is NOT a repro: set device_token to garbage in config.toml, restart the player, watch the HDMI output show the TOKEN REJECTED screen while `journalctl -u projector-player -n 50` logs 401s.


### M5. REASON says 'The console did not accept this projector' for any curl failure; console detail discarded; permanent errors retried for 20 minutes

- Severity: **Medium**. Area: Provisioning. Where: `tools/flasher/firstboot.py:449`.
- Existing test covers it: **partial**. tools/flasher/tests/test_firstboot.py::test_provision_retries_enrollment_and_install exercises enroll failure (curl rc 22, then a 200 without a token) and the installer give-up screen, and test_setup_screen_in_firstrun_and_provision pins the generic REASON string; nothing checks what the owner sees for a network drop versus a 401/400, that a 401 gives up immediately, or that the console's detail reaches the log. The prototype adds test_provision_reason_names_the_real_failure (curl 7 x3 -> 'could not be reached' on the give-up screen; (401, detail) -> one enroll call, 'This card is out of date ... Make a new card with the flasher.'; (400, detail) -> detail shown; 429 then 503 then 200 -> keeps retrying and succeeds), which fails on the current script and passes with the fix.

**What is wrong.** enroll() sets one REASON for every non-zero outcome: network drop (curl rc 6/7/28), TLS failure (rc 60), a 5xx or 429 from the console, or a real 401. The give-up screen then tells the owner the console rejected the projector when the network simply dropped. The log line (line 448) states 'HTTP 401 means ... re-flash the card' regardless of the actual status, and `curl -f -o $out` throws away the console's own detail ('invalid enrollment key', 'device_id must be ...', 'Too many failed attempts'). A genuinely rotated key (401) or a bad name (400) is permanent, yet the loop still burns 20 tries over 20 minutes before showing anything.
