On retry the screen (line 500) also reverts to 'Step 2 of 4: joining the network' even when the installer failed after the network was fine.


**How to reproduce.** cd <checkout>/tools/flasher && python -c "import sys,pathlib,tempfile; sys.path[:0]=['tests','.']; import firstboot, test_firstboot as t; r=t._provision_harness(pathlib.Path(tempfile.mkdtemp()), firstboot.render_provision(t.cfg()), [7,7,7]); print(r['rc']); print(r['tty'][r['tty'].rindex(chr(27)+'[2J'):]); print([l for l in r['log'].splitlines() if 'enrollment failed' in l][0])" -> rc 1, screen "The console did not accept this projector (enrollment failed).", log "enrollment failed (curl rc=7). HTTP 401 means ..."; the tty also contains "Step 2 of 4: joining the network" alongside "Try 2 did not finish".


**Fix.**

```text
Smaller than the proposed fix and verified against the harness (98 flasher tests pass with the diff below; prototype at docs/audits/2026-09-20-pi-os/repro/fix-prototype/{firstboot.py,test_firstboot.py}). In render_provision(), replace lines 435-452 (body of enroll()) with:

  local out rc code detail
  out=$(mktemp) || return 1
  code=$(ENROLL_KEY=... DEVICE_ID=... DEVICE_NAME=... python3 -c {q(body)} \
    | curl -sS --max-time 30 -X POST "$CONSOLE/api/enroll" -H "content-type: application/json" -d @- -o "$out" -w "%{http_code}")
  rc=$?
  if [ "$rc" -eq 0 ] && [ "$code" = 200 ]; then
    DEVICE_TOKEN=$(python3 -c 'import json, sys; print(json.load(sys.stdin)["token"])' <"$out")
    CMS_URL=$(python3 -c 'import json, sys; print(json.load(sys.stdin).get("cms_url") or "")' <"$out")
    [ -n "$CMS_URL" ] || CMS_URL="$CONSOLE"
  fi
  # The body's detail field is safe to log (never the token): it names what the console objected to.
  detail=$(python3 -c 'import json, sys; print(json.load(sys.stdin).get("detail") or "")' <"$out" 2>/dev/null)
  rm -f "$out"
  [ -n "$DEVICE_TOKEN" ] && { echo "enrolled as $DEVICE_ID at $CMS_URL"; return 0; }
  case "$rc:$code" in
    0:401) REASON="This card is out of date: the console's enrollment key has changed. Make a new card with the flasher."; FATAL=1 ;;
    0:429) REASON="The console asked this projector to wait before trying again." ;;
    0:4[0-9][0-9]) REASON="The console rejected this projector (${detail:-HTTP $code})."; FATAL=1 ;;
    0:*) REASON="The console answered with an error (HTTP $code${detail:+: $detail})." ;;
    *) REASON="The console could not be reached (network problem, curl error $rc)." ;;
  esac
  echo "enrollment failed (curl rc=$rc) HTTP ${code:-none}: $REASON"
  return 1

Line 491: `if [ "$TRIES" -ge 20 ] || [ -n "$FATAL" ]; then` (401/400 give up at once; 429/5xx/network keep the 20-try loop). Line 500: `screen "Setting up this projector" "Try $TRIES did not finish: $REASON" "Trying again in a minute."` (shows the real reason instead of a wrong step number; REASON is already 'The player did not install.' when the installer fails, line 479). No `DEVICE_TOKEN=` reset is needed: the token is only assigned on a 200. Skipped the rc-60 clock hint: the script already sets the clock from the console's Date header (lines 460-463) before the loop. Tests to adjust: tests/test_firstboot.py:94 (REASON marker -> 'The console could not be reached'), :320 (exact curl line gains -w "%{http_code}" and loses -f), :495 ('Try 2 did not finish: The player did not install.'), and the curl stub in _provision_harness (:408-409) must print the status: `'[ "$rc" = 0 ] || exit "$rc"'`, then `code=$(sed -n 2p "$REC/resp.$n"); case "$code" in [0-9][0-9][0-9]) tail -n +3 ...;; *) code=200; tail -n +2 ...;; esac`, `printf "%s" "$code"`, with (status, body) tuples accepted in `responses` (:398). test_provision_retries_enrollment_and_install's 'enrollment failed (curl rc=22)' / '(curl rc=0)' assertions keep passing because the log line keeps that prefix.
```


**Verify on hardware.** On a Pi that showed "Setup did not finish.": read /boot/firmware/setup-failed.log (or `sudo cat /var/log/projection5000-provision.log`) and note the "enrollment failed (curl rc=N)" lines: rc 6/7/28 = network, rc 22 = any HTTP 4xx/5xx with no status shown. To force the two cases: `sudo sed -i 's#^CONSOLE=.*#CONSOLE=https://127.0.0.1:1#' /usr/local/sbin/projection5000-provision.sh && sudo systemctl start projection5000-provision.service` (network case), and separately edit ENROLL_KEY= to a wrong value (401 case); both end on the same "The console did not accept this projector" screen.


### M6. Network wait blames the Wi-Fi name and password for every cause, including wired Pis and a down console, and never gives up

- Severity: **Medium**. Area: Provisioning. Where: `tools/flasher/firstboot.py:470`.
- Existing test covers it: **partial**. tools/flasher/tests/test_firstboot.py::test_setup_screen_in_firstrun_and_provision (line 88) only asserts the substring "check the Wi-Fi name and password" exists in the Wi-Fi render; _provision_harness (line 403) makes every /api/health probe succeed, so the wait loop, its hint and the missing give-up are never exercised. Test that would cover it: add a `health_fails: int` arg to _provision_harness (curl stub exits 7 for the first N /api/health calls) and a stub `ip` in bin_dir that prints "default via 10.0.0.1 dev eth0" or nothing; then (a) cfg(ethernet_only=True, ssid="") + no route: tty contains "No network cable found" and never "Wi-Fi"; (b) cfg() + no route: tty contains 'Could not join the Wi-Fi network "Venue WiFi"' and "make a new card"; (c) cfg() + route present: tty contains "console at https://projectors.photogen5000.com is not answering"; (d) with health_fails=40 (sleep 15 is already replaced by sleep 0 in the harness) boot_log is not None even though the script then goes on to install. Plus a pure render test: "Wi-Fi" not in render_provision(cfg(ethernet_only=True, ssid="")). The proposed fix keeps "check the Wi-Fi name and password" as a substring so the existing mark at line 88 still passes; only the mark 'cp /var/log/projection5000-provision.log "$BOOT/setup-failed.log"' (line 90) needs changing (use p.rindex or drop it from the ordered list), because the cp line now also appears earlier inside the wait loop and p.index finds the first occurrence.
- Also reported as: First-boot screen says "check the Wi-Fi name and password" on an Ethernet-only card.

**What is wrong.** The health loop (lines 467-473) is the only place a stuck network is reported, and after 8 probes it always prints 'This is taking longer than usual: check the Wi-Fi name and password.' The script is rendered without any knowledge of ethernet_only, so a wired Pi with an unplugged cable tells the owner to check Wi-Fi; a Pi that is on the network but cannot reach the console (console down, DNS failure, TLS failing because the clock is stale, captive portal) gets the same text; and for the one case where the text is right (wrong password) it does not say that the only fix is a new card. Because this loop never exits, setup-failed.log is never written for the most common first-boot failure, so the owner has nothing to send from a PC either. On Pi OS, NetworkManager stops retrying a Wi-Fi connection after 4 failed attempts (connection.autoconnect-retries), so the Pi really does sit there until re-flashed.


**How to reproduce.** cd "<checkout>" && python -c "import sys; sys.path.insert(0,'tools/flasher'); import firstboot; c=firstboot.sample_config(); c.update(ethernet_only=True, ssid=''); p=firstboot.render_provision(c); print('check the Wi-Fi name and password' in p, any(w in p.lower() for w in ('ethernet','cable','wired')), 'nmcli' in p or 'ip route' in p)"  -> True False False. Code trace: firstboot.py:467 `until curl -fsS ... /api/health` has no break; :470-471 prints the Wi-Fi hint for every cause; :491-498 (setup-failed.log) is only reachable after the loop exits.


**Fix.**

```text
Minimal version of the finder's fix, in render_provision (tools/flasher/firstboot.py:466-473), no nmcli, no extra status file:

Python, before `lines = [`:
    if c["ethernet_only"]:
        net_hint = "No network cable found: plug the projector into the router."
    else:
        net_hint = f'Could not join the Wi-Fi network "{c["ssid"]}": check the Wi-Fi name and password, then make a new card.'

Replace lines 466-473 with:
    "WAITS=0",
    f"NET_HINT={q(net_hint)}",
    'until curl -fsS --max-time 10 "$CONSOLE/api/health" >/dev/null; do',
    "  WAITS=$((WAITS + 1))",
    '  if ip route show default 2>/dev/null | grep -q .; then',
    '    HINT="On the network, but the console at $CONSOLE is not answering. Check the internet connection."',
    "  else",
    '    HINT="$NET_HINT"',
    "  fi",
    '  echo "waiting for console at $CONSOLE ($HINT)"',
    '  [ "$WAITS" -lt 8 ] || screen "Setting up this projector" "Step 2 of 4: joining the network" "$HINT"',
    '  if [ $((WAITS % 40)) -eq 0 ]; then  # every 10 minutes: park the log where a PC can read it',
    '    BOOT=/boot/firmware; [ -d "$BOOT" ] || BOOT=/boot',
    '    cp /var/log/projection5000-provision.log "$BOOT/setup-failed.log" 2>/dev/null; sync',
    "  fi",
    "  sleep 15",
    "done",

Why not the finder's give-up after 30 minutes: the wait is recoverable (cable plugged in later, console back up) and provision.service only runs on boot, so exiting would leave the Pi needing a power cycle; copying the log while continuing to wait gives the owner the file without that cost. Why `ip route show default` instead of nmcli: it answers the only question that changes the text (is there any network at all), works for wired and Wi-Fi, and needs no parsing; iproute2 is on Pi OS Lite. The Python-side `c["ssid"]` is the same dict _firstrun already reads at line 301, so no new config plumbing. Tests: test_setup_screen_in_firstrun_and_provision keeps passing except the cp mark at line 90 (first occurrence moves into the wait loop; use rindex or remove that mark); test_provision_* harness runs are unaffected because /api/health always succeeds there (the `ip` stub is only needed by the new tests).
```


**Verify on hardware.** On a wired-only card booted with the cable unplugged, wait 3 minutes and read tty1 (expect the Wi-Fi hint). Then: sudo cat /var/log/projection5000-provision.log (only "waiting for console at ..." lines, no GAVE UP); ls /boot/firmware/setup-failed.log (absent); nmcli -t -f NAME,TYPE,DEVICE,STATE con show; ip route show default; journalctl -u NetworkManager -n 50 --no-pager. Repeat with a Wi-Fi card whose console_url points at a dead host: nmcli shows the Wi-Fi connection activated and ip route has a default, yet tty1 still says to check the Wi-Fi name and password.


### M7. Give-up screen names a Pi path and gives no action; unit never retries by itself after exit 1

- Severity: **Medium**. Area: Provisioning. Where: `tools/flasher/firstboot.py:496`.
- Existing test covers it: **partial**. tools/flasher/tests/test_firstboot.py::test_setup_screen_in_firstrun_and_provision (line 91 mark) and ::test_provision_retries_enrollment_and_install (line 492 'Log: /boot/firmware/setup-failed.log' in last) pin the current screen text and must be updated to the new wording. Nothing asserts Restart= in the unit; add to test_firstrun_script (around line 56): assert "Restart=on-failure" in s and "RestartSec=30min" in s. Keep line 493 (boot_log really exists) as is.
- Also reported as: "Setup did not finish." screen gives no action and points at a Linux path.

**What is wrong.** After 20 failed enroll/install attempts (about 20 minutes; a console that is simply unreachable never gets here because the /api/health wait at lines 466-473 is unbounded) the HDMI screen shows 'Setup did not finish.', REASON, and 'Log: /boot/firmware/setup-failed.log'. A non-technical owner cannot use a Pi path, does not know the file shows up as setup-failed.log on the 'bootfs' drive in a PC, and is not told that a power-cycle retries. The log's own advice (line 492, 'run sudo systemctl start ...') requires SSH they do not have. The unit at lines 316-328 has no Restart=, so a transient apt/pip mirror or console-side error leaves the Pi parked on the failure screen until someone unplugs it.


**How to reproduce.** cd "<checkout>/tools/flasher" && python -m pytest -q tests/test_firstboot.py -k "provision_retries_enrollment_and_install" (harness with install_fails=True: rc=1, tty ends with 'Setup did not finish.' ... 'Log: /boot/firmware/setup-failed.log'). Code trace: firstboot.py:491-497 give-up branch; 316-328 unit without Restart=.


**Fix.**

```text
firstboot.py:496 replace with:
    '    screen "Setup did not finish." "$REASON" "Unplug the projector and plug it back in to try again." \\',
    '      "Still failing? Put the card in a computer and send Matt the file" "setup-failed.log from the bootfs drive."',
(each hint is its own screen() argument so it centres on one line and fits 80 columns).
firstboot.py:492 change the log line to: 'echo "GAVE UP after $TRIES attempts; the service retries in 30 minutes or on the next boot."'
firstboot.py:323-324 (between Type=simple and ExecStart) add: "Restart=on-failure", "RestartSec=30min". Do not add StartLimitIntervalSec=0: the script runs 20+ minutes before exit 1 and RestartSec is 30 min, so the default start limit (5 starts in 10 s) can never trip. Keep exit 1 so the failure screen stays up until the retry. Update the two asserts named in test_note and add the Restart assert to test_firstrun_script.
```


**Verify on hardware.** On a Pi that reached the failure screen: systemctl show projection5000-provision -p Restart -p Result -p ActiveState (expect Restart=no, Result=exit-code, ActiveState=failed); journalctl -u projection5000-provision -n 50; tail -5 /var/log/projection5000-provision.log; ls -l /boot/firmware/setup-failed.log. Then power-cycle and confirm 'install attempt 1' reappears in the log.


### M8. apt calls run unattended (provision service, nightly systemd-run) with no DEBIAN_FRONTEND, no confold, no dpkg --configure -a, and they upgrade packages every night

- Severity: **Medium**. Area: Installer. Where: `player/deploy/install-player.sh:180`.
- Existing test covers it: **no**. player/tests/test_updater.py replaces install-player.sh with INSTALLER_STUB (lines 188-201) and shims apt-get only for update-os.sh (line 329 asserts its three hardened calls), so the installer's apt block never runs there. player/tests/test_installer_pi.py extracts only pi_caps/the camera gate/the wyze block (caps_and_wyze_blocks, lines 46-51); line 107 only checks by string index that screen_init precedes "apt-get update". tools/flasher/tests/test_firstboot.py renders provision.sh and never reaches the installer. A covering test: in test_installer_pi.py extract the block from 'echo "==> Installing system dependencies"' to 'echo "==> Creating user' with apt-get/dpkg/dpkg-query shims that log "$*" plus "$DEBIAN_FRONTEND"; assert that with UPGRADE=1 and all packages reported installed no apt-get call is logged, and that with UPGRADE=0 the log contains "apt-get install -y --no-upgrade -o Dpkg::Options::=--force-confold" preceded by "dpkg --configure -a" and DEBIAN_FRONTEND=noninteractive. The existing assertion at line 107 keeps passing with the fix (the "apt-get update" string still follows screen_init).

**What is wrong.** install-player.sh:176-180 run `apt-get update` and `apt-get install -y git rsync mpv ffmpeg python3 python3-venv python3-pip libgl1 libegl1 v4l-utils` on every invocation, including `--upgrade`, which update-player.sh:157 runs (inside a systemd-run transient unit with stdin on /dev/null) whenever the release sha changed, and which the flasher's provision.service runs on first boot. `apt-get install` of an already-installed package upgrades it when a newer candidate exists, so a player update also performs a partial OS upgrade of those packages without `--force-confold` or DEBIAN_FRONTEND=noninteractive (update-os.sh:63-65 sets both). Neither install-player.sh nor update-os.sh runs `dpkg --configure -a`, so a dpkg run interrupted by a power cut (most likely during update-os.sh's full upgrade) leaves apt refusing every later `apt-get install` with "dpkg was interrupted, you must manually run 'dpkg --configure -a'": every subsequent player update fails at line 180, update-player.sh restores .prev and reports "install-player.sh --upgrade failed (see /var/lib/projector-player/update.log)" to the console, and a provision retry fails 20 times, until someone with SSH runs dpkg by hand. `set -e` (line 27) also turns a non-transient `apt-get update` failure (hash-sum mismatch during a mirror sync, Release not-valid-yet on a clock-skewed Pi, 4xx/5xx from pkg.cloudflare.com whose sources entry line 323 leaves installed) into a rolled-back update even though the code checkout already succeeded. Not every night: update-player.sh:145-149 skips install-player.sh entirely when the sha is unchanged, and nightly auto-update is opt-in (manifest update.auto, default off). Plain DNS/connect failures of a mirror are warnings (exit 0) and do not trigger the rollback.


**How to reproduce.** Code trace: player/player/updater.py:190 run_update_player -> update-player.sh:177 systemd-run (stdin /dev/null) -> update-player.sh:145 only when sha differs from RELEASE -> :157 `bash install-player.sh --upgrade` -> install-player.sh:176 `apt-get update`, :180 `apt-get install -y ...` (grep -n DEBIAN_FRONTEND player/deploy/install-player.sh returns nothing; grep -n 'dpkg --configure' player/deploy/*.sh returns nothing). Any non-zero exit (apt refusing with "dpkg was interrupted", or `apt-get update` exit 100 on a non-transient index failure) trips `set -euo pipefail` at :27; update-player.sh:158 fail -> :110 restore_prev -> :112 write_status false "install-player.sh --upgrade failed (see ...)". Harness: in player/tests/test_updater.py the apt-get shim at :208 always exits 0 and install-player.sh is stubbed at :201, so replacing the stub with a copy of the real installer and an `apt-get` shim that `exit 100`s reproduces the rollback and the unfriendly status message.


**Fix.**

```text
Replace lines 176-180 with:

PKGS=(git rsync mpv ffmpeg python3 python3-venv python3-pip libgl1 libegl1 v4l-utils)
export DEBIAN_FRONTEND=noninteractive
# --upgrade with everything already present skips apt entirely: update-os.sh owns OS upgrades,
# so a code update never changes mpv/ffmpeg and never depends on the mirrors being up.
if [[ "${UPGRADE}" == 1 ]] && ! dpkg-query -W -f='${Status}\n' "${PKGS[@]}" 2>&1 | grep -qv '^install ok installed$'; then
    echo "    system packages already installed; skipping apt"
else
    dpkg --configure -a || true   # finish a dpkg run cut short by a power loss; otherwise apt refuses to run
    apt-get update
    apt-get install -y --no-upgrade -o Dpkg::Options::=--force-confold "${PKGS[@]}"
fi

Keep `set -e` for the fresh-install path (a missing mpv must fail loudly). The finder's "non-fatal with || echo WARNING" variant is unnecessary once the --upgrade path skips apt when nothing is missing. Apply the same one-line `dpkg --configure -a || true` before `apt-get update` in update-os.sh:64 (test_updater.py:329 asserts only the apt-get shim calls, so it still passes; dpkg is not on PATH in the harness and `|| true` covers it). No existing test needs changing.
```


**Verify on hardware.** sudo dpkg --audit; ls -la /var/lib/dpkg/updates/ (any files besides tmp.i mean an interrupted run); sudo tail -n 300 /var/lib/projector-player/update.log | grep -n -i 'dpkg was interrupted\|conffile prompt\|Some index files failed\|will be upgraded\|FAILED'; journalctl -u projector-player-update -n 200; journalctl -u projector-os-update -n 200; cat /var/lib/projector-player/update-status.json; to reproduce the stuck state on a spare Pi: sudo touch /var/lib/dpkg/updates/0000 then sudo /opt/piplayer/player/deploy/update-player.sh main and read update-status.json (expect ok=false with the install-player.sh message), then sudo rm /var/lib/dpkg/updates/0000 && sudo dpkg --configure -a to clean up.


### M9. hwdec line is only appended for bcm283[567]: the Pi 4 (bcm2711) is left on auto-safe, which can never pick the Pi 4's H.264 hardware decoder

- Severity: **Medium**. Area: Installer. Where: `player/deploy/install-player.sh:213`.
- Existing test covers it: **partial**. player/tests/test_installer_pi.py::test_mpv_conf_gets_hwdec_on_videocore_iv_boards[pi4] (lines 151-152) asserts `conf == dist and "v4l2m2m" not in res.stdout`, i.e. it enshrines the current behaviour and will FAIL after the fix; change the pi4 branch to expect a last line of `hwdec=drm,v4l2m2m-copy`, the model string in conf, and `f"{model}: hwdec=drm,v4l2m2m-copy"` in stdout, leaving the pi5 branch as is. Also update the docstring at line 3 and the test name. test_audit2_player.py:459-471 and test_installer_pi.py:153-156 (custom existing mpv.conf survives an upgrade) are unaffected by the fix, including the regenerate-when-identical extension, because both write a file that differs from the dist. A new assertion to add to the pi4 case: pre-create mpv.conf equal to the dist, run the block again, and assert the hwdec line is now appended (covers the upgrade-migration part of the fix).
- Also reported as: Pi 4/400 gets no hardware H.264 decode: hwdec=auto-safe never selects v4l2m2m and the installer only adds it for VideoCore IV.

**What is wrong.** Line 213 appends `hwdec=v4l2m2m-copy` only when the device-tree compatible matches bcm2835/6/7. A Pi 4 / 400 (brcm,bcm2711) keeps deploy/mpv.conf's `hwdec=auto-safe`. mpv's auto/auto-safe whitelist (video/decode/vd_lavc.c hwdec_autoprobe_info) contains drm and drm-copy but not v4l2m2m/v4l2m2m-copy, and on the Pi 4 the only H.264 hardware decoder is the firmware codec behind V4L2 M2M (bcm2835-codec); the drm (v4l2-request) path is rpivid, HEVC only. So on the most common board every 1080p H.264 item is software-decoded by four Cortex-A72 cores, which is borderline at 1080p30 and drops frames at 1080p60 or with bigger bitrates, while the mpv.conf comment (line 19-20) itself names `hwdec=v4l2m2m-copy` as the Pi 4 H.264 fix and leaves it to a manual edit no non-technical owner will make. test_installer_pi.py line 151-152 enshrines the Pi 4 having no hwdec line.


**How to reproduce.** Code trace: pi_caps reads /proc/device-tree/compatible = 'raspberrypi,4-model-b\0brcm,bcm2711'; `[[ "${PI_SOC}" == *bcm283[567]* ]]` is false; mpv.conf ends with hwdec=auto-safe. Harness: tests/test_installer_pi.py::test_mpv_conf_gets_hwdec_on_videocore_iv_boards[pi4] passes with `assert conf == dist`. mpv source: curl -fsSL https://raw.githubusercontent.com/mpv-player/mpv/master/video/decode/vd_lavc.c | grep -n WHITELIST shows no v4l2m2m entry.


**Fix.**

In player/deploy/install-player.sh replace lines 211-221 with a per-SoC table, and apply it also on upgrade when the existing file is still the untouched shipped default:

```
apply_hwdec() {   # append the board's hwdec line; later lines win in mpv.conf
    local hwdec="" why=""
    case "${PI_SOC}" in
        *bcm2711*)     hwdec="drm,v4l2m2m-copy"; why="Pi 4 family: HEVC via drm (rpivid), H.264 via the V4L2 M2M firmware decoder" ;;
        *bcm283[567]*) hwdec="v4l2m2m-copy";     why="VideoCore IV: the V4L2 M2M copy path is the H.264 hardware decoder" ;;
    esac
    [[ -n "${hwdec}" ]] || return 0
    echo "    ${PI_MODEL}: hwdec=${hwdec} (${why})"
    printf '\n# %s: %s\nhwdec=%s\n' "${PI_MODEL}" "${why}" "${hwdec}" >> "${MPV_CONF}"
}
if [[ -f "${MPV_CONF}" ]]; then
    cp "${SRC_DIR}/deploy/mpv.conf" "${MPV_CONF}.dist"
    if cmp -s "${SRC_DIR}/deploy/mpv.conf" "${MPV_CONF}"; then
        apply_hwdec          # file is the untouched default: safe to bring it up to date
    else
        echo "    NOTE: existing ${MPV_CONF} kept; the new default is at ${MPV_CONF}.dist"
    fi
else
    cp "${SRC_DIR}/deploy/mpv.conf" "${MPV_CONF}"
    apply_hwdec
fi
```
Nothing for bcm2712 (Pi 5 has no H.264 hardware; auto-safe already picks drm for HEVC). Then update the prose that now contradicts the installer: deploy/mpv.conf lines 16-20 (say the installer appends the board's hwdec line; keep the manual-override note), README.md lines 159-165 and docs/hardware.md line 87 (Pi 4 H.264 now uses the firmware decoder out of the box). Update test_installer_pi.py as described in test_note.


**Verify on hardware.** On a Pi 4/400 running the current install while a 1080p H.264 item plays: `journalctl -u projector-mpv.service -b | grep -i 'hardware decoding'` (expect no "Using hardware decoding" line) and `top -bn1 | grep mpv` (expect well over 100% CPU). Confirm the decoder exists: `v4l2-ctl --list-devices | grep -A3 bcm2835-codec` and `ffmpeg -hide_banner -decoders | grep v4l2m2m` (expect h264_v4l2m2m). Then test the proposed line: `sudo systemctl stop projector-mpv.service; sudo -u projector mpv --vo=gpu --gpu-context=drm --hwdec=drm,v4l2m2m-copy --msg-level=vd=v --length=20 /var/lib/projector-player/media/<file>.mp4 2>&1 | grep -i 'hwdec\|hardware decoding'` (expect "Using hardware decoding (v4l2m2m-copy)" and CPU under ~40%); repeat with an HEVC file and expect "(drm)" or software fallback without a crash.


### M10. Python dependencies unpinned and unhashed, and the whole venv is rebuilt from PyPI on every nightly update

- Severity: **Medium**. Area: Installer. Where: `player/deploy/install-player.sh:227`.
- Existing test covers it: **no**. Nothing covers lines 225-227. player/tests/test_installer_pi.py only extracts pi_caps/camera/mpv.conf blocks (caps_and_wyze_blocks) and never runs the venv block; player/tests/test_updater.py (lines 196-201) replaces install-player.sh with a stub that just writes RELEASE, so the updater harness never exercises pip. tools/flasher/tests/test_firstboot.py only checks the rendered provision.sh text. The proposed fix breaks no existing test. A covering test, following the block-extraction pattern in test_installer_pi.py: slice SRC from 'echo "==> Creating virtualenv"' to 'chown -R root:root', run it under bash with shims `python3() { echo "python3 $*" >> "$LOG"; }` and a fake `${INSTALL_DIR}.prev/.venv/bin/pip`; case A (prev venv present, identical requirements.txt) asserts no `python3 -m venv` / pip call in LOG and `.venv` exists in INSTALL_DIR; case B (requirements differ or no .prev) asserts venv creation and `pip install -r` without `--upgrade pip`. Add a second assertion test that every line of player/requirements.txt matches `^[A-Za-z0-9_.-]+==` so drift back to `>=` fails CI.
- Also reported as: Every update pip-installs unpinned requirements (and the latest pip) from PyPI as root.

**What is wrong.** requirements.txt is three `>=` lines (requests, pillow, broadlink, which pulls in cryptography) with no upper bounds, constraints or hashes, and install-player.sh:226 also self-upgrades pip to the newest release. A hand-run `--upgrade` after git pull reuses the existing venv (python3 -m venv is idempotent without --clear), but update-player.sh:151-155 moves /opt/piplayer/player (venv included) to .prev before calling --upgrade, so lines 225-227 recreate the venv and download every dependency from PyPI (piwheels on armhf) on every update whose sha differs from RELEASE (update-player.sh:145-149 is the only skip). With the default player_release=main, any commit to the public repo, including cloud-only changes, triggers that rebuild; with auto-update set to nightly (off by default, owner opt-in in Settings) it happens unattended in the 03:00-05:00 window. Each projector therefore silently picks up whatever pillow/cryptography/requests major is current at that moment, two Pis flashed a month apart run different dependency code at the same RELEASE sha, the post-update check (update-player.sh:84-104) only catches a daemon that is not active or restarts 3+ times, and a PyPI/piwheels outage during the window makes the update fail and roll back to .prev (reported as failed on the Devices page).


**How to reproduce.** cat player/requirements.txt (3 unpinned lines). Code trace: update-player.sh:145-149 returns only when sha == RELEASE; otherwise :151-155 `rm -rf PREV_DIR; mv INSTALL_DIR PREV_DIR` then :157 `install-player.sh --upgrade`; install-player.sh:225 `python3 -m venv` on an empty tree, :226 `pip install --upgrade pip`, :227 `pip install -r requirements.txt` with no --require-hashes or -c constraints. Harness confirms nothing covers it: player/tests/test_updater.py INSTALLER_STUB (lines 191-201) stands in for install-player.sh and never creates a venv.


**Fix.**

1) Pin the full dependency closure with exact versions (generate once with `pip-compile --no-header --strip-extras requirements.in` on a Pi or with `--python-version 3.13`), without `--require-hashes`: piwheels armhf wheels have different hashes from PyPI so hash mode would fail on Pi Zero/1/2 v1.1. Keep `pip install -r` (no `--no-deps`) so pip still resolves, but every package is now `==`. 2) Delete install-player.sh:226 (`pip install --upgrade pip`); the ensurepip-bundled pip from Debian's python3-venv is sufficient. 3) Replace lines 224-227 with a reuse-or-rebuild block:
```
echo "==> Creating virtualenv"
PREV_VENV="${INSTALL_DIR}.prev/.venv"
if [[ "${UPGRADE}" == 1 && -d "${PREV_VENV}" ]] \
   && cmp -s "${INSTALL_DIR}.prev/requirements.txt" "${INSTALL_DIR}/requirements.txt" \
   && "${PREV_VENV}/bin/python" -c 'import requests, PIL' 2>/dev/null; then
    echo "    requirements unchanged: keeping the previous virtualenv"
    cp -a "${PREV_VENV}" "${INSTALL_DIR}/.venv"
else
    python3 -m venv "${INSTALL_DIR}/.venv"
    "${INSTALL_DIR}/.venv/bin/pip" install -r "${INSTALL_DIR}/requirements.txt"
fi
```
The copy lands at the venv's original absolute path (/opt/piplayer/player/.venv), so its shebangs and pyvenv.cfg stay valid; the import check guards against a half-built .prev venv and falls back to a full rebuild. This removes the nightly PyPI dependency and most of the update runtime on Pi 3/Zero 2. No existing test needs changing.


**Verify on hardware.** /opt/piplayer/player/.venv/bin/pip freeze; grep -c 'Collecting' /var/lib/projector-player/update.log; grep -E 'Successfully installed|already at|updated ' /var/lib/projector-player/update.log | tail -20; ls -ld /opt/piplayer/player.prev/.venv /opt/piplayer/player/.venv


### M11. Tarball installs write RELEASE=unknown: the console shows '<version>+unknown' and the first nightly update always reinstalls

- Severity: **Medium**. Area: Installer. Where: `player/deploy/install-player.sh:238`.
- Existing test covers it: **no**. Not covered. player/tests/test_updater.py::test_player_version_carries_the_release_sha (line 66) only checks the "+sha[:7]" formatting given a RELEASE file. tools/flasher/tests/test_flasher.py::test_player_archive_holds_the_player_tree (line 172) lists archive members but does not require player/RELEASE. player/tests/test_installer_pi.py never runs the RELEASE block. test_updater.py's fake_pi fixture (line 216-235) seeds RELEASE with a real sha, so the "already at" path is only tested from a correct RELEASE, never from the flashed 'unknown' state. Tests to add: (1) in test_flasher.py::test_player_archive_holds_the_player_tree assert "player/RELEASE" in names and that tar.extractfile("player/RELEASE").read().decode().strip().split("+")[0] == subprocess git rev-parse HEAD (skip if git absent), and keep the uid==0 loop; (2) in test_installer_pi.py extract the RELEASE block (SRC from 'RELEASE_SHA=' to 'chmod 644 "${INSTALL_DIR}/RELEASE"'), run it under bash with `git() { return 1; }`, SRC_DIR containing RELEASE=abc123 and INSTALL_DIR in tmp_path, and assert INSTALL_DIR/RELEASE == "abc123"; a second run with PIPLAYER_RELEASE_SHA=def456 must still win. No existing test needs changing; the flasher archive test only gains assertions.
- Also reported as: First-boot installs record RELEASE=unknown so the console cannot tell which build a card carried.

**What is wrong.** The flasher's build_player_archive (tools/flasher/flasher.py:258-277) tars player/ without .git and provision.sh does not set PIPLAYER_RELEASE_SHA, so on every flashed Pi `git -C SRC_DIR rev-parse HEAD` fails and line 238 writes 'unknown'. updater.player_version() then reports e.g. '0.3.0+unknown' to the console for every new projector, which reads as a fault to a non-technical owner, and update-player.sh compares the fetched sha with 'unknown' (line 145) so the first nightly run always does a full reinstall, restart and post-check even when the flashed code is already main's HEAD.


**How to reproduce.** grep -n 'RELEASE\|PIPLAYER_RELEASE_SHA' tools/flasher/flasher.py tools/flasher/firstboot.py (no hits that write a RELEASE file); docs/audits/2026-09-20-pi-os/rendered/provision.sh line 87 passes only DEVICE_ID/DEVICE_TOKEN/CMS_URL; player/player/updater.py:41-44 formats the sha verbatim.


**Fix.** tools/flasher/flasher.py build_player_archive (after tar.add at line 274): compute `sha = subprocess.run(["git","-C",str(src),"rev-parse","HEAD"],capture_output=True,text=True).stdout.strip()` and, if `git status --porcelain -- .` in src is non-empty, append "+dirty"; if git fails or sha is empty, skip the file. Then `ti = tarfile.TarInfo("player/RELEASE"); data = (sha+"\n").encode(); ti.size = len(data); ti.mode = 0o644; ti.uid = ti.gid = 0; ti.uname = ti.gname = "root"; ti.mtime = int(time.time()); tar.addfile(ti, io.BytesIO(data))` (uid/gid 0 is required by test_flasher.py:179-180). Add `import subprocess` to flasher.py. player/deploy/install-player.sh:238 becomes: `RELEASE_SHA="${PIPLAYER_RELEASE_SHA:-$(git -C "${SRC_DIR}" rev-parse HEAD 2>/dev/null || cat "${SRC_DIR}/RELEASE" 2>/dev/null || echo unknown)}"`. Order matters: env from update-player.sh:157 first, live git (dev checkout) second, the shipped RELEASE third. The rsync at line 195 already copies SRC_DIR/RELEASE into INSTALL_DIR before line 239 overwrites it with the same value, so no other change is needed; update-player.sh:145 then matches main HEAD on the first nightly and writes "already at <sha>". Update the header comment at install-player.sh:22 to mention the shipped RELEASE fallback.


**Verify on hardware.**

```text
cat /opt/piplayer/player/RELEASE   # expect "unknown" on a flashed Pi
sudo journalctl -u projection5000-provision -n 200 --no-pager | grep -i release
cat /var/lib/projector-player/update-status.json   # after the first update-player: "updated unknown -> <sha>" instead of "already at <sha>"
```


### M12. ARMv6 cloudflared: raw 'latest' binary with no checksum, no run check, never refreshed (contrary to its own comment), and it contradicts the docs' 'no remote access on Zero / Pi 1'

- Severity: **Medium**. Area: Installer. Where: `player/deploy/install-player.sh:305`.
- Existing test covers it: **partial**. player/tests/test_tunnel.py::test_installer_cloudflared_block[armv6] and [armv6-offline] run the armv6l branch under fake curl/dpkg/apt-get and assert the raw download, chmod/mv and the offline warning, so the branch is exercised but its behaviour is locked in, not questioned; no test asserts a smoke test, a pinned URL/checksum, or that --upgrade re-checks the binary, and none checks the docs. test_missing_binary_writes_the_token_but_never_restarts covers the daemon when /usr/bin/cloudflared is absent (token kept, no restart, one warning), which is exactly the state the corrected fix leaves armv6 boards in. After the fix, change the two armv6 cases to assert: no curl call in calls.log, no usr-bin-cloudflared file, stdout contains "Remote access is not available on", stderr empty; drop the "armv6-offline" parametrization and the FAKE_CURL_RC plumbing, and reword the docstring (lines 156-161).

**What is wrong.** The raw cloudflared-linux-arm asset is built by Cloudflare's release pipeline with TARGET_ARM=5 (.ci/scripts/linux/build-packages.sh), so the ARMv6 branch at install-player.sh:307-317 installs the correct binary and the SIGILL crash-loop scenario does not apply. What remains: (a) line 305's `command -v cloudflared` short-circuit means the raw binary (313-314) and the GitHub .deb fallback (334) are installed exactly once and never refreshed by `--upgrade` (update-player.sh:157) or by update-os.sh (apt only refreshes the apt-repo install), contradicting the comment on line 311 "re-run the installer to refresh it"; (b) docs/adding-a-pi.md:97-98 and :103 and docs/hardware.md:101 tell the owner the Zero and Pi 1 get no remote access, while the installer and console (every device gets a tunnel at enrollment) give them one.


**How to reproduce.** Code trace: pi_caps (install-player.sh:53) sets PI_MACHINE=armv6l -> line 307 -> curl to ${CLOUDFLARED_BIN}.new, chmod, mv (313-314). Any later run, including update-player.sh:157 `install-player.sh --upgrade`: line 305 is true -> "already installed", the branch is never re-entered, so the binary is never updated; the .deb fallback at 334 behaves the same. Harness: cd player && python -m pytest -q tests/test_tunnel.py -k installer_cloudflared_block (armv6 cases pass, confirming the download path; no case exercises a refresh). Upstream: curl -sSL https://raw.githubusercontent.com/cloudflare/cloudflared/master/.ci/scripts/linux/build-packages.sh | grep -n TARGET_ARM shows `export TARGET_ARM=5` for arch=arm, refuting the unset-GOARM claim. Docs: sed -n 97,103p docs/adding-a-pi.md; sed -n 100,102p docs/hardware.md.


**Fix.**

```text
Drop the branch rather than harden it: armv6 boards never get the Wyze bridge (install-player.sh:166-168 forces WITH_WYZE=0) and the tunnel's sole ingress is that bridge on 127.0.0.1:5000, so a working cloudflared there would serve nothing. Replace lines 307-317 with:

elif [[ "${PI_MACHINE}" == armv6l ]]; then
    # Pi Zero / Zero W / Pi 1: no camera bridge on these boards (WITH_WYZE=0
    # above), so the tunnel has nothing to serve; the unit's
    # ConditionPathExists=/usr/bin/cloudflared keeps it skipped, not looping.
    echo "    Remote access is not available on ${PI_MODEL} (no camera bridge on this board); skipping cloudflared"

That matches adding-a-pi.md:97-98/:123-124 and hardware.md:101-102 as written (no doc change needed), keeps the unit skipped via ConditionPathExists, and tunnel.py:56 already logs the one plain warning when the console sends a token. Update test_tunnel.py::test_installer_cloudflared_block as described in test_note. Do not adopt the finder's "make it real" alternative: pinning a version plus sha256 means hand-bumping the installer for every cloudflared release with no apt path to keep it current, and the result would still serve nothing on these boards. If the console must not create tunnels for these devices at all, that is a separate console-side finding (pi_model is already in the sync query).
```


**Verify on hardware.** On a Pi Zero W after install: /usr/bin/cloudflared --version; echo rc=$? (expect a version line and rc=0). After the console has issued a token: systemctl status projector-cloudflared.service; journalctl -u projector-cloudflared.service -n 50 | grep -c 'status=4/ILL' (expect 0). To confirm no refresh: note the version, run sudo bash /opt/piplayer/player/deploy/install-player.sh --upgrade, then /usr/bin/cloudflared --version again (unchanged, and the log says "already installed").


### M13. mpv holds no display while idle, so the Linux text console shows on the projector after every boot and whenever the daemon is down

- Severity: **Medium**. Area: systemd units and mpv.conf. Where: `player/deploy/projector-mpv.service:16`.
- Existing test covers it: **no**. No test in player/tests or tools/flasher/tests asserts the mpv startup flags; test_installer_pi.py::test_mpv_conf_gets_hwdec_on_videocore_iv_boards and test_audit2_player.py::test_installer_preserves_mpv_conf_and_config_extras only compare the installed mpv.conf to the shipped file, so neither breaks with either fix. A covering test: in player/tests/test_installer_pi.py, read DEPLOY / "projector-mpv.service", find the ExecStart= line and assert "--force-window=yes" and "--idle=yes" are both present (a static guard against the flag being dropped). The daemon-side optional change (reconcile before the first sync) would be covered by a test that builds a state with a cached manifest and files on disk, calls the new startup path, and asserts a loadfile happens before any cms.sync_calls.

**What is wrong.** With idle=yes and no force-window, mpv creates the DRM video output only when the first file loads, and drops it again after playlist-clear/stop. Until the daemon pushes something, the projector shows the fbcon text console (kernel and systemd boot lines, or the installer's setup screen). The gap after a normal reboot is several seconds: daemon.py lines 423-426 show the boot screen only when there is no cached manifest, otherwise the first run_cycle does a network sync (up to the 15 s manifest timeout when the network is slow) before _reconcile_mpv pushes the cached playlist. While the daemon is crash-looping or on a config error (35 s cycles) the console stays up, and the kernel's 10 min consoleblank then blanks HDMI. Cosmetic, but it is raw Linux text on a wall for a non-technical audience.


**How to reproduce.** Code trace (no hardware): player/deploy/projector-mpv.service:16 ExecStart=/usr/bin/mpv --input-ipc-server=/tmp/projector-mpv.sock --idle=yes; mpv.conf:13 idle=yes with no force-window; grep -rn force-window player tools -> nothing. daemon.main(): load_local_manifest -> state.last_manifest set -> daemon.py:423 logs only (boot screen only in the elif at :425-426) -> run_cycle :429 -> sync_once :311 (network GET first; on_progress only from sync.py:172/208/233, none on the first cycle since last_full_verify defaults to now at daemon.py:117) -> _reconcile_mpv :352 -> _push_playlist :253 is the first loadfile. Until then mpv has no VO and the kernel console (boot log, getty masked by firstboot.py:256 / install-player.sh:409) is what the HDMI output shows. Config error path: config.py:74 sleeps 30 s + RestartSec=5 -> mpv never gets a loadfile.


**Fix.** Change player/deploy/projector-mpv.service line 16 to: ExecStart=/usr/bin/mpv --input-ipc-server=/tmp/projector-mpv.sock --idle=yes --force-window=yes. The service file is overwritten on every install/update (install-player.sh:376), so this reaches already-deployed Pis; mpv.conf is preserved across upgrades (install-player.sh:203-212) and a change there would only reach fresh flashes. Optionally also add force-window=yes after idle=yes in player/deploy/mpv.conf for anyone running mpv by hand, and add a one-line comment on the service explaining it keeps a black DRM window up while idle so the text console never shows. No existing test needs changing. Optional second step (separate change): in daemon.main() after building state, call _reconcile_mpv(cfg, mpv, state, screens) once before the while loop so cached content starts before the first network sync; test_daemon tests call run_cycle directly and are unaffected.


**Verify on hardware.**

```text
cat /proc/cmdline   # no quiet/splash/consoleblank=0 expected
cat /sys/module/kernel/parameters/consoleblank   # expect 600
sudo systemctl stop projector-player; sudo systemctl restart projector-mpv; sleep 3
echo '{"command":["get_property","vo-configured"]}' | socat - /tmp/projector-mpv.sock   # expect false: no VO while idle
sudo cat /sys/kernel/debug/dri/*/clients   # mpv absent or not master while idle
# look at the projector: kernel/systemd boot text should be visible
sudo systemctl start projector-player; journalctl -u projector-player -b -n 50   # time from "piplayer v... starting" to the first playlist push
# after adding --force-window=yes: repeat; vo-configured should be true immediately and the projector black while idle
```


### M14. Wyze bridge runs an unpinned :latest image and re-pulls it in ExecStartPre under the 90 s start timeout

- Severity: **Medium**. Area: systemd units and mpv.conf. Where: `player/deploy/projector-wyze-bridge.service:24`.
- Existing test covers it: **no**. No test checks pinning, the absence of a start-time pull, or a start timeout. player/tests/test_installer_pi.py:75 asserts the literal "docker pull mrlt8/wyze-bridge:latest" (it will need the pinned string after the fix); player/tests/test_camera_config.py:213-219 only checks --env-file, ConditionPathExists and the sudoers lines. Add to the deploy section of test_camera_config.py: parse the image from the unit's ExecStart (regex r"^\s*(mrlt8/wyze-bridge\S+)$" over the continuation lines), assert ":latest" not in it and "docker pull" not in unit, and assert f"docker pull {image}" is in install-player.sh so the two literals cannot drift. Note the installer harness (caps_and_wyze_blocks at test_installer_pi.py:47-53) extracts only the wyze block with no SRC_DIR, so a fix that makes the installer read the image out of the unit file at run time would break that harness; keep the installer's pull a literal.
- Also reported as: Wyze bridge pulls mrlt8/wyze-bridge:latest on every unit start with no version pin or memory limit; Unofficial wyze-bridge container is :latest, pulled at install, every nightly upgrade and every unit start, and it holds the Wyze account credentials.

**What is wrong.** The unit pulls mrlt8/wyze-bridge:latest in ExecStartPre on every start (line 24) and runs that tag (line 29); the installer pre-pulls the same tag (install-player.sh:350) and docs/camera.md:75 presents the restart-time pull as the bridge's update mechanism. (1) Supply chain: a new upstream :latest is fetched at an arbitrary reboot, so a breaking upstream release (renamed env vars, changed ports) silently kills the room camera and the console only sees camera_error text. (2) The unit has no TimeoutStartSec, so the pull runs under the 90 s default; the leading '-' ignores the exit status but not the timeout, so a pull of a new multi-hundred-MB image over a slow Pi 3 / Zero 2 W Wi-Fi link times out, the unit fails, Restart=always retries every 10 s and re-pulls until every layer is cached (a single layer that needs more than 90 s never completes). The daemon's `sudo systemctl restart` (30 s budget, camera_config.py:81-82) hitting the same pull surfaces one cycle of raw Python text as camera_error ("camera config: Command '['sudo', ...]' timed out after 30 seconds") and then self-heals because wyze.env is unchanged on the next cycle.


**How to reproduce.** Read player/deploy/projector-wyze-bridge.service:23-32 (pull and run both use :latest, no TimeoutStartSec, Restart=always/RestartSec=10). docs/camera.md:75 documents the restart-time pull as intended. install-player.sh:348-350 pre-pulls with a comment naming the daemon's 30 s budget. Daemon side: cd player && python -c that mocks camera_config.fetch, write_wyze_env=[True, False] and subprocess.run raising TimeoutExpired, then calls maybe_apply twice: cycle 1 returns False with camera.error = "camera config: Command '['sudo', '-n', '/bin/systemctl', 'restart', 'projector-wyze-bridge.service']' timed out after 30 seconds", cycle 2 returns True and stores the version.


**Fix.** Keep the finder's direction but simplify to a 4-line change guarded by one test: (1) projector-wyze-bridge.service: delete line 24 (the ExecStartPre pull) and change line 29 to the pinned tag, e.g. `mrlt8/wyze-bridge:2.10.3` (pick the current upstream release when applying and confirm it with `docker manifest inspect mrlt8/wyze-bridge:<tag>` that an arm64 layer exists; an @sha256 digest is stricter but harder to read). No TimeoutStartSec is needed once the pull is gone: for Type=simple, TimeoutStartSec only covers ExecStartPre, and `docker run` pulls a missing image itself without that clock. Keep line 23 (`docker rm -f`). (2) install-player.sh:350: `docker pull mrlt8/wyze-bridge:2.10.3 || true` and reword the comment at 348-349 (the pre-pull now exists so the pinned image is cached before the daemon's 30 s restart, and so `install-player.sh --upgrade` via update-player.sh is the only path that ever moves the bridge version). (3) docs/camera.md:75: replace "(`sudo systemctl restart projector-wyze-bridge.service` pulls the latest image)" with "(the bridge version is pinned in the player release; a player update is what moves it)". (4) Tests: change test_installer_pi.py:75 to the pinned string, and add the drift test described in test_note. An `Environment=WYZE_IMAGE=...` + `${WYZE_IMAGE}` in the unit is optional and only cosmetic since the installer must keep its own literal for the harness. Do NOT introduce a sed placeholder rendered at cp time (install-player.sh:380): the harness prelude in test_installer_pi.py does not define such a variable and `set -u` would fail the block.


**Verify on hardware.**

```text
systemctl show -p TimeoutStartUSec -p Restart -p RestartUSec projector-wyze-bridge.service
journalctl -u projector-wyze-bridge -b | grep -i 'start-pre operation timed out\|Pulling\|Downloaded newer\|Image is up to date\|Scheduled restart'
docker images mrlt8/wyze-bridge --digests
docker image inspect mrlt8/wyze-bridge:latest --format '{{.Size}}'
journalctl -u projector-player -n 200 | grep -i 'camera config\|bridge restart'
```


### M15. After a player fault the content is retried only every 10 polls (5 min), and nothing re-pushes on HDMI hot-plug

- Severity: **Medium**. Area: Player daemon. Where: `player/player/daemon.py:227`.
- Existing test covers it: **partial**. player/tests/test_daemon.py::test_player_fault_retries_content_slowly (line 509) asserts recovery within FAULT_RETRY_CYCLES cycles and exactly one retry push, i.e. it pins the slow cadence as intended; test_mpv_idle_for_four_cycles_shows_player_fault (line 447) asserts no re-push while the fault screen is up. Nothing covers a display appearing. Add test_hdmi_reconnect_repushes_at_once: monkeypatch daemon.DRM_SYSFS to tmp_path, write tmp_path/"card1-HDMI-A-1"/"status" = "disconnected", seed a.mp4, run 4 cycles with mpv.current = None to reach the fault screen, then write "connected" and assert one run_cycle gives names(mpv) == ["a.mp4"], screens.current is None, and exactly one extra loadfile; a second test asserts a status file that stays "disconnected" still only re-pushes at cycle 10 (existing behaviour). Existing tests need no change.
- Also reported as: Projector switched on later can stay black up to 5 minutes (fault retry every 10 cycles) and mpv DRM hot-plug is unverified.

**What is wrong.** When mpv's VO failed because no display was connected at boot, every entry fails, the fault screen goes up after 4 cycles, and _reconcile_mpv re-pushes only when idle_streak % FAULT_RETRY_CYCLES == 0, i.e. every 300 s at the default poll. A projector switched on in the morning while the Pi was already up therefore stays black for up to 5 minutes for no reason; a re-push is a handful of IPC commands, so the slow cadence buys nothing. If the display went away while mpv was already playing (projector powered off overnight with the Pi on), mpv keeps page-flipping so the daemon never sees a fault and never re-pushes; whether vc4 brings the output back without a fresh modeset is unverified, and the boot config does not force the connector (no video=HDMI-A-1:...D in cmdline.txt).


**How to reproduce.** Code trace: daemon.py:225-226 increment idle_cycles/idle_streak each idle cycle; line 227 re-pushes only at streak%10==0; line 230 shows the fault screen from streak 4; line 235 (`elif idle_cycles >= 2`) is shadowed once streak>=4; line 240 is the only reset of idle_streak and line 243 resets only idle_cycles after a push, so after a failed push the streak keeps counting 11, 12, ... and the next retry is at 20. Harness: cd "<checkout>/player" && python -m pytest -q tests/test_daemon.py -k player_fault_retries_content_slowly (passes; the loop runs 6 extra cycles after the 4 fault cycles before content returns, streak 10). Default poll_interval_seconds=30 at player/player/config.py:100, so 10 cycles = 300 s.


**Fix.**

Keep the 10-cycle fallback and add a connector watch (sysfs is world-readable, no root, no DRM master needed; vc4 HDMI has HPD so the cached status updates on hot-plug). In player/player/daemon.py:

```python
DRM_SYSFS = Path("/sys/class/drm")   # overridable in tests

def _hdmi_connected() -> bool | None:
    """True/False from the HDMI connector status files, None when there are none (not a Pi, tests)."""
    files = list(DRM_SYSFS.glob("card*-HDMI-A-*/status"))
    if not files:
        return None
    try:
        return any(f.read_text().strip() == "connected" for f in files)
    except OSError:
        return None
```
Add `hdmi_connected: bool | None = None` to PlayerState. In _reconcile_mpv replace lines 227-229 with:
```python
            connected = _hdmi_connected()
            just_plugged = connected and state.hdmi_connected is False
            state.hdmi_connected = connected
            if screens is not None and (just_plugged or state.idle_streak % FAULT_RETRY_CYCLES == 0):
                log.warning("retrying the playlist after the player fault (%s idle cycles%s)",
                            state.idle_streak, ", display connected" if just_plugged else "")
                need_push = True
```
and in the `else:` at line 238-240 also record `state.hdmi_connected = _hdmi_connected()` so the disconnected->connected edge is seen from a healthy state too. Do NOT set FAULT_RETRY_CYCLES = 1 or re-push every cycle (see reason). For the unplug-during-playback case, only if hardware_verify step 2 shows the picture does not return: on the same edge when not on the fault screen, restart mpv (systemctl restart projector-mpv via the existing restart-mpv path) so the VO re-probes the connector; and, in the flasher/firstboot area, append `video=HDMI-A-1:D` (force-connected, mode from EDID, no fixed 1920x1080 which a WXGA projector may not take) to cmdline.txt in patch_cmdline.


**Verify on hardware.** 1) Boot the Pi with the projector off, wait 2 min, power the projector on, time until content appears (expect up to 5 min). journalctl -u projector-mpv -n 100 | grep -i 'video output\|connector\|drm'; journalctl -u projector-player -n 200 | grep -i 'retrying the playlist\|player fault\|idle with'. 2) With content playing: cat /sys/class/drm/card*-HDMI-A-1/status; unplug or power off the projector for 5 min; cat the status again (expect 'disconnected'); power on; cat again and watch whether the picture returns within 30 s. If it stays black while journalctl -u projector-player shows player_status playing (no 'retrying' lines), the unplug-while-playing case is confirmed and a connector-status watch is needed. 3) On the same box: systemctl restart projector-mpv with the projector on should bring the picture back immediately, confirming a fresh modeset is all that is required.


### M16. The 24 h full re-hash runs at daemon-start time plus 24 h, competes with playback for SD bandwidth and blocks commands/screenshots for its duration

- Severity: **Medium**. Area: Player daemon. Where: `player/player/daemon.py:305`.
- Existing test covers it: **partial**. player/tests/test_daemon.py::test_periodic_full_verify_every_24h (line 265) covers only the 24 h cadence; nothing tests when in the day it fires or that playback/commands are unaffected. With the proposed gate that test must monkeypatch daemon.updater.in_window to return True (the fake mpv is playing after the first run_cycle, so the idle branch is False), or stop the fake mpv first. Add test_periodic_full_verify_waits_for_idle_or_window: seed, run_cycle, set last_full_verify 24 h back, monkeypatch in_window False with mpv playing -> spy sees verify_all False; monkeypatch in_window True -> True; state.force_verify=True with in_window False -> True (force bypasses the gate). Add test_hash_file_yields_between_chunks in test_sync.py: monkeypatch sync.time.sleep with a counter, hash a 3 MB file, assert sleep called 3 times.

**What is wrong.** last_full_verify is initialised to the daemon's start time (daemon.py:117) and re-anchored to "now" after each run (daemon.py:324), so the full re-hash lands at the wall-clock time of the last daemon (re)start every day; the nightly auto-update does not restart the daemon when already current (update-player.sh:146-150), so a power-cut reboot at 09:00 pins it to 09:00 daily, during show hours. With verify_all, _ensure_item bypasses the media index and _hash_file reads every file in 1 MB chunks without pausing (sync.py:40-47, 231-234). run_cycle is single-threaded, so commands, screenshots and status reporting wait for the hash (daemon.py:309, 371-380); the last-seen update is sent before the hash (sync.py:399), so a verify longer than 180 s shows the device as "offline" in the console (cloud/src/pages/devices.js:27,71). force-sync from the console triggers the same work immediately (daemon.py:440). Whether mpv visibly stutters from the SD contention on a Pi 3 / Zero 2 W needs hardware to confirm.


**How to reproduce.** Code trace: daemon.py:117 (anchor), 305-307 (24 h check), 324 (re-anchor), 309 vs 371-380 (commands/screenshots after sync_once), 428-440 (single loop, force-sync sets force_verify); sync.py:231-234 (index bypass), 40-47 (unthrottled hash), 399 (status sent before hash); update-player.sh:146-150 (no restart when current); cloud/src/pages/devices.js:27,71 (offline after 180 s). Harness: cd player && python -m pytest -q tests/test_daemon.py -k "full_verify" shows only cadence is tested.


**Fix.**

```text
Minimal version, reuse updater.in_window and the mpv idle state already queried in run_cycle. daemon.py lines 305-307 become:

    verify_due = time.monotonic() - state.last_full_verify >= FULL_VERIFY_INTERVAL_SECONDS
    if verify_due and not state.force_verify:
        # daily re-hash only when nothing is playing or in the owner's update window (default 03:00-05:00)
        playing = bool(mpv.get_property("playlist-count")) and not mpv.get_property("idle-active")
        window = str(((state.last_manifest or {}).get("update") or {}).get("window") or "03:00-05:00")
        verify_due = (not playing) or updater.in_window(window, datetime.now())
    verify_all = state.force_verify or verify_due

sync.py _hash_file (line 43-46): add `time.sleep(0.02)` after `h.update(chunk)` (import time), which caps hashing at roughly 50 MB/s so mpv keeps SD bandwidth; force-sync keeps its immediate behaviour (owner asked for it). Drop the marker-file persistence: with the window gate a restart only delays one verify by at most a day, which is acceptable. Tests: update test_periodic_full_verify_every_24h to monkeypatch daemon.updater.in_window to lambda *a: True (or it will see [False, False, False] because the fake mpv is playing); add the two tests named in test_note.
```


**Verify on hardware.** On a Pi 3 or Zero 2 W with a 2 GB+ playlist playing: 1) note the daemon start time: systemctl show projector-player -p ActiveEnterTimestamp; 2) send force-sync from the console and time it: journalctl -u projector-player -f | grep -E "verifying integrity|sync complete" (difference is the stall); 3) during the hash send a screenshot or restart-mpv command from the console and confirm it is not answered until "sync complete"; 4) if the stall exceeds 180 s, check the console devices page shows the device offline during it; 5) watch the projector for stutter and run: journalctl -u projector-mpv --since "-5min" | grep -iE "drop|cache|underrun"; iostat -x 5 /dev/mmcblk0 during the hash to see mpv read await.


### M17. No free-space check before a download: an oversized item fills the card, is retried every poll, and the STORAGE FULL screen itself cannot be written

- Severity: **Medium**. Area: Player daemon. Where: `player/player/sync.py:199`.
- Existing test covers it: **partial**. player/tests/test_daemon.py:413 test_enospc_during_download_shows_storage_full and player/tests/test_sync.py:395 test_enospc_is_reported_as_no_space_left cover the ENOSPC-to-STORAGE-FULL phrase path when the PNG can still be written. Nothing covers: (a) an oversized item being refused by size before any write, (b) render_png raising ENOSPC (screens.current stays None, no loadfile), (c) ENOSPC from save_media_index escaping sync_once and producing the "other" screen. Tests to add: test_sync.py: monkeypatch shutil.disk_usage to return free=100 MiB, item size_bytes=1 GiB, assert sync_once returns err containing the filename and "SD card" and that no .part exists; test_daemon.py: monkeypatch screens_mod.render_png to raise OSError(ENOSPC) alongside the existing full() download stub and assert the screen still lands (self.dir under a tmp RUNTIME_DIRECTORY) or, at minimum, that state.storage_full is True and the sync_error names the item.

**What is wrong.** _download_item opens the .part (sync.py:199) and streams until out.write (sync.py:205) raises ENOSPC; the .part is kept for resume but nothing compares size_bytes minus the .part size against shutil.disk_usage(cfg.media_dir).free (no free-space check exists anywhere under player/). An item larger than the free space fills the data partition to the projector user's limit, every later item fails with the same reason, and every poll re-issues a Range request (sync.py:164-167) that writes another chunk and fails again. The console only sees "N of M items missing: no space left on device" (sync.py:367-370), which does not name the item that does not fit. When nothing else is on disk (new playlist, or the oversized item is first), the daemon tries the STORAGE FULL screen (daemon.py:152-153) but render_png (screens.py:393) writes the PNG to the same partition, raises ENOSPC, status.show swallows it (status.py:38-42) and mpv is left black or on the previous screen. When other items are already present they keep playing and only the console text is affected. The daemon runs as User=projector (deploy/projector-player.service:8), so the ext4 root reserve keeps the OS alive and the owner can recover by removing the item from the playlist (the orphaned .part is pruned at sync.py:273-276), but nothing tells them which item to remove.


**How to reproduce.** cd "<checkout>/player" && python -m pytest -q -p no:cacheprovider --rootdir=. --noconftest docs/audits/2026-09-20-pi-os/repro/test_storage_full_repro.py (3 passed): test_storage_full_screen_needs_disk (both _download_item and render_png raise ENOSPC: storage_full True, screens.current None, no loadfile), test_no_free_space_check_anywhere (no disk_usage/statvfs in sync.py), test_part_kept_after_enospc (.part survives, second call sends Range: bytes=4-). Code trace: sync.py:163-209 (no size check), :436-439 (per-item failure kept, retried every poll), :367-370 (error text without item name), daemon.py:152-153 and :245-250, status.py:36-42, screens.py:389-395.


**Fix.**

```text
In _download_item, immediately after the completed-.part shortcut (after line 161) and before the `for attempt in (1, 2):` loop at line 163, add a free-space gate at the single choke point every caller routes through:

    size = item.get("size_bytes") or 0
    if size:
        have = part.stat().st_size if part.is_file() else 0
        free = shutil.disk_usage(cfg.media_dir).free
        if size - have > free - FREE_SPACE_MARGIN:   # FREE_SPACE_MARGIN = 200 * 1024 * 1024 at module top
            raise SyncError(f"{filename} needs {_mb(size - have)} but the SD card has {_mb(free)} free")

(add `import shutil`; reuse or lift screens._mb into sync.py as a 1-line helper: f"{n / 1024 / 1024:.0f} MB"). Because SyncError is caught per item at sync.py:436-439, the item is named in sync_error via _describe_error/_format_sync_error, smaller items still download, and no .part is created so nothing is retried every poll. Keep the existing ENOSPC path as the fallback for the case where the console omits size_bytes.

Second, make the status screen independent of the data partition: in deploy/projector-player.service add `RuntimeDirectory=projector-player`, and in status.py:22 use `self.dir = Path(os.environ["RUNTIME_DIRECTORY"]) if os.environ.get("RUNTIME_DIRECTORY") else cfg.manifest_path.parent / "screens"`. /run is tmpfs, systemd creates it 0755 owned by the projector user (readable by the mpv unit, which runs as the same user), and tests keep using the tmp_path dir since the variable is unset under pytest.

Optional hardening for the amplifier: wrap save_media_index/_save_local_manifest at sync.py:432-435 in try/except OSError that appends ("media index", _describe_error(e)) to failures so a full card still yields the STORAGE FULL phrase instead of escaping to the generic handler. No existing test changes are required for any of the three edits.
```


**Verify on hardware.** df -h /var/lib/projector-player; ls -la /var/lib/projector-player/media/*.part; sudo -u projector touch /var/lib/projector-player/screens/probe && echo "projector can still write" ; sudo journalctl -u projector-player -n 300 | grep -i 'space\|could not render\|resuming'


### M18. Media files and JSON caches are renamed into place without fsync: after a power cut a zero-filled file can be trusted as verified for 24 h

- Severity: **Medium**. Area: Player daemon. Where: `player/player/sync.py:215`.
- Existing test covers it: **no**. No test in player/tests/test_sync.py touches durability; test_verified_download_is_recorded_in_media_index (237) and test_touched_file_is_rehashed_and_corruption_repaired (265) only cover the index round trip and mtime-based re-hash, never fsync-before-rename. Nothing monkeypatches Path.replace or the file object, and all tests use real tmp files, so adding os.fsync breaks nothing: I applied the fix in a scratch copy and the full suite passed (278 = 277 + 1 new). The test that covers it (verified passing with the fix, fails without it): monkeypatch os.fsync and Path.replace to append to an events list, set cms.files["a.mp4"] = b"A"*10, cms.set_playlist(["a.mp4"]), sync_once(cfg), then assert events.index("fsync") < events.index(("replace", "a.mp4.part")) and events.count("fsync") >= 2 (media file plus media_index.json).

**What is wrong.** _download_item writes the .part (sync.py:199-209) and renames it into place (215) without flush/fsync; _ensure_item then records size+mtime from an in-memory stat (242) and _matches_index (103-111) accepts any file with matching size+mtime without hashing. On ext4 with delayed allocation the common power-cut outcome is a file truncated to the flushed prefix, which the size check catches. But with dioread_nolock (ext4 default on the Trixie 6.12 kernel) writeback allocates unwritten extents and journals the on-disk size at IO submit, while the unwritten-to-written conversion is committed later; a power cut after the first commit and before the second leaves the file at full size and mtime with a zero-reading tail. media_index.json is renamed over an existing file, so auto_da_alloc flushes it and it records that exact size and mtime. daemon.py:117 sets last_full_verify to process start, so the reboot does not verify: the corrupt file plays (mpv fails to open a non-faststart MP4, or freezes at the zero tail) until 24 h after boot or a console force-sync. Window is a few seconds after 'downloaded' is logged while the SD is still draining the write queue; typically only the last file(s) of a sync are affected, not the whole playlist. JSON caches have the same gap but self-heal: an empty or garbled media_index.json or manifest.json is treated as absent and everything is re-hashed.


**How to reproduce.** Code trace: sync.py 199-209 (write, no fsync), 215 (rename), 242 (index entry from in-memory stat), 231/111 (size+mtime short-circuit); daemon.py 117 (last_full_verify = boot time), 305-306 (verify only after 24 h). Kernel side: fs/ext4/inode.c ext4_da_do_write_end (i_disksize not advanced for delayed buffers), mpage_map_and_submit_extent ('Update on-disk size after IO is submitted'), mpage_map_one_extent + ext4_end_io_end (unwritten extents converted in a later handle when dioread_nolock). Not reproducible without an ext4 device; the ordinary truncation case is caught by the size check, only the unwritten-tail case slips through.


**Fix.** Add `import os` to sync.py. In _download_item, inside the `with part.open(mode) as out:` block after the chunk loop (line 208): `out.flush(); os.fsync(out.fileno())`. In _write_json_atomic replace lines 63-64 with: `with tmp.open("w") as f: f.write(json.dumps(data, indent=2)); f.flush(); os.fsync(f.fileno())` then `tmp.replace(path)`. Drop the directory-fsync suggestion: a lost rename after a crash just leaves the fully written .part, which the existing complete-.part branch (sync.py:153-161) hashes and renames on the next sync, and os.fsync on a directory fd fails on Windows where the test suite runs. Optional: in that same branch (156-159) fsync the .part before replace, since its bytes came from a process that died; open it "rb", os.fsync(f.fileno()) works on Linux.


**Verify on hardware.** On the Pi: `grep -E 'delalloc|dioread_nolock|auto_da_alloc' /proc/fs/ext4/mmcblk0p2/options` (confirm defaults; use the root device from `findmnt -no SOURCE /`). Assign a new 200 MB video from the console, run `journalctl -u projector-player -f` and pull the plug 1-3 s after the line 'downloaded <file>' appears (repeat 5-10 times; the window is narrow). After each boot: `F=/var/lib/projector-player/media/<file>; stat -c '%s %Y' "$F"; python3 -c "import json;print(json.load(open('/var/lib/projector-player/media_index.json')).get('<file>'))"; filefrag -v "$F" | grep -c unwritten; sha256sum "$F"` and compare the sha256 with the manifest item. A hit is: stat size == index size, mtime matches, filefrag shows unwritten extents (or `cmp` shows a zero tail), sha256 differs, and `journalctl -u projector-player -b | grep -E 'verifying|hash mismatch|redownloading'` shows no re-hash of that file on the post-crash sync. A truncated size with the file re-downloaded on the next sync is the benign case.


### M19. Per-item download failures reach the console and the projector as Python exception class names

- Severity: **Medium**. Area: Player daemon. Where: `player/player/sync.py:359`.
- Existing test covers it: **partial**. player/tests/test_sync.py:167 (test_dropped_download_leaves_part_and_resumes_with_range) exercises exactly this path but asserts the current 'ConnectionError' wording, so it must be updated to 'download failed: big.mp4: lost the connection to the console'. test_daemon.py:220/222/442 and test_sync.py:74/103/116 only pin the HTTP 404 wording and are unaffected. Add a direct unit test of _describe_error covering ReadTimeout, ConnectTimeout, SSLError, ConnectionError and ChunkedEncodingError, asserting each result contains no exception class name (the scratch test at docs/audits/2026-09-20-pi-os/repro/test_verify_describe.py is a ready template).
- Also reported as: Console "Sync problem" and screen show exception class names, hashes and HTTP codes for failed downloads.

**What is wrong.** _describe_error returns type(e).__name__ for any RequestException that is not an HTTPError, so the console dashboard shows 'Sync problem: download failed: 1a2b3c4d5e6f7890_holiday.mp4: ReadTimeout' (or ConnectTimeout, SSLError, ChunkedEncodingError, ConnectionError) and the ERROR screen shows the same line when nothing plays. For the non-technical owner none of these words say what to do, and the SSLError case hides the clock problem described elsewhere.


**How to reproduce.** Scratch test docs/audits/2026-09-20-pi-os/repro/test_timeout_reason_text.py: cd "<checkout>/player" && python -m pytest -q -p no:cacheprovider <that file> -> 4 passed, proving sync_once returns 'download failed: a.mp4: ReadTimeout' / ConnectTimeout / SSLError / ChunkedEncodingError and the next sync_once(cfg, sync_error=err) sends it as the sync_error query param. Code trace: sync.py:359-360 -> 436-439 -> 371-373 -> daemon.py:309-310 -> sync.py:127 -> cloud/src/api.js:55 -> dashboard.js:30 / devices.js:375; projector via daemon.py:173 -> screens.py:196. Note the class name only appears when exactly one item fails; with two or more failures sync.py:375 lists filenames only.


**Fix.**

```text
Replace lines 359-360 of _describe_error with, in this order (SSLError and ConnectTimeout are ConnectionError subclasses; ConnectTimeout is also a Timeout):
    if isinstance(e, requests.exceptions.SSLError):
        return "secure connection failed (check the Pi's clock)"
    if isinstance(e, requests.ConnectionError):   # also ConnectTimeout, mid-transfer drops
        return "lost the connection to the console"
    if isinstance(e, requests.Timeout):           # ReadTimeout
        return "the download stalled"
    if isinstance(e, requests.RequestException):  # ChunkedEncodingError etc.
        return "the download was cut off"
Keep the class name for diagnostics in the log line at sync.py:438: log.warning("item %s unavailable: %s (%s, will retry next poll)", filename, reason, type(e).__name__). Update the assertion at player/tests/test_sync.py:167 to 'download failed: big.mp4: lost the connection to the console'. No other existing test changes.
```


**Verify on hardware.** On a Pi with the console reachable, unplug Ethernet/Wi-Fi mid-download of a large item, then: journalctl -u projector-player -n 50 | grep unavailable  (currently prints 'ConnectionError'/'ReadTimeout'); the console device card shows the same word after 'Sync problem:'. For the clock case: sudo date -s '2020-01-01' then restart projector-player and watch for 'SSLError' in the same log and on the ERROR screen.


### M20. Stale media is pruned only after every new download has already failed for lack of space

- Severity: **Medium**. Area: Player daemon. Where: `player/player/sync.py:441`.
- Existing test covers it: **no**. tests/test_sync.py::test_prune_removes_stale_media_parts_and_temp_files and ::test_enospc_is_reported_as_no_space_left, and tests/test_daemon.py::test_enospc_during_download_shows_storage_full, check what is pruned and how ENOSPC is worded, never the ordering. Add to player/tests/test_sync.py: test_prune_runs_before_downloads: seed media_dir/old.mp4, set_playlist(["new.mp4"]), monkeypatch sync._download_item to append "download <name>" and raise OSError(errno.ENOSPC, ...), wrap sync._prune_stale to append "prune"; assert order == ["prune", "download new.mp4"] and old.mp4 is gone. Verified it passes with the fix and fails without it. No existing test needs changing.

**What is wrong.** sync_once downloads every new item first (425-439) and only then calls _prune_stale for the files the old playlist no longer needs. On a card that is nearly full a playlist swap therefore fails every download with ENOSPC on the first poll, reports 'N of M items missing: no space left on device' to the console, shows the STORAGE FULL screen (nothing on disk to play), and only succeeds on the second poll after the prune freed the space. The owner sees a false storage-full warning and 30 s of the wrong screen every time they swap a large playlist.


**How to reproduce.** Scratch test test_prune_runs_after_downloads: with old.mp4 seeded and new.mp4 assigned, patched _download_item raising ENOSPC records the order ['download new.mp4', 'prune'] and err contains 'no space left'.


**Fix.** In sync_once (player/player/sync.py), replace lines 422-429 and 441 with: before the loop, `wanted = {item.get("filename") or "?" for item in items}` then `_prune_stale(cfg, keep_filenames=wanted, index=index)`; remove `wanted.add(filename)` from the loop and delete the post-loop `_prune_stale(...)` call at line 441. Keep `index_before` capture at line 418 and the post-loop `save_media_index` as is. Verified on a scratch copy: 278 tests pass.


**Verify on hardware.** On a Pi whose card has less free space than the new playlist but more than enough once the old playlist is removed: assign the new playlist in the console, then run `journalctl -u projector-player -n 200 --no-pager` and expect, in order, "item <name> unavailable: no space left on device (will retry next poll)" for each item, then "removing stale media: <old file>", then the STORAGE FULL screen for one poll interval, then "downloaded <name>" on the following poll. `df -h /` before and after confirms the space was available all along.


### M21. apt-get upgrade never installs the new versioned kernel/firmware packages on Pi OS, and /var/run/reboot-required is not created on Debian, so update-os reports 'N not upgraded' forever and reboot_required is dead

- Severity: **Medium**. Area: Updates and commands. Where: `player/deploy/update-os.sh:65`.
- Existing test covers it: **partial**. player/tests/test_updater.py::test_update_os_script_in_a_fake_root (line 338) runs the script against apt-get/systemctl/python3 shims; it pins the raw apt summary as the message (line 343: "3 upgraded, 0 newly installed, 0 to remove and 0 not upgraded."), pins "; reboot required" as a suffix (line 348) and creates var/run/reboot-required by hand (line 345), so it asserts the current behaviour and will need its three message assertions changed by the fix. It does not exercise kept-back packages, --with-new-pkgs, or how reboot-required comes to exist; test_updater.py:99 test_update_os_status_asks_for_a_reboot only feeds a hand-written status file to updater.pending_status. Test to add (same fake_pi harness): a `dpkg-query` shim that prints the contents of a state file, an apt-get shim that on `upgrade` asserts `--with-new-pkgs` is in "$*" and, when FAKE_KERNEL_BUMP=1, rewrites that state file with a newer linux-image line; assert reboot_required is True and the message is the plain-English "restart" text, and that without the bump (and no reboot-required file) reboot_required is False and the message is the "no restart needed" text. Also assert the shim log contains "apt-get -y --with-new-pkgs".

**What is wrong.** Raspberry Pi OS Bookworm/Trixie ships the kernel as a metapackage (linux-image-rpi-v8 / -rpi-2712 / -rpi-v6 ...) depending on a versioned linux-image-6.x.y+rpt-... package; every kernel bump requires installing a new package. 'apt-get upgrade' (line 65) never installs packages that are not already installed, so the metapackage is 'kept back' on every run and the kernel, raspi-firmware and related packages never move (Raspberry Pi's own docs prescribe full-upgrade for this reason). The summary line the owner sees in the console is apt's own text, e.g. '3 upgraded, 0 newly installed, 0 to remove and 7 not upgraded.', with no hint that the important updates are the ones not applied. Separately, the reboot decision (line 70) keys off /var/run/reboot-required, which is written by Ubuntu's update-notifier-common; plain Debian and Raspberry Pi OS do not ship anything that creates it, so even once the kernel is upgraded the Pi would keep running the old kernel until an unrelated reboot, while the docs promise a reboot after kernel updates.


**How to reproduce.** Code trace player/deploy/update-os.sh:64-73 plus the package index: `curl -sSL http://archive.raspberrypi.com/debian/dists/trixie/main/binary-arm64/Packages.gz | gunzip -c | awk 'BEGIN{RS="";FS="\n"} /^Package: linux-image-rpi-(v8|2712)\n/' | grep -E '^(Package|Version|Depends):'` shows Depends: linux-base-rpi-v8 (= 1:6.18.50-1+rpt1), linux-image-6.18.50+rpt-rpi-v8 (= 1:6.18.50-1+rpt1), a versioned name that changes on every kernel release, which apt-get upgrade without --with-new-pkgs refuses to install. `curl -s https://packages.debian.org/trixie/update-notifier-common` returns 'No such package', and RPi-Distro/pi-gen stage0-2 package lists contain neither needrestart nor update-notifier, so /var/run/reboot-required is never written on the supported images. The harness test_update_os_script_in_a_fake_root (player/tests/test_updater.py:338) fakes apt-get and creates reboot-required by hand, so it passes regardless.


**Fix.**

```text
In player/deploy/update-os.sh replace lines 64-74 with:

    apt-get update
    local before after
```
