```text
    before="$(installed_kernel_bits)"
    apt-get -y --with-new-pkgs -o Dpkg::Options::=--force-confold -o DPkg::Lock::Timeout=300 upgrade
    apt-get -y autoremove
    trap - ERR
    after="$(installed_kernel_bits)"
    local summary upgraded held msg reboot=false
    summary="$(grep -E '^[0-9]+ upgraded, ' "${LOG_FILE}" | tail -n 1 || true)"
    upgraded="${summary%% upgraded*}"; upgraded="${upgraded:-0}"
    held="${summary##* and }"; held="${held%% not upgraded*}"; held="${held:-0}"
    if [[ "${upgraded}" == 0 ]]; then msg="No system updates were needed"
    else msg="Installed ${upgraded} system update(s)"; fi
    [[ "${held}" != 0 ]] && msg="${msg}; ${held} could not be installed automatically"
    if [[ "${before}" != "${after}" || -f "${ROOT}/var/run/reboot-required" ]]; then
        reboot=true; msg="${msg}; the projector will restart shortly to finish"
    fi
    write_status true "${msg}" "${reboot}"

and add above main():

    # Installed kernel/firmware/libc versions; a change after upgrade means a reboot is needed
    # (Pi OS ships no update-notifier, so /var/run/reboot-required is never written for us).
    installed_kernel_bits() {
        command -v dpkg-query >/dev/null 2>&1 || return 0
        dpkg-query -W -f '${Package} ${Version}\n' 2>/dev/null \
            | grep -E '^(linux-image-|raspi-firmware |rpi-eeprom |libc6 )' || true
    }

Why not the finder's exact dpkg-query: naming rpi-eeprom / linux-image-* as arguments makes dpkg-query exit 1 when a name matches nothing (rpi-eeprom is absent on Pi 3 / Zero / armhf), which under set -e plus the ERR trap turns every run on those boards into "apt failed at line N". Listing everything and filtering never fails. --with-new-pkgs rather than full-upgrade keeps "never removes packages" on a kiosk; if apt still reports N not upgraded after this (a transition that needs removals), the owner sees "could not be installed automatically" instead of a success. Update the header comment (lines 7-8), docs/automation.md:283-284 (quoted command) and the three message assertions in test_update_os_script_in_a_fake_root; the FAKE_APT_RC=100 branch keeps working unchanged.
```


**Verify on hardware.** On a Pi that has not been updated for a few weeks: sudo apt-get update; sudo apt-get -s upgrade | grep -B1 -A3 'kept back'  (expect linux-image-rpi-v8 / linux-image-rpi-2712 listed and 'N not upgraded' > 0); apt-cache policy linux-image-rpi-v8 linux-image-rpi-2712  (Installed < Candidate); dpkg -l 'update-notifier*' needrestart  (expect none installed); ls -l /usr/share/update-notifier/notify-reboot-required  (expect No such file); grep -rl reboot-required /etc/kernel/postinst.d /var/lib/dpkg/info/*.postinst  (expect nothing); then sudo /opt/piplayer/player/deploy/update-os.sh, wait, cat /var/lib/projector-player/update-status.json  (expect reboot_required false and 'N not upgraded' in message) and ls /var/run/reboot-required  (expect absent); uname -r vs dpkg -l 'linux-image-6*' | grep ^ii  (running kernel unchanged).


### M22. After a post-check rollback the nightly auto-update reinstalls the same broken commit every night (install, crash loop, roll back, repeat)

- Severity: **Medium**. Area: Updates and commands. Where: `player/deploy/update-player.sh:145`.
- Existing test covers it: **no**. No test asserts a rolled-back sha is not retried. test_nightly_update_runs_once_per_window and test_auto_update_due_rules (player/tests/test_updater.py:131-157) cover only the 20 h gap. Worse, test_update_player_script_end_to_end_in_a_fake_root (test_updater.py:318-325, the "a sha instead of a branch, with --then-os" step) deliberately reinstalls the just-rolled-back sha2 and asserts success, so it enshrines the current behaviour; after the fix that step must use a fresh commit (git checkout main; sha4 = commit(repo, "0.2.2"); run(sha4, "--then-os")) and a new step should be added right after the rollback: run("update-player.sh", "main") -> status ok False, message startswith "not installing", RELEASE still sha1, prev absent, no new systemctl restart lines in shim_log; then a fresh commit on main installs and clears the block (update-blocked file gone). A daemon-side unit test is not needed since the block lives in the script.

**What is wrong.** The nightly run is gated only by the 20 h gap since the status file's `started` (updater.py:175), and update-player.sh's only "nothing to do" check is sha == RELEASE (update-player.sh:145). postcheck() rolls RELEASE back to the previous sha (lines 98-101) but records nowhere that the new sha failed, so the next nightly run (and any manual "Update all players" click) reinstalls the same sha: move good code to .prev, full install as root, restart into a crash loop, wait 2 min, roll back, report another failure. Repeats every night until a new commit is pushed or player_release is pinned.


**How to reproduce.** cd <checkout>/player && python -m pytest -q -s docs/audits/2026-09-20-pi-os/repro/retry/test_retry.py -p no:cacheprovider  (uses test_updater.fake_pi: update-player.sh main with one commit ahead, --postcheck FAKE_ACTIVE=failed FAKE_RESTARTS=5, assert RELEASE back at sha1 and ok=false, assert updater.auto_update_due(nightly, started+24h at 03:30, started) is True, run update-player.sh main again and assert message == "updated sha1 -> sha2" and RELEASE == sha2; passes, i.e. the retry happens).


**Fix.** Keep the fix entirely in update-player.sh (covers nightly runs and manual console clicks; the daemon cannot know the sha before the clone). Four lines: (1) next to STATUS_FILE at line 31 add BLOCK_FILE="${DATA_DIR}/update-blocked" (sha of the last update that was rolled back). (2) In postcheck() right after line 98 (REF="$(cat RELEASE)" of the broken install) add: echo "${REF}" > "${BLOCK_FILE}". (3) In update() after the "already at" block (after line 149) add: if [[ -f "${BLOCK_FILE}" && "${sha}" == "$(cat "${BLOCK_FILE}")" ]]; then write_status false "not installing ${sha:0:7} again: the last update to this version failed and was undone. Publish a new version and it will be tried."; rm -rf "${SRC_DIR}"; return 0; fi. (4) After the successful write_status at line 161 add: rm -f "${BLOCK_FILE}". Returning 0 mirrors the "already at" path so --then-os still runs the OS update; use fail instead if a blocked player update should also skip the OS step. The finder's alternative (skip in maybe_auto_update when read_status() has ok=false) is not needed and would also suppress legitimate retries after a transient install failure (network, apt) that never reached postcheck; do not add it. The nightly run still does a cheap clone each night but never installs; a new commit on the ref installs normally and clears the block. Note the tarball fallback (line 140, sha="${REF}") stores a branch name, so the block only works with git present, which install-player.sh:180 guarantees on any installed Pi.


**Verify on hardware.** On a Pi with auto=nightly pointing at a branch with a deliberately crashing commit: sudo grep -c 'rolling back' /var/lib/projector-player/update.log on two consecutive mornings (expect the count to grow by one per night); cat /var/lib/projector-player/update-status.json each morning (expect ok=false, ref=<same sha>, a new started/finished each day); journalctl -u projector-player-update --since yesterday | grep 'installing' (expect the same sha installed again).


### M23. Updates run install-player.sh as root from whatever commit a public-repo ref points at, with no signature, hash or allow-list; nightly follows the moving 'main' branch by default

- Severity: **Medium**. Area: Updates and commands. Where: `player/deploy/update-player.sh:157`.
- Existing test covers it: **partial**. player/tests/test_updater.py:55 test_bad_ref_never_reaches_sudo covers argument injection only. test_update_player_script_end_to_end_in_a_fake_root (line 267) runs the script with "main" and demonstrates that whatever the branch head is gets installed; nothing asserts any integrity or ref-kind restriction. Tests the proposed fix touches: test_nightly_update_runs_once_per_window (line 143) asserts runs.args == [["sudo","-n",SCRIPT,"v6.0"]] and must gain "--auto"; add to the end-to-end test: fake_pi.run("update-player.sh","main","--auto") -> status ok false with the plain-English branch message and RELEASE unchanged, then git tag v0.2.2 in fake_pi.repo and fake_pi.run("update-player.sh","v0.2.2","--auto") -> ok true. The finder's sha-only variant would instead break test_update_commands_run_the_scripts_with_the_manifest_ref (line 42, expects "v6.0" and "main" to reach sudo) and the end-to-end test's "main" runs.
- Also reported as: Player updates are not signed or hash-checked; console-chosen ref runs as root on every Pi.

**What is wrong.** update() clones https://github.com/Mattkillsyou/piplayer.git at the console's player_release (updater.py:71 and :188 default to 'main', cloud/src/db.js:107 defaults the setting to 'main') and executes the checkout's install-player.sh as root (update-player.sh:157), which rewrites sudoers, systemd units and the venv. TLS to GitHub is the only integrity control: there is no tag/commit signature check, no pinned sha, and no allow-list of refs (update-player.sh:118 and updater.py:31 accept any branch/tag/sha; the tarball fallback at :138-140 does not even resolve a sha). Anyone who can push to the public repository (leaked token, compromised laptop, account takeover) gets root on every Pi on the next 'Update all players' click, and within one nightly window once the owner has switched auto_update from its default 'off' (db.js:108) to 'nightly'. A mistaken push to main lands on the fleet the same way with no staging step. The regex plus sudo env_reset stop argument and REPO_URL injection, so this is a trust-model gap rather than an injection bug.


**How to reproduce.** Code trace: cloud/src/db.js:107 (player_release defaults to 'main'), manifest.js:251 (sent as update.release); player/player/updater.py:31,57-62 (any ref matching GIT_REF reaches sudo), :71 and :188 (default 'main'); player/deploy/update-player.sh:118 (same regex), :128-134 (clone at ref, no verification), :138-140 (tarball path, sha is just the ref string), :157 (root runs install-player.sh from the checkout); install-player.sh:277-298 (that script rewrites sudoers). grep -rn 'verify-tag\|verify-commit' player/deploy player/player returns nothing. Harness: cd player && python -m pytest -q tests/test_updater.py -k bad_ref shows only the regex is tested.


**Fix.** The finder's fix is heavier than needed and partly ineffective: resolving refs server-side means a GitHub API call in the Worker, the Pi-side ^[0-9a-f]{40}$ gate breaks test_update_commands_run_the_scripts_with_the_manifest_ref and the end-to-end test, the tarball path (update-player.sh:138-140) cannot verify a sha anyway, and a sha does not stop an attacker who already controls the console or repo. It only stops "the branch moved", which is the realistic case, so do that on the Pi with no console dependency: (1) updater.py:188 call run_update_player(ref, auto=True) and have run_update_player append "--auto" (sudoers line "update-player.sh *" already allows it); (2) update-player.sh:39-43 accept --auto (AUTO=1) alongside --then-os; (3) after line 134, when AUTO=1 and REF is not ^[0-9a-f]{40}$ and `git -C "$SRC_DIR" describe --exact-match --tags HEAD` fails (tarball path: REF not ^v[0-9]), call fail "Nightly update skipped: '${REF}' is a branch, not a release tag. In Settings set Player release to a tag such as v6.0, or use Update player to install this branch now." (fail before PREV_MADE only writes status and removes SRC_DIR, so the running player is untouched and the console shows the message on next sync); (4) console: db.js:107 default player_release to the current tag instead of "main" and add one sentence to settings.js:269 saying nightly only installs tags. Signed tags via git verify-tag are not worth it here: the signing key would live on the same laptop that holds the push token.


**Verify on hardware.** On the Pi: cat /opt/piplayer/player/RELEASE (shows which commit is installed); sudo cat /etc/sudoers.d/projector-player (confirms update-player.sh * is NOPASSWD); grep -n 'verify-tag\|verify-commit' /opt/piplayer/player/deploy/update-player.sh (expect no output); tail -n 50 /var/lib/projector-player/update.log (shows 'checkout <sha>' then 'installing <sha>' with no verification step between).


### M24. Every update failure the owner sees in the console is developer text (bash line numbers, script names, log paths, git jargon)

- Severity: **Medium**. Area: Updates and commands. Where: `player/deploy/update-player.sh:182`.
- Existing test covers it: **partial**. Existing tests pin the technical wording rather than prevent it: player/tests/test_updater.py:276 (already at <sha>), :286 (updated <sha> -> <sha>), :307 (rolled back to <sha>: player failed with 5 restarts), :316 (install-player.sh --upgrade failed), :326 (log line, fine to keep), :333 (ref not found), :343/:348 (raw apt summary), :351 (apt failed at line), and :63 (rc=1 sudo: a password is required). All of those assertions must be updated to the new sentences. A test that would cover the requirement: in test_update_player_script_in_a_fake_root and test_update_os_script_in_a_fake_root, after every fake_pi.run(), assert not re.search(r"line \d+|/var/lib|\.sh\b|rc=\d|\b[0-9a-f]{40}\b", fake_pi.status()["message"]) and that the message is non-empty; plus a unit test for the new updater helper mapping 'Unit projector-player-update.service already exists' stderr to the owner sentence.
- Also reported as: Update status on the console: "git clone failed for main", "error at line 157 (see /var/lib/projector-player/update.log)", 40-char shas.

**What is wrong.** The message field of update-status.json goes verbatim to the Devices page. The texts written are 'error at line 123 (see /var/lib/projector-player/update.log)' (line 182 ERR trap), 'install-player.sh --upgrade failed (see ...)' (158), 'git clone failed for main' (131), 'ref not found: v7' (132), 'not a piplayer checkout: /opt/piplayer/src-...' (142), 'bad ref: ...' (119), 'rolled back to 1a2b3c...: player failed with 5 restarts after the update' (101), and in update-os.sh 'apt failed at line 64 (see ...)' (61) plus apt's raw '3 upgraded, 0 newly installed, 0 to remove and 7 not upgraded.' (69-73). The daemon adds 'update-player main failed: rc=1 Failed to start transient service unit: Unit projector-player-update.service already exists' when two updates overlap (updater.py 53). None of this tells the owner what to do; the brief requires plain English for anything reported to the console.


**How to reproduce.** Code trace: update-player.sh:182 (ERR trap text) and :101/:119/:131/:132/:142/:158 -> write_status :57-71 writes message verbatim -> player/player/updater.py:120-126 pending_status copies it into the update_status param -> player/player/daemon.py:285 -> cloud/src/api.js:132 last_update_message -> cloud/src/pages/devices.js:233-235 "Update failed <ref> · …: <message>". Overlap case: updater.py:53 -> commands.py:169 -> devices.js:205 "done · update-player main failed: rc=1 Failed to start transient service unit…". Harness: cd <checkout>/player && python -m pytest -q tests/test_updater.py -k "fake_root" (passes today, asserting the technical strings at lines 307, 316, 333, 344, 351).


**Fix.** Keep the finder's approach but tighten it: (1) In update-player.sh and update-os.sh change fail() to take two args, fail <owner sentence> [technical detail]: log "FAILED: ${1}${2:+ (${2})}" then write_status false "$1". The ERR traps become trap 'fail "The update stopped unexpectedly. The projector keeps its current software." "line ${LINENO}"' ERR (update-player.sh:182) and trap 'fail "The system update could not finish. The projector keeps working on its current software." "apt at line ${LINENO}"' ERR (update-os.sh:61); the log path is dropped from the message because update.log is the file the log line is already written to. (2) Map the explicit fails: :119 'The version chosen in Settings is not valid'; :131 'Could not download the update. Check the projector has internet and try again.' (git clone with no --branch only fails for network/auth); :132 'The version chosen in Settings (${REF}) does not exist'; :139 'Could not download version ${REF} (no internet, or that version does not exist)'; :142 'The download did not contain the projector software'; :158 'The update could not be installed. The previous version was put back.' (restore_prev already runs first); :101 'The update was installed but the player would not start, so version ${PREV_VERSION:0:7} was put back'. Successes: :146 'Already on the latest version (${sha:0:7})'; :161 'Updated from ${PREV_VERSION:0:7} to ${sha:0:7}'. update-os.sh:71/73: 'System packages updated (${summary:-done}). Restarting to finish.' / 'System packages updated (${summary:-done})' with summary reduced to its leading '<n> upgraded' via ${summary%%,*}. Do NOT promise 'will try again tonight': that is only true when the manifest update block has auto=nightly. (3) updater.py:_run (line 49-53): log the raw rc/stderr with log.warning and return an owner sentence: if 'already exists' in res.stderr -> f"{what}: another update is still running. Wait a few minutes and try again."; otherwise f"{what}: the player could not start the update (see the player log)". commands.py:169 checks 'failed' in result to decide whether to report, so either keep the word 'failed' in the daemon sentence ('...update failed to start...') or change that check to a sentinel; simplest is to keep 'failed' in the text. (4) Update the test_updater.py assertions listed in test_note and add the no-jargon regex check.


**Verify on hardware.** On a Pi, from the console queue an update to a non-existent version (Settings > release = no-such-ref, then Update player). Then: sudo cat /var/lib/projector-player/update-status.json ; sudo tail -n 40 /var/lib/projector-player/update.log ; journalctl -u projector-player -n 100 | grep -i "command id" ; and check the Devices page shows 'Update failed ... : ref not found: no-such-ref'. Queue two updates within a few seconds and confirm the second command's result reads 'update-player main failed: rc=1 Failed to start transient service unit: Unit projector-player-update.service already exists.'


### M25. Nightly auto-update follows main HEAD with no staged rollout; rollback only catches crash loops

- Severity: **Medium**. Area: Updates and commands. Where: `player/player/updater.py:188`.
- Existing test covers it: **partial**. player/tests/test_updater.py::test_update_player_script_end_to_end_in_a_fake_root covers postcheck ok vs crash-loop rollback of projector-player.service only (the systemctl shim at line 207 answers the same for every unit). test_nightly_update_runs_once_per_window and test_update_commands_run_the_scripts_with_the_manifest_ref cover the manifest ref and the "main" fallback. Nothing covers mpv crash-looping after an update or enabling nightly with release=main. Test to add: extend the shim to key on the unit name (e.g. FAKE_MPV_ACTIVE/FAKE_MPV_RESTARTS), run --postcheck with player active/0 and mpv failed/5, assert RELEASE is back at sha1 and the status message names projector-mpv.service. Console: a pages_settings test posting auto_update=nightly with player_release=main expecting 400.

**What is wrong.** With auto_update=nightly the fleet installs whatever main is at 03:00. The postcheck only detects a daemon that is not active or restarted 3+ times; a version that starts but plays nothing, or breaks mpv config, is not rolled back.


**How to reproduce.** Code trace: updater.maybe_auto_update (updater.py:178-192) -> run_update_player("main") -> update-player.sh:128 clones main -> install-player.sh --upgrade -> postcheck at :84 only checks is-active and NRestarts. Harness: cd player && python -m pytest -q tests/test_updater.py -k "postcheck" shows rollback fires only for FAKE_ACTIVE=failed / FAKE_RESTARTS>=3; run fake_pi.run("update-player.sh", "--postcheck", FAKE_ACTIVE="active", FAKE_RESTARTS="0") after a build whose daemon never plays and observe "post-check ok" in update.log.


**Fix.** 1) update-player.sh postcheck(): check both units, not just the player. Loop over "${SERVICE}" projector-mpv.service; the first that is not active or has NRestarts >= MAX_RESTARTS triggers the existing rollback, and the status message names that unit ("rolled back to <sha>: projector-mpv.service active with 5 restarts after the update"). Keep the player as the first unit so the existing log/message strings and test assertions are unchanged. 2) In update(), restart projector-mpv.service together with the player after arming the timer (systemctl restart projector-mpv.service "${SERVICE}") so NRestarts starts from zero for the check and old mpv crashes do not cause a false rollback; the console already warns "Playback restarts on each Pi". Update the shim_log assertion at test_updater.py:289-290 accordingly and reset-failed/restart both units in the rollback branch. Do NOT check playback/idle in the postcheck: an empty schedule at 03:02 is normal and would roll back good releases. 3) Console guard, one line in cloud/src/pages/settings.js after line 333: if autoUpdate === "nightly" && release === "main" fail(400, "Nightly updates need a pinned release such as v0.5.0, not main"). Leave the "main" fallbacks in updater.py:71/188 and db.js:107 alone; a hardcoded tag would go stale every release.


**Verify on hardware.** On a Pi with auto_update=nightly: sudo cat /var/lib/projector-player/update.log | tail -20 (look for "== update-player main" and "post-check ok"); cat /opt/piplayer/player/RELEASE vs git ls-remote https://github.com/Mattkillsyou/piplayer.git main to confirm it tracks HEAD; journalctl -u projector-player -n 200 to see whether playback started after the restart.


### M26. Console can select a Wyze camera on a Pi that has no bridge; the failure is logged at INFO and the owner sees 'Connection refused' forever

- Severity: **Medium**. Area: Projector, camera, tunnel. Where: `player/player/camera_config.py:97`.
- Existing test covers it: **partial**. player/tests/test_camera_config.py::test_apply_wyze_restarts_bridge_only_when_credentials_change (lines 106-109) drives the rc=1 branch but asserts only len(runs.args)==3, no log level and no camera.error. player/tests/test_camera.py::test_capture_failure_sets_error_and_logs_once_per_10_min uses source rtsp, so it neither covers nor would break under the fix. Tests to add: in test_camera.py, test_wyze_failure_names_missing_or_stopped_bridge: cam_cfg with camera_source="wyze", monkeypatch camera_mod.BRIDGE_UNIT_FILE to tmp_path/"unit"; with the file missing and ffmpeg rc 1, cam.error startswith "The Wyze camera bridge is not installed"; touch the file and make the fake run return rc 3 for a cmd starting with "systemctl", cam.error == "The Wyze camera bridge on this Pi is not running"; rc 0 for systemctl and the ffmpeg text is kept. In test_camera_config.py extend the rc=1 branch with caplog.at_level("WARNING", logger="piplayer.camera_config") and assert a record containing "bridge restart failed". No existing test needs changing.

**What is wrong.** camera_supported (pi_info.py:33-40) is a board rule (arm64 and >= 900 MB), not "bridge installed", and the console's cameraConfig() (cloud/src/pages/devices.js:181) resolves every device without an override to source "wyze" as soon as the Wyze account is set on Settings, bumping camera_config_version (db.js:167). So a Pi 4/5 installed without --with-wyze (README.md:135, adding-a-pi.md:190, or a card flashed before the account was entered) is handed a Wyze config on its very first sync without anyone picking it. apply() writes wyze.env, restart_bridge() gets rc=5 "Unit projector-wyze-bridge.service not found" (the sudoers line exists on every install, install-player.sh:288, the unit only with --with-wyze, line 379), camera_config.py:97 logs that at INFO and discards it, maybe_apply() records the version as applied, and the capture thread aims at rtsp://127.0.0.1:8554/<cam>; camera_error becomes "ffmpeg failed (exit 1): ... Connection refused" every cycle. Nothing on the console or in the warning says the bridge is missing on this Pi; docs/camera.md:284 sends the owner to `docker ps` on a machine without Docker. The same INFO swallowing hides a bridge that fails to restart for any other reason (sudoers line missing: rc=1 "a password is required").


**How to reproduce.** Scratch script docs/audits/2026-09-20-pi-os/repro/repro_wyze_bridge.py (run from <checkout>/player with the cms venv python): monkeypatches camera_config.subprocess.run to return rc=5 "Unit projector-wyze-bridge.service not found." and camera_config.fetch to a wyze answer, then calls maybe_apply(cfg, {"camera_config_version": 3}, cam, state). Output: applied=True, state version=3, cam=("wyze", "rtsp://127.0.0.1:8554/lobby-cam", error=""), and the only log records are INFO "camera config: wyze.env updated, bridge restart failed: rc=5 ..." and INFO "camera config v3 applied: source=wyze". Code trace: camera_config.py:96-97 (log.info only), :125 (version recorded), camera.py:54-56 (ffmpeg exit 1 text becomes the error), daemon.py:280 (sent as camera_error); console side devices.js:181 (site default "wyze"), devices.js:381 (form shown unless camera_supported === 0).


**Fix.**

```text
1) player/player/camera.py: add `from pathlib import Path`, `BRIDGE_UNIT = "projector-wyze-bridge.service"`, `BRIDGE_UNIT_FILE = Path("/etc/systemd/system/projector-wyze-bridge.service")` and

def bridge_problem(source: str) -> str:
    """Plain-English reason a Wyze grab fails when the bridge itself is the problem, "" otherwise."""
    if source != "wyze":
        return ""
    if not BRIDGE_UNIT_FILE.is_file():
        return "The Wyze camera bridge is not installed on this Pi (run the installer with --with-wyze)"
    try:
        if subprocess.run(["systemctl", "is-active", "--quiet", BRIDGE_UNIT], timeout=10).returncode != 0:
            return "The Wyze camera bridge on this Pi is not running"
    except (OSError, subprocess.SubprocessError):
        pass
    return ""

and in capture_once line 118 replace `self._fail(f"{e}")` with `self._fail(bridge_problem(self.source) or f"{e}")` (no sudo needed for is-active; runs only on a failed cycle, so no extra load). This is the durable place: apply()'s set_source clears camera.error and _fail overwrites it every cycle, so setting it in apply() as the finder proposed would be lost within one interval.
2) player/player/camera_config.py:80-85: log the failure at WARNING inside restart_bridge (line 97 stays INFO for the success case):
    msg = f"bridge restart failed: rc={res.returncode} {res.stderr.strip()[:200]}"
    log.warning("camera config: %s (bridge not installed? re-run install-player.sh --with-wyze --upgrade)", msg)
    return msg
3) docs/camera.md:284: the "Connection refused" row should say "for Wyze the bridge is not installed (installer run without --with-wyze) or not running" and list `systemctl status projector-wyze-bridge.service` before `docker ps`.
4) Drop the optional pi_info.camera_supported change: it would make the console say "Camera is not supported on this Pi model" (devices.js:381), which is false, and hide the RTSP option that needs no bridge.
Optional follow-up (separate finding): have the installer `systemctl restart` the wyze unit on --upgrade when wyze.env exists, so the documented remedy works without a reboot.
```


**Verify on hardware.** On a Pi 4/5 installed without --with-wyze, with the Wyze account set on the console Settings page: systemctl status projector-wyze-bridge.service (expect "could not be found"); ls /etc/systemd/system/projector-wyze-bridge.service; sudo cat /var/lib/projector-player/wyze.env (expect it written); journalctl -u projector-player.service | grep -i 'bridge restart' (expect an INFO line with rc=5 ... not found); journalctl -u projector-player.service | grep 'camera snapshot failed' (expect "ffmpeg failed (exit 1): ... Connection refused"); on the console Devices page the device's camera warning shows the Connection refused text with no mention of the missing bridge.


### M27. CEC power-on sends Image View On only; the projector wakes but may stay on its previous input

- Severity: **Medium**. Area: Projector, camera, tunnel. Where: `player/player/projector.py:91`.
- Existing test covers it: **no**. player/tests/test_projector.py::test_cec_claims_playback_then_sends_image_view_on_or_standby (line 156) pins the two-message sequence and would fail after the fix only if the fake's -S stdout carried a PA; with stdout="" it still passes, so it does not cover the defect. test_power_via_cec (line 209) is unaffected. Add: (1) fake run() returns stdout "Physical Address : 1.0.0.0" for argv containing "-S"; cec_power("on") -> third call == ["cec-ctl","-d","/dev/cec0","--active-source","phys-addr=1.0.0.0"], cec_power("off") -> no active-source call; (2) cec.fail.add("phys-addr=1.0.0.0") -> cec_power("on") does not raise and Projector.power("on") returns "projector on sent via cec"; (3) stdout "Physical Address : f.f.f.f" -> no active-source call.

**What is wrong.** cec_power('on') (projector.py:90-91) claims a playback address and sends only <Image View On> to logical address 0. Per CEC One Touch Play the display leaves standby on <Image View On> but switches to the sender's HDMI port only on <Active Source>, which the player never sends; a projector that resumes on its last input (VGA, the other HDMI) wakes showing "No signal" while the console lamp says "on" with no error. Independently, cec-ctl exits 0 when the message is Not Acknowledged or times out (cec-ctl.cpp: transmit loop `if (doioctl(...)) continue;`, status only printed via cec_status2s to stdout, main returns 0); _cec (projector.py:83) checks only the exit code and throws the captured stdout away, so an unpowered or unwired projector yields "projector on sent via cec" and projector_state=on. The fake cec-ctl in player/tests/test_projector.py:143 models rc=1+stderr, a failure shape real cec-ctl never produces.


**How to reproduce.** Code trace: projector.py:90 `_cec("--playback","-S")`, :91 `_cec("--to","0","--image-view-on")`, no --active-source anywhere (grep -rn "active-source" player/ cloud/ docs/ returns nothing). NACK path: projector.py:81-84 runs cec-ctl with capture_output and raises only on returncode != 0; cec-ctl main() returns 0 after a NACKed transmit (v4l-utils utils/cec-ctl/cec-ctl.cpp transmit loop + final `return 0;`), so Projector.power falls through to line 136 (`self.state, self.error = want, ""`) and returns "projector on sent via cec". Harness: cd <checkout>/player && python -m pytest -q tests/test_projector.py -k cec (passes; shows the fake models rc=1/stderr, not the real rc=0/stdout shape).


**Fix.**

Return stdout from _cec and reuse the PA the `--playback -S` call already prints; send Active Source best-effort on power-on only:

```python
import re
...
def _cec(*args: str) -> str:
    res = subprocess.run(["cec-ctl", "-d", CEC_DEVICE, *args], capture_output=True, text=True,
                         timeout=CEC_TIMEOUT_SECONDS)
    if res.returncode != 0:
        raise RuntimeError(f"cec-ctl {' '.join(args)} failed: rc={res.returncode} {res.stderr.strip()[:120]}")
    return res.stdout

_PA = re.compile(r"Physical Address\s*:\s*(\d\.\d\.\d\.\d)")

def cec_power(want: str) -> None:
    # claim a playback logical address first: a fresh adapter is unregistered and
    # the projector ignores standby from an unregistered source
    info = _cec("--playback", "-S")
    if want != "on":
        _cec("--to", "0", "--standby")
        return
    _cec("--to", "0", "--image-view-on")
    # One Touch Play: Image View On wakes the display, Active Source makes it
    # switch to our port. Best effort: the PA is f.f.f.f/absent while the
    # projector's HDMI is unpowered, and a NACK here must not fail the power-on.
    m = _PA.search(info)
    if m and m.group(1) != "f.f.f.f":
        try:
            _cec("--active-source", f"phys-addr={m.group(1)}")
        except RuntimeError as e:
            log.warning("cec active-source skipped: %s", e)
```

Also update docs/automation.md:670-672 (and the projector-on row at :572) to say CEC now asks the projector to switch to the Pi's HDMI port after waking, and that projectors whose HDMI port is unpowered in standby still need to be left on the Pi's input. Existing test to keep as-is: test_cec_claims_playback_then_sends_image_view_on_or_standby still passes because the fake's stdout is empty; add the three tests in test_note. ponytail: PA is read before Image View On, so a projector whose HDMI only powers up after waking gets no Active Source; if hardware shows that is the common case, re-read `cec-ctl -d /dev/cec0` after a short wait (or poll until the PA is valid, bounded by CEC_TIMEOUT_SECONDS) before sending it. If the hardware check shows cec-ctl exits 0 on NACK, additionally raise in _cec when "Not Acknowledged" appears in res.stdout for the --to 0 messages (not for --active-source, which is broadcast).


**Verify on hardware.** On the Pi with the projector on a non-Pi input: ls -l /dev/cec*; sudo -u projector cec-ctl -d /dev/cec0 --playback -S (note the "Physical Address" line; f.f.f.f means no EDID from the projector in standby). Then: sudo -u projector cec-ctl -d /dev/cec0 --to 0 --image-view-on; echo rc=$?  (does the projector wake AND switch to the Pi? if it wakes on the old input the input-switch half is confirmed). Then: PA=$(cec-ctl -d /dev/cec0 -S | sed -n 's/.*Physical Address *: *//p' | head -1); sudo -u projector cec-ctl -d /dev/cec0 --active-source phys-addr=$PA  (does it switch now?). NACK exit code: unplug the projector's mains, then sudo -u projector cec-ctl -d /dev/cec0 --to 0 --standby; echo rc=$?  (expect "Tx, Not Acknowledged" on stdout and rc=0). Then issue projector-on from the console and check: journalctl -u projector-player -n 100 | grep -i projector  and the console lamp/result text (expect "projector on sent via cec" with the projector unplugged).


### M28. A tunnel that cannot connect is invisible to the console: it says 'Tunnel ready' while cloudflared is failing on the Pi

- Severity: **Medium**. Area: Projector, camera, tunnel. Where: `player/player/tunnel.py:60`.
- Existing test covers it: **no**. No test in player/tests or tools/flasher/tests checks any post-restart unit state or a tunnel status param; test_tunnel.py::test_missing_binary_writes_the_token_but_never_restarts (line 88) and ::test_daemon_applies_the_manifest_token (line 114) only pin the silent path. Constraints on any fix: test_missing_binary... asserts exactly one WARNING on logger piplayer.tunnel across two maybe_apply calls, so a per-cycle warning must NOT go in maybe_apply; test_daemon_applies_the_manifest_token asserts runs.args == [RESTART] exactly and the `runs` fixture (line 39) fakes tunnel.subprocess.run, so a new `systemctl show` call made through tunnel.subprocess.run from run_cycle would break it unless that test's fixture is extended (or the status probe uses a separate function that the fixture also stubs). Test to add in player/tests/test_tunnel.py: `test_daemon_reports_tunnel_error` using the `runs` fixture with a fake `systemctl show` output: (a) BINARY unlinked + token present -> sync params contain tunnel_error="Remote camera access is not installed on this Pi"; (b) ActiveState=failed -> tunnel_error mentions "keeps stopping"; (c) ActiveState=active -> tunnel_error==""; (d) no token file -> no tunnel_error param at all. Console: cloud/test/tunnel.test.js gains a case that a sync with ?tunnel_error=... renders the warn box in the Camera section and that the badge reads the Cloudflare status.

**What is wrong.** The daemon writes the token and restarts the unit once, logging only the restart command's exit status. Whether cloudflared actually registered with the edge (bad/revoked token, outbound 7844 UDP/TCP blocked by the site router, clock skew, binary present but the ARMv6 raw build SIGILLs, unit skipped because /usr/bin/cloudflared is missing on a board where the download failed) is never observed and never reported; the sync status contract has no tunnel field. The console meanwhile sets camera_live_url and shows 'Tunnel ready: https://...'; the owner clicks Live and gets a Cloudflare 1033 error page with no hint that the Pi side is the problem. The missing-binary case is logged once on the Pi (line 56) and nowhere the owner looks.


**How to reproduce.** Code trace: maybe_apply -> restart_service() -> returns text only; no `systemctl is-active` check, no sync param; daemon.py:272-283 builds status without any tunnel key. test_missing_binary_writes_the_token_but_never_restarts confirms the silent path.


**Fix.** Two small pieces, dropping the journalctl parsing (log lines are not plain English and the projector user may not be in systemd-journal). (1) Pi, player/player/tunnel.py: add `def error(cfg) -> str | None` returning None when token_path(cfg) is missing (feature off, send nothing), else: BINARY missing -> "Remote camera access is not installed on this Pi (cloudflared is missing)"; run `["systemctl","show","-p","ActiveState,NRestarts","--value",UNIT]` (no sudo) and map ActiveState failed/activating or NRestarts>0 -> "The remote camera link keeps stopping on this Pi (cloudflared cannot start: check the token or that this board is supported)"; active -> "". In daemon.py after line 282: `err = tunnel.error(cfg); if err is not None: status["tunnel_error"] = err` (same "" clears semantics as projector_error). Keep it out of maybe_apply so the once-only WARNING test still passes; extend the `runs` fixture to answer the `show` call. (2) Console: migration `ALTER TABLE devices ADD COLUMN tunnel_error TEXT`; api.js sync reads `tunnel_error` exactly like projector_error (line 60) and stores it; devices.js Camera section shows it in the warn style used for camera_error at line 121. For the edge-connectivity case the Pi cannot see (7844 blocked, clock skew), replace the static badge at devices.js:301 with the tunnel's `status` from `GET ${acct}/cfd_tunnel/${tunnel_id}` (healthy -> "tunnel · connected", down/inactive -> "tunnel · Pi not connected", warn style), fetched alongside the existing per-sync tunnelToken call at api.js:110 or lazily on the device page. Reword devices.js:533 to "Tunnel created: https://... (shows as connected once the Pi picks up the token)". Minimum-viable Pi-only step if the console change is deferred: log tunnel.error() at WARNING once per change of value.


**Verify on hardware.** On the Pi: systemctl is-active projector-cloudflared.service; systemctl status projector-cloudflared.service --no-pager; journalctl -u projector-cloudflared.service -n 30 --no-pager (look for "Registered tunnel connection" vs "failed to connect"/"Unauthorized"); ls -l /usr/bin/cloudflared /var/lib/projector-player/tunnel.token; cloudflared --version. Then in the console open Devices and confirm the row still shows the green "tunnel · host" badge with no error regardless of what journalctl showed. Cross-check the edge view: curl -s -H "Authorization: Bearer $CF_TOKEN" https://api.cloudflare.com/client/v4/accounts/$ACCT/cfd_tunnel/$TUNNEL_ID | jq .result.status


### M29. Installed player has no clock guard or fallback: with NTP blocked, a stored or nightly-unplugged Pi fails TLS for good and its nightly update window drifts into show hours

- Severity: **Medium**. Area: Player daemon. Where: `player/deploy/projector-player.service:3`.
- Existing test covers it: **no**. grep of player/tests for SSLError, 'not yet valid', NTPSynchronized or timesync returns nothing; test_daemon.py has no SSLError path and test_updater.py tests in_window/auto_update_due only with a trusted clock. A regression test would monkeypatch sync_once to raise requests.exceptions.SSLError('certificate is not yet valid') and assert the screen reason is a clock message rather than 'offline'.

**What is wrong.** Installed system never re-checks or seeds the clock after first boot. projector-player.service:3-4 orders only after network-online.target; no player code, deploy script or unit references timedatectl/NTPSynchronized. With NTP (UDP 123) blocked at the venue: (1) a Pi stored longer than the age of the console's Cloudflare edge certificate (Universal SSL, ~90-day, so roughly 2-3 months) boots with fake-hwclock's last saved time, TLS fails with 'certificate is not yet valid', daemon.py:337-340 maps the SSLError (a RequestException) to last_failure='unreachable', screens.py:184-187 tells the owner to check the console address, and the 300 s backoff retries forever because nothing corrects the clock (a re-flash does, via provision.sh's Date-header seed at firstboot.py:460-463, but only until the next long power-off); (2) a Pi on a wall switch cut nightly restores the last hourly fake-hwclock save on each boot, so updater.py:182 datetime.now() drifts ~12 h per day and auto_update_due fires the 03:00-05:00 window during show hours within a day or two, restarting the daemon mid-show.


**How to reproduce.** Trace: projector-player.service:3 (After=projector-mpv.service network-online.target; no time-sync); daemon.py:337-340 `except requests.RequestException` -> last_failure='unreachable' -> _failure_screen shows OFFLINE with the 'check the console address' help line (screens.py:184-187); updater.py:182 `now = datetime.now().astimezone()` with no sync check; compare firstboot.py:455-463 where provision.sh does set the clock from the console's Date header. Unit test sketch: monkeypatch sync_once to raise requests.exceptions.SSLError('certificate is not yet valid') and assert the OFFLINE screen and no clock action.


**Fix.** Add to projector-player.service: After=time-sync.target plus ExecStartPre=+/usr/local/sbin/projector-clock-fallback.sh (the + runs it as root under User=projector), the script being the same loop provision.sh already has (wait up to ~30 s for `timedatectl show -p NTPSynchronized --value` = yes, else `date -s` from the Date header of `curl -sSk -D - $CMS_URL/api/health`), installed by install-player.sh from the env file's CMS_URL. In daemon.py:337 catch requests.exceptions.SSLError before RequestException and, when str(e) contains 'not yet valid' or 'has expired', set last_failure='clock' with a screens.py line such as 'This projector's clock is wrong; it needs internet time (NTP) or a restart once the network allows it', and have updater.maybe_auto_update skip when `timedatectl show -p NTPSynchronized --value` != yes (subprocess, cached per poll) so the nightly window is never judged on an unsynced clock.


**Verify on hardware.** On the Pi: `timedatectl` (look at 'System clock synchronized' and 'NTP service'); `journalctl -u systemd-timesyncd -n 20`; `journalctl -u projector-player -n 200 | grep -i -E 'certificate|SSL|unreachable'`; `systemctl cat fake-hwclock.timer` or `ls -l /etc/fake-hwclock.data` to see the hourly save. Reproduce: `sudo systemctl stop systemd-timesyncd && sudo date -s '2025-01-01 12:00' && sudo systemctl restart projector-player`, then watch the OFFLINE screen persist and the log show 'certificate is not yet valid' every backoff; `sudo date -s '03:10'` with a nightly update block in the manifest and confirm `journalctl -u projector-player | grep auto-update` shows update-player starting.


### M30. Console "Camera:" error shows ffmpeg exit codes, raw stderr (can include the RTSP URL with credentials) and urllib3 connection dumps

- Severity: **Medium**. Area: Plain English. Where: `player/player/camera.py:118`.
- Existing test covers it: **partial**. Existing tests pin the current jargon rather than plain English: player/tests/test_camera.py:85-99 (match "exit 1"/"no JPEG"/"too large"/"timed out" on str(CameraError)), :122 ("Connection refused" in cam.error), :134 ("HTTP 500" in cam.error), :141 ("cannot connect" in cam.error), test_camera_config.py:203 (cam.error.startswith("camera config: ")). test_daemon.py:576-578 and test_camera.py:176 set cam.error directly, so they are unaffected by the fix. With the fix below, test_camera.py:85-99 keep passing (str(e) stays the detail); :122, :134, :141 and test_camera_config.py:203 must change to assert the plain text in cam.error and the detail in caplog. No test asserts that cam.error is free of the RTSP URL or credentials; add one: ffmpeg["result"] = CompletedProcess([], 1, stdout=b"", stderr=b"rtsp://user:secret@cam/x: Connection refused"); cam.capture_once(); assert "secret" not in cam.error and cam.error == camera_mod.TEXT_NO_PICTURE and "Connection refused" in caplog.text and "secret" not in caplog.text.
- Also reported as: camera_error and projector_error shown on the console are raw tool output, not plain English; Errors reported to the console are developer-speak (ffmpeg stderr, cec-ctl rc, exception names, script line numbers).

**What is wrong.** camera_error is built from raw CameraError texts and str(e): "ffmpeg timed out after 15s (camera unreachable?)" (camera.py:51), "cannot run ffmpeg: [Errno 2] ..." (53), "ffmpeg failed (exit 1): Error opening input files: Connection refused" (56), "ffmpeg produced no JPEG frame" (59), "snapshot too large (N bytes, max 2097152)" (61), "upload failed: HTTP 413 <80 chars of body>" (77), str(requests.RequestException) at 118 (urllib3 "HTTPSConnectionPool(host=...): Max retries exceeded with url: ... (Caused by NewConnectionError(...") and "TypeError: ..." at 121; camera_config.py:123 adds "camera config: [Errno 13] Permission denied: '/var/lib/projector-player/wyze.env.tmp'". daemon.py:280 sends it as camera_error and cloud/src/pages/devices.js:122 shows it verbatim under "Camera:". The owner is non-technical, so none of this is plain English. The claim that the console line carries the RTSP password is NOT true on the supported OS: Trixie's ffmpeg 7.1 prints the URL on the penultimate stderr line ("Error opening input file rtsp://user:pass@host/x.") and the final line ("Error opening input files: <reason>") is the only one taken (detail[-1]); a timeout never reads stderr. The rtsp_url with credentials does go to the local journal via log.info at camera.py:105 and 136 (minor, local only).


**How to reproduce.** cd <checkout>/player && python -m pytest -q tests/test_camera.py -k "grab_failures or timeout" (5 pass, asserting the jargon strings 'exit 1', 'no JPEG', 'too large', 'timed out'). Trace: grab_jpeg raises CameraError (camera.py:51/53/56/59/61) or upload raises (77) or requests raises -> capture_once:117-121 -> _fail -> self.error -> daemon.py:280 status["camera_error"] -> cloud/src/api.js:56 -> devices.js:122 "Camera: ...". For the credential claim: ffmpeg -nostdin -loglevel error -i http://user:secret@127.0.0.1:1/x -frames:v 1 -f image2 - ; last stderr line is "Error opening input files: <reason>" (no URL) on ffmpeg >= 6.1.


**Fix.** Keep the finder's direction but make it smaller and keep str(CameraError) as the technical detail so test_camera.py:85-99 still pass. In camera.py: (1) `class CameraError(Exception): def __init__(self, user, detail=""): super().__init__(detail or user); self.user = user` plus module constants TEXT_NO_ANSWER = "The camera did not answer. Check that it is powered on and on the same network as the projector.", TEXT_NO_PICTURE = "The projector could not get a picture from the camera. Check the camera and its stream address on the console; a camera that was just set up can take a few minutes.", TEXT_TOO_BIG = "The camera picture is too big to send; lower the camera's resolution.", TEXT_UPLOAD = "The picture could not be sent to the console; the projector will try again.", TEXT_CONFIG = "The camera settings from the console could not be applied; the projector will try again." (2) grab_jpeg: line 51 raise CameraError(TEXT_NO_ANSWER, f"ffmpeg timed out after {CAPTURE_TIMEOUT_SECONDS}s"); 53 CameraError(TEXT_NO_PICTURE, f"cannot run {ffmpeg}: {e}"); 56 CameraError(TEXT_NO_PICTURE, f"ffmpeg failed (exit {proc.returncode}): {detail[-1] if detail else 'no output'}"); 59 CameraError(TEXT_NO_PICTURE, "ffmpeg produced no JPEG frame"); 61 CameraError(TEXT_TOO_BIG, f"snapshot too large ({len(data)} bytes, max {MAX_JPEG_BYTES})"); 41 CameraError(TEXT_NO_PICTURE, f"snapshot file: {e}"); 77 CameraError(TEXT_UPLOAD, f"upload failed: HTTP {r.status_code} {r.text[:80]}"). (3) capture_once: `except CameraError as e: self._fail(e.user, str(e))`, `except requests.RequestException as e: self._fail(TEXT_UPLOAD, str(e))`, `except Exception as e: self._fail(TEXT_NO_PICTURE, f"{type(e).__name__}: {e}")`. (4) `_fail(self, user, detail)`: self.error = user; detail = re.sub(r"://[^/@\s]+@", "://***@", detail)[:ERROR_MAX_LEN]; rate-limited log.warning("camera snapshot failed: %s (%s)", user, detail) (import re; the redaction keeps a pasted journalctl from carrying the camera password). (5) camera_config.py:123: `camera.error = TEXT_CONFIG` (import from .camera; the OSError is already logged on line 122). Tests to update: test_camera.py:122 -> assert cam.error == camera_mod.TEXT_NO_PICTURE and "Connection refused" in caplog.text; :134 -> cam.error == TEXT_UPLOAD; :141 -> cam.error == TEXT_UPLOAD; test_camera_config.py:203 -> cam.error == camera.TEXT_CONFIG. ERROR_MAX_LEN can stay for the log detail only. Skipped: per-source wording (wyze vs rtsp); add if owners report the "stream address" hint confuses Wyze users.


**Verify on hardware.** On the console set the device's camera source to RTSP with rtsp://user:secret@10.255.255.1/x, wait one capture interval, read the Camera line on the Devices page (expect "ffmpeg timed out after 15s (camera unreachable?)"). Then point it at a host that refuses instantly (rtsp://user:secret@127.0.0.1:1/x) and confirm the Camera line is "ffmpeg failed (exit 1): Error opening input files: Connection refused" with no password. On the Pi: ffmpeg -version | head -1; ffmpeg -nostdin -loglevel error -rtsp_transport tcp -i rtsp://user:secret@127.0.0.1:1/x -frames:v 1 -f image2 - 2>&1 >/dev/null | tail -1; journalctl -u projector-player -n 50 | grep -E 'camera (snapshot failed|source updated|capture started)'


### M31. Projector power errors and results expose cec-ctl command lines, rc codes, code keys and transport names

- Severity: **Medium**. Area: Plain English. Where: `player/player/projector.py:84`.
- Existing test covers it: **yes**. tests/test_projector.py pins the current technical strings and would all need their expected text updated: test_send_code_rejects_bad_codes_and_failed_auth (:96-101), test_nothing_discovered_is_a_clear_error (:108), test_learn_gives_up_after_the_window (:131), test_cec_failure_surfaces_stderr (:169, rename to test_cec_failure_is_plain_english_and_logs_detail and assert the cec-ctl detail via caplog), test_power_retries_once_and_records_state (:179, :183-184), test_power_without_a_code_or_with_control_none_fails_cleanly (:191-195), test_power_via_cec (:212), test_learn_returns_the_code_as_json (:224), test_auto_mode_failure_is_reported_not_retried_until_want_changes (:254), test_projector_commands_report_their_result (:280, :282), test_run_cycle_reports_projector_state_and_applies_auto_mode (:326). Add one assertion in the cec failure test that neither "cec-ctl" nor "rc=" appears in p.error and one in test_power_retries that a raw OSError("timed out") becomes the generic Broadlink sentence, so a future regression to str(e) fails.

**What is wrong.** projector_error and the command result are built from RuntimeError texts: "cec-ctl --playback -S failed: rc=1 Failed to open /dev/cec0: No such file or directory" (84, the everyday case when the projector or lead has no CEC), "projector control is none" (126, owner pressed Projector on before choosing a control), "no power_on code learned yet" (121, console calls it "Power On"), "no Broadlink RM found on the LAN (set the RM4 host on the console)" (42), "Broadlink auth failed for 192.168.1.20" (45), "bad IR code: ..." (53), "nothing learned in 30 s (press the remote at the RM4)" (75), plus any socket/ModuleNotFoundError text via the broad except at 132. They are prefixed "projector on: " (133) for the console's Projector box and "projector-on failed: " (135) for the command line; success reads "projector on sent via broadlink" (137).


**How to reproduce.** cd "<checkout>/player" && python -m pytest -q tests/test_projector.py -k "cec_failure or control_none or retries_once"  (passes today because the tests pin the raw strings: test_projector.py:169 expects "cec-ctl --to 0 --standby failed: rc=1 Transmit failed", :191 "projector-off failed: no power_off code learned yet", :193 "projector-on failed: projector control is none"). Trace: projector.py:84 -> :129-131 (retry) -> :132-135 -> daemon.py:282 (projector_error sync param) -> cloud/src/api.js:60 (stored as-is, 500 chars) -> cloud/src/pages/dashboard.js:33 and devices.js:264 (rendered verbatim) and devices.js:205 (command result rendered verbatim).


**Fix.**

```text
Keep the finder's plain-English texts at the raise sites (42, 45, 53/55, 75, 84, 121, 126) but make the boundary itself safe, so any exception type reads well and the detail still reaches the journal:

1. _cec (80-84): on rc != 0, log.warning("cec-ctl %s failed: rc=%s %s", " ".join(args), res.returncode, res.stderr.strip()[:120]) then raise RuntimeError("the projector did not answer over HDMI-CEC; check that CEC is switched on in the projector's menu and that the HDMI lead supports it"). Also wrap the subprocess.run in try/except (FileNotFoundError, subprocess.TimeoutExpired) with the same plain sentence (cec-ctl missing or hung otherwise leaks "[Errno 2] No such file or directory: 'cec-ctl'").
2. Add near the top: IR_CODE_LABELS = {"power_on": "Power On", "power_off": "Power Off", "input_hdmi1": "Input HDMI1"} (mirrors cloud/src/pages/devices.js:241) and a helper
   def _plain(e: BaseException) -> str:
       return str(e) if isinstance(e, RuntimeError) else "could not reach the Broadlink remote; check it is plugged in and on the same network"
   RuntimeErrors are the module's own sentences; anything else (OSError "timed out", broadlink socket errors, ModuleNotFoundError) gets the generic sentence, and log.warning at 130/134 keeps the real text (change 134 to log.warning("%s (%s)", self.error, e)).
3. power(): 121 -> f"the {IR_CODE_LABELS.get(f'power_{want}', want)} button has not been learned yet (use Learn {label} under Projector on the console)"; 126 -> "choose how the projector is controlled (broadlink or cec) under Projector on the console first"; 133 -> self.error = f"Could not switch the projector {want}: {_plain(e)}"[:ERROR_MAX_LEN]; 135 -> return f"Could not switch the projector {want}: {_plain(e)}"[:300]; 137 -> return f"Sent the power-{want} signal to the projector" (IR/CEC are fire-and-forget, so do not claim "switched").
4. learn(): 145 -> f"Could not learn {IR_CODE_LABELS.get(name, name)}: {_plain(e)}"[:300]. The console (api.js:162-169) only parses the JSON success shape, so the failure wording is free.
5. Update the tests listed in test_note to the new sentences.
```


**Verify on hardware.** On the console set the device's projector control to HDMI-CEC with a projector or HDMI lead that has no CEC, press Projector on, then read the Projector line on the Dashboard and the Devices page and the command's result text in the command history. On the Pi: sudo -u projector-admin cec-ctl -d /dev/cec0 --playback -S; echo rc=$?; cec-ctl -d /dev/cec0 --to 0 --image-view-on; echo rc=$?; journalctl -u projector-player -n 200 | grep -i "projector"


### M32. OFFLINE screen advises checking "the console address and that the console is running"; TLS/clock failures are also called unreachable

- Severity: **Medium**. Area: Plain English. Where: `player/player/screens.py:187`.
- Existing test covers it: **partial**. Existing: player/tests/test_screens.py:72-73 and player/tests/test_daemon.py:99 pin the current strings ("no cached content", "playing cached content", "last contact 4 min ago") and will need their assertions updated to the new wording; test_daemon.py:92-101 and :307-312 cover the ConnectionError -> offline path but nothing raises SSLError. Missing test (add to player/tests/test_daemon.py): monkeypatch cms.get to raise requests.exceptions.SSLError("certificate is not yet valid"), run_cycle with screens, assert state.last_failure == "unreachable", screens.current.kind == "offline", screens.current.reason == "tls" and "clock" in screen_texts(screens). Add to test_screens.py: texts(state("offline", reason="tls")) contains the clock help line, and headline stays "OFFLINE".

**What is wrong.** The offline screen (screens.py:185-187) reads "console unreachable: https://..." / "last contact 4 min ago" / "no cached content" / "Check network, the console address and that the console is running." The console is a Cloudflare Worker (cloud/wrangler.toml) the owner cannot "run", and CMS_URL is baked into /etc/projector-player/env at flash time (firstboot.py:412-443), so two of the three suggestions are dead ends and "cached content" is jargon. daemon.py:151 also always passes cached=False, so the "playing cached content" wording is dead: the offline screen is only ever shown when nothing is playing. Separately, daemon.py:337-338 maps every non-HTTP RequestException, including requests.exceptions.SSLError (a ConnectionError subclass), to last_failure="unreachable", so a TLS failure (stale clock after a long power-off on a board without a battery-backed RTC, before systemd-timesyncd corrects it; projector-player.service is not ordered after time-sync.target) or a certificate problem shows the same OFFLINE screen and log line and sends the owner to check the network. Scope of the TLS half: on screen only when nothing is cached (a Pi with cached media keeps playing and only the journal says "CMS unreachable: SSLError"); first boot is covered by firstrun's NTP wait (firstboot.py:455-463).


**How to reproduce.** cd "<checkout>/player" && python -c "import requests; print(requests.exceptions.SSLError.__mro__[:3]); from player.screens import *; print([i['text'] for i in layout(ScreenState(kind='offline', last_contact='4 min ago', console_url='https://x'))][-4:])"  -> SSLError is a ConnectionError/RequestException subclass and the four strings print as described. Code trace: daemon.py:337-339 (single RequestException branch, no SSLError branch anywhere in daemon.py/sync.py) -> daemon.py:150-151 shows kind="offline" with cached=False -> screens.py:185-187 text.


**Fix.**

```text
Smaller than the finder's fix: reuse the existing ScreenState.reason field and the existing "unreachable" failure kind instead of adding a new last_failure value and a new _failure_screen branch.

screens.py:184-187:
    if k == "offline":
        help_ = ("The secure connection to the console failed, usually because this projector's clock is wrong. "
                 "It fixes itself a minute or two after it gets online."
                 if state.reason == "tls" else
                 "Check the projector's Wi-Fi or network cable. It keeps trying on its own.")
        return (["This projector cannot reach the console.",
                 f"last heard from it {state.last_contact or 'never'}",
                 "nothing saved on this projector to play"], help_)
(drop the cached branch: daemon.py:151 is the only caller and always passes cached=False; keep the field so nothing else breaks, or delete it and the test_screens.py:73 line together.)

daemon.py:337-339:
    except requests.RequestException as e:
        state.last_failure, state.failure_text = "unreachable", str(e)
        state.tls_failure = isinstance(e, requests.exceptions.SSLError)
        log.warning("CMS unreachable: %s (will retry)", e)
plus `tls_failure: bool = False` on PlayerState (daemon.py:120 area), reset to False at daemon.py:315, and daemon.py:151:
        screens.show(screens.state("offline", device_name=name, reason="tls" if state.tls_failure else "",
                                   last_contact=_age_text(state.last_contact)))

Tests to update: player/tests/test_screens.py:72-73 (new strings; drop or adapt the cached=True line), player/tests/test_daemon.py:99 ("no cached content" -> "nothing saved on this projector to play"). test_daemon.py:98 and :312 still pass because last_failure and the log line are unchanged. Do not add After=time-sync.target to projector-player.service: without systemd-time-wait-sync it does nothing, and with it the player would never start while offline.
```


**Verify on hardware.** On a Pi with no cached media (fresh device, or `sudo rm -rf /var/lib/projector-player/*` after noting it will re-download): sudo timedatectl set-ntp false && sudo date -s '2020-01-01' && sudo systemctl restart projector-player; watch the HDMI output for the OFFLINE screen with "Check network, the console address..."; journalctl -u projector-player -n 30 | grep -i 'unreachable' should show "CMS unreachable: HTTPSConnectionPool(...) SSLError ... certificate is not yet valid"; then sudo timedatectl set-ntp true and confirm the next cycle syncs. Also check ordering: systemctl show projector-player -p After | grep -c time-sync (expect 0 today).


### M33. TOKEN REJECTED screen tells the owner to "re-run the installer" and shows a "TOKEN" pill

- Severity: **Medium**. Area: Plain English. Where: `player/player/screens.py:189`.
- Existing test covers it: **yes**. player/tests/test_screens.py:71 (asserts a line containing "token"), :74 (pins the old wording), :95 (pins TOKEN REJECTED) and player/tests/test_daemon.py:361 (pins the old wording via screen_texts) all lock in the current text and must be updated: :71 -> assert not any("token" in x.lower() ...), :74 -> new wording plus assert "NOT PAIRED" in texts(ALL[5]) to pin the pill, :95 -> "NOT RECOGNISED", test_daemon.py:361 -> new wording. No existing test asserts the pill label; the added NOT PAIRED assertion covers screens.py:215.

**What is wrong.** On any 401/403 from the console (device deleted on the Devices page, card re-flashed so the token rotated, token regenerated) the projector shows "TOKEN REJECTED" / "the console refused this device's token" / "regenerate the token on the Devices page and re-run the installer", with the status pill reading "TOKEN" (line 215 uses state.reason as the pill label). The owner never ran an installer: their tool is the flasher app, and the fix is to flash the card again (the same device_id re-enrolls the same device). "Token" is developer vocabulary. This is a common path: deleting a device on the console is a normal thing to do.


**How to reproduce.** cd "<checkout>/player" && python -c "from player.screens import *; print([i['text'] for i in layout(ScreenState(kind='error', reason='token', device_id='lobby', console_url='https://x'))])" -> [..., 'TOKEN', ..., 'TOKEN REJECTED', "the console refused this device's token", 'lobby', 'regenerate the token on the Devices page and re-run the installer']. Trigger: daemon.py:334 (401/403 -> last_failure='token'), daemon.py:148-149 shows it; console returns 401 for a deleted device (cloud/src/auth.js:275) and 403 for id mismatch (cloud/src/api.js:23). Harness: tests/test_daemon.py:355-361 drives the 401 cycle.


**Fix.** screens.py:71: ERROR_HEADLINES = {"token": "NOT RECOGNISED", "player": "PLAYER FAULT", "storage": "STORAGE FULL"}; add ERROR_PILLS = {"token": "not paired", "player": "fault", "storage": "disk full"}. screens.py:190-191: return ["the console no longer recognises this projector", state.device_id, "flash its card again with the flasher app (its name is kept)"], "" (keep under ~69 chars so _fit does not ellipsise it at mono 42 over SAFE_W; the finder's 71-char line gets truncated). screens.py:215: style, label = PILLS.get(state.kind, ("fault", ERROR_PILLS.get(state.reason, "error"))). Optionally daemon.py:100-102 log text still says "check device_token in config"; that is journal-only and fine to leave. Update the four assertions named in test_note. Verified in a scratch copy: 277 pass.


**Verify on hardware.** On the console, open Devices and click "New token" (or delete the device) for the projector; within one poll interval the projector should show TOKEN REJECTED with the "re-run the installer" line. On the Pi: journalctl -u projector-player -n 50 | grep -i 'HTTP 40[13]'. Then re-flash the card with the same device name in the flasher app and confirm the projector re-pairs without any installer step.


### M34. ERROR screen shows raw developer text (HTTP status, CMS, cms_url/device_id, URLs, exception names) with an "OTHER" pill and no help line

- Severity: **Medium**. Area: Plain English. Where: `player/player/screens.py:215`.
- Existing test covers it: **partial**. tests/test_daemon.py:294 (test_http_errors_are_not_reported_as_unreachable) pins the log text for 401/403/404/500 but not the screen; tests/test_daemon.py:434 (test_every_item_failing_shows_error_screen) pins reason=='other' and the raw "download failed: a.mp4: HTTP 404" line on screen but asserts nothing about the pill label or a help line; tests/test_screens.py:92 pins headline('other')=='ERROR' only. A covering test: in test_screens.py add `def test_other_error_has_plain_pill_and_help(): t = texts(state("error", reason="other", message="x")); assert "OTHER" not in t and "PROBLEM" in t and any("keeps trying" in s for s in t)`; in test_daemon.py add a 404 case asserting screens.current.message has no "cms_url" or "http" URL and contains "HTTP 404".
- Also reported as: HTTP 5xx/404 on the sync puts the raw requests message with the full query URL on the projector ERROR screen.

**What is wrong.** The primary defect is in screens.py: line 215 uses state.reason as the pill label (renders "OTHER") and line 196 returns the raw message with no help line for reason other. daemon.py:100-105, :346 and sync.py:373 supply the developer-facing message text that reaches the screen via daemon.py:155 and :172-173. Finder's description is otherwise accurate; the cited daemon.py:104 is one of several sources, not the rendering point.


**How to reproduce.** cd player && python -c "from player.screens import *; print([i['text'] for i in layout(ScreenState(kind='error', reason='other', console_url='https://projectors.photogen5000.com', message='not found on CMS (HTTP 404) - check cms_url/device_id: https://projectors.photogen5000.com/api/sync/lobby-projector'))][5:])"  -> ['OTHER', ..., 'ERROR', 'not found on CMS (HTTP 404) - chec…n5000.com/api/sync/lobby-projector']. Also python -m pytest -q tests/test_daemon.py -k 'idle_screen or missing' (test_daemon.py:442 shows 'download failed: a.mp4: HTTP 404' on screen).


**Fix.** screens.py:215: `style, label = PILLS.get(state.kind, ("fault", "problem"))` (fixed word for every error reason; the headline already distinguishes token/player/storage). screens.py:195: `return [state.message, "Remove some videos from this playlist on the console, or use a bigger memory card."], ""`. screens.py:196: `return [state.message], "The projector keeps trying by itself. If this stays for more than an hour, check its network and the console."`. daemon.py:_describe_http_error: leave 401/403 unchanged (log-only, screen uses the fixed token text); change 404 to `return f"The console did not recognise this projector's address (HTTP 404)"` and the default to `return f"The console answered with an error (HTTP {status}); it usually clears by itself"`; move the URL to the log by changing daemon.py:335 to `log.warning("%s (will retry): %s", state.failure_text, e.response.url if e.response is not None else "")`. Keeping "(HTTP 404)"/"(HTTP 500)" in the text means test_daemon.py:294 still passes unchanged. daemon.py:346: `state.failure_text = "Something unexpected went wrong; the projector will try again"` (log.exception on the next line already records the traceback). sync.py "download failed: a.mp4: HTTP 404" is left alone (it is also the sync_error reported to the console and pinned by test_daemon.py:220/222/442); the new help line is what tells the owner what to do. No existing test needs changing.


**Verify on hardware.** On the Pi: sudo sed -i 's#^cms_url = .*#cms_url = "https://projectors.photogen5000.com/nope"#' /etc/projector-player/config.toml && sudo systemctl restart projector-player; watch the projector for the ERROR screen with pill OTHER and the "not found on CMS (HTTP 404) - check cms_url/device_id: ..." line; journalctl -u projector-player -n 20; then restore cms_url in config.toml and restart the service.


### M35. README and hardware.md say the flasher writes the [camera] table and takes Wyze credentials; it has no camera fields

- Severity: **Medium**. Area: Docs versus code. Where: `README.md:94`.
- Existing test covers it: **no**. No test reads README.md or docs/hardware.md. tools/flasher/tests/test_firstboot.py::test_provision_with_wyze_flag (line 327) already pins the code behaviour (with_wyze only adds "--with-wyze"; nothing else, no [camera] table), so the code side needs nothing. A docs guard would be a small test in tools/flasher/tests that asserts README.md and docs/hardware.md do not contain "write the player's `[camera]` table" or "camera fields", but that is optional hygiene for a text-only fix. The proposed fix changes no code and breaks no existing test in player/tests or tools/flasher/tests.

**What is wrong.** README.md:94 states "The flasher may also write the player's [camera] table (source, rtsp_url, wyze_camera, snapshot_interval_seconds, live_url)" and hardware.md:186-188 tells the owner to "Tell the flasher the camera name and the Wyze credentials under Advanced if your build has the camera fields; otherwise you set [camera] and wyze.env on the Pi after it enrolls". No build has them: flasher.py contains no camera/wyze form field (the only reference is cfg['with_wyze'] at line 1204/1221, set from the console's wyze_configured), firstboot.py renders no [camera] table, and the only camera-related thing on the card is the --with-wyze installer flag (firstboot.py:410). On a cloud site the camera comes from section D with nothing typed on the Pi, so the "otherwise" advice is also wrong for the main path.


**How to reproduce.** cd "<checkout>" && grep -in 'camera\|wyze\|rtsp\|snapshot_interval\|live_url' tools/flasher/flasher.py tools/flasher/firstboot.py (only flasher.py:1204,1214,1221-1223 and firstboot.py:404-410, all the with_wyze bool); python -c "import sys; sys.path.insert(0,'tools/flasher'); import firstboot; c=firstboot.sample_config(); c['with_wyze']=True; import re; print([l for l in (firstboot.render_provision(c)+firstboot.render_firstrun(c)).splitlines() if re.search('camera|wyze|toml',l,re.I)])" prints one line, the install-player.sh --with-wyze call; sed -n '110,124p' tools/flasher/README.md shows the Advanced fields with no camera entry.


**Fix.**

```text
README.md:94, replace the sentence "The flasher may also write the player's `[camera]` table (...)...section D)." with: "When the console has a Wyze account the flasher makes the installer run with `--with-wyze` (Docker and the Wyze bridge go on at install); the camera itself is configured on the console ([docs/automation.md](docs/automation.md) section D) or, on a Python console, by hand on the Pi ([docs/camera.md](docs/camera.md))."

docs/hardware.md:186-188, replace "Tell the flasher the camera name and the Wyze credentials under "Advanced" if your build has the camera fields; otherwise you set `[camera]` and `wyze.env` on the Pi after it enrolls." with: "Nothing to enter in the flasher. Enter the Wyze account once on the console's Settings page and name the camera after the device in the Wyze app; the Pi picks it up from the console on its first sync ([automation.md](automation.md) section D). On a Python console set `[camera]` and `wyze.env` on the Pi instead ([camera.md](camera.md))."
```


### M36. Pi 3 row promises the camera on the A+, which has 512 MB and is refused by the installer

- Severity: **Medium**. Area: Docs versus code. Where: `docs/adding-a-pi.md:93`.
- Existing test covers it: **partial**. player/tests/test_pi_info.py:52 (test_camera_rule_matches_the_installer) and the installer harness cover the 512 MB => no camera rule; tools/flasher/tests/test_pimodel.py:67 asserts pi3 has camera=True but no test checks the A+ wording. A covering test: in test_table_order_and_arch add `assert "A+" in pimodel.get("pi3").hint and "no camera" in pimodel.get("pi3").hint.lower()` and, for the docs, a small test that greps docs/adding-a-pi.md and tools/flasher/README.md for the Pi 3 row and asserts "A+" appears alongside "512 MB" / "no camera" (same pattern as the Zero 2 W row).

**What is wrong.** The row "Raspberry Pi 3 (B, B+, A+) | Camera: Yes" (and the flasher hint at tools/flasher/README.md:60 / pimodel.py:18-19, camera=True) lumps the 3 A+ in with the 1 GB boards. The A+ has 512 MB, so pi_caps in install-player.sh:44,61 (MemTotal >= 900000 kB) sets CAMERA_SUPPORTED=0, --with-wyze is downgraded with "Camera bridge: not supported" (line 165-169), and pi_info.py:40 reports camera_supported=0 so the console shows "Camera is not supported on this Pi model." The Zero 2 W row already says "No (512 MB is not enough)" for exactly the same rule, so the table contradicts itself as well as the code.


**How to reproduce.** Code trace: pimodel.py:18-19 (camera=True, ram_mb=512) vs pimodel.py:4-5 docstring (needs 1 GB) vs player/deploy/install-player.sh:44,60-62,165-168 and player/player/pi_info.py:40. Quick check: cd "<checkout>/tools/flasher" && python -c "import pimodel; print([(m.key, m.camera, m.ram_mb) for m in pimodel.MODELS if m.camera and m.ram_mb < 1024])" prints [('pi3', True, 512)]. player/tests/test_pi_info.py:52 shows 430000 kB (512 MB) is refused.


**Fix.** Keep the label "Raspberry Pi 3 (B, B+, A+)" (test_pimodel.py:195-196 and the flasher.json key "pi3" depend on it) and fix the three pieces of text: (1) tools/flasher/pimodel.py:19 hint -> "1080p video, remote access. Camera on the B / B+ (1 GB); the A+ has 512 MB, no camera. Slower updates." (2) docs/adding-a-pi.md:93 Camera cell -> "B / B+: Yes. A+: No (512 MB is not enough for the camera bridge, same as the Zero 2 W)". (3) tools/flasher/README.md:60 "What to expect" -> "1080p video, remote access. Camera on the B / B+; the A+ (512 MB) has no camera. Slower updates." Leave camera=True / ram_mb=512 in the tuple alone: nothing in flasher.py reads them and changing camera would break test_pimodel.py:67. Do not split the pimodel entry: it would break test_pimodel.py:62 and :67 and change the flasher.json key set for no functional gain.


**Verify on hardware.** On a Pi 3 A+: awk '/MemTotal/' /proc/meminfo (expect about 430000 kB); sudo bash deploy/install-player.sh --with-wyze --upgrade 2>&1 | grep 'Camera bridge' (expect "Camera bridge: not supported on Raspberry Pi 3 Model A Plus ..."); then in the console the device page shows "Camera is not supported on this Pi model."


### M37. "Which Pi?" says the Zero and Pi 1 get no tunnel; the installer installs cloudflared on them

- Severity: **Medium**. Area: Docs versus code. Where: `docs/adding-a-pi.md:102`.
- Existing test covers it: **partial**. player/tests/test_tunnel.py::test_installer_cloudflared_block[armv6] and [armv6-offline] (lines 154-201) prove the armv6l branch downloads cloudflared-linux-arm, and test_unit_runs_cloudflared_with_the_token_file_and_tolerates_its_absence (line 133) asserts the unit is copied and enabled unconditionally. No test reads docs/adding-a-pi.md or docs/hardware.md. A small guard would be a test in player/tests/test_tunnel.py that reads docs/adding-a-pi.md and asserts neither "Remote access" nor "no tunnel on the Zero" appears, and that the model table has no fourth Yes/No column (or that its "Camera live view" values equal the Camera column row by row). None of the existing 277 player tests or 97 flasher tests would need changing for the proposed doc edit.
- Also reported as: Docs say Zero/Pi 1 get no remote access while the installer installs the raw ARMv6 cloudflared.

**What is wrong.** adding-a-pi.md:97-98 mark the Zero/Zero W and Pi 1 rows "Remote access: No" and line 102-104 explain "the Zero and Pi 1 do not get it because the tunnel program's package does not run on their chip"; line 123-124 and hardware.md:101-102 repeat "no tunnel on the Zero and Pi 1". install-player.sh:307-317 does the opposite: on armv6l it downloads the raw GOARM=5 cloudflared-linux-arm binary to /usr/bin/cloudflared, and projector-cloudflared.service is installed and enabled on every board (lines 378, 403). The column is also mislabelled: the tunnel only forwards <id>-cam.photogen5000.com to http://127.0.0.1:5000 (projector-cloudflared.service:18-20), the Wyze bridge web player, so it is the camera live view, not remote access to the Pi, and it is meaningless on the rows marked "Camera: No" (Zero 2 W, Pi 2 V1.1) where nothing listens on 5000.


**How to reproduce.** cd "<checkout>" && sed -n 97,104p docs/adding-a-pi.md && sed -n 123,124p docs/adding-a-pi.md && sed -n 101,102p docs/hardware.md && sed -n 304,317p player/deploy/install-player.sh && sed -n 378p player/deploy/install-player.sh && sed -n 403p player/deploy/install-player.sh && sed -n 17p cloud/src/cloudflare.js && sed -n 285p cloud/src/api.js. Harness: cd player && python -m pytest -q tests/test_tunnel.py -k armv6 (asserts the raw cloudflared-linux-arm download on a Zero W model string).


**Fix.** Simpler than the finder's rename: drop the column, because a "Camera live view" column would be identical to the Camera column row for row. Edit docs/adding-a-pi.md: line 89 header becomes "| Pi model (as in the flasher) | Plays | Camera | Wi-Fi |", line 90 loses one "---", rows 91-98 lose the fourth cell. Replace lines 102-104 ("Remote access" is the console's tunnel ... does not run on their chip.) with: "The console's live camera view (its tunnel to the Pi) works on every row that has a camera; the tunnel program itself is installed on every board (a special build on the Zero and Pi 1)." Replace lines 123-124 "(no camera bridge, hardware video decoding on, no tunnel on the Zero and Pi 1)" with "(no camera bridge, hardware video decoding on)". In docs/hardware.md:100-102 delete ", and no remote access on the Zero and Pi 1" so the sentence ends at "32-bit chip." Leave README.md:143 alone (that "Remote access" is the Tailscale/CMS section, a different thing). No code or test changes.


**Verify on hardware.** On a Pi Zero W after first boot: cloudflared --version (expect a version, not "command not found"); systemctl status projector-cloudflared.service (expect enabled, with "Condition check resulted in ... being skipped" until /var/lib/projector-player/tunnel.token exists). Once enrolled: ls -l /var/lib/projector-player/tunnel.token; journalctl -u projector-cloudflared -n 50 (expect a registered tunnel); curl -sI http://127.0.0.1:5000 (expect connection refused, nothing behind the tunnel on a no-camera board).


### M38. Section C says the flasher sets the Pi time zone to the console's; it uses the Windows PC's zone

- Severity: **Medium**. Area: Docs versus code. Where: `docs/automation.md:300`.
- Existing test covers it: **no**. tools/flasher/tests/test_firstboot.py:54 only checks that cfg["timezone"] reaches set_timezone; test_console.py:147-153 and test_flasher.py ENROLLMENT (line 32, which even carries timezone "UTC") never assert what happens to the console's timezone, and no test reads the doc.
