A cheap guard: in test_flasher.py, build card_cfg from FORM with timezone "Europe/Berlin" and assert the flashed cfg keeps it after fetch_key against a stub returning timezone "UTC", pinning the documented "Windows/Advanced wins" behaviour.

**What is wrong.** "the flasher sets the Pi timezone to the console timezone (timedatectl set-timezone in firstrun.sh), so keep the two in step". The flasher fills the time zone from the flashing PC (flasher.py:645 winlocale.timezone(), tools/flasher/README.md:92-95 "taken from Windows"), fetch_key (flasher.py:1219-1223) reads only enrollment_key and wyze_configured from /api/operator/enrollment and ignores the timezone the console returns (console.py:92), and firstrun.sh writes that Windows zone (firstboot.py:305-309, imager_custom set_timezone with timedatectl only as fallback). A card flashed by a laptop in another zone, or by an operator travelling, evaluates auto_update_window on the wrong clock; the doc tells the owner the console setting keeps them in step when nothing does.


**How to reproduce.** cd "<checkout>" && python -c "import sys; sys.path.insert(0,'tools/flasher'); import firstboot; c=firstboot.sample_config(); c['timezone']='Europe/Berlin'; print([l for l in firstboot.render_firstrun(c).splitlines() if 'timezone' in l])" prints set_timezone Europe/Berlin / timedatectl set-timezone Europe/Berlin (zone comes only from cfg). grep -n "timezone" tools/flasher/flasher.py shows the only sources are winlocale.timezone() (line 645) and the form value (line 1197); grep -n "r\[" in fetch_key (lines 1219-1223) shows only enrollment_key and wyze_configured are consumed. grep -rn timezone player/player shows no code sets the Pi zone; updater.py:182 uses datetime.now().astimezone().


**Fix.** Doc-only change in docs/automation.md:300-302: replace "the flasher sets the Pi timezone to the console timezone (`timedatectl set-timezone` in `firstrun.sh`), so keep the two in step, or change it on the Pi with `sudo timedatectl set-timezone`" with "the flasher writes the time zone of the PC that flashed the card (Advanced in the flasher lets you change it before flashing), so check it matches the site's time zone on the console's Settings page, or fix it on the Pi with `sudo timedatectl set-timezone <Area/City>` (`timedatectl` shows the current one)". Do not change fetch_key: a console-supplied zone would override an explicit Advanced choice and still never applies to cards flashed with a device token or baked key.


**Verify on hardware.** On the Pi: timedatectl | grep "Time zone" and compare with the console's Settings time zone; then journalctl -u projector-player -n 200 | grep -i "auto update" to see the local hour the window fired against the console's configured auto_update_window.


### M39. "never blindly" is false: auto mode sends the wanted code once after every player restart, which flips a toggle-remote projector

- Severity: **Medium**. Area: Docs versus code. Where: `docs/automation.md:599`.
- Existing test covers it: **partial**. player/tests/test_projector.py::test_auto_mode_applies_want_on_change_only (lines 229-236) asserts that a fresh Projector() sends on the first maybe_apply, i.e. the restart resend is intended and pinned. No test covers a toggle remote (same packet for power_on and power_off) or a daemon restart, and no test can cover a doc sentence. A test that would document the behaviour: in test_projector.py, build p = Projector(); p.set_block(block(mode="auto", want="on")); assert p.maybe_apply() is True and rm.sent == [PACKET], then p2 = Projector() (simulated restart) with the same block; assert p2.maybe_apply() is True and len(rm.sent) == 2, named test_auto_mode_resends_want_after_restart. That test passes against the current code and fails if someone later persists applied_want without thinking about the power-cut case. The doc fix changes no test.

**What is wrong.** Learning flow step 3 tells the owner that with a single toggle button "learn the same button as both power_on and power_off; auto mode still behaves, because the player only sends a code when the wanted state changes, never blindly", naming only a half-failed send as the weak spot. Projector.applied_want (projector.py:105) is plain memory, None at every daemon start, and maybe_apply (projector.py:155-161) sends whenever want != applied_want, so the first auto-mode manifest after any restart (reboot command, crash restart, install-player.sh --upgrade, update-player including the nightly auto-update at 03:00-05:00) sends the code regardless of the projector's real state. With a toggle code that switches an off projector on at 3 am and then inverts every scheduled transition for the day; the console lamp shows the sent state, so nothing on the Devices page reveals it. test_projector.py:229-233 asserts this first send, so the behaviour is intended, only the doc denies it.


**How to reproduce.** Code trace: projector.py:105 applied_want=None on construction; daemon.py:126/419 constructs it once per process; daemon.py:376 set_block(manifest["projector"]) then daemon.py:387 maybe_apply(); projector.py:155 `want == self.applied_want` is False on the first cycle so projector.py:161 power(want) sends. Harness: cd "<checkout>/player" && python -m pytest -q tests/test_projector.py -k applies_want_on_change_only (passes, asserting the first-cycle send on a fresh instance; a second Projector() with the same block also returns True, which is the restart case).


**Fix.** Doc only, no code change. Replace lines 596-602 of docs/automation.md (from "Some remotes have separate on" through "the safer choice.)") with: "Some remotes have separate on and off buttons, some a single toggle. Prefer the discrete on and off buttons where the remote has them: the player sends the wanted state once every time it starts (after a reboot, a software update or the nightly auto-update) and then only when the wanted state changes, so with discrete codes a projector that was switched off at the wall comes back on its own. With a single toggle button learned as both power_on and power_off, that start-up send, or a send that half-fails and is retried, can switch the projector the wrong way, and the console lamp will not show it because it reports what was sent, not what the projector is doing. Use a toggle button in auto mode only if you accept that the projector may be wrong until the next scheduled change or until you press On or Off on the console." Do NOT persist applied_want: a Pi that reboots after a power cut has a projector that is also off, so resending the wanted state on start is the correct recovery for discrete codes, and a persisted value would leave that projector off until the next scheduled change. No existing test changes; optionally add test_auto_mode_resends_want_after_restart to player/tests/test_projector.py (two fresh Projector() instances with the same auto/want=on block both send) to pin the intended behaviour.


**Verify on hardware.** On a Pi in auto mode with one toggle code learned as both power_on and power_off, projector on and console want=on: sudo systemctl restart projector-player.service; journalctl -u projector-player.service -n 50 | grep 'projector auto' shows 'projector auto: unknown -> on' within one sync and the projector turns off while the Devices page still shows On.

```text
Replace lines 174-180 with:

2. **Flash the card.** On Windows run the Projection5000 SD Flasher

   grey under it), pick the Pi model (get this right; a card made for the

```

(about 10 minutes)" string, test_firstboot.py:469 asserts the "Ready. Waiting for the first video." final screen, test_screens.py:94 asserts the NOT ASSIGNED headline, test_daemon.py:329-337 asserts the pairing screen for an unassigned device. A doc test is not worth adding; the fix changes no code, so no existing test needs changing.

The real first boot draws "Setting up this projector / Step 1..4 of 4" on the HDMI console (firstboot.py:259,430; install-player.sh:174,419), install-player.sh:174 itself says "about 10 minutes", firstboot.py:488 ends with "Ready. Waiting for the first video.", and once the daemon runs with no playlist it shows the NOT ASSIGNED status screen (daemon.py:161-167,249; screens.py:58,66), never black. adding-a-pi.md:72-77 already describes this correctly, so the two docs contradict each other; an owner reading hardware.md will think a Pi still on "Step 3 of 4" at minute six is stuck and will not recognise NOT ASSIGNED as normal.

### M42. No test that install-player.sh recovers from an interrupted dpkg (power cut during step 3), and the script has no dpkg --configure -a

- Severity: **Medium**. Area: Test gaps. Where: `player/deploy/install-player.sh:176`.
- Existing test covers it: **no**. No test runs the apt block of install-player.sh (test_installer_pi.py slices stop at line 51 / start at line 135) and test_updater.py:329 only asserts the update-os happy-path apt-get order [update, upgrade, autoremove]; test_updater.py:351 checks the "apt failed at line" message but not recovery. The test that would cover it is test_installer_repairs_an_interrupted_dpkg_before_apt in player/tests/test_installer_pi.py (corrected version in corrected_fix). If update-os.sh also gains an `apt-get -f install -y` line, test_updater.py:329's exact list must be extended; adding only `dpkg --configure -a || true` there leaves that test untouched (dpkg is not shimmed in test_updater, and `|| true` swallows the missing command).

**What is wrong.** The installer harness only runs excerpts (pi_caps, the wyze gate, the mpv.conf block, the cloudflared block, the uninstall getty block, the config block). apt-get is never exercised, and there is no test (and no code, grep finds no `dpkg --configure`/`apt-get -f`) for the case where the first-boot install is interrupted while apt is unpacking: after that, `apt-get install -y ...` (install-player.sh:180) fails every time with 'dpkg was interrupted, you must manually run dpkg --configure -a'. provision.sh retries the identical command 20 times and then shows 'Setup did not finish. The player did not install.' to an owner with no SSH. The same applies to update-player.sh --upgrade after an interrupted nightly update-os. This hides a High installer finding (common path: plug pulled during the ~10 minute step 3).


**How to reproduce.** cd <checkout>; slice install-player.sh from 'echo "==> Installing system dependencies"' to 'echo "==> Creating user', prepend `set -euo pipefail; UPGRADE=1; dpkg(){ echo "dpkg $*">>"$LOG"; [[ "$*" == "--configure -a" ]] && touch "$MARK"; }; apt-get(){ echo "apt-get $*">>"$LOG"; [[ "$1" == install && ! -e "$MARK" ]] && { echo "E: dpkg was interrupted" >&2; return 100; }; }` and run with bash: rc=100, log shows only 'apt-get update' then 'apt-get install -y ...', no dpkg --configure -a. provision.sh (rendered) lines 80-105 retry the identical command 20x then print "Setup did not finish. The player did not install."


**Fix.**

```text
1) player/deploy/install-player.sh, insert before line 176 (`apt-get update`):
    # A power cut mid-apt leaves dpkg half-configured and every later apt-get fails with
    # "dpkg was interrupted"; repair first so provision.sh's retry loop can succeed.
    dpkg --configure -a || true
    apt-get -f install -y
2) player/deploy/update-os.sh, insert before line 64 (`apt-get update`): `dpkg --configure -a || true` (the ERR trap at line 61 makes `|| true` mandatory; do not add `apt-get -f install -y` here unless test_updater.py:329's expected list is extended, since that test asserts the exact apt-get call sequence).
3) New test in player/tests/test_installer_pi.py (verified in a scratch copy: fails on current script with rc 100, passes with the fix):
def test_installer_repairs_an_interrupted_dpkg_before_apt(tmp_path):
    block = SRC[SRC.index('echo "==> Installing system dependencies"'):SRC.index('echo "==> Creating user')]
    log = tmp_path / "calls.log"
    prelude = f'set -euo pipefail; UPGRADE=1; LOG="{_posix(log)}"; touch "$LOG"\n'
    prelude += 'dpkg() { echo "dpkg $*" >> "$LOG"; [[ "$*" == "--configure -a" ]] && touch "$LOG.repaired"; }\n'
    prelude += ('apt-get() { echo "apt-get $*" >> "$LOG"; if [[ "$1" == install && ! -e "$LOG.repaired" ]]; then '
                'echo "E: dpkg was interrupted, you must manually run dpkg --configure -a" >&2; return 100; fi; }\n')
    run_bash(prelude + block)
    assert log.read_text().splitlines() == [
        "dpkg --configure -a", "apt-get -f install -y", "apt-get update",
        "apt-get install -y git rsync mpv ffmpeg python3 python3-venv python3-pip libgl1 libegl1 v4l-utils"]
UPGRADE=1 keeps screen_init/screen out of the slice, so no tty stubs are needed. No existing test in player/tests or tools/flasher/tests breaks with change 1; change 2 as stated leaves test_updater.py green.
```


**Verify on hardware.** On a Pi mid-provision: `sudo journalctl -u projection5000-provision -f` while pulling power during "Step 3 of 4"; after reboot: `sudo dpkg --audit; ls /var/lib/dpkg/updates/; sudo apt-get install -y mpv` (expect "E: dpkg was interrupted, you must manually run 'dpkg --configure -a'"); `tail -40 /var/log/projection5000-provision.log` (expect 20 identical failures then GAVE UP); repair with `sudo dpkg --configure -a && sudo systemctl start projection5000-provision.service`.


### M43. No test for a power cut mid update-player.sh: INSTALL_DIR gone, .prev left, nothing restores it at boot

- Severity: **Medium**. Area: Test gaps. Where: `player/tests/test_updater.py:267`.
- Existing test covers it: **no**. player/tests/test_updater.py::test_update_player_script_end_to_end_in_a_fake_root (line 312-320) covers an installer that exits non-zero, which fail() restores in-process; no test in player/tests or tools/flasher/tests kills the script or checks boot-time repair. The scratch test in reproduce (kill -9 $PPID stub, then run the repair command, then a second good update to prove .prev is kept when RELEASE exists) is the test that would cover it; it passes against the proposed fix and needs no change to existing tests.

**What is wrong.** No test for a process that dies between `mv INSTALL_DIR PREV_DIR` (update-player.sh:153) and the end of install-player.sh --upgrade (RELEASE written at install-player.sh:239). Reproduced in the fake root with an installer stub that SIGKILLs update-player.sh: /opt/piplayer/player missing, player.prev present, no update-status.json, no systemctl call. On the next boot projector-player.service's ExecStart (/opt/piplayer/player/.venv/bin/python) does not exist, so the daemon never comes up and the console sees the device offline. --postcheck would restore .prev (verified), but the timer has no [Install] (install-player.sh:393-401), is only armed by update-player.sh:162 after a successful install, the systemd-run transient unit does not survive a reboot, and postcheck.service's own ExecStart lives inside the missing /opt/piplayer/player/deploy, so even enabling the timer at boot would not help. The same applies to a SIGTERM (a reboot command issued during the update): no EXIT/TERM trap, only ERR.


**How to reproduce.** cd "<checkout>/player" && python -m pytest -q -s docs/audits/2026-09-20-pi-os/repro/test_powerloss_repro.py --rootdir . (reuses fake_pi/commit/INSTALLER_STUB; commit(fake_pi.repo, '0.2.1', installer=INSTALLER_STUB % 'kill -9 $PPID; sleep 1; exit 1'); fake_pi.run('update-player.sh','main') -> rc 2304, install exists False, prev exists True, status file False, shim log ''; then fake_pi.run('update-player.sh','--postcheck', FAKE_ACTIVE='failed', FAKE_RESTARTS='0') restores RELEASE == sha1). Code trace: update-player.sh:151-159 (mv then long installer, ERR trap only), install-player.sh:195-239 (deploy/ and RELEASE written last), install-player.sh:393-401 (timer without [Install]), projector-player.service:12 (ExecStart inside INSTALL_DIR).


**Fix.**

Code fix (must live outside /opt/piplayer/player, since that directory is what is missing): add one root-privileged pre-start line to player/deploy/projector-player.service, before ExecStart at line 11:

ExecStartPre=+/bin/sh -c 'if [ -d /opt/piplayer/player.prev ] && [ ! -f /opt/piplayer/player/RELEASE ]; then rm -rf /opt/piplayer/player && mv /opt/piplayer/player.prev /opt/piplayer/player; fi'

The `+` prefix runs it as root and outside the User=projector / ProtectSystem=full sandbox; the RELEASE test makes it a no-op after a completed upgrade (where .prev is deliberately kept for the post-check) and only fires when the install never finished. Restart=always/RestartSec=5 means the repair runs within 5 s of the first failed start, not only at boot. Do NOT implement it as a postcheck-timer [Install] section or as `update-player.sh --repair`: both entry points sit in the missing directory (install-player.sh:391). Optional: also write the same repair into update-player.sh's --postcheck before the is-active check so a crash-looping unit after a partial install is reported to the console as a rollback.

Test fix in player/tests/test_updater.py (reuse fake_pi, commit, INSTALLER_STUB, posix; verified passing): 
```
@needs_tools
def test_power_loss_mid_update_is_repaired_on_next_boot(fake_pi):
    release = fake_pi.install / "RELEASE"
    prev = fake_pi.install.with_name("player.prev")
    sha1 = release.read_text().strip()
    commit(fake_pi.repo, "0.2.1", installer=INSTALLER_STUB % "kill -9 $PPID; sleep 1; exit 1")
    fake_pi.run("update-player.sh", "main")
    assert not fake_pi.install.exists() and prev.exists()       # what a power cut leaves behind
    # the repair is the unit's own ExecStartPre line, run against the fake root
    unit = (DEPLOY / "projector-player.service").read_text()
    line = next(l for l in unit.splitlines() if l.startswith("ExecStartPre=+/bin/sh -c "))
    cmd = line.split(" -c ", 1)[1].strip("'").replace("/opt/piplayer", posix(fake_pi.root) + "/opt/piplayer")
    subprocess.run(["bash", "-c", cmd], check=True)
    assert release.read_text().strip() == sha1 and not prev.exists()
    # after a completed update the same line leaves .prev alone for the post-check
    (fake_pi.repo / "player" / "deploy" / "install-player.sh").write_text(INSTALLER_STUB % "")
    sha2 = commit(fake_pi.repo, "0.2.2")
    assert fake_pi.run("update-player.sh", "main").returncode == 0
    subprocess.run(["bash", "-c", cmd], check=True)
    assert release.read_text().strip() == sha2 and prev.exists()
```
Reading the line out of deploy/projector-player.service (not install-player.sh, which only copies that file at line 377) makes the test fail if the wiring is dropped. No existing test needs changing; the 277 player tests and 97 flasher tests are unaffected.


**Verify on hardware.** On a Pi with an update in flight (sudo /opt/piplayer/player/deploy/update-player.sh main, then during the pip step): sudo systemctl kill -s KILL projector-player-update.service; ls -la /opt/piplayer (expect player.prev present and player/ missing or without .venv/RELEASE); sudo reboot; after boot: systemctl status projector-player.service (expect 203/EXEC and start-limit-hit); journalctl -u projector-player -b -n 50; systemctl list-timers --all | grep postcheck (expect no scheduled run); ls -la /opt/piplayer; tail -50 /var/lib/projector-player/update.log; cat /var/lib/projector-player/update-status.json (expect stale or missing). Recovery by hand: sudo rm -rf /opt/piplayer/player && sudo mv /opt/piplayer/player.prev /opt/piplayer/player && sudo systemctl reset-failed projector-player.service && sudo systemctl restart projector-player.service.


### M44. Provision hint 'check the Wi-Fi name and password' is asserted unconditionally; no test for the Ethernet-only variant

- Severity: **Medium**. Area: Test gaps. Where: `tools/flasher/firstboot.py:471`.
- Existing test covers it: **no**. test_setup_screen_in_firstrun_and_provision (tools/flasher/tests/test_firstboot.py:74-88) pins the Wi-Fi wording for the default config only. test_firstrun_ethernet_only_and_hidden_and_no_ssh (line 115) and the static-network tests (lines 161-166, 362) render firstrun.sh for the wired card but never provision.sh. No test in player/tests touches render_provision. The proposed variant assertion in test_setup_screen_in_firstrun_and_provision (or a new test_provision_hint_matches_the_network_kind) would catch it and requires no change to any existing test, since the Wi-Fi rendering keeps the current string.

**What is wrong.** test_setup_screen_in_firstrun_and_provision asserts the string 'check the Wi-Fi name and password' is in render_provision(cfg()) but no test renders provision.sh for an Ethernet-only card (cfg(ethernet_only=True, ssid='')). render_provision (firstboot.py:471) does not look at ethernet_only, so a wired Pi that cannot reach the console after 8 waits shows an owner-facing hint about Wi-Fi settings the card never had. This is a misleading-text finding for the plain-English area that a variant test would have caught.


**How to reproduce.** cd "<checkout>" && python -c "import sys; sys.path.insert(0,'tools/flasher'); import firstboot; c=firstboot.sample_config(); c.update(ethernet_only=True, ssid=''); p=firstboot.render_provision(c); print('check the Wi-Fi name and password' in p, 'cable' in p.lower(), p==firstboot.render_provision(firstboot.sample_config()))"  -> prints True False True (wired provision.sh carries the Wi-Fi hint and is identical to the Wi-Fi one).


**Fix.** Code fix (tools/flasher/firstboot.py, at line 471): replace the literal with a per-network hint, e.g. above the list build: hint = "check the network cable is plugged in" if c["ethernet_only"] else "check the Wi-Fi name and password"; and at line 471: f'    "This is taking longer than usual: {hint}."'. Test fix (tools/flasher/tests/test_firstboot.py, extend test_setup_screen_in_firstrun_and_provision after line 92): e = firstboot.render_provision(cfg(ethernet_only=True, ssid="")); assert "This is taking longer than usual" in p and "This is taking longer than usual" in e; assert "Wi-Fi" not in e and "network cable" in e. No existing test needs changing: the default (Wi-Fi) render keeps the exact string pinned at line 88, and none of the _provision_harness tests (lines 452, 478, 486, 500) assert on the hint. Driving it through _provision_harness is not worth it: the harness curl stub answers /api/health immediately, so exercising the 8-wait path would need a new stub mode for one string check the render assertion already covers.


**Verify on hardware.** Flash an Ethernet-only card, boot with the cable unplugged, wait about 2 minutes (8 x 15 s after NTP wait): the HDMI screen shows "This is taking longer than usual: check the Wi-Fi name and password." Afterwards on the Pi: grep -c "waiting for console" /var/log/projection5000-provision.log and grep -n "Wi-Fi" /usr/local/sbin/projection5000-provision.sh


### M45. No cross-check between the flasher's per-model camera promise and the Pi's camera rule; Pi 3 (512 MB A+) mismatches today

- Severity: **Medium**. Area: Test gaps. Where: `tools/flasher/tests/test_pimodel.py:67`.
- Existing test covers it: **no**. Each side alone: tools/flasher/tests/test_pimodel.py::test_table_order_and_arch (line 67) pins which models the flasher marks camera=True; player/tests/test_pi_info.py::test_camera_rule_matches_the_installer pins the 900000 kB rule and player/tests/test_installer_pi.py exercises pi_caps in the installer. No test evaluates the Pi rule at each pimodel row's ram_mb, and no test checks the hint text agrees with the camera flag. Covering test: a new test_model_camera_matches_the_pi_rule in tools/flasher/tests/test_pimodel.py (verified in a scratch copy: fails today on pi3, passes with the fix).

**What is wrong.** Root cause is tools/flasher/pimodel.py:19 (pi3 row: camera=True with ram_mb=512), not only the missing test; the test at test_pimodel.py:67 pins the wrong value.


**How to reproduce.** python -c with tools/flasher and player on sys.path: for m in pimodel.MODELS: pi_info.camera_supported('aarch64' if m.arch=='arm64' else 'armv7l', m.arch=='arm64', m.ram_mb*1024) -> pi3: flasher camera=True, pi rule at 512 MB = False (MISMATCH); all other seven rows agree.


**Fix.**

```text
Two edits, no existing test changes. (1) tools/flasher/pimodel.py:18-19: keep one pi3 row but make it honest for the A+: Model("pi3", "Raspberry Pi 3 (B, B+, A+)", "arm64", "1080p video, remote access. Camera on the B / B+ (the 512 MB A+ cannot run the camera bridge). Slower updates.", True, 1024), and amend the docstring at lines 4-5 to say ram_mb is the lowest variant that matches the camera flag (the Pi 3 A+ is the noted exception). (2) Add to tools/flasher/tests/test_pimodel.py:

import re, sys
from pathlib import Path
REPO = Path(__file__).resolve().parents[3]
sys.path.insert(0, str(REPO / "player"))
from player import pi_info  # player/player/pi_info.py, same layout as player/tests/conftest.py

def test_model_camera_matches_the_pi_rule():
    for m in pimodel.MODELS:
        pi = pi_info.camera_supported("aarch64" if m.arch == "arm64" else "armv7l", m.arch == "arm64", m.ram_mb * 1024)
        assert pi == m.camera, f"{m.key}: flasher says camera={m.camera}, the Pi at {m.ram_mb} MB says {pi}"
        assert ("no camera" in m.hint.lower()) != m.camera or "cannot run the camera" in m.hint, m.key
    sh = (REPO / "player" / "deploy" / "install-player.sh").read_text()
    assert int(re.search(r"^CAMERA_MIN_MEM_KB=(\d+)", sh, re.M).group(1)) == pi_info.CAMERA_MIN_MEM_KB

Verified in a scratch copy: the test fails on the current tree (pi3) and passes with edit (1); test_table_order_and_arch line 67 ([pi5, pi4, pi3, pi2v12]), test_arm64_models_take_the_bundled_image and test_summary_and_dry_run_name_the_model all keep passing because key, label, arch and camera flag are unchanged. Splitting pi3 into B/B+ and A+ rows is a valid alternative but would require editing those three tests.
```


**Verify on hardware.** On a Pi 3 A+ flashed with the pi3 row: grep MemTotal /proc/meminfo (expect about 430000 kB); sudo bash /opt/projector-player/deploy/install-player.sh --upgrade 2>&1 | grep 'Camera bridge' (expect "not supported"); then check the console shows camera_supported=0 for that device despite the flasher hint promising a camera.



## Low

### L1. firstrun.sh carries the Pi user's plaintext password on the FAT partition; Imager only ships the hash

- Severity: **Low**. Area: First boot. Where: `tools/flasher/firstboot.py:271`.
- Existing test covers it: **partial**. test_firstrun_contents_and_quoting (tools/flasher/tests/test_firstboot.py:45) asserts the plaintext IS on the card, so it will fail with any fix and must flip to `assert "pa'ss $(rm -rf /)" not in s`. test_firstrun_wipes_itself_and_logs_rc:551 asserts 'userconf pi -> rc=0' and still passes (stub userconf exits 0 for any hash); test_wipe_zero_fills_before_unlink covers the wipe only. No test asserts the password is absent. Add: `s = render_firstrun(cfg(password='hunter2hunter2')); assert 'hunter2' not in s and "openssl passwd" not in s and "userconf projector-admin '*'" in s` (or, with the sha512-crypt variant, verify the rendered `$6$` hash against `openssl passwd -6 -salt <salt> <pw>` in Git Bash).

**What is wrong.** render_firstrun embeds the password in clear ('HASH=$(printf %s '<password>' | openssl passwd -6 -stdin)', rendered line 57) and hashes it on the Pi. Until finish() zero-fills firstrun.sh the plaintext sits on a partition any PC can read; after an interrupted first boot (see the cleanup finding) it stays there. Raspberry Pi Imager and userconf.txt put only the $6$ hash on the card. The Wi-Fi credential on the card is already the PBKDF2 PSK (the actual network key, same as Imager); the SSH line is public; the enrollment key / device token are only in projection5000-provision.sh, which is also wiped. firstrun.log never receives a secret: run() logs only $1 and $2, the hash is $3 of userconf, and no called tool echoes its arguments. Practical impact is small because the password is random, never shown and (see the sudo finding) not usable for anything.


**How to reproduce.** Read docs/audits/2026-09-20-pi-os/rendered/firstrun.sh line 57: HASH=$(printf %s 'correct horse battery' | openssl passwd -6 -stdin). test_firstrun_contents_and_quoting asserts shlex.quote(password) in s, i.e. the plaintext is expected on the card.


**Fix.** Minimal, no crypto on the PC: the password is never used (key-only SSH, NOPASSWD sudo, getty masked, never shown), so do not ship one at all. In render_firstrun replace lines 271-272 and the two uses of "$HASH" with a locked hash: `run /usr/lib/userconf-pi/userconf {user} '*'` and, in the fallback branch, `printf "%s:%s\n" {user} '*' | run chpasswd -e`. Use `*`, not `!` and not an empty string: OpenSSH refuses all logins (including pubkey) to an account whose shadow hash starts with `!`, and an empty hash from chpasswd -e means passwordless login. Then delete the `password` key from card_cfg/sample_config and validate_cfg line 161-162, and the secrets.token_urlsafe at flasher.py:502 and :1182 (dead once nothing consumes it); update test_validate params ('password', '', 'Pi password is required') and the cfg() fixture. If a usable password is ever wanted later, hash on the PC with a stdlib sha512-crypt (hashlib, 16-char salt, 5000 rounds) and render `userconf {user} '$6$...'`, never the clear text.


**Verify on hardware.** Not needed; settled by code. Optional on a Pi after an interrupted first boot: sudo grep -c "openssl passwd" /boot/firmware/firstrun.sh (1 means plaintext still on the card); after a completed boot: ls /boot/firmware/firstrun.sh should not exist and /boot/firmware/firstrun.ok should.


### L2. Cleanup wipes the FAT copies and edits cmdline.txt with no sync barrier: a power cut in that window strands the next boot

- Severity: **Low**. Area: First boot. Where: `tools/flasher/firstboot.py:332`.
- Existing test covers it: **no**. No existing test runs the rendered script twice or checks the sync/sed/wipe order. test_firstrun_wipes_itself_and_logs_rc (tools/flasher/tests/test_firstboot.py:547) runs it once and checks end state only; test_sed_cleanup_keeps_regdom (:196) checks only the sed. Add to test_render_firstrun an ordering assert: s.index('run systemctl enable projection5000-provision.service') < s.index('\nsync\n') < s.index(the sed line) < s.index('wipe "$BOOT/projection5000-provision.sh"') < s.index('finish'), and a bash harness test that after _run_firstrun re-creates the three FAT files plus the armed cmdline.txt and runs the script again, asserting 'rc=1' not in the second log and firstrun.sh/provision.sh gone (prototype: docs/audits/2026-09-20-pi-os/repro/test_fix_reorder.py::test_second_run_after_cut_before_wipes, passes with the reorder, fails on current code with two 'install -m -> rc=1').

**What is wrong.** Every step before the cleanup is idempotent, so a power cut anywhere up to line 332 just re-runs firstrun.sh next boot. The tail is not: lines 314-315 install provision.sh and the tarball to ext4 (no fsync; ext4 commits every 5 s), 332-333 zero-fill/unlink the FAT sources, 336 rewrites cmdline.txt, and the only sync is in finish (249) after firstrun.sh is zero-filled and unlinked (248). Because vfat has no journal and nothing orders the ext4 copies ahead of the FAT cleanup, a power cut inside that final sync can leave any subset on disk. Three stranding states, all needing a PC edit of cmdline.txt or a re-flash: (a) FAT sources gone, cmdline.txt still has systemd.run, ext4 copies not committed: firstrun.sh re-runs, both installs fail (rc=1), it still cleans cmdline and reboots, then projection5000-provision.service starts with no script and tty1 is masked, so the screen stays black. (b) firstrun.sh unlinked or, more likely, present but all zeros (its zero-fill lands in sync's data phase, the unlink and the cmdline rename are directory metadata written later) while cmdline.txt is unchanged: kernel-command-line.service fails with ENOENT/ENOEXEC; systemd-run-generator defaults FailureAction=exit, which on bare metal is a poweroff, so the Pi shuts down, and every power cycle repeats it. (c) FAT cleanup fully landed, ext4 journal not committed: no firstrun.sh, no provision unit, getty masked, black screen forever. All windows are the sub-second sync of first boot; Raspberry Pi Imager's own firstrun.sh has the same shape, hence Low.


**How to reproduce.** cd tools/flasher && SP=<scratch> python docs/audits/2026-09-20-pi-os/repro/rerun_demo.py: run 1 with the sed line dropped (stands in for "rename did not land") via test_firstboot._run_firstrun, then run 2 against the same boot dir logs "install -m -> rc=1" for projection5000-provision.sh and projection5000-player.tar.gz and "firstrun done ...: 3 step(s) failed". Code trace for the ordering: firstboot.py:314-315 (ext4 installs, no sync), :332-333 (FAT wipe/rm), :336 (sed -i rename), :248-249 (wipe firstrun.sh, first and only sync).


**Fix.** Reorder the tail of render_firstrun (firstboot.py:329-337) to: "run systemctl enable projection5000-provision.service", "sync", "# cleanup: disarm the next boot only once the ext4 copies are durable, then leave no secrets on the FAT partition", the existing sed on cmdline.txt, "sync", 'wipe "$BOOT/projection5000-provision.sh"', 'rm -f "$BOOT/{PLAYER_ARCHIVE}"', "finish" (finish unchanged: wipe firstrun.sh, sync). Two added sync lines and the sed moved up; no test changes needed. Drop the exec-redirect guard from this fix (read-only bootfs is a separate finding).


**Verify on hardware.** Cannot be forced deterministically without cutting power mid-sync. To inspect a stranded card on a PC: check /boot/firmware/cmdline.txt still contains systemd.run= and whether firstrun.sh is missing or zero bytes (ls -la /boot/firmware; wc -c /boot/firmware/firstrun.sh). On a Pi that booted: sudo journalctl -b -u kernel-command-line.service -u projection5000-provision.service -n 100; cat /boot/firmware/firstrun.log; ls -la /usr/local/sbin/projection5000-provision.sh /opt/projection5000-player.tar.gz.


### L3. firstrun.sh wipes itself even if the cmdline.txt edit failed, leaving a boot that never reaches the OS

- Severity: **Low**. Area: First boot. Where: `tools/flasher/firstboot.py:336`.
- Existing test covers it: **no**. tools/flasher/tests/test_firstboot.py::test_firstrun_wipes_itself_and_logs_rc and ::test_sed_cleanup_keeps_regdom only exercise the success path (sed works, cmdline asserted clean, firstrun.sh gone). No test stubs a failing sed. Add test_firstrun_keeps_itself_when_cmdline_edit_fails: boot, log = _run_firstrun(tmp_path, cfg(), [("sed", "exit 4\n")]); assert (boot / "firstrun.sh").exists(); assert "systemd.run=" in (boot / "cmdline.txt").read_text(); assert not (boot / "firstrun.ok").exists(); assert "keeping firstrun.sh" in log. _run_firstrun already accepts extra PATH stubs, and the hostname fallback sed is not reached because the fake imager is executable. Existing tests keep passing with the fix below: line 61's substring 'wipe "$BOOT/firstrun.sh"' still matches, line 67 endswith("finish") holds, and test_sed_cleanup_keeps_regdom's startswith("sed -i -E") filter is untouched because the sed line itself is not changed.

**What is wrong.** The sed that strips systemd.run=/run_success_action=/unit= from cmdline.txt (line 336) is not wrapped in run() and its status is never checked (set +e); finish() (lines 247-253) then wipes firstrun.sh unconditionally and exits 0, so systemd.run_success_action=reboot fires. If the sed fails but the wipe succeeds, the next boot's kernel-command-line.service ExecStart points at a file that no longer exists; the unit fails and systemd-run-generator's default failure action (exit, treated as poweroff by the system manager) powers the Pi off on every boot. The failed sed is also not counted in FAILS, so firstrun.ok can be written while cmdline.txt is still armed. The trigger (a FAT write error that breaks sed -i's temp file + rename but not the dd + rm in the same directory a moment later) is the same sed-then-rm ordering Raspberry Pi Imager's own firstrun.sh uses, so it is an edge case, not a common path.


**How to reproduce.** cd <checkout>/tools/flasher; run the scratch script that calls tests/test_firstboot.py::_run_firstrun(tmp, cfg(), [("sed", "exit 4")]): output shows firstrun.sh exists: False while cmdline.txt still reads "console=tty1 rootwait systemd.run=/boot/firmware/firstrun.sh systemd.run_success_action=reboot systemd.unit=kernel-command-line.target ...", and the log ends "firstrun done ...: 1 step(s) failed" (the one failure is the harness's set_keymap rc=3, not the sed). Code trace: firstboot.py:336 (bare sed, rc ignored) -> :337 finish -> :248 wipe firstrun.sh -> :252 exit 0 -> reboot -> next boot kernel-command-line.service ExecStart ENOENT -> FailureAction exit -> poweroff.


**Fix.**

```text
Guard the self-wipe on the cmdline actually being clean, in finish() (tools/flasher/firstboot.py:248), so every exit path is covered and the sed line (and its dedicated test) stay as they are. Replace line 248 with two lines:
'  if grep -q "systemd\\.run=" "$BOOT/cmdline.txt"; then echo "  cmdline.txt still has systemd.run=: keeping firstrun.sh so the next boot retries"; FAILS=$((FAILS + 1)); else wipe "$BOOT/firstrun.sh"; fi',
Rationale: firstrun.sh re-running is safe (set +e, idempotent imager/userconf/install steps) and far better than a boot that never reaches the OS; counting it in FAILS also keeps firstrun.ok from being written. Do not wrap the sed in run(): test_sed_cleanup_keeps_regdom selects the line by startswith("sed -i -E").
```


**Verify on hardware.** Not needed to confirm the code path. On a Pi that never comes up after first boot, mount the card on a PC: `cat /boot/firmware/cmdline.txt` still containing systemd.run= with no firstrun.sh present is this exact state; `cat /boot/firmware/firstrun.log` will show the sed error line just before "firstrun done". On a Pi that did come up: `grep -c systemd.run /boot/firmware/cmdline.txt` should print 0 and `journalctl -b -u kernel-command-line.service` should be empty.


### L4. Clock fallback runs once, before the network is confirmed, so a late network plus blocked NTP leaves TLS broken with the Wi-Fi hint

- Severity: **Low**. Area: Provisioning. Where: `tools/flasher/firstboot.py:467`.
- Existing test covers it: **no**. Not covered. tools/flasher/tests/test_firstboot.py::test_provision_contents_with_token (line 306) only asserts the strings "NTPSynchronized" and 'date -s "$D"' appear; _provision_harness (line 395) stubs timedatectl to 'yes' and its curl stub exits 0 for every /api/health call, so the fallback branch and a failing probe are never exercised. A covering test: a harness variant where the timedatectl stub prints 'no', the curl stub fails /api/health with exit 60 until a marker file exists and, when called with -D, prints "Date: Sat, 19 Sep 2026 10:00:00 GMT", and a `date` stub that records `-s` calls (creating the marker) and otherwise execs the real date; assert the log contains 'clock set from console' after at least one 'waiting for console' line, the script then reaches 'install succeeded', and rc == 0. With the current script that test hangs in the until loop (use the 120 s timeout as the failure signal, or cap WAITS in the test copy). The proposed fix passes all existing assertions: the 'date -s "$D"' and NTPSynchronized strings remain, and the health probe still precedes TRIES=0 (line 324).

**What is wrong.** The NTP wait (24 x 5 s) and the single `curl -k` Date fallback (lines 456-463) both happen before the health loop that actually waits for the network. On a first boot the clock is the image build date (weeks or months old, so a current Cloudflare certificate is 'not yet valid'). If the network only comes up after those ~2 minutes (router powering up after the Pi, slow venue DHCP) and UDP 123 is blocked so timesyncd never syncs, the fallback has already failed silently, every health probe fails with curl rc 60, and the owner is told to check the Wi-Fi password. Security-wise the -k fallback is no weaker than NTP itself (both unauthenticated) and `date -s "$D"` is properly quoted, so that part is acceptable; the defect is the ordering.


**How to reproduce.** Code trace: line 456-463 run once; line 467 `until curl -fsS ...` has no clock handling; timedatectl stays 'no' when NTP is blocked, so rc 60 repeats forever.


**Fix.**

```text
Keep the finder's idea but factor the fallback into a function and call it from the health loop, so both the pre-loop attempt and the per-probe retry share one body:

Replace lines 455-463 with:
  "# The image's clock is stale until NTP syncs; TLS and apt both need the real time. If NTP is blocked,",
  "# seed the clock from the console's Date header (no weaker than unauthenticated NTP).",
  "set_clock() {",
  '  [ "$(timedatectl show -p NTPSynchronized --value 2>/dev/null)" = yes ] && return 0',
  '  local D; D=$(curl -sSk --max-time 10 -o /dev/null -D - "$CONSOLE/api/health" | tr -d "\\r" | sed -n "s/^[Dd]ate: //p")',
  '  [ -n "$D" ] && date -s "$D" >/dev/null && echo "clock set from console: $D"',
  "}",
  "for _ in $(seq 1 6); do",
  '  [ "$(timedatectl show -p NTPSynchronized --value 2>/dev/null)" = yes ] && break',
  "  sleep 5",
  "done",
  "set_clock",

and in the health loop (line 467-473) add `  set_clock` as the first statement after `echo "waiting for console at $CONSOLE"`. The pre-loop wait shrinks from 2 min to 30 s because the loop now handles the slow case. Optional: only call set_clock in the loop when the probe failed with rc 60 (capture `curl ...; rc=$?` inside a while loop) to avoid an extra -k request every 15 s, but the extra request is harmless and the simpler form is preferable. No existing test in tools/flasher/tests/test_firstboot.py needs changing.
```


**Verify on hardware.** On a Pi with the flashed card, before first boot block outbound UDP 123 at the router and leave the Wi-Fi AP off. Power the Pi, wait 4 minutes, then enable the AP. Check: `sudo tail -f /var/log/projection5000-provision.log` should show "NTP synced: no" followed by repeating "waiting for console at ..." and, in journalctl -u projection5000-provision, curl (60) SSL certificate problem: certificate is not yet valid; `timedatectl` shows "System clock synchronized: no" with a date months old; the projector shows "check the Wi-Fi name and password". Then `sudo reboot`: with the AP already up the fallback runs and install proceeds, which confirms the ordering is the only problem.


### L5. --upgrade rewrites units, sudoers and mpv.conf.dist before the new code is proven; a rollback restores only /opt/piplayer/player

- Severity: **Low**. Area: Installer. Where: `player/deploy/update-player.sh:73`.
- Existing test covers it: **no**. player/tests/test_updater.py::test_update_player_script_end_to_end_in_a_fake_root exercises both rollback paths (fail() after a broken installer, postcheck with FAKE_ACTIVE=failed) but its INSTALLER_STUB never writes units or sudoers and the fake root has no etc/, so the drift is invisible. test_installer_pi.py only runs pi_caps/wyze excerpts of install-player.sh, never the unit block. A covering test: extend the fake_pi fixture to create root/etc/systemd/system/projector-player.service and root/etc/sudoers.d/projector-player with marker content "old", give INSTALLER_STUB a line that overwrites both with "new", run update-player.sh main, then --postcheck with FAKE_ACTIVE=failed FAKE_RESTARTS=5, and assert both files read "old" again, that .prev/.etc no longer exists, and that shim_log gained "systemctl daemon-reload" before the reset-failed/restart pair. Repeat once for the broken-installer branch. The existing exact shim_log assertions at lines 289-290 and 308-309 keep passing as long as daemon-reload is only issued when a snapshot existed.
- Also reported as: Rollback puts the old code back under the new checkout's systemd units, sudoers and mpv.conf.dist, which are never reverted.

**What is wrong.** Lines 276-298 and 376-403 write the sudoers drop-in, the four unit files and the postcheck units from the new checkout on every --upgrade. update-player.sh's fail() and postcheck() roll back by moving player.prev over the install dir only, so after a rollback the previous daemon runs under the new version's unit files and sudoers rules. Today the units are stable so this is harmless, but the first release that changes ExecStart, EnvironmentFile, DeviceAllow or a sudoers path turns the rollback into a second failure with no further fallback, and nothing in the script or the tests guards against it.


**How to reproduce.** Code trace (no Pi needed): update-player.sh:151-157 moves the running tree to .prev and runs the new checkout's install-player.sh --upgrade, which rewrites sudoers (install-player.sh:277-297) and units (376-401). A crash-looping daemon then triggers postcheck() (update-player.sh:84-104) -> restore_prev() (73-79), which moves only /opt/piplayer/player.prev back and restarts the service (103) under the new checkout's unit files and sudoers. Harness confirmation: cd player && python -m pytest -q tests/test_updater.py -k rollback; the INSTALLER_STUB at tests/test_updater.py:175-201 shows the /etc side is not modelled, so the asymmetry is invisible to the suite.


**Fix.**

The proposed fix is heavier than needed: shipping deploy/ into the install tree does not help because the sudoers drop-in and the postcheck units are heredocs inside install-player.sh (277-296, 385-401), not files, and re-running install-player.sh from the restored tree during a rollback means apt-get/pip/network in the one situation where the Pi is already unhealthy. Smaller and exact: snapshot what is live before the upgrade and put it back on rollback, all inside update-player.sh, no change to install-player.sh.

In update(), right after `mv "${INSTALL_DIR}" "${PREV_DIR}"; PREV_MADE=1` (line 153-154):
```
        mkdir -p "${PREV_DIR}/.etc"
        cp -a "${ROOT}"/etc/systemd/system/projector-*.service "${ROOT}"/etc/systemd/system/projector-*.timer \
              "${ROOT}/etc/sudoers.d/projector-player" "${PREV_DIR}/.etc/" 2>/dev/null || true
```
In restore_prev() after the mv (line 77):
```
        if [[ -d "${INSTALL_DIR}/.etc" ]] && compgen -G "${INSTALL_DIR}/.etc/*" >/dev/null; then
            log "restoring the unit files and sudoers drop-in that ran with ${INSTALL_DIR}"
            cp -a "${INSTALL_DIR}"/.etc/projector-*.service "${INSTALL_DIR}"/.etc/projector-*.timer "${ROOT}/etc/systemd/system/" 2>/dev/null || true
            [[ -f "${INSTALL_DIR}/.etc/projector-player" ]] && install -m 440 "${INSTALL_DIR}/.etc/projector-player" "${ROOT}/etc/sudoers.d/projector-player"
            systemctl daemon-reload 2>/dev/null || true
        fi
        rm -rf "${INSTALL_DIR}/.etc"
```
This restores exactly the files that were live (including the wyze unit only when it existed), works in the PIPLAYER_ROOT fake root because the etc paths are prefixed, and leaves the existing test assertions unchanged (the fake root has no etc/, so no daemon-reload line appears). Ceiling: a unit the new release added but the old one never had stays on disk, enabled but harmless; note it with a comment rather than reconciling.


**Verify on hardware.** Not required (settled by code trace). If wanted on a Pi after any update: `ls -l --time-style=full-iso /etc/systemd/system/projector-*.service /etc/systemd/system/projector-player-postcheck.timer /etc/sudoers.d/projector-player /opt/piplayer/player/RELEASE /opt/piplayer/player.prev/RELEASE` shows the /etc files carry the new update's mtime; after `sudo systemctl kill -s SIGKILL projector-player.service` repeated 4 times within 2 min of an update to force the postcheck rollback, `sudo tail -n 20 /var/lib/projector-player/update.log` shows "rolling back" while the /etc mtimes above are unchanged.


### L6. Status PNGs and the 8 MB nowplaying overlay are written to the SD card on every screen change

- Severity: **Low**. Area: systemd units and mpv.conf. Where: `player/player/status.py:22`.
- Existing test covers it: **no**. No test checks where screens are written or that the unit declares a runtime dir. player/tests/test_status.py:28 and :49 assert the files appear under cfg.manifest_path.parent/"screens", which pins the current SD location but is satisfied by the env fallback in the fix (RUNTIME_DIRECTORY is unset under pytest). A covering test: in test_status.py, monkeypatch.setenv("RUNTIME_DIRECTORY", str(tmp_path / "run")) and assert StatusScreens(cfg, ...).dir == tmp_path / "run" / "screens" and that show() writes there; plus a unit-file assertion in test_audit2_player.py (pattern at :326) that deploy/projector-player.service contains "RuntimeDirectory=projector-player".

**What is wrong.** StatusScreens.dir (status.py:22) is manifest_path.parent/screens = /var/lib/projector-player/screens on the SD card. During a download with nothing playing, daemon.py:291-303 redraws the syncing screen every 2 s with a new bytes_done, so show() (status.py:34-37) writes a fresh ~93 KB PNG each time; now_playing() (status.py:64) writes an 8.3 MB raw BGRA file on every playlist change. Both are throwaway frames for mpv and belong on tmpfs. Impact is minor: the concurrent media download writes far more to the same card, and playlist changes are rare. Screenshots already use tempfile.mkstemp in /tmp (screenshots.py:20).


**How to reproduce.** cd player && python -c "from pathlib import Path; from player.screens import *; s=ScreenState(kind='syncing',device_id='a',console_url='x',version='1',device_name='H',phase='download',progress_done=1,progress_total=3,current_file='a.mp4',bytes_done=1000,bytes_total=5000); print(render_png(s, Path('scratch/syncing.png')).stat().st_size); n=ScreenState(kind='nowplaying',device_id='a',console_url='x',version='1',playlist_name='S',item_count=3,source=None); print(render_overlay_bgra(n, Path('scratch/nowplaying.bgra'))[0].stat().st_size)"  -> 93466 and 8294400. Code trace: install-player.sh:263 manifest_path -> status.py:22 dir -> daemon.py:291-303 (2 s syncing redraw, bytes_done differs each call so status.py:34 never dedupes) -> screens.py:389-395 write; daemon.py:260 -> status.py:64 -> screens.py:398-408 write.


**Fix.** Two-line change. deploy/projector-player.service: add `RuntimeDirectory=projector-player` under [Service] (systemd creates /run/projector-player owned by projector, tmpfs, writable despite ProtectSystem=full, and exports $RUNTIME_DIRECTORY). player/player/status.py:22: `self.dir = Path(os.environ.get("RUNTIME_DIRECTORY") or cfg.manifest_path.parent) / "screens"` (add `import os`). Existing tests keep passing because the fallback is the current path. Optional: /var/lib/projector-player/screens left by old installs is harmless; no cleanup needed. Note RuntimeDirectory is wiped on service stop, which is fine: mpv has already decoded the PNG it shows, the overlay is removed after 8 s, and the daemon re-renders the boot screen on every start (daemon.py:426).


**Verify on hardware.** findmnt /tmp; findmnt /run; ls -la --time-style=full-iso /var/lib/projector-player/screens; then push a playlist with a large new video from the console and run: sudo apt-get install -y inotify-tools && inotifywait -m -e close_write --timefmt %T --format '%T %w%f' /var/lib/projector-player/screens (expect syncing.png every ~2 s during the download and an 8.3 MB nowplaying.bgra when playback starts); journalctl -u projector-player -n 200 | grep -i "status screen"


### L7. TimeoutStopSec=45 is shorter than ir-learn and CEC command paths, and the unit comment miscounts the longest blocking call

- Severity: **Low**. Area: Player daemon. Where: `player/deploy/projector-player.service:20`.
- Existing test covers it: **partial**. player/tests/test_audit2_player.py::test_network_read_timeouts_fit_inside_the_stop_grace_period (line 306) reads TimeoutStopSec from the unit but only asserts the manifest/media/screenshot HTTP read timeouts are below it; nothing checks the command paths. test_projector.py::test_learn_gives_up_after_the_window (125) and test_projector_commands_report_their_result (273) exercise the blocking paths but never against the unit. Add one test next to the existing grace-period test: stop_sec from the unit; report = (commands.REPORT_RETRIES+1)*10 + commands.REPORT_RETRIES*commands._RETRY_DELAY_SECONDS; assert projector.DISCOVER_TIMEOUT_SECONDS + projector.LEARN_TIMEOUT_SECONDS + report < stop_sec and 2*2*projector.CEC_TIMEOUT_SECONDS + report < stop_sec. Raising the value to 120 keeps the existing test green (it only asserts read < stop_sec); no test needs changing.

**What is wrong.** The unit says the longest blocking call is the command-result retries (3 x 10 s + 2 s), but execute_commands can block much longer without checking the stop flag: ir-learn does Broadlink hello/discover (5 s) + auth + a 30 s learn loop (projector.py 61-75) and then _report_result (up to 32 s); projector-on/off over CEC runs two cec-ctl calls of 15 s each, retried once (up to 60 s), then the report. A systemctl stop (update-player.sh restarts the service) during those ends in SIGKILL; for ir-learn the command is recorded only after the action (commands.py 190), so it is re-run on the next sync and the owner is asked to press the remote again.


**How to reproduce.** Code trace: daemon.py:378 is the last stop check before execute_commands; commands.py:134-187 loops over all manifest commands with no _stop check; commands.py:179 -> projector.py:59-75 (5 s discover + auth + 30 s sleep(1) loop, SIGTERM only sets the flag at daemon.py:46-49 and sleep resumes); commands.py:177 -> projector.py:113-131 -> projector.py:80-91 (2 x 15 s cec-ctl, repeated once); commands.py:97-118 report retries 32 s. cloud/src/manifest.js:137-139 returns every pending command, so several ir-learns run consecutively. Ledger entry for ir-learn/projector commands written only at commands.py:185, after the action.


**Fix.** Take only the first half of the proposed fix: set TimeoutStopSec=120 in player/deploy/projector-player.service:20 and rewrite the comment at 15-19 to name the real worst cases (ir-learn: 5 s RM4 discover + 30 s learn window + 32 s result report; projector-on/off over CEC: 2 x 2 x 15 s cec-ctl + 32 s report). Drop the second half: recording ir-learn before acting would be wrong, because a kill mid-learn would then make the daemon answer the re-delivered command with "already executed: ir-learn:power_on" and the owner's press would be lost for good with no code stored; re-running the learn (current behaviour) is the desired recovery. Plumbing should_stop into learn_code is not worth the diff (execute_commands has no stop parameter today) given the window only matters during an update restart or reboot.


**Verify on hardware.** On a Pi with an RM4 configured: queue ir-learn:power_on from the console, wait for the player log to show "executing command ... ir-learn", then run `sudo systemctl restart projector-player.service; journalctl -u projector-player -n 50` and look for "State 'stop-sigterm' timed out. Killing." followed by the same command id being executed again after the restart.


### L8. OFFLINE screen says 'last contact never' after any daemon restart

- Severity: **Low**. Area: Player daemon. Where: `player/player/daemon.py:151`.
- Existing test covers it: **no**. player/tests/test_daemon.py::test_no_cache_and_offline_shows_offline_screen only exercises the no-manifest path and asserts "no cached content", never the last-contact text; test_status.py:32 and test_screens.py:23 pass fixed strings ("never", "4 min ago") into the screen and do not touch daemon seeding. A covering test: seed_local(cfg, cms, ["a.mp4"]) then delete the media file, set cms.unreachable, build state as main() does (PlayerState(last_manifest=load_local_manifest(cfg)) plus the new seeding), run_cycle with screens, assert screens.current.kind == "offline" and "never" not in screen_texts(screens) and the formatted mtime is present. A second assertion after one successful cycle: cfg.manifest_path.stat().st_mtime advanced even though the hash did not change. Neither existing test needs changing; test_status.py:32 still passes because "never" remains the no-manifest text.

**What is wrong.** state.last_contact lives only in PlayerState (monotonic, None at start) and is set solely at daemon.py:316 after a successful sync, so after a daemon restart the OFFLINE screen reads "last contact never". The screen is only shown when nothing is playable: with no cached manifest (daemon.py:203, where "never" is accurate) or when the cached manifest has items but none are on disk (daemon.py:249 -> 171 -> 151). The latter is reachable because sync.py:444-445 saves the manifest even when every media download failed, so a device that reached the console but got no media, then restarted while offline, tells the owner it was never in contact.


**How to reproduce.** Code trace: daemon.py:122 (default None), 316 (only assignment), 419-422 (PlayerState rebuilt without it), 249 -> 171 -> 151 -> 129-131 ("never"), screens.py:185 (text). Precondition that makes it reachable: sync.py:435-445 saves the manifest even when all downloads failed, leaving items but no files on disk. Harness: none needed; run cd player && python -m pytest -q tests/test_status.py -k offline to see the existing screen tests only cover a literal string.


**Fix.** Keep monotonic for the in-process age; add a wall-clock fallback rendered as a timestamp, not an age, so a stale pre-NTP clock cannot produce "0 s ago" or a negative number. 1) daemon.py:316, after state.last_contact = time.monotonic(): touch the cache so its mtime means last contact, not last playlist change: try: os.utime(cfg.manifest_path) except OSError: pass (add import os). 2) PlayerState: add last_contact_wall: float | None = None. 3) main() daemon.py:419-422: if state.last_manifest: state.last_contact_wall = cfg.manifest_path.stat().st_mtime (inside try/except OSError). 4) _age_text(state) instead of _age_text(since): if state.last_contact is not None keep the monotonic branches; elif state.last_contact_wall: return time.strftime("%d %b %H:%M", time.localtime(state.last_contact_wall)) + " (before the player last restarted)"; else "never". Update the single caller at :151. Skipped: a separate marker file (the manifest already exists whenever a sync succeeded) and wall-clock ages (unsafe without an RTC).


**Verify on hardware.** On a Pi that has synced but has no media on disk (e.g. rm /var/lib/piplayer/media/* leaving manifest.json), block the console (sudo iptables -A OUTPUT -d <console-ip> -j DROP), then sudo systemctl restart projector-player and read the HDMI screen / journalctl -u projector-player -n 50: the OFFLINE screen shows "last contact never" although manifest.json's mtime is recent (stat /var/lib/piplayer/manifest.json).


### L9. Several failures never reach the console: cloudflared down, mpv restart loop, screen render failures

- Severity: **Low**. Area: Player daemon. Where: `player/player/daemon.py:276`.
- Existing test covers it: **partial**. player/tests/test_daemon.py::test_mpv_down_then_back (line 148-158) covers the mpv-down status and the pid change after mpv.restart(); nothing asserts a restart count reaches the console, nothing covers cloudflared state, and status.py's render-failure branch is only exercised indirectly. Add to test_daemon.py: seed_local, run_cycle, then mpv.restart() + run_cycle three times and assert "the player restarted 3 times in the last hour" in cms.sync_calls[-1]["sync_error"]; a fourth run_cycle without restart must keep the note (window), and with time.monotonic patched forward 3601 s it must drop. The existing sync_error assertions (test_daemon.py:220-228, test_sync.py:70-77) keep passing because they never restart mpv.

**What is wrong.** The status assembled in run_cycle covers mpv-down, camera_error, projector_error and update_status only. A dead or crash-looping cloudflared (token revoked, binary missing: tunnel.py 60 logs once) leaves the owner with a broken live view and no hint; mpv crash-looping (Restart=always every 5 s) is usually sampled alive so player_status flips between idle and mpv-down with no restart count; a screen that cannot be rendered (status.py 38-42) is only a journal warning. The wyze bridge dying does surface, as an ffmpeg error string in camera_error.


**How to reproduce.** Code trace: daemon.py:276-285 (status keys), :57-58 (mpv-down only when dead at sample), :189-196 (restart detected, log only); tunnel.py:40-63 (log only, no status), deploy/projector-cloudflared.service:24-25 and projector-mpv.service:17-18 (systemd restarts hide the loop from a 30 s poll); status.py:36-42 (render failure returns False after a warning). Console: alerts.js:17 has no tunnel kind; cloudflare.js has no cfd_tunnel status read.


**Fix.** Pi side, daemon.py only: add `restarts: list[float] = field(default_factory=list)` to PlayerState (after line 114); in _reconcile_mpv where the pid change is detected (line 194, the "mpv restarted" branch, not the first-connect branch) append time.monotonic() and prune entries older than 3600 s; in run_cycle replace the `sync_error=state.last_sync_error` argument at line 310 with `sync_error=_with_restart_note(state)` where `_with_restart_note` returns state.last_sync_error joined with "; " to f"the player restarted {n} times in the last hour" when n >= 3 (fetch_manifest already truncates to SYNC_ERROR_MAX_LEN=200 at sync.py:127, so no length handling needed). Keep the note out of state.last_sync_error itself so the storage-full check at line 317 and the on-screen error text at 153/173 are unchanged. The console needs no change: last_error already renders as the "Sync problem:" banner (dashboard.js:30, devices.js:375) and raises the sync-error alert (alerts.js:45). For the tunnel, do it in the console at page-render time, not per sync: GET /accounts/{acct}/cfd_tunnel/{tunnel_id} returns `status` (inactive/degraded/healthy/down); show it next to the live-camera link on the Devices page for tunnelled devices only. Do not add a daemon param for it. Leave the render-failure branch alone (font missing or read-only data dir is an install defect, not a runtime one).


**Verify on hardware.** On the Pi: systemctl status projector-cloudflared --no-pager; journalctl -u projector-cloudflared -b -n 50 (a revoked token loops with "Unauthorized" every 10 s); journalctl -u projector-mpv -b | grep -c 'Started'; journalctl -u projector-player -b | grep -c 'mpv restarted'; then open the device in the console and confirm no alert or status text mentions the tunnel or the restart count.


### L10. Static status screens carry a HH:MM:SS clock that freezes at render time

- Severity: **Low**. Area: Player daemon. Where: `player/player/screens.py:226`.
- Existing test covers it: **no**. No test asserts the footer clock stays current. Two tests codify the current behaviour and must change with the fix: player/tests/test_screens.py:11 passes clock="12:00:00" in COMMON and :52 asserts "12:00:00" in the footer text (drop the kwarg and the assertion); player/tests/test_status.py:24 and :30 pass clock="10:00:00"/"10:00:01" to prove dedup ignores the clock (drop the kwargs; the dedup assertion at :30 still holds with plain equality). Regression test to add in test_screens.py: for every full-screen state in ALL[:-1], assert not re.search(r"\d\d:\d\d:\d\d", "\n".join(texts(st))) (three groups on purpose: the waiting screen's "next: ... at 09:00" is a legitimate HH:MM and must stay). Verified today: layout(ScreenState(kind="offline", ...)) yields 'player v1 · 20:21:25' with no clock passed, and test_screens.py + test_status.py currently pass (29 tests).

**What is wrong.** _compose_full prints datetime.now().strftime('%H:%M:%S') in the footer, but StatusScreens.show (status.py 34) dedups states ignoring the clock, so a screen is rendered once and the PNG sits in mpv with image-display-duration=inf. An OFFLINE screen shows the seconds at which it was rendered for hours (the scratch test shows zero re-renders over 5 simulated hours with last_contact 'never'), which reads as a frozen Pi to the owner.


**How to reproduce.** Scratch test test_screen_clock_frozen: after the first OFFLINE render, 3 more cycles 5 h later issue 0 further loadfile commands and the footer still says the original time.


**Fix.** Drop the clock, do not replace it with a live one. player/player/screens.py:226-227: right = _right(f"player v{state.version}", RIGHT, foot_y, "mono", 28, "dim", half). Delete the clock field at screens.py:98 (nothing outside tests sets it; datetime import stays for _next_rule_text at :155-156). player/player/status.py:34: simplify to `if self.current == state: return False` and drop the `from dataclasses import replace` at :3; update the docstring at :33. Tests: remove clock= from test_screens.py:11 and test_status.py:24/:30, remove the "12:00:00" assertion at test_screens.py:52, add the no-HH:MM:SS regression test described in test_note. Keep "last contact N min ago" as is: it already re-renders whenever its text changes. Skip the mpv-OSD live clock: mpv exposes no wall-clock property, it would need a Lua script plus per-second redraws for a footer decoration.


**Verify on hardware.** On a Pi with the console unreachable, note the HH:MM:SS in the bottom-right of the OFFLINE screen, wait 2 minutes, confirm it has not advanced; journalctl -u projector-player -n 200 | grep 'showing status screen' shows no new loads in that window (with last_contact 'never'), or one per minute when a previous contact time exists.


### L11. SAFE_FILENAME admits '.', '..' and names ending in '.part'

- Severity: **Low**. Area: Player daemon. Where: `player/player/sync.py:16`.
- Existing test covers it: **partial**. player/tests/test_sync.py::test_unsafe_filename_is_a_per_item_failure only exercises "../etc/passwd" (rejected by the slash). No test feeds ".", ".." or a name ending in ".part" through the manifest. A covering test: in test_sync.py, set_playlist(["ok.mp4"]) then append items with filename "x.mp4.part", "." and ".." and assert each name appears in the returned sync_error and media_dir contains only ok.mp4 after two polls (the .part case currently passes silently with err == "" and changed == True on every poll, which is the strongest assertion to add).

**What is wrong.** ^[A-Za-z0-9._-]+$ accepts '.' and '..' (target becomes media_dir or its parent; the rename of '...part' onto a directory raises OSError, a per-item failure, no traversal) and any name ending in '.part', which _prune_stale (275-278) treats as a partial download of the base name and deletes whenever the base is not in the playlist, so such an item would be re-downloaded every poll. The console never produces these names (uploads.js finalMediaName yields <sha16>_<stem><ext>), so this only matters for a compromised or foreign console.


**How to reproduce.** Code trace: sync.py 16, 142-147, 275-278; regex accepts '..' and 'x.mp4.part'.


**Fix.** One-line regex change, no other code touched: SAFE_FILENAME = re.compile(r"^(?!\.)(?!.*\.part$)[A-Za-z0-9._-]+$"). Rejecting any leading "." covers ".", "..", hidden names that _prune_stale (273) would never remove, and ".download_*.tmp" names that cleanup_stale_temp would delete; the second lookahead covers the PART_SUFFIX collision. Verified: the full player suite (277) passes with this regex patched in, and the scratch repro then reports both names as "refusing unsafe filename" per-item failures. The finder's version (explicit filename in (".", "..") plus endswith(PART_SUFFIX) in the refuse branch) is also correct but must be duplicated at both 142 and 226 and leaves hidden names admitted. No existing test needs changing.


### L12. A manifest item missing filename/sha256/url aborts the whole sync with a Python KeyError on the projector

- Severity: **Low**. Area: Player daemon. Where: `player/player/sync.py:225`.
- Existing test covers it: **no**. Not covered. Closest is player/tests/test_sync.py:149 test_unsafe_filename_is_a_per_item_failure (per-item failure with a bad filename), which stays valid. Add test_item_missing_filename_is_a_per_item_failure in player/tests/test_sync.py: cms.files["ok.mp4"]=b"x"; cms.set_playlist(["ok.mp4"]); bad=dict(cms.manifest["playlist"]["items"][0], position=1); del bad["filename"]; append it; changed, manifest, err = sync_once(cfg); assert err == "download failed: ?: item is missing its filename or link"; assert (cfg.media_dir/"ok.mp4").is_file(); assert missing_files(cfg, manifest) == [] and [p.name for p,_ in build_mpv_items(cfg, manifest)] == ["ok.mp4"]. Repeat with del bad["url"] and del bad["sha256"].

**What is wrong.** sync_once tolerates a missing filename at sync.py:428 (item.get) but _ensure_item indexes item["filename"] at 225 (and item["sha256"] at 231/157/212, item["url"] at 176). KeyError is not in the per-item except at 436, so one malformed item aborts the loop: items before it are downloaded but the media index and local manifest are never saved (442-445), items after it are skipped, and the daemon's catch-all at daemon.py:345-346 records last_failure="sync", failure_text="KeyError: 'filename'" and backs off up to 300 s, repeating every poll. A Pi already playing keeps its previous playlist (state.last_manifest is unchanged); a fresh Pi never completes its first sync and shows "KeyError: 'filename'" on the ERROR screen via daemon.py:203/155. missing_files and build_mpv_items cannot hit the KeyError in practice because the malformed manifest is never saved. The current console always sends these fields (media.filename/sha256 NOT NULL, url built in manifest.js:213), so this is a robustness gap against an older or foreign console rather than a live bug.


**How to reproduce.** cd player; copy tests/conftest.py to a scratch dir with PLAYER_ROOT pointed at player/ and tests/ on sys.path; test: cms.files["a.mp4"]=b"A"*10; cms.files["b.mp4"]=b"B"*10; cms.set_playlist(["a.mp4","b.mp4"]); del cms.manifest["playlist"]["items"][1]["filename"]; with pytest.raises(KeyError) as ei: sync_once(cfg); assert ei.value.args==("filename",); assert (cfg.media_dir/"a.mp4").exists() and not cfg.manifest_path.exists(). Code trace: sync.py:432 -> 225 KeyError -> not matched at 436 -> daemon.py:345-346 -> daemon.py:203 -> 155 on a fresh Pi.


**Fix.** player/player/sync.py: add a separate clause after line 436 so the console gets plain English rather than str(KeyError): `except KeyError as e: reason = f"item is missing its {e.args[0]}" if e.args and e.args[0] in ("filename","url","sha256") else "item is missing a field"; log.warning(...); failures.append((filename, reason))` (or simply the fixed text "item is missing its filename or link"). In missing_files (line 317) and build_mpv_items (line 328-329) skip items where not item.get("filename") (e.g. `for item in playlist["items"]: name = item.get("filename"); if not name: continue`). Also change player/player/daemon.py:72 to `items[pos].get("filename")` guarded by `if items[pos].get("filename")`, otherwise the status builder raises the same KeyError every poll once the manifest is saved. Drop TypeError from the fix: a non-dict item fails at line 428 outside the try and is not worth handling. No existing test changes.


**Verify on hardware.** Not needed (reproduced with the harness). If a foreign console is suspected on a live Pi: journalctl -u projector-player -n 200 | grep -A3 "unexpected error in sync loop" will show the KeyError traceback from daemon.py:346.


### L13. The sudoers '*' on update-player.sh lets the projector user run --postcheck at will, i.e. roll the code back to .prev without any command from the console

- Severity: **Low**. Area: Updates and commands. Where: `player/deploy/install-player.sh:292`.
- Existing test covers it: **no**. Nothing covers the sudoers argument pattern. player/tests/test_updater.py:295-311 runs `update-player.sh --postcheck` directly as an unprivileged user under PIPLAYER_ROOT, bypassing sudo entirely, and the sudoers text assertions in player/tests/test_tunnel.py:142-143 and player/tests/test_camera_config.py:218-219 only check the systemctl restart lines. A covering test (same style, in test_tunnel.py or test_installer_pi.py): `installer = (DEPLOY / "install-player.sh").read_text(); assert "NOPASSWD: /opt/piplayer/player/deploy/update-player.sh [A-Za-z0-9]*" in installer; assert "NOPASSWD: /usr/bin/bash /opt/piplayer/player/deploy/update-player.sh [A-Za-z0-9]*" in installer; assert "update-player.sh *\n" not in installer`. Real allow/deny behaviour needs sudo on a Pi (see hardware_verify). No existing test changes if the .prev deletion is dropped from the fix.

**What is wrong.** The sudoers rules at install-player.sh:292-293 ('update-player.sh *') match any argument string, including '--postcheck', and update-player.sh:169-172 runs postcheck() as root with no check that the caller is the timer unit rather than sudo. Impact is small: postcheck only rolls back when the service is already inactive or has auto-restarted 3+ times and .prev exists (update-player.sh:89-96), and the same rule already lets the projector user install any public ref as root by design (updater.py:59), which subsumes a rollback to .prev. The update window is only enforced for the daemon's own auto-update (updater.py:173), so the wildcard does not add "outside the window" capability either. Hygiene: the rule is slightly broader than the daemon needs.


**How to reproduce.** Code trace: install-player.sh:292 grants 'update-player.sh *'; update-player.sh:37 sets REF="$1", line 169 tests REF == "--postcheck" and line 171 calls postcheck() with no SUDO_USER/caller check. Harness: cd player && python -m pytest -q tests/test_updater.py -k update_player (lines 295-308 show --postcheck is a no-op when active with <3 restarts and rolls back only when failed/5 restarts).


**Fix.**

```text
In player/deploy/install-player.sh replace lines 292-293 with:
projector ALL=(ALL) NOPASSWD: /opt/piplayer/player/deploy/update-player.sh [A-Za-z0-9]*
projector ALL=(ALL) NOPASSWD: /usr/bin/bash /opt/piplayer/player/deploy/update-player.sh [A-Za-z0-9]*
sudoers matches the whole argument string with fnmatch, so `[A-Za-z0-9]*` still allows `v6.0` and `v6.0 --then-os` (the two forms updater.py:59-61 issues) while rejecting any leading `-` such as `--postcheck`. No second `--then-os` rule is needed. Leave projector-player-postcheck.service (install-player.sh:391) as is: it already runs `--postcheck` as root without sudo. Do NOT delete .prev after a passing post-check: it breaks player/tests/test_updater.py:298 and removes the owner's recovery copy. Optional one-line belt-and-braces in update-player.sh main() before line 170: `[[ -n "${SUDO_USER:-}" ]] && { echo "--postcheck is run by the post-check timer, not via sudo" >&2; exit 1; }`.
```


**Verify on hardware.** sudo -n -u projector sudo -n -l /opt/piplayer/player/deploy/update-player.sh --postcheck   (today: prints the command, i.e. allowed; after the fix: "not allowed"). Then confirm the timer path still works: sudo systemctl start projector-player-postcheck.service && tail -n 3 /var/lib/projector-player/update.log (expect "post-check ok").


### L14. Tarball fallback writes the ref name into RELEASE, so a branch ref reports 'already at main' forever and player_version becomes 0.2.0+main

- Severity: **Low**. Area: Updates and commands. Where: `player/deploy/update-player.sh:140`.
- Existing test covers it: **no**. player/tests/test_updater.py::test_update_player_script_end_to_end_in_a_fake_root only exercises the git branch (needs_tools skips the test when git is missing, and fake_pi.run puts the real git on PATH). Nothing in player/tests or tools/flasher/tests runs the tarball fallback. A test would call fake_pi.run("update-player.sh", "main", PATH=<shims dir plus a coreutils dir without git>) (extra kwargs override PATH in the harness) and assert status ok=False with message "git is not installed on this projector..." and that RELEASE is unchanged; it must be Linux-only (skipif not sys.platform.startswith("linux")) because Git Bash on the Windows dev box cannot provide coreutils without git. Deleting the fallback breaks no existing test.

**What is wrong.** When git is missing the tarball fallback sets sha="${REF}" (update-player.sh:140); install-player.sh:239 writes it into RELEASE, so for one cycle update-status.json reports "updated <sha> -> main" and player_version() (updater.py:44) reports "0.2.0+main". It does NOT get stuck: install-player.sh:180 installs git during that same --upgrade, so the next run uses git, gets a real sha, and updates normally. Only reachable on a hand-built Pi without git.


**How to reproduce.** Trace update-player.sh:127-141 with git absent, then install-player.sh:180 (unconditional apt-get install git) and :238-239 (RELEASE=PIPLAYER_RELEASE_SHA). No harness: test_updater.py runs with real git on PATH.


**Fix.**

```text
Delete the tarball fallback (lines 135-141) and fail in plain English; the installer puts git on every Pi anyway, so the branch only exists for hand-built machines:

    if ! command -v git >/dev/null 2>&1; then
        fail "git is not installed on this projector; run: sudo bash ${INSTALL_DIR}/deploy/install-player.sh --upgrade"
    fi
    if ! git clone --quiet --depth 1 --branch "${REF}" "${REPO_URL}" "${SRC_DIR}" 2>&1; then
        ...existing clone/checkout...
    fi
    sha="$(git -C "${SRC_DIR}" rev-parse HEAD)"

and drop "(tarball via curl if git is missing)" from the header comment at line 9. If the fallback must stay, resolve the sha first and download by sha so RELEASE and the comparison at line 145 stay correct for branches, tags and shas alike:

    sha="$(curl -fsSL "https://api.github.com/repos/${REPO_URL#https://github.com/}" | sed 's/\.git$//')/commits/${REF}" | python3 -c 'import json,sys; print(json.load(sys.stdin)["sha"])')" || fail "could not look up ${REF} on GitHub"
    curl -fsSL "${REPO_URL%.git}/archive/${sha}.tar.gz" | tar -xz --strip-components=1 -C "${SRC_DIR}" || fail "download failed for ${REF}"

Either change leaves test_updater.py green (the git branch is untouched).
```


**Verify on hardware.** On a Pi without git: sudo apt-get remove -y git; sudo /opt/piplayer/player/deploy/update-player.sh main; sleep 60; cat /opt/piplayer/player/RELEASE (expect "main"); command -v git (expect present now); sudo /opt/piplayer/player/deploy/update-player.sh main; sleep 60; cat /opt/piplayer/player/RELEASE (expect a 40-char sha, not "already at main" in /var/lib/projector-player/update.log).


### L15. With --then-os the OS status overwrites the player-update status before the restarted daemon has reported it if its first sync fails

- Severity: **Low**. Area: Updates and commands. Where: `player/deploy/update-player.sh:186`.
- Existing test covers it: **no**. player/tests/test_updater.py::test_update_player_script_end_to_end_in_a_fake_root (line 324-327) runs --then-os and asserts only that the final ref is "os"; it does not check that the player result survives. With the fix below, add to that test: assert fake_pi.status()["message"].startswith(f"updated {sha1} -> {sha2}; then system packages: "). test_update_os_script_in_a_fake_root is unaffected (no env var set standalone). A unit test for the lost-report path itself is not needed once the message is merged.

**What is wrong.** There is a single update-status.json. update() writes 'updated a -> b', restarts the service and execs update-os.sh (line 186), which rewrites the file with ref 'os' when apt finishes (update-os.sh 43).
