The new daemon has to complete one successful sync in between; if the console is unreachable for those minutes (DNS not yet up after the restart, console redeploying), the marker never matches and the player result is lost: the console only ever sees the OS result. A quick apt run (nothing to upgrade, ~20 s) makes the race realistic. Confirmed in the fake root: after 'main --then-os' the file holds only the 'os' status.


**How to reproduce.** Scratch harness on fake_pi: run update-player.sh main --then-os; fake_pi.status()['ref'] == 'os' and the 'updated' message exists only in update.log.


**Fix.** Carry the player result into the chained OS run instead of a second status file. In player/deploy/update-player.sh write_status() (line 57-71) keep the last message: add `LAST_STATUS_MSG="$2"` before the log line. Change line 186 to: `PIPLAYER_CHAINED_MSG="${LAST_STATUS_MSG:-}" exec bash "${INSTALL_DIR}/deploy/update-os.sh"`. In player/deploy/update-os.sh write_status() (line 31-45) prefix the message: pass `"${PIPLAYER_CHAINED_MSG:+${PIPLAYER_CHAINED_MSG}; then system packages: }$2"` as the message argument (keep ref "os" so the console and last_player_update_started behave as today). The 300-char cap in the python heredoc still applies. Standalone update-os.sh runs are unchanged (variable unset). Update test_update_player_script_end_to_end_in_a_fake_root line 327 to also assert the merged message as noted in test_note; no other test changes.


**Verify on hardware.** On the Pi after an update-all: sudo grep -E 'update-(player|os): status' /var/lib/projector-player/update.log | tail -n 3 (shows both status writes); cat /var/lib/projector-player/update-status.json (holds only ref "os"); journalctl -u projector-player -n 200 | grep -i update_status (whether the daemon synced the player record before the os one overwrote it). Cross-check with the console audit log: two device_update_reported rows expected, only one present when the race is lost.


### L16. Remotely managed tunnel: the Pi proxies whatever ingress the Cloudflare account says, and the bridge UI it fronts has no login of its own

- Severity: **Low**. Area: Projector, camera, tunnel. Where: `player/deploy/projector-wyze-bridge.service:27`.
- Existing test covers it: **no**. No test in player/tests or tools/flasher/tests covers this (the Pi has no code path to test; player/tests/test_tunnel.py only covers token write + restart). cloud/test/tunnel.test.js lines 112-132 and 137-150 pin the provision call order and the exact PUT /configurations body (INGRESS constant), and cloud/e2e/run_tunnel_e2e.py lines 200-230 pin the same sequence, so the fix below changes those expectations: ensureAccess moves before putIngress and the ingress body gains originRequest.access. A test that would cover it: in tunnel.test.js assert fake.state.ingress["tun-1"].ingress[0].originRequest.access equals {required: true, teamName: CF.CF_TEAM_NAME, audTag: [state.apps[0].aud]} and that provision throws (no ingress written) when the access/apps POST is refused.

**What is wrong.** The tunnel is config_src cloudflare (cloudflare.js:63), so its ingress lives in the account; anyone holding CF_API_TOKEN (a worker secret) or dashboard access can point a new hostname at ssh://127.0.0.1:22 or rtsp 8554 on every Pi, without an Access policy, and cloudflared on the Pi will serve it. The only service it is meant to expose, wyze-bridge on 127.0.0.1:5000, runs with WB_AUTH=false and lets a visitor watch and also control the cameras (power, PTZ, restart) once past Access; if the Access application is deleted in the dashboard, or a Recreate on an existing tunnel_id fails at the ensureAccess step while the older policy has been edited away, nothing on the Pi notices and the camera page is anonymous on the public hostname. The Pi cannot verify that Access is in front; this is a design property to state in the docs rather than a code bug, mitigated by key-only SSH.


**How to reproduce.** Code trace: cloudflare.js:63 config_src "cloudflare"; :67-71 putIngress replaces the whole ingress from the worker (any 127.0.0.1 service); projector-cloudflared.service:24 runs with the token only; tunnel.py:44-47 keeps the old token when the manifest sends tunnel: null; api.js:107-115 tunnelBlock only fetches the token, never re-reads /access/apps; projector-wyze-bridge.service:27 WB_AUTH=false. Doc trap: automation.md:1170-1175 tells the owner to delete the Access application in the dashboard when retiring a Pi (Tunnels, DNS, Access, "or leave them"); deleting only the Access app while the Pi still runs makes <device>-cam.photogen5000.com anonymous.


**Fix.** 1) Docs only for the trust boundary: add to docs/automation.md section G (near line 1017, the CF_API_TOKEN row) one paragraph stating that the tunnel is remotely managed, so whoever holds CF_API_TOKEN or Zero Trust dashboard access can point any hostname at any localhost port on every Pi, that the bridge page has no login of its own, and that the Access application must not be deleted by hand (use Recreate tunnel to repair it). 2) Fail closed on the Pi without touching the Pi: in cloud/src/cloudflare.js have ensureAccess return {id, aud} (the Access API returns aud on GET/POST access/apps), reorder provision() so ensureAccess runs before putIngress, and give putIngress the app: config.ingress[0] becomes { hostname, service: INGRESS_SERVICE, originRequest: { access: { required: true, teamName: env.CF_TEAM_NAME, audTag: [aud] } } }. cloudflared then validates the Cf-Access-Jwt-Assertion against that aud and returns 403 for anything not issued by that Access app, so a deleted app, a bypassed policy or a dashboard-added anonymous hostname on the same tunnel config still cannot reach 127.0.0.1:5000. Add CF_TEAM_NAME (the Zero Trust team name, e.g. from https://<team>.cloudflareaccess.com) to SECRET_NAMES at cloudflare.js:22 so configured() reports it as missing in the Settings badge, and document it in the automation.md G secrets table. Do NOT set WB_AUTH=true (it also gates RTSP on 8554 and would break the player's snapshots via WYZE_BRIDGE_RTSP, and the sandboxed live iframe would need a second login), and do not add a per-sync GET /access/apps (doubles the API calls api.js:105 already budgets; the originRequest check makes it unnecessary). Update cloud/test/tunnel.test.js (call order at lines 115-124 and 143-149, INGRESS constant and calls[2].body index) and cloud/e2e/run_tunnel_e2e.py (lines 200-230) for the new order and body.


**Verify on hardware.** On the Pi: sudo -u projector curl -sI http://127.0.0.1:5000/ | head -1 (expect HTTP 200 with no auth); ss -ltnp | grep -E ':5000|:8554|:22'; systemctl status projector-cloudflared.service; then from any machine with no Access cookie: curl -sI https://<device>-cam.photogen5000.com/ | head -1 (expect a 302 to the Access login; a 200 means the app is gone and the page is anonymous). In the Cloudflare dashboard confirm Zero Trust -> Access -> Applications lists p5k-<device_id> camera.


### L17. Full RTSP URL including user:password is written to the journal on every source change and start

- Severity: **Low**. Area: Projector, camera, tunnel. Where: `player/player/camera.py:105`.
- Existing test covers it: **no**. No test in player/tests inspects log text or camera_error for the URL: tests/test_camera.py:112 (test_capture_failure_sets_error_and_logs_once_per_10_min) counts "camera snapshot failed" records and asserts "Connection refused" in cam.error only; tests/test_camera_config.py:75-128 check cam.rtsp_url attribute values, never caplog. Add to tests/test_camera.py, reusing the cam_cfg/ffmpeg/caplog fixtures: build CameraCapture with camera_rtsp_url="rtsp://user:secret@cam.test/live", call cam.set_source("rtsp", "rtsp://user:secret@cam.test/two") and cam.run() (with _stop pre-set) under caplog.at_level("INFO", logger="piplayer.camera"), set ffmpeg["result"] to returncode 1 with stderr b"rtsp://user:secret@cam.test/two: Connection refused", call capture_once(), then assert "secret" not in any r.message and "secret" not in cam.error while "***" and "Connection refused" are present. Proposed fix passes the existing suite unchanged.

**What is wrong.** Lines 105 and 136 log self.rtsp_url verbatim ('camera capture started: rtsp://user:pass@192.168.1.20:554/stream1', 'camera source updated: ...'). camera.md says the credentials sit in a mode-600 config.toml; the journal is readable by the adm/systemd-journal groups (projector-admin, the flasher's SSH user, is in adm on Raspberry Pi OS) and ends up in any `journalctl` paste an owner sends for support. On ffmpeg builds older than 6.1 the last stderr line is '<url>: Connection refused', which would put the password in camera_error on the console and in its DB as well; Trixie's ffmpeg 7 prints 'Error opening input files: ...' so that part is version-dependent.


**How to reproduce.** cd player && python -c "import logging,io; from player import camera; buf=io.StringIO(); lg=logging.getLogger('piplayer.camera'); lg.addHandler(logging.StreamHandler(buf)); lg.setLevel(logging.INFO); c=camera.CameraCapture.__new__(camera.CameraCapture); c.source='rtsp'; c.rtsp_url='rtsp://u:s3cret@192.168.1.20:554/s'; c.interval=10; c.error=''; c.set_source('rtsp','rtsp://u:other@cam/2'); print(buf.getvalue())" prints both passwords in clear text.


**Fix.** In player/player/camera.py add `import re` and, after the constants, `_CREDS = re.compile(r"://[^/]*@")` plus `def redact(text: str) -> str: return _CREDS.sub("://***@", text)`. Use it in three places: line 56 `raise CameraError(f"ffmpeg failed (exit {proc.returncode}): {redact(detail[-1]) if detail else 'no output'}")`; line 105 `log.info("camera source updated: %s %s -> %s %s", self.source, redact(self.rtsp_url), source, redact(rtsp_url))`; line 136 `log.info("camera capture started: %s every %ds", redact(self.rtsp_url) if self.rtsp_url else "(no camera yet)", self.interval)`. Greedy `[^/]*` (rather than `[^/@]*`) redacts a password containing a raw `@`; URLs with no userinfo are untouched because the class cannot cross the path `/`. Redacting at line 56 covers both the journal warning (line 133) and the console camera_error (daemon.py:280) regardless of ffmpeg version.


**Verify on hardware.** journalctl -u projector-player.service | grep -E 'camera (capture started|source updated)' (expect the full rtsp://user:pass@ URL); ls -l /var/log/journal && id projector-admin (confirm adm/systemd-journal membership makes it readable without sudo); ffmpeg -version | head -1 and ffmpeg -loglevel error -rtsp_transport tcp -i rtsp://u:p@127.0.0.1:1/x -frames:v 1 -f image2 - 2>&1 | tail -1 (confirm the last stderr line on the shipped ffmpeg does not contain the URL).


### L18. Snapshot period is interval plus capture time, so the console's STALE badge (3 x interval) fires falsely at short intervals

- Severity: **Low**. Area: Projector, camera, tunnel. Where: `player/player/camera.py:138`.
- Existing test covers it: **no**. No existing test measures cadence. player/tests/test_camera.py::test_thread_loops_and_stops only counts uploads with interval=0.01; test_hung_ffmpeg_never_blocks_the_sync_loop only checks the sync loop is not blocked. A covering test: monkeypatch cam.capture_once to record time.monotonic() and sleep 0.2, set cam.interval = 0.3, run ~1.5 s, assert the mean gap between captures is within 0.05 s of 0.3 (fails at ~0.5 on current code). With the 0.1 s floor below, test_thread_loops_and_stops keeps passing unchanged and stays fast; the finder's 1.0 s floor would also pass but slow that test to ~2 s.

**What is wrong.** run() (camera.py:138-139) calls capture_once() and then waits the full `interval`, so the real snapshot period is interval + ffmpeg time (up to 15 s) + upload time (up to 15 s). The console marks the camera STALE when the last snapshot is older than 3 x camera_interval (cloud/src/pages/devices.js:68), which the camera loop exceeds whenever capture+upload takes longer than 2 x interval: more than 10 s at the minimum interval of 5, more than 20 s at the default 10. That is not the common case (a grab from a running RTSP stream is 1-3 s) but is plausible at interval 5 with the Wyze bridge starting the stream on demand plus a slow Wi-Fi upload, so a healthy camera can show STALE and docs/camera.md:19,293 then tell the owner the Pi stopped uploading. The screenshot loop already schedules by deadline (screenshots.py:68); the camera thread is the odd one out.


**How to reproduce.** Standalone script (no pytest, the scratch conftest points at a stale rootdir): PYTHONPATH=player python - with CameraCapture.__new__, interval=5, a fake _stop whose wait() records its argument and is_set() after 3 waits, capture_once patched to sleep 0.05 s; cam.run() records waits [5, 5, 5], independent of capture time. Then: period = interval + capture + upload; STALE (devices.js:68) iff period > 3*interval iff capture+upload > 2*interval.


**Fix.**

```text
player/player/camera.py:137-139, replace the loop body with deadline scheduling and a small floor so the loop cannot spin when a capture takes longer than the interval:

    while not self._stop.is_set():
        t0 = time.monotonic()
        self.capture_once()
        self._stop.wait(max(0.1, self.interval - (time.monotonic() - t0)))

Use a 0.1 s floor rather than the finder's 1.0 s: production intervals are >= CAMERA_MIN_INTERVAL (5 s) so the floor never matters there, and 0.1 keeps test_thread_loops_and_stops (interval 0.01) fast. No other callers of run() exist; update_interval() still takes effect on the next wait. Optionally add the test described in test_note.
```


**Verify on hardware.** On a Pi with the Wyze bridge: sudo -u projector bash -c 'time ffmpeg -nostdin -loglevel error -rtsp_transport tcp -i rtsp://127.0.0.1:8554/<cam> -frames:v 1 -q:v 5 -f image2 - | wc -c'  (run 3 times, a few seconds apart, so the bridge's on-demand start is included). Then set camera_interval to 5 on the console Settings page, wait a minute, and watch the CAM chip on the Devices page: if the ffmpeg time plus upload exceeds 10 s, the chip alternates between live and STALE while journalctl -u projector-player -n 100 shows no "camera snapshot failed" line.


### L19. The 30 s subprocess timeout on `sudo systemctl restart` raises PermissionError, not TimeoutExpired; tunnel.py does not catch it

- Severity: **Low**. Area: Projector, camera, tunnel. Where: `player/player/tunnel.py:33`.
- Existing test covers it: **partial**. player/tests/test_tunnel.py:106-110 (test_write_failure_is_logged_and_retried) and player/tests/test_camera_config.py:192 (test_slow_bridge_restart_does_not_kill_the_cycle) inject subprocess.TimeoutExpired, which the real call can never raise; neither injects PermissionError, and no test asserts the sudoers argv matches what the code runs after a change. The test that would cover it: monkeypatch subprocess.run to raise PermissionError(errno.EPERM, "Operation not permitted") in both files and assert run_cycle returns a manifest, commands still execute (tunnel path), the token stays written, and the logged/camera_error text is plain English. If --no-block is adopted, also assert RESTART == the argv in the sudoers lines (test_tunnel.py:18/142-143, test_camera_config.py:72/218-219).

**What is wrong.** subprocess.run(['sudo','-n','/bin/systemctl','restart',UNIT], timeout=30) in tunnel.py:33 (and camera_config.py:81) cannot enforce its timeout: on TimeoutExpired run() calls process.kill(), os.kill on the root-owned sudo process fails with EPERM (daemon runs as projector), PermissionError replaces TimeoutExpired, and Popen.__exit__ then blocks in wait() with no timeout until the restart job finishes (the child is reaped, not orphaned). Net effect: the poll cycle blocks for the whole restart (cloudflared stop up to TimeoutStopSec 90 s; bridge ExecStartPre docker pull of the unpinned :latest tag, unbounded), then a PermissionError surfaces from a restart that already completed. tunnel.py:61 catches only subprocess.SubprocessError, so the PermissionError escapes to daemon.py:388's generic handler and the rest of that cycle (screenshot, commands, auto-update, projector.maybe_apply) is skipped once, with a traceback in the journal. camera_config.py:121 catches it under OSError but sets camera.error to 'camera config: [Errno 1] Operation not permitted', which reaches the console as camera_error until the next clean snapshot cycle. Both slow-restart tests inject TimeoutExpired, a path the real call never takes.


**How to reproduce.** Code trace: tunnel.py:33-34 -> CPython subprocess.run `except TimeoutExpired: process.kill()` -> POSIX send_signal -> os.kill(sudo_pid, SIGKILL) -> EPERM (sudo runs with ruid/euid/suid 0, daemon is projector) -> PermissionError propagates; Popen.__exit__ then self.wait() (no timeout) until sudo exits. Local model in the scratch dir: patch subprocess.Popen.kill to raise PermissionError(1,'Operation not permitted'), run a 4 s child with timeout=1, capture_output=True: prints 'PermissionError ... isinstance SubprocessError: False' after 4.0 s. tunnel.py:61 catches subprocess.SubprocessError only -> daemon.py:388 handler.


**Fix.** Make the restart fire-and-forget (both units are Restart=always, the daemon only logs the result): in tunnel.py:33 and camera_config.py:81 run ["sudo","-n","/bin/systemctl","restart","--no-block",UNIT] with no timeout, and reword the success text to "cloudflared restart requested" / "bridge restart requested". sudoers matches the full argv, so update player/deploy/install-player.sh:288-291 to "NOPASSWD: /bin/systemctl restart --no-block projector-wyze-bridge.service" (and the /usr/bin twin, and the cloudflared pair); update-player.sh:157 re-runs install-player.sh --upgrade so the sudoers change ships with the code. Belt and braces: change tunnel.py:61 to `except (OSError, subprocess.SubprocessError) as e:` so any future kill/exec error is logged as "cloudflared restart failed" and never skips the rest of the cycle. Delete the now-stale comment at install-player.sh:348-349. Tests to update: test_tunnel.py:18 RESTART and :142-143, test_camera_config.py:72 and :218-219 (new argv), and change the slow_run stubs at test_tunnel.py:106-108 and test_camera_config.py:194-195 to raise PermissionError(errno.EPERM, "Operation not permitted") so they pin the real failure mode. If the smaller diff is preferred instead: only widen the except at tunnel.py:61 to OSError and drop the useless timeout= at tunnel.py:34, camera_config.py:82 and commands.py:33; that stops the cycle skip but keeps the daemon blocked for the full restart. Leave restart-mpv blocking (the daemon waits for the new socket right after).


**Verify on hardware.**

```text
On the Pi as root: `docker rmi -f mrlt8/wyze-bridge:latest` (forces the unit's ExecStartPre pull), then `sudo -u projector python3 -c "import subprocess,time; t=time.monotonic()
try: subprocess.run(['sudo','-n','/bin/systemctl','restart','projector-wyze-bridge.service'],capture_output=True,text=True,timeout=5)
except Exception as e: print(type(e).__name__, e)
print('elapsed', round(time.monotonic()-t,1))"` ; expect `PermissionError [Errno 1] Operation not permitted` printed only after the pull finished (elapsed well over 5 s), not TimeoutExpired at 5 s; then `ps -o pid,ruser,user,cmd -C sudo,systemctl` shows no leftover sudo/systemctl (reaped). To see why kill fails: while any `sudo -n /bin/systemctl restart projector-mpv.service` runs as projector, `ps -o pid,ruid,euid,suid,cmd -C sudo` shows ruid 0. Daemon side after a token change with a Live stream open: `journalctl -u projector-player -n 200 | grep -A15 'unexpected error after sync'` should show the PermissionError traceback from tunnel.restart_service.
```


### L20. Command results on the console: "queued resync", "executing mpv restart", "reboot failed: rc=1 ...", "exception: TypeError(...)"

- Severity: **Low**. Area: Plain English. Where: `player/player/commands.py:175`.
- Existing test covers it: **yes**. The current strings are pinned (so any change is caught): player/tests/test_commands.py:27-28,39,42,48,54,56,63,84-85,94,159-161,168,171,174; test_audit2_player.py:282,432,443; test_daemon.py:289,624; test_projector.py:286,292; test_updater.py:43-46,56-57,63. No test asserts the text is plain English; test_commands.py:52-56 (reboot failure is posted) is the one that guards the line-168 contract and would catch the finder's broken fix. Suggested addition: one assertion in test_commands.py that every posted result string contains none of "rc=", "mpv", "resync", "exception", "Error(".

**What is wrong.** Each command result is stored verbatim by the console (cloud/src/api.js:148) and shown on the Devices page as "done · <result>" (cloud/src/pages/devices.js:205). player/player/commands.py returns developer wording: "queued resync" (175), "executing reboot" / "executing mpv restart" / "executing update-player" (155-156), "reboot issued" (26), "mpv restart issued" (36), "already executed: reboot" (145), "unknown command: x" (181), "reboot failed: rc=1 sudo: a password is required" (27) / "restart failed: rc=..." (37), and "exception: TypeError(...)" (166, 184). updater.run_command failure strings follow the same shape ("update-player main failed: rc=1 ..."). A failed reboot additionally reads "done · reboot failed: ...", i.e. labelled done while it failed.


**How to reproduce.** cd "<checkout>/player" && python -m pytest -q tests/test_commands.py  (assertions at :56 "reboot failed: rc=1 sudo: a password is required", :85 "queued resync", :94 "unknown command: dance", :171 "update-player main failed: rc=1 ..." show the exact strings POSTed to /api/commands/{id}/result; cloud/src/pages/devices.js:205 renders them unchanged).


**Fix.** Keep line 168's contract (failure text must contain "failed") and reword within it; log stderr instead of showing it. commands.py: 26 "restart started"; 27 log.warning("reboot rc=%d %s", ...) then return "restart failed (details are in the player log)"; 36 "player restart started"; 37 same pattern "player restart failed (details are in the player log)"; 145 f"already done: {action}"; 155-156 {"reboot": "restarting the projector computer", "restart-mpv": "restarting the player", "update-player": "installing the player update", "update-all": "installing the player and system updates", "update-os": "installing system updates"}.get(action, f"running {action}"); 166 and 184 "the command failed unexpectedly (details are in the player log)"; 168 simplify to `if "failed" in result:` with a comment "updater/_run_* failure text always contains 'failed'"; 175 "checking all files now"; 181 f"this player does not understand the command '{action}'". updater.py:53 log stderr, return f"{what} failed (details are in the player log)"; :58 f"update-player failed: '{ref}' is not a valid version". Do NOT use "could not be started" or any text without "failed" unless line 168 is changed to a boolean, otherwise failures silently stop being reported. Tests to update: test_commands.py:27-28,39,42,48,54,56,63,84-85,94,159-161,168,171,174; test_audit2_player.py:282,432,443; test_daemon.py:289,624; test_projector.py:286,292; test_updater.py:43-46,56-57,63.


**Verify on hardware.** On a Pi with the console reachable: press Reboot on the Devices page, then `sudo systemctl stop projector-player; sudo sed -i 's#/sbin/reboot#/sbin/reboot-missing#' ...` is not needed; instead temporarily remove the sudoers entry (`sudo mv /etc/sudoers.d/projector-player /root/`), press Reboot again, and confirm the Devices page shows a failure line (not just "executing reboot") and `journalctl -u projector-player -n 50` holds the sudo stderr. Restore the sudoers file afterwards.


### L21. NOW PLAYING overlay says "3 items · via device-default" / "via schedule:Night" / "via group-default"

- Severity: **Low**. Area: Plain English. Where: `player/player/screens.py:265`.
- Existing test covers it: **partial**. player/tests/test_screens.py:77 test_nowplaying_layout asserts the current internal-key wording ("3 items · via device-default"), so it locks in the defect rather than guarding against it; it must be updated to the new string. test_long_filename_and_url_are_truncated_into_safe_area (line 89) exercises item_count=1 with source=None but only checks safe-area fit. Add parametrized cases to test_nowplaying_layout: ("device-default", 3) -> "3 items · this projector's playlist"; ("group-default", 1) -> "1 item · group playlist"; ("schedule:Night", 2) -> "2 items · scheduled: Night"; (None, 2) -> "2 items · from the console".

**What is wrong.** The overlay's third line is f"{item_count} items · via {source}" where source is the manifest's playlist.source: "device-default", "group-default" or "schedule:<name>" (cloud/src/manifest.js:74-76). Everyone in the room sees "via device-default" for eight seconds after every playlist change. It is an internal key, not a sentence.


**How to reproduce.** cd "<checkout>/player" && python -c "from player.screens import *; print([i['text'] for i in layout(ScreenState(kind='nowplaying', playlist_name='Ads', item_count=3, source='schedule:Night'))])" -> ['NOW PLAYING', 'Ads', '3 items · via schedule:Night']. Same for source='device-default' / 'group-default'. Existing test player/tests/test_screens.py:78-79 pins the raw form.


**Fix.**

```text
In player/player/screens.py add above _compose_overlay:

def _source_label(source: str | None) -> str:
    if source and source.startswith("schedule:"):
        return f"scheduled: {source[len('schedule:'):]}"
    return {"device-default": "this projector's playlist", "group-default": "group playlist"}.get(source or "", "from the console")

and replace line 265 with:

    n = state.item_count
    sub = _item(f"{n} item{'' if n == 1 else 's'}{DOT}{_source_label(state.source)}", 0, 0, "mono", 30, "body", max_width=max_w)

Update player/tests/test_screens.py:79 to assert t == ["NOW PLAYING", "Day", "3 items · this projector's playlist"] and add the parametrized cases named in test_note. No other test references the overlay text (test_status.py:47-49 only checks the overlay-add command and BGRA size).
```


**Verify on hardware.** On a Pi with an assigned playlist, watch the projector for 8 s after a playlist change (or push a new playlist from the console); the bottom-left overlay's third line should read e.g. "3 items · this projector's playlist" after the fix. journalctl -u projector-player -n 50 | grep -i 'now-playing' confirms the overlay rendered.


### L22. First-boot narrative ends at "Ready. Waiting for the first video." and never mentions the NOT ASSIGNED screen the owner then sees

- Severity: **Low**. Area: Docs versus code. Where: `docs/adding-a-pi.md:76`.
- Existing test covers it: **no**. Nothing tests the docs. tools/flasher/tests/test_firstboot.py:89 and :469 only check that the rendered script contains the "Ready. Waiting for the first video." line, which the fix does not change. A cheap guard would be a test in player/tests that reads docs/adding-a-pi.md and asserts every value of screens.HEADLINES (BOOTING, NOT ASSIGNED, STANDING BY, SYNCING, OFFLINE) appears in it, so a renamed headline cannot silently drift from the guide; optional, the finding is Low.

**What is wrong.** adding-a-pi.md:76-77 says the sequence ends with "Ready. Waiting for the first video." That line is drawn on the text console by provision.sh (firstboot.py:488) after install-player.sh has already started projector-mpv and projector-player (install-player.sh:427-429). Within seconds the daemon loads its own status screen into mpv (daemon.py:426 boot, then daemon.py:166-167 pairing), whose big headline is BOOTING and then NOT ASSIGNED (screens.py:65-66, with the detail "assign a playlist to this device in the console"), and mpv's KMS output covers the text console. So what the owner actually stares at until step 6 is NOT ASSIGNED, a screen no doc names.


**How to reproduce.** Trace: install-player.sh:427-429 starts mpv and the daemon; firstboot.py:488 draws Ready afterwards. daemon.py:426 shows "boot"; first sync gives playlist null (manifest.js:72-76,194) so daemon.py:166-167 shows "pairing" = NOT ASSIGNED (screens.py:66,172) via status.py:41 loadfile into mpv on DRM. grep -rn "NOT ASSIGNED" docs/ README.md returns nothing.


**Fix.** In docs/adding-a-pi.md, after "Ready. Waiting for the first video." (line 76-77) add: "A few seconds later the player replaces that with its own screen: BOOTING, then NOT ASSIGNED, which stays until you assign a playlist (section 6). If the Settings page names a default playlist for new devices, the Pi skips NOT ASSIGNED and shows SYNCING, then the video. STANDING BY means the playlist is empty or no schedule rule is active right now." Doc-only change; no test needs updating.


**Verify on hardware.** Watch HDMI through the end of a flasher first boot on a device with no default playlist; then: sudo journalctl -u projector-player.service -n 50 | grep 'showing status screen' (expect boot then pairing) and sudo journalctl -u projection5000-provision.service -n 20 to see when the Ready line was drawn relative to those.


### L23. "the installed copy under /opt/piplayer/player has no deploy/ directory" is stale; and the flasher leaves a full copy at /opt/projection5000-src

- Severity: **Low**. Area: Docs versus code. Where: `docs/adding-a-pi.md:353`.
- Existing test covers it: **partial**. tools/flasher/tests/test_firstboot.py::test_provision_enrolls_then_installs (line 462) asserts the provision script and tarball are removed but never checks the extracted /opt/projection5000-src (mapped to tmp+"/src" at line 430). player/tests/test_updater.py:197-199 (INSTALLER_STUB) copies deploy/*.sh into the fake install dir, which mirrors the real installer creating deploy/, but no test reads the docs. If the cleanup is adopted, add `assert not (Path(tmp) / "src").is_dir()` next to line 462; no existing test would break.

**What is wrong.** docs/adding-a-pi.md:352-353 claims /opt/piplayer/player has no deploy/ directory; install-player.sh:235-237 creates it with update-player.sh and update-os.sh (install-player.sh alone is missing). On flasher-made Pis, provision.sh (firstboot.py:481-487) leaves the full unpacked tree at /opt/projection5000-src/player, including deploy/install-player.sh, since the success branch removes only the tarball and the provision script. Note that copy is the version the card carried: after console-driven updates it is older than the running code, which is fine for --uninstall but not for a fresh --upgrade. README.md:310-322 and docs/automation.md:483-484 assume a ~/piplayer checkout that flasher-made Pis do not have.


**How to reproduce.** grep -n 'deploy' player/deploy/install-player.sh (lines 195-199 exclude deploy/ from rsync; 235-237 re-create it with the two update scripts). grep -n 'projection5000-src' tools/flasher/firstboot.py (481-483 unpack; 485-487 success branch removes only the unit, the sbin script and "$SRC"). grep -n 'PLAYER_EXCLUDE' tools/flasher/flasher.py (77: deploy is not excluded from the tarball; 1421-1422 selfcheck requires player/deploy/install-player.sh). Harness: cd tools/flasher && python -m pytest -q tests/test_firstboot.py -k provision; the tmp/src dir passed at test_firstboot.py:430 is left in place after a successful run (add `print(Path(tmp,'src').exists())` to see it).


**Fix.** Minimal and correct: (1) docs/adding-a-pi.md:353-354 change the parenthetical to "(the installed copy under `/opt/piplayer/player` carries only the two update scripts, not the installer)" and keep the clone recipe as is; it works on flasher-made Pis because the installer installs git. Do NOT tell owners to run `--upgrade` from /opt/projection5000-src: that is the flash-time snapshot, so it would downgrade a console-updated player and write RELEASE=unknown (install-player.sh:237). It is acceptable only for `--uninstall`; if mentioned at all, say so explicitly. (2) README.md:314 "Upgrading a player in place" and docs/automation.md:475: prefix with "clone the repo first if this Pi was made by the flasher (see adding-a-pi.md, Removing a Pi)"; the automation.md case genuinely needs the manual `--with-wyze --upgrade` because the console update path (update-player.sh:157) only re-enables Wyze when the unit already exists (install-player.sh:150-152). (3) Optional hygiene: in tools/flasher/firstboot.py after line 486 add `rm -rf /opt/projection5000-src` to the success branch so the stale snapshot cannot be mistaken for a current checkout, and assert it in test_provision_enrolls_then_installs.


**Verify on hardware.** On a flasher-made Pi: ls /opt/piplayer/player/deploy (expect update-player.sh update-os.sh only) ; ls /opt/projection5000-src/player/deploy (expect install-player.sh update-player.sh update-os.sh) ; ls ~/piplayer 2>&1 (expect no such directory) ; cat /opt/projection5000-src/player/RELEASE 2>/dev/null; cat /opt/piplayer/player/RELEASE (differ after any console update).


### L24. Section B (referenced from the hardware checklist) describes a "Sign in" button and "Signed in as" header the flasher does not have

- Severity: **Low**. Area: Docs versus code. Where: `docs/automation.md:126`.
- Existing test covers it: **partial**. Covered for the code: tools/flasher/tests/test_flasher.py::test_flash_connects_in_the_browser_then_makes_the_card (asserts account_btn "Connect"/"Disconnect", account_label "Connected as matt"/"Not connected", and that no visible widget text contains "Sign in" or "Signed in") and ::test_gui_shows_exactly_the_per_pi_fields (Advanced visible texts end with "Account", "Connect"). Not covered for the doc. A doc-drift test would be a few lines in test_flasher.py: read ../../docs/automation.md and docs/hardware.md and assert "click **Sign in**", "Signed in as", "Sign out", "Sign in button" and "enter the console URL and your operator token" do not appear; the doc fix breaks no existing test.

**What is wrong.** docs/automation.md:126-139 and the operator steps at :152-158 describe a "Sign in" button, a "Signed in as <username>" header and a "Sign out" item under Advanced. The flasher has none of these: the only account control is the "Connect"/"Disconnect" button under Advanced (flasher.py:658, 745-751) with a "Connected as <you>" / "Not connected" label, and FLASH runs the browser approval by itself the first time (flasher.py:739-740; README.md:30-34, 133-140). hardware.md:178 sends the owner to section B for the first-run flow and additionally says to "enter the console URL and your operator token", which the tool never asks for. An owner following either doc will look for a button and a header line that are not on the screen.


**How to reproduce.** grep -n 'text="Connect"\|Connected as\|Signed in as\|Sign in' tools/flasher/flasher.py (button text Connect at 658; labels at 747-751; "Signed in as" only in log/error strings at 824, 853, 1218). grep -n 'Sign in\|Signed in\|Sign out' docs/automation.md (lines 126, 137, 139, 152, 158). tools/flasher/tests/test_flasher.py:294 and :601 assert "Sign in"/"Signed in" are not shown anywhere in the UI.


**Fix.** Edit docs/automation.md only (no code change, no test changes needed). Line 71: "the flasher's Sign in button gets one" -> "the flasher gets one the first time FLASH is pressed". Lines 125-139: replace the Sign in sentence with "Pressing **FLASH** on a PC that has no stored token calls POST /api/operator/device-code, opens the browser at verification_url?code=<user_code> (or shows the URL and code when no browser opens), sets the status line to 'Approve this computer in the browser window that just opened, then the card is made automatically.' and polls POST /api/operator/device-token every few seconds for up to 10 minutes; the flash continues by itself once approved. Connect under Advanced runs the same flow by hand." Replace lines 136-137 with "Advanced then shows 'Connected as <username>' with Disconnect, which deletes the stored token." Line 139: "(a 401 brings the Sign in button back)" -> "(a 401 drops the token, so the next FLASH asks for approval again)". Operator steps 152-158: "1. Start the flasher, fill in the card and press **FLASH**. The browser opens the console's /authorize page with the code filled in (sign in to the console first if it asks; any editor or admin account works). 2. Check that the page names your PC ('Sign in the SD Flasher on <hostname>?') and click **Approve**. The flasher picks the token up within a few seconds, finishes the card, and Advanced shows 'Connected as <username>'." Line 173: "then shows Sign in again, and a fresh Sign in mints" -> "then asks for approval again on the next FLASH (or Connect under Advanced), which mints". Also docs/hardware.md:177-178: replace "On first run enter the console URL and your operator token; the flasher fetches the enrollment key itself" with "The first time on a PC a browser page opens; approve it as an editor or admin and the flasher fetches the enrollment key itself".


### L25. Section C says use "Update all players" to queue update-os; the button queues update-player only

- Severity: **Low**. Area: Docs versus code. Where: `docs/automation.md:299`.
- Existing test covers it: **no**. No test in player/tests or tools/flasher/tests touches docs or the console button. cloud/test/pages_devices.test.js:65 pins the fleet form's confirm text and lines 362-380 pin that POST /devices/update-all queues exactly the posted command (update-player by default, update-os accepted), which documents the real behaviour but cannot catch a doc contradiction. A doc-vs-UI mismatch is not worth a test; if the fleet button is ever extended to update-os, extend pages_devices.test.js:65 to assert the new hidden input instead.

**What is wrong.** "Auto mode never runs update-os; queue that yourself or with **Update all players**" contradicts the same section's line 200-202 ("queues update-player for every device") and the console: the fleet form posts a hidden command=update-player (cloud/src/pages/devices.js:527) and its title says "Queue update-player on every device". An owner pressing it to patch the OS across the fleet gets no OS update.


**How to reproduce.** grep -n 'Update all players\|Auto mode never runs' docs/automation.md (lines 201, 298-299); grep -n 'name="command" value=' cloud/src/pages/devices.js shows the only fleet form (line 527) hard-codes update-player; grep -n 'action="/devices/update-all"' cloud/src/pages/devices.js returns a single form.


**Fix.** docs/automation.md:298-299, replace "Auto mode never runs `update-os`; queue that yourself or with **Update all players**." with "Auto mode never runs `update-os`; queue `update-os` (or `update-all`) yourself from each device's Recent commands area. **Update all players** only queues `update-player`." Optional follow-up, not required: the handler already accepts command=update-os / update-all (devices.js:23, 622-623, tested at pages_devices.test.js:380), so a fleet "Update OS on all" button is a 3-line form addition if the owner actually wants it.


### L26. Section D says the fetched camera config always wins; the code (and camera.md) fall back to config.toml when the console answers none

- Severity: **Low**. Area: Docs versus code. Where: `docs/automation.md:448`.
- Existing test covers it: **partial**. player/tests/test_camera_config.py::test_console_none_keeps_config_toml_camera covers the code behaviour (none from the console keeps config.toml's camera). No test can cover doc prose; a doc-consistency test is not worth adding.

**What is wrong.** "the fetched configuration wins whenever the manifest carries the key" and "Set none on a device that has no camera so its bridge stays down" (line 401-402) read as if source=none on the console silences the Pi. camera_config.apply (camera_config.py:99-101) treats an empty URL from the console as "use config.toml's camera", and camera.md:159-162 documents exactly that ("the console cannot switch off a hand-configured camera"). An owner with a hand-set [camera] who selects none on the console keeps getting snapshots and will not know why.


**How to reproduce.** cd "<checkout>/player" && python -m pytest -q tests/test_camera_config.py::test_console_none_keeps_config_toml_camera (passes: apply(local, {"source": "none"}, cam) returns "rtsp://local/1" and cam.source stays "rtsp"). Code trace: player/player/camera_config.py:91 source="none" -> :98 rtsp_url() returns "" -> :99-100 falls back to cfg.camera_source/cfg.camera_rtsp_url -> :101 set_source("rtsp", "rtsp://local/1"). Compare docs/automation.md:448-449 ("the fetched configuration wins whenever the manifest carries the key") and :400-401 ("Set none ... so its bridge stays down").


**Fix.** Two doc edits, no code change. (1) docs/automation.md:446-449 replace "the fetched configuration wins whenever the manifest carries the key" with "the fetched configuration wins whenever it names a camera (`wyze` or `rtsp`); a console answer of `none` leaves a hand-written `[camera]` table in charge (see camera.md), so delete that table to switch a camera off from the console." (2) docs/automation.md:400-402 change "Set `none` on a device that has no camera so its bridge stays down" to "Set `none` on a device that has no camera (the bridge is left as it is, and a hand-written `[camera]` table in config.toml still applies)". No test changes needed.


**Verify on hardware.** On a Pi with a [camera] table in /etc/projector-player/config.toml and the device set to none on the console: journalctl -u projector-player.service -n 200 | grep "camera config" shows "camera config vN applied: source=rtsp" (or wyze) rather than source=none, and snapshots keep appearing on the Devices page.


### L27. camera.md troubleshooting: "camera capture started" is logged even with no camera configured

- Severity: **Low**. Area: Docs versus code. Where: `docs/camera.md:267`.
- Existing test covers it: **no**. No test asserts the "camera capture started" log text or its "(no camera yet)" suffix. A caplog check in player/tests/test_camera.py (start CameraCapture with source none, assert "(no camera yet)" in the INFO record; start with an rtsp_url, assert the URL appears) would pin the wording the docs rely on. Not required for a docs fix.

**What is wrong.** The first troubleshooting bullet says a running camera thread "logs camera capture started at boot" as the way to tell whether [camera] is in effect. Since zero-config the thread always starts (daemon.py:415-416) and logs "camera capture started: (no camera yet) every 10s" when nothing is configured (camera.py:136), so the line is present on every Pi and proves nothing; the owner grepping for it concludes the camera is set up.


**How to reproduce.** grep -n 'no camera yet' player/player/camera.py (line 136); grep -n 'CameraCapture(cfg)' player/player/daemon.py (line 415, unconditional); sed -n 266,267p docs/camera.md.


**Fix.** Replace docs/camera.md:266-267 "a running camera thread logs `camera capture started` at boot." with: "the camera thread always starts and logs `camera capture started: ... every 10s`; if it says `(no camera yet)` nothing is configured, if it shows an `rtsp://` URL the camera is in use. A console-set camera also logs `camera config v<n> applied: source=<name>` after the first sync, and any later change logs `camera source updated: ...`." Docs-only; no tests change.


**Verify on hardware.** On a Pi with no [camera] table: journalctl -u projector-player.service -b | grep 'camera capture started' should print "camera capture started: (no camera yet) every 10s".


### L28. Tests pin technical camera_error / sync_error strings instead of guarding plain English

- Severity: **Low**. Area: Test gaps. Where: `player/tests/test_camera.py:90`.
- Existing test covers it: **no**. No test guards console-facing wording. test_screens.py::test_error_and_offline_wording covers only projector-screen phrases. test_camera.py::test_grab_failures_raise_clear_errors, test_grab_timeout and test_sync.py::test_failing_item_does_not_abort_sync, test_sha256_mismatch..., test_dropped_download_leaves_part_and_resumes_with_range are the tests that currently pin the jargon and would need their match= / == fragments updated in the same change as any rewrite.

**What is wrong.** camera_error, sync_error and projector_error are sent verbatim to the console (daemon.py:280-282) and rendered to the owner unchanged (cloud/src/pages/devices.js:122, dashboard.js:33). camera.py:120 additionally prefixes the exception class name, so the console shows e.g. 'CameraError: ffmpeg failed (exit 1): Connection refused'. The suite pins this jargon (test_camera.py:85-92 match='exit 1'/'no JPEG'/'too large'; test_sync.py:167 == 'download failed: big.mp4: ConnectionError', :144 'sha256 mismatch', :75/:103/:116 'HTTP 404') and has no negative-word guard; test_screens.py:70 only asserts positive phrases for on-screen text, so no precedent exists for console params either.


**How to reproduce.** cd "<checkout>/player" && grep -n "CameraError(" player/camera.py (lines 41-77) && sed -n 118,130p player/camera.py (type(e).__name__ prefix) && grep -n "match=\|download failed" tests/test_camera.py tests/test_sync.py && grep -n "camera_error" ../cloud/src/pages/devices.js


**Fix.** Keep it in the existing files, no new module. Add to player/tests/conftest.py: JARGON = ("ffmpeg", "HTTP", "errno", "rc=", "Error", "exit ", "JPEG", "sha256", "Traceback"). In test_camera.py::test_grab_failures_raise_clear_errors and test_grab_timeout, capture the exception (with pytest.raises(CameraError, match=text) as e:) and add assert not any(j in str(e.value) for j in JARGON). In test_sync.py, at each produced sync_error (lines 103, 116, 144, 167) and in test_projector's timeout case (projector.error), add the same one-line JARGON assertion. Drop the capital-letter check (house style is lowercase) and the <=200 check (already enforced by ERROR_MAX_LEN / SYNC_ERROR_MAX_LEN and cheap to assert separately if wanted). These assertions fail today, so land them together with the plain-English rewrite of camera.py:41-77, sync.py:143/214/227/373 and projector.py:133, updating the match=/== fragments in the same commit. Do not touch test_sync.py:74-75: that sync_error is a test input echoed back, not a produced message.


**Verify on hardware.** Not needed; string-only. Optional on a Pi with a dead camera URL: journalctl -u projector-player -n 50 | grep -i 'camera' and compare with the Camera: warn box on the console device page.


### L29. --uninstall is tested only for the getty unmask order; the file/unit/user removal list is unverified

- Severity: **Low**. Area: Test gaps. Where: `player/tests/test_installer_pi.py:111`.
- Existing test covers it: **partial**. Only test_uninstall_unmasks_getty_before_enabling_it (player/tests/test_installer_pi.py:111) covers lines 129-132. No test in player/tests or tools/flasher/tests runs lines 104-128 or 134-144. The test that would cover it: test_uninstall_removes_everything_it_installed (prototype verified passing, see corrected_fix). No existing test needs changing.

**What is wrong.** test_uninstall_unmasks_getty_before_enabling_it slices just the tty1 block. The rest of the uninstall branch (install-player.sh:104-145): disabling five units, rm of the unit files, sudoers drop-in, INSTALL_DIR/.prev/src-*/DATA_DIR/ETC_DIR/socket, docker rm, userdel, rmdir /opt/piplayer, and the intentional leftovers (cloudflared binary + apt list + keyring, Docker, /etc/ssh/sshd_config.d/projection5000.conf, projection5000-provision.service and /var/log/projection5000-provision.log written by firstrun/provision) have no test. A wrong path or a missing `|| true` under set -euo pipefail would abort the uninstall halfway and the suite would not notice.


**How to reproduce.** Confirmed by running the block: extract SRC from 'if [[ "${UNINSTALL}" == 1 ]]; then' to the 'fi' after 'echo "==> Player removed."', replace /etc/systemd/system/, /etc/sudoers.d/, /opt/piplayer and /tmp/projector-mpv.sock with tmp_path prefixes, set INSTALL_DIR/DATA_DIR/ETC_DIR/USER_NAME, stub systemctl/docker/userdel to append to $LOG and id to return 0, pre-create the six unit files, the sudoers file, player, player.prev, src-abc, cms, data, etc dirs and the socket, then bash it. Today: rc 0, everything but cms removed, 12 logged calls. The point is that no existing test does this, so a regression would go unnoticed.


**Fix.** Keep the finder's test but fix two things. (1) Do not rely only on the INSTALL_DIR/DATA_DIR/ETC_DIR variables: install-player.sh:135 and :137 hard-code `/opt/piplayer/src-*`, `/tmp/projector-mpv.sock` and `rmdir /opt/piplayer`, so the finder's fix as written would run `rm -rf /opt/piplayer/src-*` against the real filesystem on a Linux dev box or CI. Rewrite those literals with str.replace (no sed needed) before running: block.replace("/etc/systemd/system/", tmp+"/unit-").replace("/etc/sudoers.d/", tmp+"/sudoers-").replace("/opt/piplayer", tmp+"/opt-piplayer").replace("/tmp/projector-mpv.sock", tmp+"/mpv.sock"). (2) Slice the block as SRC[SRC.index('if [[ "${UNINSTALL}" == 1 ]]; then'):SRC.index("# --upgrade with a config.toml in place")] and append 'echo NOTREACHED' to assert the `exit 0` at line 144 fires. Stubs: systemctl/docker/userdel log to $LOG; `id() { return 0; }`; `command -v docker` needs no stub because a shell function satisfies it. Pre-create the six unit files, the sudoers file, INSTALL_DIR, INSTALL_DIR.prev, opt-piplayer/src-abc, DATA_DIR, ETC_DIR, mpv.sock, plus opt-piplayer/cms and a cloudflared.list stand-in. Assert rc 0, "Player removed." in stdout and NOTREACHED absent, every pre-created removable path gone, cms dir and cloudflared.list untouched, and the log contains `systemctl disable --now` for projector-player.service projector-mpv.service / projector-wyze-bridge.service / projector-player-postcheck.timer / projector-cloudflared.service, `docker rm -f projector-wyze-bridge`, and `userdel projector`. Add `assert "projection5000-provision" not in block` with a comment that the firstrun provision unit and /etc/ssh/sshd_config.d/projection5000.conf are deliberately left (or add them to line 115/127 if the owner wants a clean uninstall). A working prototype is at docs/audits/2026-09-20-pi-os/repro/test_uninstall_probe.py (run from player/ with --rootdir=.); it passes against the current script.


**Verify on hardware.** sudo bash /opt/piplayer/player/deploy/install-player.sh --uninstall; echo rc=$?; ls /etc/systemd/system | grep -i -E 'projector|projection'; ls /etc/sudoers.d; ls -d /opt/piplayer/* /var/lib/projector-player /etc/projector-player 2>&1; id projector; systemctl is-enabled getty@tty1.service


### L30. Sudoers drop-in contents are never parsed or cross-checked against the daemon's sudo calls; visudo -c not asserted

- Severity: **Low**. Area: Test gaps. Where: `player/tests/test_tunnel.py:141`.
- Existing test covers it: **partial**. test_tunnel.py::test_unit_runs_cloudflared_with_the_token_file_and_tolerates_its_absence (lines 142-143) and test_camera_config.py lines 218-219 grep four of the sixteen rules as substrings. No test parses the heredoc, asserts the exact rule set, checks chmod 440 / visudo -c, or cross-checks the daemon's sudo command literals. The proposed test_sudoers_dropin_is_minimal_and_matches_the_daemon in player/tests/test_installer_pi.py would cover it; no existing test needs changing.

**What is wrong.** test_unit_runs_cloudflared_with_the_token_file_and_tolerates_its_absence (test_tunnel.py:142-143) and test_wyze_bridge... (test_camera_config.py:218-219) grep four NOPASSWD lines; nothing else inspects the /etc/sudoers.d/projector-player heredoc (install-player.sh:277-296), the chmod 440 (:297) or the visudo -c (:298). A future edit could add ALL, drop the projector user restriction, widen the wildcard to update-os.sh, or delete the visudo -c line and the suite would stay green. The daemon's sudo argv literals (commands.py:24,32; camera_config.py:81; tunnel.py:33; updater.py:59,66) are pinned individually but never cross-checked against the rule set, so a renamed unit or moved script would only surface on the Pi as "sudo: a password is required" (install-player.sh:299-302 smoke-tests only the mpv rule, and only as a non-fatal WARNING).


**How to reproduce.** grep -rn 'visudo\|sudoers\|NOPASSWD' player/tests tools/flasher/tests -> test_tunnel.py:3 (docstring), :142-143 and test_camera_config.py:218-219 only. grep -n 'def test_' player/tests/test_installer_pi.py shows no test executes the sudoers block. Mutation check: delete install-player.sh:298 (visudo -c) or change :293 to 'update-os.sh *' in a scratch copy and rerun pytest: still 277 pass.


**Fix.**

```text
Add to player/tests/test_installer_pi.py (SRC is already loaded there at line 14), no other test changes needed:

def test_sudoers_dropin_is_minimal_and_matches_the_daemon():
    from player import camera_config, tunnel, updater
    tail = SRC[SRC.index("cat > /etc/sudoers.d/projector-player <<'EOF'\n"):]
    body = tail.split("<<'EOF'\n", 1)[1].split("\nEOF\n", 1)[0]
    cmds = set()
    for line in (l for l in body.splitlines() if l and not l.startswith("#")):
        m = re.fullmatch(r"projector ALL=\(ALL\) NOPASSWD: (/\S+(?: \S+)*)", line)
        assert m, line
        cmds.add(m.group(1))
    assert cmds == {
        "/sbin/reboot", "/usr/sbin/reboot",
        *(f"{b}/systemctl restart projector-{u}.service"
          for b in ("/bin", "/usr/bin") for u in ("mpv", "player", "wyze-bridge", "cloudflared")),
        "/opt/piplayer/player/deploy/update-player.sh *",
        "/usr/bin/bash /opt/piplayer/player/deploy/update-player.sh *",
        "/opt/piplayer/player/deploy/update-os.sh",
        "/usr/bin/bash /opt/piplayer/player/deploy/update-os.sh",
    }
    assert "chmod 440 /etc/sudoers.d/projector-player\nvisudo -c -f /etc/sudoers.d/projector-player" in tail
    # sudo matches the literal path the daemon passes (no canonicalising), so each call must hit a rule
    calls = {
        "/sbin/reboot",
        "/bin/systemctl restart projector-mpv.service",
        f"/bin/systemctl restart {camera_config.BRIDGE_UNIT}",
        f"/bin/systemctl restart {tunnel.UNIT}",
        f"{updater.SCRIPT_DIR}/update-player.sh main",
        f"{updater.SCRIPT_DIR}/update-os.sh",
    }
    for call in calls:
        assert any(re.fullmatch(re.escape(c).replace(r"\*", ".*"), call) for c in cmds), call

Changes versus the finder's fix: the expected set must include the four `/usr/bin/bash ...` rules (install-player.sh:292, :294) which the finder's ellipsis left out; derive the daemon call strings from the module constants (BRIDGE_UNIT, UNIT, SCRIPT_DIR) rather than regex-grepping `["sudo","-n",...]` literals out of source, since updater.py builds them with f-strings and a source grep would be brittle. Verified: this test passes against the current worktree (prototype in docs/audits/2026-09-20-pi-os/repro/sudoers/test_sudoers.py).
```


**Verify on hardware.** On a Pi after install: sudo -n -u projector sudo -n -l ; sudo visudo -c -f /etc/sudoers.d/projector-player ; stat -c '%a %U' /etc/sudoers.d/projector-player (expect 440 root) ; sudo -n -u projector sudo -n /bin/systemctl restart projector-mpv.service && echo ok


### L31. No CI workflow and no shellcheck run in the test suite; scripts are only checked with bash -n

- Severity: **Low**. Area: Test gaps. Where: `player/tests/test_tunnel.py:206`.
- Existing test covers it: **no**. test_installer_parses (player/tests/test_tunnel.py:205) and test_scripts_parse_in_bash (tools/flasher/tests/test_firstboot.py:357) only run bash -n; nothing runs shellcheck and update-player.sh / update-os.sh are not even bash -n checked. A test_scripts_pass_shellcheck in each suite (skipif shellcheck not on PATH) would cover it.

**What is wrong.** The repository has no .github/workflows (ls fails) and no test invokes shellcheck; test_installer_parses (bash -n install-player.sh) and test_scripts_parse_in_bash (bash -n on rendered firstrun/provision variants) only catch syntax errors, not the quoting/`set -e` pitfalls shellcheck reports. update-player.sh and update-os.sh are not even bash -n checked outside the end-to-end run. Today shellcheck is clean (already run), which is exactly when a guard is cheap to add so it stays clean.


**How to reproduce.** ls .github -> no such directory; grep -rn shellcheck player tools -> no matches.


**Fix.** Fix is correct and minimal; two refinements. (1) Put the player-side test next to test_installer_parses in player/tests/test_tunnel.py (where DEPLOY is defined and bash -n already lives) and widen that existing test to all three scripts: `@pytest.mark.parametrize("name", ["install-player.sh", "update-player.sh", "update-os.sh"])` so update-*.sh get bash -n even where shellcheck is absent; then add `@pytest.mark.skipif(shutil.which("shellcheck") is None, reason="shellcheck not installed") def test_scripts_pass_shellcheck(): for p in sorted(DEPLOY.glob("*.sh")): res = subprocess.run(["shellcheck", "-S", "warning", str(p)], capture_output=True, text=True); assert res.returncode == 0, res.stdout`. (2) In tools/flasher/tests/test_firstboot.py, inside the existing loop in test_scripts_parse_in_bash (line 357) after the bash -n call, add `if shutil.which("shellcheck"): subprocess.run(["shellcheck", "-s", "bash", "-S", "warning", str(p)], check=True)` (shutil is already imported) rather than duplicating the render table in a second test. Use `-S warning` (verified: all 7 scripts pass at -S style today, but warning is the level that stays cheap to keep clean). CI: minimal .github/workflows/tests.yml on ubuntu-latest: `sudo apt-get install -y shellcheck`, then `pip install -r cms/requirements` equivalents and run `pytest -q` in player/ and `pytest -q tests/test_firstboot.py` in tools/flasher/. No existing test changes or breaks; the new tests skip on this Windows box because shellcheck.exe lives only in the npx cache, so add it to PATH or pass its path via a SHELLCHECK env var if local enforcement is wanted.


### L32. provision.sh clock fallback (no NTP: date -s from the console's Date header) is never run by the harness

- Severity: **Low**. Area: Test gaps. Where: `tools/flasher/tests/test_firstboot.py:395`.
- Existing test covers it: **partial**. test_provision_contents_with_token (test_firstboot.py:306) asserts only string presence of NTPSynchronized and date -s "$D". No test in player/tests or tools/flasher/tests runs the no-NTP branch. The proposed test_provision_seeds_the_clock_from_the_console_when_ntp_is_off would cover it; no existing test needs changing (verified: 98 pass with the patched harness).

**What is wrong.** _provision_harness stubs timedatectl as `echo yes`, so every provision test takes the NTP-synced branch; test_provision_contents_with_token only greps for 'NTPSynchronized' and 'date -s "$D"'. The 24x `sleep 5` wait loop, the `curl -sSk ... -D -` header fetch, the sed on 'Date:' and the `date -s` call (provision.sh:60-68) have no runtime coverage, and the harness's curl stub answers /api/health with no output and exits 0, so D would be empty. A wrong sed expression, a curl that returns the Cloudflare Access redirect without a Date header, or a `date -s` failure would only show up on a Pi whose RTC-less clock is at the image build date (TLS then fails against the console and step 2 never finishes). Also 'sleep 5' is not replaced by the harness, so a no-NTP test would take 2 minutes unless it is.


**How to reproduce.** Read _provision_harness lines 376-446: timedatectl stub is a constant 'yes'; the curl stub's health branch is `echo "$url" >>"$REC/health"; exit 0` (no headers). No test passes a timedatectl override.


**Fix.**

Minimal, verified on a scratch copy (all 97 existing tests still pass, new test passes). In tools/flasher/tests/test_firstboot.py:
1. Signature: `def _provision_harness(tmp_path, script, responses, install_fails=False, ntp="yes", console_date="")`.
2. Line 395: `stub("timedatectl", f"echo {ntp}\n")` and add `stub("date", f'[ "$1" = -s ] && {{ echo "$2" >>{shlex.quote(_msys(rec))}/date; exit 0; }}; echo Thu Jan 1 00:00:00 UTC 2026\n')` (a full stub, not exec of the real date: `date -s` needs root and the script only uses `$(date)` for log text).
3. curl stub: `'out=; url=; hdr='` and add `-D) hdr=1;;` to the case; health branch becomes `f'case "$url" in */api/health) echo "$url" >>"$REC/health"; [ -z "$hdr" ] || printf "HTTP/1.1 200 OK\\r\\n%s\\r\\n" {shlex.quote("Date: " + console_date if console_date else "")}; exit 0;; esac'` (real curl -D output has CRLF, which exercises the `tr -d "\r"`).
4. Append `.replace("sleep 5", "sleep 0")` to the replace chain at line 435 (safe: "sleep 5" is not a substring of "sleep 60"/"sleep 15").
5. Return dict: add `"date_set": read("date").splitlines()`.
6. New test:
```
@pytest.mark.skipif(shutil.which("bash") is None, reason="bash not available")
def test_provision_seeds_the_clock_from_the_console_when_ntp_is_off(tmp_path):
    s = firstboot.render_provision(cfg(token="tok-on-card-0123456789"))
    r = _provision_harness(tmp_path, s, [], ntp="no", console_date="Sun, 20 Sep 2026 12:00:00 GMT")
    assert r["rc"] == 0, r["log"]
    assert r["date_set"] == ["Sun, 20 Sep 2026 12:00:00 GMT"]
    assert "clock set from console: Sun, 20 Sep 2026 12:00:00 GMT" in r["log"] and "NTP synced: no" in r["log"]
    # No Date header (console behind a redirect): nothing is set, install still proceeds.
    (tmp_path / "b").mkdir()
    r = _provision_harness(tmp_path / "b", s, [], ntp="no")
    assert r["rc"] == 0, r["log"]
    assert r["date_set"] == [] and "clock set from console" not in r["log"] and "NTP synced: no" in r["log"]
```
Note the finder's version used `exec /usr/bin/date` (does not exist on Git Bash) and forgot to mkdir the second tmp dir; both corrected above.


**Verify on hardware.** On a Pi with the player card: sudo timedatectl set-ntp false; sudo date -s '2024-01-01'; sudo systemctl start projection5000-provision.service; tail -f /var/log/projection5000-provision.log (expect 'clock set from console: <RFC 1123 date>' then 'NTP synced: no' and step 2 proceeding). Also: curl -sSk --max-time 10 -o /dev/null -D - https://<console>/api/health | grep -i '^date:' to confirm the Cloudflare edge returns a Date header even on the Access redirect.


### L33. Give-up path only tested for installer failure: enrollment-rejected REASON, the WAITS>=8 hint and secret-free setup-failed.log are not asserted

- Severity: **Low**. Area: Test gaps. Where: `tools/flasher/tests/test_firstboot.py:474`.
- Existing test covers it: **partial**. test_provision_retries_enrollment_and_install (tools/flasher/tests/test_firstboot.py:474) covers the installer give-up screen and the parked log; test_provision_enrolls_then_installs (:448) covers secret hygiene only on the success path. No test covers the enrollment-rejected REASON, the slow-network hint, or a secret-free setup-failed.log. The three tests in the fix close all of it; nothing existing needs changing.

**What is wrong.** test_provision_retries_enrollment_and_install checks the give-up screen after 3 failed installs ('The player did not install.') and that setup-failed.log holds 'GAVE UP'. Not covered: (a) giving up after 20 (harness: 3) consecutive enrollment failures, i.e. a rotated enrollment key (curl 22 / HTTP 401), where the screen must read 'The console did not accept this projector (enrollment failed).' and the log must carry the 're-flash the card' advice; (b) the setup-failed.log copy in the token-on-card variant must not contain the device token (the only secret assertions, `key not in r['log']`, live in the success test); (c) the 'This is taking longer than usual' hint after 8 failed health checks, since the curl stub always answers health with rc 0. All three are owner-facing texts in the no-SSH failure path.


**How to reproduce.** Static: grep tests/test_firstboot.py for 'did not accept', 'taking longer', 're-flash': only line 94 (string order in rendered script), none in the harness tests. Dynamic proof the code is right and the tests are simply missing: docs/audits/2026-09-20-pi-os/repro/test_giveup_probe.py (uses _provision_harness with responses [22,22,23] and cfg(token=...) install_fails=True) passes both assertions sets.


**Fix.**

```text
Additive only, no existing test changes. (1) Extend the harness signature at test_firstboot.py:376 with `health_fails: int = 0`, write `(rec / "health-fails").write_text(str(health_fails))` next to the install-fails marker, and change the curl stub line at :403 to: 'case "$url" in */api/health) echo "$url" >>"$REC/health"; [ "$(wc -l <"$REC/health")" -gt "$(cat "$REC/health-fails")" ] && exit 0 || exit 7;; esac'. (2) Add after :494:

@pytest.mark.skipif(shutil.which("bash") is None, reason="bash not available")
def test_provision_gives_up_when_the_console_rejects_enrollment(tmp_path):
    key = "aB0-_" * 8 + "=="
    r = _provision_harness(tmp_path, firstboot.render_provision(cfg(enrollment_key=key)), [22, 22, 22])
    assert r["rc"] == 1 and r["enroll_calls"] == 3, r["log"]
    last = r["tty"][r["tty"].rindex("\033[2J"):]
    assert "Setup did not finish." in last and "The console did not accept this projector" in last
    assert r["boot_log"] and "re-flash the card" in r["boot_log"] and "GAVE UP after 3 attempts" in r["boot_log"]
    assert key not in r["boot_log"] and key not in r["tty"]

@pytest.mark.skipif(shutil.which("bash") is None, reason="bash not available")
def test_provision_give_up_log_on_card_holds_no_token(tmp_path):
    r = _provision_harness(tmp_path, firstboot.render_provision(cfg(token="tok-on-card-0123456789")), [], install_fails=True)
    assert r["rc"] == 1 and r["enroll_calls"] == 0
    assert r["boot_log"] and "tok-on-card" not in r["boot_log"] and "tok-on-card" not in r["tty"]

@pytest.mark.skipif(shutil.which("bash") is None, reason="bash not available")
def test_provision_shows_the_slow_network_hint_after_eight_failed_health_checks(tmp_path):
    r = _provision_harness(tmp_path, firstboot.render_provision(cfg(token="tok-on-card-0123456789")), [], health_fails=8)
    assert r["rc"] == 0 and r["health_calls"] == 9, r["log"]
    assert r["tty"].count("This is taking longer than usual") == 1

Verified: all three pass against the current script in a scratch run (sleep 15 is already stubbed to 0 by the harness, so 8 polls cost nothing).
```


**Verify on hardware.** Not needed; harness settles it. On a Pi with a rotated key, after 20 attempts: cat /boot/firmware/setup-failed.log should end in 'GAVE UP after 20 attempts' and grep -c 'aB0' (the key) should print 0; the HDMI screen should read 'The console did not accept this projector (enrollment failed).'


### L34. firstrun.sh idempotence (second boot after a power cut before finish) is not tested

- Severity: **Low**. Area: Test gaps. Where: `tools/flasher/tests/test_firstboot.py:540`.
- Existing test covers it: **no**. No existing test in tools/flasher/tests or player/tests runs the rendered firstrun.sh twice. test_firstrun_wipes_itself_and_logs_rc and test_firstrun_key_and_static_ip_under_bash (test_firstboot.py:547, :569) are single-run. The proposed test_firstrun_survives_a_second_run would cover it.

**What is wrong.** test_firstrun_wipes_itself_and_logs_rc and test_firstrun_key_and_static_ip_under_bash run the rendered firstrun.sh exactly once (one subprocess.run in _run_firstrun, test_firstboot.py:540). Because cmdline.txt keeps systemd.run=... until firstrun.sh:139 and the script wipes itself only in finish (:15), a power cut before the end re-runs every step on the next boot. Re-running under the same harness completes with rc 0, keeps one authorized_keys line and identical sshd/NM files; only if the cut landed after the FAT provision files were removed (:137-138) but before the cmdline sed and sync persisted does the retry log 'install -m -> rc=1' twice and skip firstrun.ok (a cut during the earlier steps retries cleanly and firstrun.ok is written). Nothing asserts any of this, so a future non-idempotent step (>> on authorized_keys, useradd without the id guard, mkdir without -p) would go unnoticed.


**How to reproduce.** cd <checkout>/tools/flasher && run a scratch pytest that imports tests/test_firstboot._run_firstrun, cfg, PUBKEY, _msys with the getent/chown/install stubs from test_firstrun_key_and_static_ip_under_bash, then re-writes boot/firstrun.sh (same .replace() chain) and boot/cmdline.txt (with firstboot.CMDLINE_ARGS) and runs bash on it again. Observed: rc 0; firstrun.log has two 'firstrun start' and two 'firstrun done' lines; first run '2 step(s) failed' (set_keymap stub rc=3, raspi-config missing), second run '4 step(s) failed' with the extra two being 'install -m -> rc=1'; authorized_keys has one key; sshd drop-in and NM keyfile identical; sbin-projection5000-provision.sh intact; no firstrun.ok.


**Fix.** Two small pieces. (1) tools/flasher/firstboot.py, the two `run install -m` lines (rendered firstrun.sh:119-120): guard each with the source's existence so a retried boot does not count already-moved files as failures, e.g. `[ -f "$BOOT/projection5000-provision.sh" ] && run install -m 0700 "$BOOT/projection5000-provision.sh" /usr/local/sbin/projection5000-provision.sh` and the same for the tarball; then a retried first boot ends with 0 failed and writes firstrun.ok, matching README.md:203/385. (2) tools/flasher/tests/test_firstboot.py: split the substitution block of _run_firstrun (lines 523-532) into `_rendered_firstrun(tmp_path, c)` returning the substituted script (same replacements, bin_dir = tmp_path / "bin"), have _run_firstrun call it (signature unchanged, so the two existing bash tests need no change), and add test_firstrun_survives_a_second_run: run _run_firstrun with the key/static-IP stubs from test_firstrun_key_and_static_ip_under_bash, record the sshd drop-in and NM keyfile bytes and the first run's failed count (parse `(\d+) step\(s\) failed`), then write boot/firstrun.sh from _rendered_firstrun and restore cmdline.txt with firstboot.CMDLINE_ARGS (the power-cut state), run bash again with the same env, and assert rc == 0, log.count("firstrun done") == 2, authorized_keys has exactly one ssh-ed25519 line, sshd drop-in and NM keyfile are byte-identical, the sbin provision copy is intact, firstrun.sh is gone and cmdline.txt is cleaned, and the second run's failed count equals the first run's (do not hardcode an absolute number: the harness already fails raspi-config rc=127 and set_keymap rc=3, so counts differ per cfg variant; without change (1) assert first + 2 instead). Verified the scratch version of this test passes against the current script apart from the failed-count line.


**Verify on hardware.** On a fresh card: pull power about 20 s into the first boot (during set_keymap/set_wlan), power up again, wait for the reboot, then on the PC or via SSH: cat /boot/firmware/firstrun.log (expect two 'firstrun start' lines, every step rc=0 in the second run, a final 'firstrun done ...: 0 step(s) failed') and ls /boot/firmware/firstrun.ok; then ssh -i %APPDATA%\Projection5000\ssh\id_ed25519 projector-admin@<hostname>.local 'wc -l ~/.ssh/authorized_keys; cat /etc/ssh/sshd_config.d/projection5000.conf; systemctl is-enabled projection5000-provision.service'.



## Verify on hardware

Nothing in this audit ran on a Pi. These findings carry a "Verify on hardware" block with the exact commands; run them on one Pi 5, one Pi 4, one Pi 3 and one Zero W (32-bit) before fixing: H1, H2, H3, H4, H5, H6, H7, H8, M1, M2, M3, M4, M5, M6, M7, M8, M9, M10, M11, M12, M13, M14, M15, M16, M17, M18, M19, M20, M21, M22, M23, M24, M25, M26, M27, M28, M29, M30, M31, M32, M33, M34, M36, M37, M38, M39, M41, M42, M43, M44, M45, L1, L2, L3, L4, L5, L6, L7, L8, L9, L10, L12, L13, L14, L15, L16, L17, L18, L19, L20, L21, L22, L23, L26, L27, L28, L29, L30, L32, L33, L34.


## Checked and rejected

Suspects that did not survive verification, kept here so the same ground is not covered twice.

- `firstrun-kernel-messages-over-setup-screen`: The premise about cmdline.txt is right (pi-gen master stage1/00-boot-files/files/cmdline.txt is "console=serial0,115200 console=tty1 root=ROOTDEV rootfstype=ext4 fsck.repair=yes rootwait resize" and no other pi-gen step adds quiet; tools/flasher/firstboot.py:508-514 only appends CMDLINE_ARGS from line 41), but the conclusion is wrong because the console log level is already lowered by the OS: raspberrypi-sys-mods 1:20260914 (the version in the trixie arm64 and armhf Packages indexes, downloaded and unpacked from archive.raspberrypi.com) ships /etc/sysctl.d/98-rpi.conf containing "kernel.printk = 3 4 1 3", and firstrun.sh:7 depends on that same package (imager_custom), so it is always installed. systemd-sysctl.service is Before=sysinit.target; the systemd.run= unit (kernel-command-line.service, systemd-run-generator, no DefaultDependencies=no) is therefore After=sysinit.target basic.target, and projection5000-provision.service (firstrun.sh:121-133) is WantedBy=multi-user.target After=network-online.target, so by the time screen_init/screen run (rendered firstrun.sh:44-45, provision.sh:33-34) console_loglevel is 3 and only KERN_EMERG/ALERT/CRIT reach tty1; the brcmfmac, wlan0 authenticated, usb and cfg80211 KERN_INFO lines named in the finding are never printed under the Step 1/Step 2 text. Kernel boot spew emitted before sysctl runs is wiped by the ESC[2J ESC[H in screen() (firstboot.py:66). The only residual is systemd's own "[ OK ] ..." status lines: systemd 257 (Debian 257.13-1~deb13u1) prints them only while the manager is INITIALIZING/STARTING/STOPPING (manager_should_show_status in src/core/manager.c), so at most one or two lines such as "Reached target Multi-User System" can race provision.sh's first screen() in the tens of milliseconds after the Type=simple service forks, and nothing prints during the later apt install; that is cosmetic and is not the defect described. Boot-2 kernel/systemd text scrolling between Step 1 and Step 2 (before provision.sh draws) is ordinary Pi boot output, not text under the setup screen.

- `installer-vc4-gpu-mem-not-set`: The factual part holds: nothing in the repo touches gpu_mem (grep of player/deploy/*.sh, tools/flasher/*.py: only the comment at install-player.sh:43), and install-player.sh:213-220 only appends hwdec=v4l2m2m-copy to mpv.conf. But the defect as described rests on a wrong mechanism. On Trixie the V4L2 M2M decoder is the bcm2835-codec kernel driver: its capture (decoded-frame) buffers, which double as the H.264 reference/DPB pool, are videobuf2 dma-contig allocations from Linux CMA, handed to the firmware in zero-copy mode via vc-sm-cma; they are not carved out of gpu_mem. The gpu_mem=128 advice comes from the old MMAL/OMX (omxplayer) era where output frames lived in the GPU relocatable heap. The firmware default (76 MB on 1 GB boards) is what Raspberry Pi OS ships for exactly this stateful-V4L2 path, so 1080p H.264 decode is not expected to fail allocation there; if it stutters on a Pi 3 the docs already say so (docs/make-master-image.md:31, docs/adding-a-pi.md:96). The proposed fix would also be harmful: gpu_mem=128 on 512 MB boards (Zero 2 W, Zero, Pi 1) removes a quarter of RAM from a Python daemon plus mpv, and on 1 GB boards drops MemTotal under CAMERA_MIN_MEM_KB (install-player.sh:44, pi_info.py:12), as the finder concedes. The finder also self-rates confidence as low.

- `ssl-clock-error-shown-as-offline-backoff`: The routing is real (player/player/daemon.py:337-340 catches requests.RequestException, of which SSLError is a subclass, and sets last_failure="unreachable"), but the scenario and impact do not hold. (1) First boot is already handled: the rendered provision.sh (from tools/flasher/firstboot.py) waits up to 120 s for timedatectl NTPSynchronized=yes and otherwise sets the clock from the console's HTTP Date header (curl -k) before enroll() and install-player.sh run; projector-player.service is only enabled/started by install-player.sh:403 after that, so the daemon never polls with the build-epoch clock on first boot. (2) On later boots (no RTC / long unplug) run_cycle still calls _reconcile_mpv (daemon.py:352), which pushes the cached manifest loaded at daemon.py:420-421; the OFFLINE screen is only shown when last_manifest is None (daemon.py:202-205) or nothing is on disk (daemon.py:246-253), so a Pi that has synced once keeps playing cached content and the owner never sees "check network". (3) The backoff claim is overstated: state.backoff resets to poll_interval on the first success (daemon.py:313), so a clock that timesyncd fixes within seconds of network-online costs at most one already-scheduled interval (30 s -> 60 s after one failure); reaching 300 s requires four consecutive failures over ~7.5 min, i.e. NTP itself broken, which "check network" describes correctly. The only residue is a journal line "CMS unreachable: ...certificate is not yet valid..." and the same OFFLINE wording, which is cosmetic.

- `update-os-reboots-immediately-while-playing`: The code fact is accurate: player/player/daemon.py:320-322 calls _run_reboot() right after the update_status param is accepted, with no check of player_status or time of day. But the two claims that turn this into a defect are false. (1) "The only warning is in docs/automation.md": cloud/src/pages/devices.js:434 and :435 put a confirm dialog on both per-device buttons, "Update OS packages on <name>? The Pi may reboot." and "Update player and OS on <name>? The Pi may reboot.", and the button hint reads "reboots if the OS asks for it". The owner is told at click time, in plain English, and must confirm. (2) "'Update all players' with --then-os can take a whole fleet down": the fleet form at devices.js:525-528 submits a hidden command=update-player only; there is no UI that queues update-os or update-all fleet-wide, and its confirm says "Playback restarts on each Pi." The reboot is also not "on the very next sync" after the click: apt-get update/upgrade/autoremove runs first in a detached unit (deploy/update-os.sh:55-66), and the reboot only follows a successful report (test_daemon.py:620-636 pins that). What remains is an explicitly requested, confirmed, documented reboot: a design preference (defer to the quiet window), not a bug. The rubric's Medium requires "wrong under plausible conditions" or "misleading text"; the text shown to the owner matches what happens.

- `nightly-already-at-churns-console-status`: The behaviour exists exactly as described (update-player.sh:145-149 writes "already at <sha>" with ok=true; updater.py:105-131 reports any new mtime; cloud/src/api.js:137-139 overwrites last_update_*), but it is a documented, load-bearing design, not a defect. (1) That write is the cross-restart 20 h guard for the nightly: updater.py:191-201 last_player_update_started reads `started` from update-status.json and auto_update_due (updater.py:204-211) uses it; suppress the write and a daemon restart inside the window re-clones the repo every restart. (2) It is the only outcome the console ever gets for a manual update-player at an unchanged ref: commands.py:150-159 reports just "executing update-player" and defers the result to update_status, so without the write the owner clicks Update and sees nothing (docs/automation.md:231 promises the "already at" report).
