(3) The console text is literally true, not misleading: devices.js:229-236 renders "Update ok main · 6 h ago: already at <sha>", and devices.js:29 documents that a failed update is a fault "until the next report"; a transient failure that no longer reproduces is resolved, a persistent one re-fails nightly and stays red, and every report is kept in the audit log (api.js:140, device_update_reported) plus /var/lib/projector-player/update.log. The proposed fix is also unimplementable as stated: update-status.json has no sha field (only ref, message, previous_version), so "same sha and ok=true" cannot be tested without parsing the message. "When was the code last changed" is a console feature request (a separate column), not a Pi-side bug.

- `postcheck-misses-slow-crash-loops`: The timing arithmetic is accurate (install-player.sh:398 OnActiveSec=2min; update-player.sh:162 arms the timer before the restart at :164; projector-player.service:14 RestartSec=5 and :20 TimeoutStopSec=45 mean NRestarts>=3 at update-player.sh:89 needs a death within roughly 22-37 s of each start), but the failure class it needs does not exist in the daemon. Every trigger the finding names is inside a blanket handler: sync/manifest at player/daemon.py:345-348, mpv reconcile at :353-354, screenshot/camera/commands/auto-update at :388-389; the only code outside those handlers in the loop (_gather_mpv_status :56-78, updater.pending_status :105-127, StatusScreens.tick status.py:71-75) goes through MpvClient._request (mpv_client.py:81-109), which swallows OSError/timeout and returns None. A Python exception on the first screenshot, camera snapshot or a large manifest therefore logs and continues; it cannot produce a 45 s crash loop. What remains is a native crash or OOM-kill, which is not a common path and would hit the .prev code equally. The "nothing retries or reverts" claim is also overstated: a daemon that runs 45 s per cycle still completes a sync (poll 30 s, config.py:100), so the console's update block `release` (updater.py:188, commands.py:129-130) can pin the previous ref and update-player will install it (sha != PREV_VERSION at update-player.sh:145). The 3-restart threshold is tuned to the only deaths the daemon can actually have: import/startup failures.

- `ffmpeg-input-scheme-unrestricted`: The code fact is accurate (player/player/camera_config.py:47 returns the console value unchecked and player/player/camera.py:47 passes it as `-i`), but neither attacker in the description gains anything. A malicious editor cannot get a non-rtsp value into the console: the only write path for camera_rtsp_url is the Devices form, which rejects anything not matching RTSP_URL_RE = /^rtsps?:\/\/[^\s"'<>]{1,2040}$/ (cloud/src/pages/devices.js:164 and :688, "camera_rtsp_url must be an rtsp:// or rtsps:// URL"), and cameraConfig() at devices.js:183 only ever forwards that stored value. A compromised console (Worker/D1) already holds every secret the ffmpeg tty trick could exfiltrate: it issued the device token in config.toml, it is the sole sender of the Wyze credentials in wyze.env and of the tunnel token, and it can already push any http(s) URL as a media item that the Pi fetches with no host restriction (player/player/sync.py:176) plus reboot/update commands, so the LAN-probe and file-read gains are strictly subsumed by existing console powers. An allowlist in grab_jpeg would be harmless defense in depth, but there is no reachable defect at the trust boundary as described.

- `player-fault-screen-mpv`: The text exists exactly as cited (player/player/screens.py:193 "restart mpv from the console or reboot"; player/player/daemon.py:232 "mpv could not start any of N items"), but the defect as described does not hold. The finding's core claim, that "reboot is not something they can do without SSH", is false: the console's Devices page has a "Reboot Pi" button right next to "Restart mpv" (cloud/src/pages/devices.js:429-430; COMMANDS at devices.js:16 includes "reboot"; the Pi executes both in player/player/commands.py:149-160). The help line therefore maps one-to-one onto the two console buttons, and its use of the word "mpv" matches the console's own button label, so a non-technical owner can find the action by name. The daemon also keeps retrying on its own (daemon.py:227-229, every FAULT_RETRY_CYCLES idle cycles), so plugging the HDMI lead back in later recovers without any owner action. What remains is a wording preference (the word "mpv" and the terse "could not start any of 3 items"), which is a cosmetic nit, not misleading text, and the audit brief says not to pad with style nits. If kept at all, it belongs at Low with the description corrected.

- `tests-projector-auto-resend-after-restart`: The code behaviour is as described (player/player/projector.py:105 applied_want is in-memory, :155-159 change-only gate, daemon.py:126 fresh Projector per PlayerState, daemon.py:387 maybe_apply every cycle; a two-Projector script re-sends the packet), but it is not a defect in any supported configuration. power_on/power_off are learned as separate names (cloud/src/manifest.js:85, devices.js:241 "Power On"/"Power Off") and CEC uses absolute --image-view-on/--standby (projector.py:91), so re-asserting the current want after a restart is idempotent and is the correct reconcile: after a power cut that drops the Pi and the projector together (the common restart on a projection install), the Pi must send "on" again, and the proposed fix (persist applied_want) would leave the projector dark until the console next flips want. The harm scenario needs a toggle-only remote learned into both names, which the design cannot support anyway: the retry at projector.py:131 double-toggles when a send times out after transmitting, and any drift between real and tracked state inverts the projector on the next want change. The "every nightly update" premise is also wrong: update-player.sh:145-149 returns "already at <sha>" without restarting the service when nothing changed, so restarts are real updates, reboots and crashes only. Residual (Low, separate finding): the Devices page does not tell the owner that Power On and Power Off must be distinct buttons.

- `tests-per-board-matrix-incomplete`: The two missing boards are input-equivalent to rows already in the matrix, so no installer branch is untested. The installer decides everything from PI_ARCH, PI_MEM_KB, PI_SOC and PI_MACHINE (player/deploy/install-player.sh:50-64 pi_caps; :61 camera gate = arm64 && mem >= 900000; :213 hwdec = PI_SOC matches bcm283[567]; :307 cloudflared = PI_MACHINE armv6l). Pi 2 B V1.2 (arm64, aarch64, ~935000 kB, bcm2837) is byte-for-byte the "pi3" row at test_installer_pi.py:21 except the model string; Pi 1 B+/A+ (armhf, armv6l, bcm2835, <900000 kB) is the "zero" row at :24 except the model string. Pi 5 is absent from the gate parametrize at :56 but its gate inputs (arm64, 7975000) take the same branch as "pi4", and Pi 5 is in the hwdec parametrize at :132 where its SOC (bcm2712) actually differs. All four gate combinations are asserted (arm64+enough: pi4/pi3; arm64+short: zero2; armhf+enough: pi2; armhf+short: zero), all five SoCs are asserted for hwdec, and test_tunnel.py:169 runs the armv6l cloudflared branch. test_pi_info.py:53-54 likewise already covers the Pi 1 inputs ("armv6l", False, 430000, commented "Zero / Pi 1") and the Pi 2 V1.2 inputs are identical to its Pi 3 line (:51). Adding the rows would only re-run the same branches with a different label; the claimed risk ("Docker on a 512 MB board", "no hwdec on a Pi 1") is already asserted via zero2 and zero.

- `armhf-armv6-pip-deps`: The premise ("PyPI has no armv6l wheels, so pip falls back to a source build") ignores piwheels, which is wired in on every Raspberry Pi OS Trixie image. player/deploy/install-player.sh:180 apt-installs python3-venv and python3-pip; Debian trixie python3.13-venv Depends on python3-pip-whl, and the Raspberry Pi fork python3-pip-whl 25.1.1+dfsg-1+rpt1 (archive.raspberrypi.com, debian/python3-pip-whl.install: "debian/pip.conf etc", changelog "add pip.conf to use piwheels") installs /etc/pip.conf with extra-index-url=https://www.piwheels.org/simple. pip reads the global /etc/pip.conf inside a venv too (the venv pip.conf is additive, not a replacement), and the venv pip created at install-player.sh:225-227 therefore resolves against piwheels. piwheels' project pages (checked 2026-09-21) list Trixie/Python 3.13 wheels for both armv6l and armv7l: pillow-12.3.0-cp313-cp313-linux_armv6l.whl / linux_armv7l.whl and cryptography-50.0.1-cp313-abi3-linux_armv6l.whl / linux_armv7l.whl (cryptography is broadlink's only compiled transitive dep; broadlink itself is pure Python). Nothing in requirements.txt needs libjpeg-dev/rustc on these boards; build-essential is even in the Lite image (pi-gen stage2/01-sys-tweaks/00-packages), though it is not needed. Residual, much smaller than claimed: requirements.txt:1-3 has floor pins only, so in the window between a new Pillow/cryptography PyPI release and piwheels finishing its build, pip's resolver will pick the newer sdist from PyPI and attempt a source build (which fails on rustc/libjpeg-dev); an upper bound or exact pin would close that, and the piwheels Pillow wheel also expects libopenjp2-7/liblcms2-2/libwebpmux3/libwebpdemux2 at runtime (libopenjp2-7 and liblcms2-2 come with ffmpeg/mpv; the webp ones only affect WebP decoding, which Pillow degrades gracefully without).

- `mpv-socket-in-tmp`: The cited lines are accurate (projector-mpv.service:14 RuntimeDirectory=projector-mpv, :16 --input-ipc-server=/tmp/projector-mpv.sock) but neither claimed defect exists on this box. (1) Stale socket after a crash: mpv's input/ipc-unix.c calls unlink(ipc_un.sun_path) immediately before bind(), so a leftover /tmp/projector-mpv.sock owned by projector is removed on every restart; the finding's own hardware_verify (SIGKILL, wait, reconnect) succeeds. (2) "Any local user can pre-create/replace the path": the only accounts on the Pi are root, projector-admin (created with -G sudo, firstboot.py:276) and the system user projector (install-player.sh:184), and mpv, the player daemon and cloudflared all run as projector (projector-mpv.service:9, projector-player.service:8, projector-cloudflared.service:16); the wyze-bridge container mounts nothing from host /tmp. There is no lower-privileged principal that could gain anything by squatting the path, so this is not a security hole, and systemd-tmpfiles age cleanup skips sockets listed in /proc/net/unix so a live socket is never swept. projector-player.service already lists /tmp in ReadWritePaths and has no PrivateTmp, so the current path works; moving it to /run/projector-mpv would be a harmless hygiene change requiring edits to install-player.sh:264, config.py:99, README.md:266, docs/adding-a-pi.md:331 and docs/make-master-image.md:67, but it fixes nothing real.

- `os-update-immediate-reboot`: The immediate reboot is deliberate, owner-confirmed and tested, not a defect. update-os only runs on an explicit console command (player/player/updater.py:29 COMMANDS; player/player/commands.py:149-162); the nightly auto-update path calls run_update_player(ref) with no OS step (updater.py:179-193), so "regardless of the auto-update window" is inapplicable: there is no automated path that reaches daemon.py:320. Before the command is queued the console shows "Upgrade OS packages on <name>? It reboots afterwards if the kernel changed." (cms/app/templates/devices.html:258) and "Update all" is confirmed too (devices.html:263). update-os.sh's header (player/deploy/update-os.sh:8-9) and docs/automation.md:286-290 document exactly this sequence ("reports the status on its next sync and then reboots ... issue it inside the quiet window"). Playback being interrupted is the same consequence as the owner pressing "Reboot Pi", which they were told to expect when they clicked the button. Deferring the reboot to a window would leave the Pi on a half-applied kernel/libc for hours after the owner asked for the update and saw it reported, which is worse. The only real nit is that the confirm text says "if the kernel changed" while /var/run/reboot-required is also set by libc/systemd upgrades; that is wording, not this finding.

- `critic-0-32-bit-boards-pi-zero-zero-w-pi-1-pi-2-v`: The finding's premise ("nothing guards" the 32-bit wheel supply, "only succeeds if piwheels serves cp313/armv6l") is wrong on both counts. (1) Raspberry Pi OS Trixie armhf ships piwheels preconfigured: the current image the flasher resolves (raspios_lite_armhf_latest -> 2026-09-15-raspios-trixie-armhf-lite, per its .info package list) contains python3-pip-whl 25.1.1+dfsg-1+rpt1, and I unpacked that exact .deb from archive.raspberrypi.com: it installs /etc/pip.conf with `extra-index-url=https://www.piwheels.org/simple`, a site-wide config the venv's pip at install-player.sh:225-227 reads (the apt line at :180 installs python3-venv/python3-pip from the same repo, so it is present even on an older image). (2) piwheels does serve Trixie/cp313 wheels for every compiled dependency: `pip download --only-binary=:all: --platform linux_armv6l --python-version 3.13 --abi cp313/abi3 --extra-index-url https://www.piwheels.org/simple -r player/requirements.txt` resolves fully (pillow-12.3.0-cp313-cp313-linux_armv6l.whl, cryptography-50.0.1-cp313-abi3-linux_armv6l.whl, cffi-2.1.1, plus pure wheels), and identically for linux_armv7l; the same command against PyPI alone fails, confirming piwheels is the (already configured) supplier. So no source build, no Rust and no libjpeg-dev are needed on Pi Zero/Zero W/Pi 1/Pi 2 v1.1, and the "every first boot fails" claim does not hold. Only a narrow residual remains: `pillow>=10` / `broadlink>=0.18` are unpinned, so in the hours after a new Pillow/cryptography release lands on PyPI before piwheels has built it, pip would choose the newer sdist over the older piwheels wheel and the source build would fail (retry loop in provision.sh then keeps retrying until piwheels catches up). That is a Low hygiene point, fixed by `--prefer-binary` (one flag), not the Medium defect described. The claim that the harness never exercises the pip step is accurate but is not itself a defect.

- `critic-2-screenshots-every-60-s-are-written-throu`: The code trace is accurate (player/player/screenshots.py:20 mkstemp in the default temp dir, :25 mpv writes the JPEG there; projector-player.service:22 ReadWritePaths=/tmp with no PrivateTmp, so it is the real /tmp), but the premise that /tmp landing on the SD card is "left to the OS default" cuts the other way on the supported OS. All flasher images are Raspberry Pi OS Lite Trixie (Debian 13), and Debian 13 / Raspberry Pi OS Trixie mount /tmp as tmpfs by default via systemd's tmp.mount (confirmed by Raspberry Pi's own rpi-sb-provisioner issue #254, "on trixie /tmp is now a tmpfs"). So the 1,440 daily screenshot writes go to RAM on every supported board, and moving mkstemp to a RuntimeDirectory would change nothing. The finding also cites an "SD-wear checklist item (tmpfs)" that does not exist anywhere in the repo (grep for tmpfs/tmp.mount/wear returns only the endurance-card note in docs/hardware.md:20). The only residual case is a hand-installed Bookworm host via install-player.sh (its header says Bookworm is accepted), where /tmp is disk-backed; that is outside the flasher path the audit scopes and the finding's own size math (200 KB every 60 s) is well within what the recommended endurance card handles.


## What was checked and found sound

- **firstrun**: Checked and found sound (against the real pios/trixie sources of userconf-pi, raspberrypi-sys-mods imager_custom, raspi-config, pi-gen and the Pi OS cloud-init 25.2+rpt20 patches, not guessed): boot order on Trixie is initramfs resize_early/set_partuuid (rewrites cmdline.txt diskid and strips ' resize', leaving our systemd.run tokens intact, no extra reboot) then boot 1 = kernel-command-line.target running firstrun.sh, boot 2 = multi-user with provision.service; the imager_fixup hook is not triggered (it only matches /boot/firstrun.sh). The Trixie imager_custom set_wlan writes a NetworkManager keyfile directly (uuid is a Depends), so there is no live-join timer that could give up; hidden SSID (-h -> hidden=true), open network (empty psk -> no wifi-security), static Wi-Fi and wired-static keyfiles, and the fallback keyfile all render correctly, and NM generates a UUID for keyfiles without one. do_wifi_country appends cfg80211.ieee80211_regdom after our tokens and the token-wise sed keeps it (tested). Hostname: do_hostname falls back to /etc/hostname + /etc/hosts sed when dbus is absent. Key-only SSH is enforced: Debian's sshd_config Includes sshd_config.d/*.conf first (first match wins), the only stock drop-in (rename_user.conf, Banner only) is removed by cancel-rename, and Pi OS cloud-init's patched cloud.cfg has no users: key and the stock seed files are comment-only, so cloud-init on boot 2 creates no user, writes no 50-cloud-init.conf and keeps the hostname (rpt20 patch); root has '*'. userconf renames uid 1000 pi (home, group, subuid, shell) and is idempotent on a re-run; HOME_DIR via getent survives a half-done rename. The serial console getty is a password prompt nobody can pass. The setup screen is a no-op without a writable tty, falls back to 25x80 when stty size reports 0, setterm/setfont failures are swallowed, and setterm's --blank/--powersave/--cursor are escape sequences on stdout (tty1), so they work under systemd. 32-bit boards: identical script and packages. wipe() zero-fills in place before unlink and the log never receives a secret (run() logs $1 $2; hash is $3; no tool echoes arguments). firstrun.log, a cleaned cmdline.txt and (unreachably, see finding) firstrun.ok are all that remain after success; after an interrupted boot the re-run heals everything except the sub-second cleanup window. shellcheck was clean per the task and was not repeated; 97 flasher tests pass.

- **provision**: Read every line of tools/flasher/firstboot.py, the rendered provision.sh and provision-token.sh, the provision tests and harness in tools/flasher/tests/test_firstboot.py, cloud/src/api.js enroll() (239-286), auth.js throttle constants, player/deploy/install-player.sh and the mpv/player units. Sound: the real order is unit After=network-online -> NTP wait -> single Date fallback -> infinite health loop -> 20x(enroll if no token; rm/extract; installer) with 60 s spacing -> disable unit, rm script and archive -> 'Ready'; on give-up exit 1, the enabled unit runs again on the next boot and re-enrolls (same token per console), so a re-flash is not needed for transient causes. Secret hygiene checked: the enrollment key travels via environment into python json.dumps and stdin into curl (never argv), the response lands in a 0600 mktemp that is deleted, only DEVICE_ID and CMS_URL are echoed, install-player.sh has no set -x and never prints DEVICE_TOKEN or the Wi-Fi PSK, so /var/log/projection5000-provision.log and the setup-failed.log copy contain no secret (the harness asserts key and token are absent). shlex quoting of name/key/URL holds (hostile-name test passes through bash -n and the harness). Token-on-card variant never calls /api/enroll and sets CMS_URL=CONSOLE. Idempotence of the install loop is fine apart from the dpkg-interrupted case: rm -rf of /opt/projection5000-src, useradd/usermod/venv/rsync/pip re-runs, and a token kept in memory across failed installer attempts are all correct. Enrollment throttling (10 failures per IP, 60 s lock) is harmless against the 60 s retry spacing. The -k Date fallback is no weaker than NTP and date -s is safely quoted. Tests: tools/flasher/tests/test_firstboot.py (97) pass; the provision harness reproduces the REASON misattribution with curl rc 7.

- **installer**: Read every line of install-player.sh, test_installer_pi.py, pi_info.py, requirements.txt, plus the four unit files, mpv.conf, update-player.sh, update-os.sh, the rendered firstrun.sh/provision.sh and the flasher's tarball builder; ran tests/test_installer_pi.py (14 pass). Sound: the capability gate (arm64 and MemTotal >= 900000 kB) is implemented identically in pi_caps and pi_info.camera_supported (dpkg arch vs aarch64+64-bit interpreter give the same answer, including the 64-bit-kernel-over-armhf case), the harness covers all six boards and the off-Pi case; the sudoers drop-in is tightly scoped (exact systemctl restart argv for the four units, both /bin and /usr/bin spellings which the daemon uses, reboot, and the two root-owned scripts; the `*` on update-player.sh is bounded by the script's own ref regex and the fixed second argument, Debian's env_reset strips PIPLAYER_ROOT/PIPLAYER_REPO_URL, and the install tree is chowned root:root so the daemon cannot edit what it is allowed to sudo); the daemon's sudo invocations in commands.py, camera_config.py, tunnel.py and updater.py all match a rule; config.toml/wyze.env are 600 owned by projector, DATA_DIR is 700 and root-written update.log/update-status.json are readable because projector owns the directory; --uninstall unmasks getty@tty1 before enabling it (tested), stops/removes every unit including the postcheck timer, the sudoers file, the trees and the user, and its documented leftovers (cloudflared, Docker, the apt list) are deliberate; a plain re-run is idempotent (guarded useradd, rsync with .venv/deploy excluded, venv reuse, KEEP_KEYS carrying non-installer TOML keys); the Cloudflare apt repo publishes arm64 and armhf so Pi 2 V1.1 gets the .deb path correctly; piwheels serves cp313 wheels for cryptography on linux_armv6l/armv7l so the 32-bit Trixie pip install is feasible provided the image's /etc/pip.conf (piwheels extra-index) is present, which I could not confirm offline; getty@tty1 disable/stop in the installer is a harmless no-op after firstrun's mask; hwdec=v4l2m2m-copy for bcm283[567] is the right decoder on those boards and mpv reads the last hwdec line. Not flagged as findings: (ALL) vs (root) in sudoers (cosmetic), the nightly chown -R of DATA_DIR making update-status.json projector-writable (trivial), the KEEP_KEYS TOML rewrite not quoting DEVICE_TOKEN/WYZE_CAMERA (console-issued values), and Pis installed before the hwdec logic keeping their old mpv.conf (only .dist is refreshed; a log NOTE the owner never sees).

- **units**: Checked and found sound: restart policies (mpv has StartLimitIntervalSec=0; player RestartSec=5 and cloudflared RestartSec=10 can never hit the default 5-starts-in-10-s limit; config._fail sleeps 30 s and resets SIGTERM to default so systemctl stop is immediate and the journal is not spun); TimeoutStopSec=45 is covered by test_audit2_player; both update scripts re-exec through systemd-run so root apt/cp work outside the daemon's ProtectSystem=full namespace, and sudo/systemctl/reboot/cec-ctl all work inside it (/run and /dev are untouched, the daemon has no DeviceAllow and inherits the video group for /dev/cec0); ProtectHome does not hit the projector home under /var/lib; no PrivateTmp anywhere so the /tmp IPC socket and screenshot temp files are shared correctly; mpv unlinks the socket path before bind and on exit, the socket is 0755 so only projector/root can connect, live sockets are exempt from systemd-tmpfiles cleanup and a hostile pre-created path would need a second local user that does not exist; DeviceAllow list covers drm/render, alsa, v4l2, media and dma_heap for the v4l2m2m and v4l2-request paths and the unit has the matching supplementary groups; the /dev/tty open failure in mpv's VT switcher is a harmless warning for a service; RuntimeDirectory keeps the pipewire/pulse probes from touching /run/user; cloudflared token comes from a 0600 file via env with exec, both ConditionPathExists lines are right and a systemctl restart re-evaluates them, --no-autoupdate is set; Wyze ports are bound to 127.0.0.1 only and the extra container ports (1935/8888/8889/8189) are not published; SD-card wear is change-driven everywhere (manifest/index/status PNG writes only on a state change, the 8 MB overlay only on a playlist change, camera snapshots stay in memory, screenshot temp files are unlinked, per-cycle logging is silent when nothing changes) and the offline screen's once-a-minute re-render is a cosmetic flicker at most; the 1 GB camera cut-off matches pi_info.py; the missing MemoryMax on the bridge is acceptable because streams are on demand. Time sync: the player unit has no After=time-sync.target, but on Debian that target is reached without a synced clock unless systemd-time-wait-sync is enabled (which would block an offline kiosk), the clock is restored from timesyncd's clock file at boot, and a certificate-not-yet-valid failure after a months-long power-off only shows the OFFLINE screen on an uncached device for one 60 s backoff before it self-heals, so no change recommended. Pi 5 4K: H.264 is software there as the README already says; HEVC uses the drm path; hdmi_enable_4kp60 is a Pi 4 firmware knob and status stills are 1080p. install-player.sh 375-432: unit copies, daemon-reload, enable set, postcheck oneshot+timer (OnActiveSec=2min lines up with 3 restarts of a 35 s config-error loop), getty disable tolerates the flasher's mask, restart order mpv-then-player is right.

- **daemon**: Read every line of daemon.py, sync.py, mpv_client.py, status.py and the strings/_details/render of screens.py, plus commands.py, updater.py, tunnel.py, projector.py, camera.py, screenshots.py, the systemd units and the console's api.js/alerts.js/uploads.js for the contract. Sound: mpv restart detection by pid resets applied_hash and re-pushes (also during a CMS outage); mpv-down is reported and clears when the socket returns; seamless reload and the stale-entry swap (covered by test_mpv_client/test_audit2_player); resume from .part with Range, 206 hashing the existing prefix, 416 restart-once then error, 200-to-Range restart from byte 0, a complete .part finished locally; per-item failure isolation with ENOSPC keyed text placed first; the size+mtime media index short-circuit; SyncInterrupted keeps the .part and stops hashing between chunks; camera_error/projector_error are sent as '' to clear; update_status is reported once and marked only after the console took it, with the reboot after update-os; the commands ledger is written before destructive actions; syncing screens never interrupt playing content and a status screen is reported as idle; 401/403 map to the TOKEN screen whose text matches docs; token is stripped from the cached manifest; Pillow 1080p renders (about 6 MB RGB, PNG level 1) and the 8 MB BGRA overlay are fine for 512 MB boards; poll sleep breaks on force-sync and on stop. Scratch reproductions live in docs/audits/2026-09-20-pi-os/repro/daemon_audit/test_audit_daemon.py (no repo file touched).

- **updates**: Read every line of updater.py, commands.py, update-player.sh, update-os.sh, the sudoers and postcheck sections of install-player.sh (plus the rest of the installer for the --upgrade path), test_updater.py, test_commands.py, docs/automation.md section C, the projector-player unit, and the relevant parts of daemon.py, projector.py, cloud/src/manifest.js and api.js. Ran the full player suite baseline and a scratch harness on the existing fake-root fixture to confirm the rollback-then-retry loop and the --then-os status overwrite. Sound: command execution order (ledger written and 'executing' reported before reboot/restart-mpv/update-*, re-delivery answered without re-running, 200-entry cap, atomic write via tmp+replace, corrupt ledger tolerated); reused ids after a console DB restore are told apart by issued_at, which the cloud manifest does send (the comment in commands.py 45-48 saying it does not is stale but harmless); no console string reaches a shell: every subprocess is an argv list, ir-learn:<name> only lands in JSON, projector-on/off map to fixed cec-ctl/broadlink calls, and update refs are validated twice (GIT_REF in the daemon and the same regex in the script, both rejecting a leading '-' and whitespace, so sudo argument injection through the '*' rule is not possible); sudo -n with Debian's default env_reset strips PIPLAYER_ROOT/PIPLAYER_REPO_URL, and the install tree is root-owned so the projector user cannot edit the scripts it is allowed to sudo; the systemd-run detach with a fixed unit name gives a real single-update lock; the fail trap restores .prev on any installer error and cleans src-*; the 500-char update_status cap, once-only reporting by mtime marker, and the 20 h / window logic (including midnight wrap and local tz) behave as documented; write_status is atomic and world-readable; update-os keeps config files (--force-confold) and runs non-interactively. Not raised as findings: update.log grows without rotation (a few hundred KB per real update), a full clone for sha refs fetches whole history into /opt, and the daemon's 60 s subprocess timeout only matters if systemd-run is missing, which it never is on Pi OS.

- **peripherals**: Read every line of projector.py, camera.py, camera_config.py, tunnel.py, their four test files, automation.md E and G, camera.md and cloudflare.js 1-130, plus the daemon integration (daemon.py 272-442), sync.py manifest cache, the three units and the installer's cec/docker/sudoers blocks. Sound: IR codes are validated base64 and rejected when empty, broadlink is imported lazily, learn polls through ReadError/StorageError with a 30 s window, commands are idempotent on redelivery; the projector user is in the video group so /dev/cec0 (root:video 0660) is reachable and the unit sets no PrivateDevices; tunnel.token is written 0600 atomically, compared whitespace-insensitively, stripped from the world-readable manifest cache (sync.py:71), never logged, passed to cloudflared via TUNNEL_TOKEN in the environment not argv, and the unit is skipped by ConditionPathExists when the file or binary is missing (missing binary logged once); the console stores tunnel_id only after the Access app exists, so a fresh tunnel never goes public without a policy; wyze.env is 0600, rewritten only on change, read by dockerd as root, '=' inside passwords is fine (docker splits on the first '='), embedded newlines cannot come from a web form (the .strip() would however corrupt a password with leading/trailing spaces: edge, not filed); ffmpeg runs from an argv list with -nostdin, a 15 s timeout, one capture in flight in a daemon thread that catches every exception, JPEG magic check and a 2 MiB cap; CAMERA_MIN_INTERVAL floors the interval; the installer skips Docker and the bridge on non-arm64 or <900 MB boards and pi_info reports camera_supported by the same rule so the console hides the camera form for them. Needs hardware, not filed: bridge memory on 1 GB arm64 boards (Pi 3, Pi 2 V1.2) alongside mpv 1080p and cloudflared: run `free -m; docker stats --no-stream; journalctl -k | grep -i -E 'oom|killed process'` after an hour of playback with the bridge streaming; /dev/cec0 being HDMI0 on Pi 5 (`cec-ctl -d /dev/cec0 -S` with the cable on each port); cec-ctl exit status on NACK (covered in the CEC finding's hardware_verify).

- **plain-english**: Read every line of screens.py, status.py, daemon.py, sync.py, camera.py, camera_config.py, projector.py, tunnel.py, updater.py, commands.py, update-player.sh, update-os.sh, firstboot.py and install-player.sh, rendered every ScreenState variant through layout() and the Ethernet-only provision script through render_provision(), and traced each string to where the console shows it (dashboard.js:30/33, devices.js:122/205/232-236/264/375). Sound as written: the BOOTING, NOT ASSIGNED (with its Devices-page help line), STANDING BY (next-rule line) and SYNCING (n of m, filename, MB) screens; the four first-boot step screens, "Ready. Waiting for the first video." and "Try N did not finish; trying again in a minute."; install-player.sh's screen texts (its echo hints and mpv.conf notes go only to the provision log); update-os.sh's success message (apt summary, "reboot required"); tunnel.py and camera_config.restart_bridge texts, which reach only the journal; the "already executed" replay logic and the JSON ir-learn result, which the console parses rather than shows; and _age_text ("4 min ago"). player_status "mpv-down" is a contract enum the console relabels itself ("player process down"), so it was left alone. The console URL and device id in every screen's header/footer are identification, not errors, and were not flagged. No em-dash sweep was done (allowed in this repo).

- **docs**: Read every line of docs/adding-a-pi.md, docs/hardware.md, docs/camera.md, docs/automation.md sections B-E and G, README.md (Deploying, Operating notes, Configuration reference, Operations) and tools/flasher/README.md, and compared them against player/deploy/install-player.sh, update-player.sh, update-os.sh, mpv.conf, the four units, tools/flasher/firstboot.py, pimodel.py, the relevant parts of flasher.py/console.py, player/player/*.py, and cloud/src/api.js and pages/devices.js where a doc claim depended on them. Found sound: the first-boot step strings, log paths (firstrun.log, firstrun.ok, /var/log/projection5000-provision.log, /boot/firmware/setup-failed.log), the "Setup did not finish" screen and its reason line, the 20 x 60 s retry loop, the random 32-character Pi password (secrets.token_urlsafe(24)) never shown, key-only SSH via sshd_config.d, the pre-hashed Wi-Fi PSK, the wipe of the two secret-bearing files; install-player.sh's package list, config.toml key preservation, mpv.conf.dist handling on upgrade, sudoers lines, the KEEP_CONFIG/--upgrade contract, --uninstall scope, the automatic hwdec=v4l2m2m-copy on VideoCore IV boards and the manual Pi 4 edit the README describes; section C's update-player flow (clone/tarball, RELEASE compare, .prev, install --upgrade, update-status.json fields, postcheck timer 2 min, NRestarts >= 3 rollback, --then-os, reboot_required reported then reboot, 20 h auto guard); section D's fetch-on-version-change, wyze.env at /var/lib (0600), bridge restart on credential change, camera_supported rule and the --with-wyze downgrade; section E's command results, one retry, act-on-change, cec-ctl sequence, learn timeout; section G's tunnel.token handling, restart only on change, ConditionPathExists on both the token and the binary; camera.md's ports, ffmpeg command, 15 s timeout, 2 MiB cap, 10 min log throttle, every warning string in its table, the [camera] key table and env overrides; README's sync/backoff/24 h verify/token-rotation behaviour and the config reference defaults; adding-a-pi.md's journal lines ("sync complete", "sync incomplete"), 401/403 texts and the sudoers check. Bookworm vs Trixie: nothing in the scripts or units requires Trixie, so the manual-path claim that both work is consistent with the code. No doc claims a password or token is shown or recorded beyond what the flasher README's security note already states.

- **tests**: Checked and found sound: FakeMpv/FakeCms fakes model the IPC framing, Range/206/416 and dropped connections faithfully; mpv crash / pid-change re-push is covered (test_daemon test_mpv_restart_detected_by_pid_and_replayed, test_mpv_restart_during_cms_outage_is_replayed, test_mpv_down_then_back, test_audit2 test_restart_mpv_command_repushes_without_waiting_for_next_poll); download resume, server ignoring Range, a stale .part (416 then restart), complete .part finished locally, ENOSPC surfaced as 'no space left on device' at item and summary level and on the storage-full screen are all covered (test_sync 161-235, 393-407; test_audit2 337-358; test_daemon 413); update-player.sh in-process failure restore, --postcheck ok/rollback, --then-os chaining, bad ref, update-os reboot_required and apt failure are covered end to end in a fake root (test_updater 267-351) and the daemon's report-then-reboot ordering is covered (test_daemon 595-637); nightly window and once-per-window guards are covered; tunnel token rotation (write only on change, restart, missing binary, failed restart, token never echoed to the console) is covered (test_tunnel 54-125); the cloudflared install block is covered for present/apt/deb/offline/armv6 cases; provision.sh enrollment retry, token-on-card bypass, installer retry without re-enrolling, secret hygiene on the success path, the setup screens and the setup-failed.log copy on give-up are covered by a real bash harness; firstrun.sh runs under bash with key-only SSH, static IP, secret wipe (zero-fill verified) and cmdline cleanup; write_firstboot_files is covered including the no-cmdline refusal; the flasher's username/password/key handling is isolated by the autouse sandbox fixture. Both suites run as stated (277 / 97 pass). Findings above are test gaps only; the four Medium ones each hide a defect confirmed by trace or scratch harness (power-loss update, dpkg-interrupted, Pi 3 A+ camera promise, wired-Pi Wi-Fi hint, projector re-send after restart).
